# Open Design Decisions

The queue of questions only Crown can answer.

**How it's used**
- **Claude Code (local, working the backlog):**
  - Never decides an `OPEN` item.
  - When a backlog job needs a decision, it adds or links an entry here, marks the job `BLOCKED (Q-xxx)` in `docs/Backlog.md`, and **moves on to the next unblocked job**. No waiting.
- **Crown:** answers whenever convenient. Ways to do it:
  - from a Claude cloud session (phone or web), talking it through and having it write the answer here and push;
  - by editing this file on GitHub;
  - by telling a local session.
- **Next backlog run:**
  1. `git pull`
  2. sees `ANSWERED`
  3. unblocks the jobs
  4. implements
  5. moves the entry to **Decided** with a link to where it was applied.

**Statuses**
- `OPEN`: waiting on Crown.
- `ANSWERED`: Crown replied; not yet applied.
- `DECIDED`: applied; recorded in the relevant design doc.
- `PARKED`: deliberately later.

**Numbering (Crown, 2026-10-26), from D-081 on:**
- **`Q-<job>`**: a question Claude Code raises while doing a backlog job takes **that job's number**: a question from B-155 is **Q-155**; a second one is Q-155b (the first stays Q-155). The number says which job it belongs to, and Claude Code never needs to pick a free number.
- **`D-xxx`**: design decisions from Crown's chat sessions (cloud), next free D number.
- Existing D-001…D-081 keep their numbers. Both kinds live in this file and use the same entry format and statuses.

**Entry format** (keep each short; one decision per entry):
```

### D-NNN — Title   (or Q-<job> — Title)
**Status:** OPEN · **Raised:** YYYY-MM-DD · **Blocks:** B-xxx, B-yyy
**Question:** one line.
**Context:** 2–4 lines, with file:line.
**Options:** A … / B … / C …
**Recommendation:** X, because …
**Answer (Crown):** _write here_
**Notes for Claude:** _optional: anything to do differently when applying it_
```

**Archive (D-132, B-340):** answered and decided entries live in [`DesignDecisions_Archive.md`](DesignDecisions_Archive.md), same numbers and format. This file keeps what still needs Crown or a job: `OPEN`, `DISCUSSED`, `PARKED`, and `ANSWERED` entries whose jobs are still `TODO` / `BLOCKED`. Once applied, an entry moves to the archive. New entries go here.

---

## Open

### D-017 — Header bar style (UI)

**Question:** Which of the four deckbuilder candidates (`UI_DeckbuilderReuse.md` §3)?
**Answer (Crown):** (2026-10-26) Pick from pictures: B-101 draws all four candidates (plus a crystal-themed one, see D-024) in the combat HUD mockup, and Crown chooses there. **Drawn (B-111, 2026-10-27):** `combat/hud/v2/header_candidates_annotated.png` shows A-E; Crown picks in B-102.

### D-078 — Combat camera: three choices the basic version made
**Status:** ANSWERED · **Raised:** 2026-10-26 (B-156) · **Blocks:** — (B-156 shipped with the recommendation; B-157 tunes it)
**Question:** B-156 had to pick three behaviours D-074 doesn't cover. Keep them?
**Context:** `CombatCameraDirector.cpp` HandleActionStarted / HandleActorTurnStarted. (1) With **camera movement off**, skill cinematics are skipped too (one static view). (2) **Enemy skills** play their cinematics like yours. (3) A cinematic runs **until it ends or the next turn starts** (not cut when the action's damage resolves).
**Options:** A keep all three / B movement-off still plays cinematics (only turn moves stop) / C enemy skills keep the pan-out view (no cinematics) / any mix.
**Recommendation:** A, because movement off is a motion-sickness setting (cinematics move the camera most), enemy cinematics are how you read an incoming hit (design §2, Defence mode), and cutting at damage would chop the end of most shots.
**Answer (Crown):** **A, keep all three** (2026-10-26): movement-off also skips cinematics; enemy skills play their cinematics; a cinematic runs until it ends or the next turn starts. **Keep for now, needs testing** (Crown): mark all three as untested defaults to check in PIE (`docs/PIE/B-156.md`). **Each also becomes a setting** (Crown, 2026-10-26): (1) cinematics on/off separate from camera movement, (2) enemy skill cinematics on/off, (3) skip a cinematic once its hit has landed. Defaults as above.
**Notes for Claude:** _optional_

### D-088 — Actions per turn (discussion: one utility then one battle action; Turn Speed stays)
**Status:** APPROVED, PLAN FIRST (Crown, 2026-11-09: build B-170's plan; code after Crown approves it) · **Raised:** 2026-10-26 (rules review, actions per turn) · **Blocks:** — (turn-system plan, B-170)
**Answer (Crown):** (2026-10-26)
- **Action pips:** each turn you get **action pips** set by your **Reflex** stat; each action (attack, skill, item, …) costs a pip, so a high-Reflex character can act **more than once per turn**.
- **No Turn Speed:** turn order becomes **fixed (round-robin)**, as the deckbuilder's turn belt. A faster-paced game; Reflex is what makes a character "fast".
- **Ice slows Action Speed** (the attack/spell animation speed stat): a full Ice bar is an Action Speed debuff for 2 holder turns. *(D-123: Action Speed is now the one **Speed**; read this as "Ice slows Speed".)*
- **Wind affects action pips:** a full Wind bar costs the target action pips (placeholder: −1 pip on their next turn, D-090).
**Supersedes / changes:**
- D-014/D-030 Wind push-back → pip loss. D-029 Ice → Action Speed slow (Crown's original wording, "slows action speed"; it had been mapped to turn speed).
- D-049 (2:1 turn cap) no longer applies: a **pip cap** replaces it.
- D-054/D-056: the Turn Speed family (`TurnSpeedBuff/Debuff`, `ModifyTurnSpeed`, TurnSpeedStone, the Turn Speed substat) is retired (enums append-only: kept, marked retired).
- ET-18 / one action per turn (kept earlier today) becomes "one action per **pip**".
**Model agreed in discussion (Crown, 2026-10-26, later the same day):**
- **Actions work like Energy:** you **gain 1 action per turn**, banked **up to your Max Actions** (set by Reflex); unused actions carry over. Start each fight with **1 action** and **3 Energy**.
- **Skills cost actions by tier** (by default): **F, E, D = 1 action**; **C, B, A = 2**; **S = 3**; plus their Energy cost. So big skills mean saving up (skip spending this turn, cast it next).
- **Energy:** start 3, gains per turn up to Max Energy (ties to D-089 C).
- **Stats and effects control both:** Max Actions, Max Energy and per-turn gains can be raised or lowered (Reflex, gear, buffs, Wind's pip loss).
**Refined (Crown, 2026-10-26, still discussion only):**
- **Base Max Actions is 2.** You **raise your Max Actions** (stats, gear, effects) to unlock **better skills**.
- **Higher tiers cost many more actions**, e.g. an **S-rank skill needs 7 actions**: you wait turns, use items and effects to build up to it. Replaces the 1/2/3 costs above. A one-per-tier ladder (F 1, E 2, D 3, C 4, B 5, A 6, S 7) is the natural reading of "S = 7"; confirm the exact ladder with Crown.
- So Max Actions becomes the key build stat for big skills, and action-gain effects and items become valuable.

**Simplified (Crown, 2026-10-26, latest; still discussion only), replacing the pool/pip ideas above:** the action-point pool was over-engineering.
- **Each turn: one battle action + one utility action**, so a turn is more tactical than one move. **The split (Crown, 2026-10-26):** **battle action** = basic attack, skill / spell, **move row**; **utility action** = **Defend**, use an item (incl. utility crystals), switch weapon or ring. So you can e.g. attack and Defend in the same turn, or move row and drink a potion. If built, this replaces the separate "one free switch per turn" (ET-19) and "free row move once per turn" (D-096) rules.
- **Energy stays, with low values** (deckbuilder-style small numbers) as the cost of skills.
- No action-point costs by tier.
- **Order (Crown, 2026-10-26):** **utility first, then battle.** Using your battle action **ends your turn**, so any utility action (Defend, item, switch) has to come before it.
- **Turn Speed stays (Crown, 2026-10-26, A):** the Reflex-pip and fixed-turn-order ideas above are dropped. Speed-based turn order, the 2:1 cap (D-049), bonus turns (D-052) and Wind's push-back (D-014) stand; Ice slows Action Speed (as Crown first said, D-029). The Turn Speed family is **not** retired (D-054/D-056 as decided).

**Notes for Claude, open details to bring to Crown (as questions in the plan, B-170):**
- Pip numbers: base pips, Reflex thresholds, the cap (suggested: 1 base, max 3).
- What Emerald's bonus turn (D-052) becomes: +1 pip, or an extra turn in the belt.
- First strike (D-069): +1 pip, or act first in the order.
- Turn order within a round (party order, alternating sides, …).
- Reflex today also widens the defense timing window; keep both jobs or split them.
- Effect durations already count the holder's own turns (D-051), unchanged.

**Confirmed by Crown (2026-11-09):** the fixed rotation and banked actions stay (Turn Speed goes; Reflex sets Max Actions). Next is B-170's plan doc (`docs/analysis/ActionPips.md`). Open for the plan: Speed is split again into Action Speed and Spell Speed (D-151), so say which one Ice slows. **Also (Crown, 2026-11-09):** effects can **give or take actions** (e.g. +1 action now, -1 action next turn), alongside Wind's pip loss; the plan lists them as effect types. **Wind (Crown, 2026-11-09):** the Wind **status bar** (build-up) takes **action pips**; the **Wind crystal effect** makes the target **lose their next turn** in the rotation. **No actions, no turn (Crown, 2026-11-09):** a unit that starts its turn with 0 action pips after the turn's gain (e.g. Wind or a lose-actions effect took them) has its turn skipped.
### D-089 — Should Energy refill each turn?
**Status:** DECIDED, BUILD (Crown, 2026-11-09: changed from C, see the change below) · **Raised:** 2026-10-26 (rules review) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** EP never refills on its own today (ET-29: only effects, items, drains and Broken Darkness absorption). Should it?
**Context:** Max Energy (Spirit) is the pool size; Efficiency (Mind) lowers costs. Deckbuilder: +1 Energy per turn, banked, no cap. Expedition 33: AP +1 per turn plus more from basic attacks and parries. With action pips (D-088), a basic attack that returns Energy becomes a use for spare pips.
**Options:** A no refill (current) / B a share of Max Energy each turn (e.g. 10%; Max Energy then also sets refill) / C Expedition 33 style: a small refill each turn **plus** Energy from basic attacks and Perfect Guards.
**Recommendation:** C, because it rewards good play (timing gives Energy back), gives basic attacks a purpose, keeps casters from running dry, and needs no new stat. Broken Darkness keeps its own absorption system.
**Answer (Crown):** **C** (2026-10-26): Energy **refills a little at the start of each of your turns**, plus extra from **basic attacks** and **Perfect Guards** (Expedition 33 style). Amounts are named constants (`// TODO: tune`); the per-turn share can scale with Max Energy. Broken Darkness keeps its own absorption system (no passive refill, ET-29 for BD).

**Changed by Crown (2026-11-09):** Energy refills **only at the start of each of your turns** (a share of Max Energy, a named constant, `// TODO: tune`). Energy from basic attacks and Perfect Guards is **not** a base rule: it comes from **gear effects** (passives) where an item grants it. Broken Darkness keeps its own absorption (no passive refill). Job: B-173.
### Q-173 — Which turns refill Energy: every turn kind, or Normal turns only?
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-173, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-089 (changed 2026-11-09) says Energy refills "at the start of each of your turns". A character also gets Bonus turns (Emerald) and a ritual's Execution turn. Do those refill too?
**Options / as built:** **A** every turn kind refills, because each runs the same turn-start step (ET-17; Q-044d: an Execution turn is still one of the caster's turns) / **B** only Normal turns refill (a check on the turn type).
**Recommendation:** A. It is what "each of your turns" says, and it keeps one turn-start step for every turn kind (Q-044d). D-089's own reference, Expedition 33, gives AP at the start of a character's turn (Crown's 2026-10-26 context); the deckbuilder's +1 Energy per turn (D-089 context) has no bonus-turn case to compare.
**Answer:** **A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown may change it (B is a one-line turn-type check in `TurnStartEnergy::Apply`'s caller).
**Notes for Claude (as built):** B-173: `ACombatOrchestrator::ProcessStartOfTurnEffects` calls `TurnStartEnergy::Apply` on both of `HandleTurnStarted`'s branches.

### D-099 — Field commands: the battle menu outside battle
**Status:** ANSWERED in part (2026-10-27; details being asked one at a time) · **Raised:** 2026-10-27 (Crown, from Q-185 first strike) · **Blocks:** — (builds on GASMigration decision 2, real-time field abilities)
**Answer (Crown):** (2026-10-27; Metaphor: ReFantazio, Kingdom Hearts)
- **The battle command menu also works on the map**, for different uses: **attacks, spells, abilities, items**.
- **Spells and skills get field utility**: a skill can have an effect outside battle, e.g. a **movement speed spell actually makes you move faster** on the map. Authored per skill.
- **Attacks and spells are free-aimed outside battle:** pick from the menu, the animation plays as in a fight, but there's **no lock-on**: you have to actually hit.
- **Abilities may have two animations:** the battle one (warped to the target) and a **field one (not warped, in place)** so it works without a target.
- **Field durations are in minutes:** a skill's duration in turns becomes minutes on the map, **1 turn = 1 minute** (a 1-turn speed buff lasts 1 minute).
- **Field skills cost Energy (EP)**, as in battle.
- **Time while the menu is open: B, it keeps running** (Kingdom Hearts), because of multiplayer (pillar 7): a shared world can't pause or slow for one player. **Noted for later:** A, slowing time while choosing (Final Fantasy VII Remake), perhaps as a solo-only option.
- **EP on the map: B** (Persona's SP model): EP does **not** refill over time; only **rest spots and items** restore it. EP spent on the map is EP you don't have in the next fight.
- **Battle skills can double as field skills** (a field use is one more side of the same skill), **and there are also field-only skills** (Crown, 2026-10-27, corrected): skills that only do something on the map (e.g. traversal). Where field-only skills are slotted: open.
- **First strike (Q-185):** landing a field attack or spell on an enemy that hasn't noticed you gives your side first strike (it acts first). Until field commands exist, a touch from outside its notice cone stands in (as built).
- **What a field hit does** (Crown, 2026-10-27): a field hit **starts the encounter with turn advantage** (your side acts first, as first strike). No outright kills on the map and no damage carried in. **It also gives status buildup** of its element, carried into the fight (Crown, 2026-10-27). **Holding the field attack with a Water source makes it an Ice attack.** (The earlier idea of an elemental field strike for elemental classes stays a note for later.)
- **Field skills as a whole: pinned for research** (Crown, 2026-10-27). Crown's ideas to explore: **movement through field skills** (flight, speed, etc.), and **evolutions changing how you move** (e.g. a character whose evolution lets them run at lightning speed). Research: `Design/FieldAbilities.md`. **Base movement: everyone gets a fast, free sprint** (Crown, 2026-10-27). **Evolutions give both** a small always-on movement bonus and one active EP-cost movement skill (Crown, 2026-10-27). **Movement order: speed and dash, then glide, flight last; dash evolves into sprint; enough speed lets you wall-run** (Crown, 2026-10-27). Where field-only skills are slotted waits on it.
- **Simplified (Crown, 2026-10-27), replacing the field-use / field-only-slot parts above:**
  - **Everyone:** a fast, free sprint.
  - **One active field skill per character**, set by **level / source** (where it comes from: innate, weapon, ring or crystal, evolution, and its level). It costs EP.
  - **Enhanced Sprint** is a field skill: it lets you **wall-run**.
  - **Evolutions** add a small always-on movement bonus and **augment the field skill to a degree** (e.g. the lightning character's skill becomes a lightning dash).
  - **Sources:** a field skill can draw on a source like battle skills do; **a crystal source still loses durability** when used (`CrystalWear.md`).
  - **Dropped:** separate field uses on battle skills, and field-only skill slots.
  - **Enhanced Sprint also runs on water** (Crown, 2026-10-27).
  - **Flight limits: A** (Crown, 2026-10-27): EP drain while flying, a height ceiling, no-fly zones; **effects can raise them** (an evolution might give unlimited flight; a character with unlimited energy: for another day). **Hidden areas in the sky** are welcome (pillar 10).
  - **Swimming:** everyone can swim (base movement).
  - **Elements change the world: A** (Crown, 2026-10-27): a few general rules defined once (element → material, element → element; materials don't change each other), Breath of the Wild style; 1-2 element shortcuts or secrets per map region.
  - **Enhanced Sprint** is also **faster than the normal sprint** (Elden Ring Nightreign's surge sprint), on top of wall-running.
  - **Every element can do every movement type, each in its own way** (Crown, 2026-10-27, refines the line below), **except Teleport, which is Void only** (Crown, 2026-10-27; Lightning's chain of blinks is its Air dash / Dash style). Movement types unlock through the three aura skills (`Design/FieldAbilities.md`). The **element decides the style**, not which movement type; an elemental source is still needed for the elemental movements (a Generic without one has only the normal dash and sprint).
  - **How a movement type unlocks: A** (Crown, 2026-10-27): each type has its **own unlock skill** (equip or learn it once: unlocked for good, for everyone); **some unlock by hidden conditions** instead (the memory condition system, D-100, pillar 10).
  - **How you get your one movement skill: A** (Crown, 2026-10-27): in the loadout you **pick a source you have available, then pick a movement type you've unlocked**; the source gives it its element style (e.g. a Void source + Teleport = a Void teleport). Strength follows the source's level.
  - **Source picks the skill: A** (Crown, 2026-10-27): the **source decides which field skill** you have (e.g. a Wind crystal glides, a Lightning one dashes) and **its level decides how strong** it is. The source also decides **what is available at all**: e.g. a Generic can't fly without an elemental source that gives flight.
  - **A range of movement** (Crown, 2026-10-27): dash, Enhanced Sprint, glide, flight, teleport, high / double jump, air dash (hover, aim, then dash; hover merged in), super launch, phase / shadow step, mark and recall, **Regroup** (teleport to a party member: co-op catch-up is this spell / movement type, not a free system; Crown, 2026-10-27) (flight hovers when stationary; no grapple, wall climb, mounts or surface surf; element styles share one mechanic per type with a few signature differences; `Design/FieldAbilities.md`). **All are locked until unlocked, except the normal dash**, which everyone has.
  - **Activation:** **double-click L3** (the sprint stick) uses your field movement skill (keyboard: a double-tap of the sprint key, `// TODO` confirm; unique input action name, D-063).
  - **Some field skills are locked** until unlocked: you must have **equipped or learned a specific skill** first. **Once unlocked, anyone can use it** with the matching source: e.g. after the lightning character's lightning speed is unlocked, **any character with a Lightning source** gets lightning speed as their field skill.
**Notes for Claude:** built on GAS (stage 4 abilities, B-043): one ability per skill with a battle and a field mode. Open details: each element's movement style (Crown) (`Design/FieldAbilities.md`).

### D-104 — PvP
**Status:** ANSWERED in part (2026-10-27; rest pinned) · **Raised:** 2026-10-27 (Crown) · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** (2026-10-27)
- **PvP is part of this game: B, planned for now**, so the co-op plumbing (D-103) allows for it. Earlier concept: a **PvP trial door** in the hub (`Resources_Design.md` "Hub→Trial door model + PvP options", June; its "Lord" wording is a placeholder).
- **Modes** (Crown, 2026-10-27): **1v1, 2v2, 3v3** and **Fracture**: **any vs any** (uneven sides such as 1v2 or 2v3, or every player for themselves).
- **Pinned for later** (Crown, 2026-10-27): what gear players bring (own builds with a tier cap, uncapped, or preset loadouts), how matches are found, rewards / stakes.
**Notes for Claude:** the co-op plumbing (B-227 onward) should not assume two sides are fixed at 3 or that only PvE fights exist; nothing else to build for PvP yet.

### D-110 — Unify the primary weapon, secondary weapon and adapter (Resonator) loadouts
**Status:** ANSWERED in part (2026-10-29) · **Raised:** 2026-10-29 (Crown) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** Crown: the primary weapon, secondary weapon and the Resonator's adapter loadout "all do the same thing in different ways"; merge them into **one loadout model**. Weapons are limited to **abilities and elemental abilities** (also valid for adapters, D-108), so the unified approach works; the open issue is **how / where weapons get attached** (crystals, evolutions). Class rules under it: **Generic: 2 weapons; Caster: 1 weapon or 1 adapter; Resonator: its adapter loadout plus a weapon, with the loadout cost rules**, which should **branch out to the other classes**. Ares's trainer adapters must still be portrayable.
**Context:** Crown wants this kind of analysis (unification that cuts code because the rules are known). Analysis: `docs/analysis/UnifiedLoadout_2026-10-29.md` (being written).
**Options:** to come from the analysis.
**Answer (Crown):** (2026-10-29, in progress, one question at a time)
- **Go: A**, the unified model, built incrementally (adapters first) per `analysis/UnifiedLoadout_2026-10-29.md`.
- **Points for everyone (A):** today's costs for every class (plain weapon / adapter 1; with a crystal, stone or fusion 2; weapon with an evolution attached 3; evolution on its own 2; adapter with an evolution 2), and **each class = a budget + a limit**: **Refractor** budget 3, **1 item** (weapon or adapter or evolution) plus its innate spell pool; **Generic** budget 6, up to **2 weapons**, and **keeps one adapter** (B: a Generic may still wear one adapter, as today's ring primary); **Resonator** budget 5, up to **1 weapon**, any number of adapters. Checks: a Refractor with an evolution has no other gear; a Generic may carry two evolved weapons (3 + 3); a Resonator with an evolution has 3 left (e.g. one weapon with an evolution).
- **Class identity:** Generic = weapons, Resonator = adapters, Refractor = innate magic (the only class with an innate element and spell pool; its lever is the innate budget, not more gear).
- **Attachments live on the item (A):** a crystal, stone, fusion or evolution belongs to the weapon / adapter instance and travels with it (saved loadouts, the shared armoury, other characters); the slot only references the item.
- **Adapters cost by state, not form (A):** 1, or 2 with an evolution; the form (ring, pistols, trainers) is look and animation only; a pair (dual pistols) is one item.
- **Note (Crown, 2026-11-08, Q-357 final):** Generic budget 6 → 5 (at most one evolved weapon); the costs are stated as frame + attachment (weapon frame 1, adapter frame 0, crystal +1, evolution on gear +2) and an evolution alone costs 3 (was 2).
- **No primary / secondary difference (A):** a Generic's two weapon slots are equal (same checks, either may hold an evolved weapon, either may be active); fixes the secondary slot's missing spell checks.
- **One active weapon at a time (A):** a Generic's two weapons switch freely; only the active one gives its abilities, stats and passives (no stacking of two evolved weapons).

### D-111 — One owned-items store per player
**Status:** ANSWERED in part (2026-10-29; details one at a time); solo gear sharing and the one party pouch **changed by D-117** (2026-11-03) · **Raised:** 2026-10-29 (`analysis/InventoryArchitecture_2026-10-29.md`) · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** (2026-10-29)
- **A: one store per player** holding every owned instance by GUID (gear, evolutions, crystal and stone stacks, outfit pieces), each entry knowing where it is (stored, carried, worn by whom) and whether it is run-only. The **armoury, storage, pouch and run bag are views** of it, not containers. Loadouts and outfits **reference** items (GUIDs), never copy them. The run is a **layer** on top (run-only flag + upgrade memory per definition, D-097). Lyra (inventory + equipment managers), Monster Hunter (box / pouch / equipment box).
- D-110 stands as step one, with three changes: the slot's GUID is the truth (B-302), the kind-agnostic owned list lands in the store (B-308), and run wear is keyed by item GUID, not asset (new job before B-304).
- **Where it lives (A):** in `UPoolSubsystem` now; moves to `AWoRPlayerState` as an owner-only replicated list when co-op lands (Lyra's placement).
- **Presets (A, Crown 2026-10-29):** two kinds: **lore loadouts** (each authored character's own loadout, read-only, from the character asset: what the loadout-match meter compares against and what a skin reveals) and **custom loadouts** (the player's, saved per character as lists of item GUIDs, up to 5 each; an item that is gone or worn elsewhere shows as an empty slot).
- **Run purchase (A, corrected by Crown 2026-10-29):** you take the **real item** (marked on the run, one instance) **as it is at home**, including crystals you attached in the hub; an item that comes with none can be given one during the run (from the pouch, at rest spots). **At run end it resets** to its state before the run (run wear gone, D-093; anything attached during the run comes off); upgrades follow D-097.
- **Per-loadout item lists retire (A):** the 10-slot party pouch (D-097) is the only place carried items live; a loadout carries no items of its own (Monster Hunter: one pouch).
- **Learned spells and abilities stay per character (A):** stored in the player's store with an owner (e.g. "Mend, owner: Orthos"); another character must learn it too. Gear is shared, skills belong to whoever learned them (Q-091; FFX / Dragon Quest).
- **Outfit pieces are store entries shared by the party (A):** an outfit set is six references; a piece unlocked once can be worn by anyone (cosmetic; Monster Hunter layered armour).
- **Give / request (A, Crown 2026-10-29):** skills move between characters by **give** (one copy: the giver loses it) and **request** (ask a teammate for theirs). **Between co-op players** gear, items and skills all move only by give / request (each player's store stays their own, personal loot D-103). **Within your own party (solo)** gear stays shared (D-020).
- **Co-op visibility (default, reference-backed):** what a player has **equipped** is visible to everyone (others see your gear and form); the rest of their store is owner-only until given (Diablo / Destiny: inspect equipped, private stash).
- **Buy for an AI character (Crown, 2026-10-29):** shops and vendors have a **"buy for"** choice that includes AI party members; in co-op **any player can buy for the AI member** (e.g. the third, recruited character), paying from their own wallet; the purchase goes to that AI character to use. **The owner is whoever bought it** (Crown, 2026-10-29): it counts as part of **the buyer's run**: the instance goes at run end, and its progress is remembered for the buyer (the roguelike layer, below).
- **The roguelike layer (Crown, 2026-10-29, corrected):** two families.
  - **Items** (crystals, stones, potions) are **consumables**, like a health potion: a crystal **used** in a run (attached, losing durability) is **used up at run end**; unused items stay in the pouch / storage (D-097).
  - **Gear** (weapons, adapters, evolutions): gear **found or bought during a run** is a run instance, gone at run end, but its **upgrade progress is remembered** per definition, so it **appears at that level the next run** it turns up in. Gear **brought from home** comes back restored (D-093).
  - **Evolutions can break from wear** in a run; a broken one can be **repaired in the run**, and it **resets outside the run** (never lost: Q-159, Q-159c, D-093).
**Notes for Claude:** bugs found by the analysis to fix regardless: run wear keyed by asset (two identical adapters share one wear record); found gear never dropped at run end; player-edited presets not saved (PIE check). Remaining details: where it lives (pool now, PlayerState with co-op), presets as GUID lists per character, the run purchase marks pieces rather than copying, the per-preset 6 x 3 item slots retire, spells / abilities stay per character, outfits as entries, co-op visibility.

### Q-064a2 — Ice break's name, and whether Water's "Frozen" moves to Ice
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-064a, cloud) · **Blocks:** — (the mechanic ships with the placeholder)
**Question:** What is Ice's break called? Water's break is "Frozen" and Water's damage over time "Frost Bite" today (`WoRGameNames::GetElementBreakName`, `SkillEffectDisplayNames`), names that fit Ice better now that Ice exists. The deckbuilder gives Water the adjective "Tide" and Ice "Frost" (`02-enums.md`, element trait table; "Ice took Frost", Crown, 2026-09-29).
**Context:** B-064a ships the placeholder "Ice break" (`WoRGameNames::GetSkillEffectTypeName(IceBreak)`, `// TODO: Crown`); Water's names are unchanged.
**Options:**
- **A** Water keeps "Frozen" and "Frost Bite"; Ice gets a new name of Crown's choosing.
- **B** Ice takes "Frozen" (and "Frost Bite" if Ice gets a damage over time); Water's break gets a new, tide-like name, matching the deckbuilder's Tide / Frost split.
- **C** Something else.
**Recommendation:** none: names and lore are Crown's (CLAUDE.md, "Recommendations").
**Answer: Crown, 2026-11-12:** Crown's naming rule: **every break is "<Element> Break"** (Fire Break, Water Break, Ice Break...) and **every damage over time is "<Element> DoT"**. No special names ("Frozen", "Frost Bite" go). Built as `LOCTEXT` "{0} Break" / "{0} DoT" with `FText::Format` around the element's name-table entry (`WoRGameNames::FormatBreakName` / `FormatDoTName`, D-061 / D-062; Ice's entry is `GetIceName`, since Ice is a damage type). A capped bar names its effect through `SkillEffectDisplayNames::GetBreakDisplayName`; a plain DoT no longer borrows its element's break name (Fire's break and a Fire DoT were both "Burn"). Follow-up branch `fix/B-064a-crown-answer-cloud` (B-064a is in dev). Build details: Q-064a3.
**Notes for Claude:** a name is one `LOCTEXT` change (and `ElementBreakNameSpec`'s expected words if Water's change).

### Q-064a3 — Crown's break / DoT names: what else they reach
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-12; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (Q-064a2's build) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** Crown's rule (Q-064a2) names breaks and DoTs by element. Three cases it doesn't spell out: (1) the physical caps (Slash Bleed, Pierce Armor Break, Impact Stunned: its trigger is `Stun`), which have no element; (2) Water's other "Frozen" names (a Water Speed Debuff "Frozen", a Water Skip Turn "Frozen Solid"), which are neither break nor DoT; (3) the other flavour names of non-break effects (Rooted, Paralyzed, Scorched, Withered...).
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):** (1) **keep the physical names** until Crown names them: the rule is "<Element>", and a physical hit has none (`ESpellElement::Generic` reads "Generic", which no player would read as Slash); (2) **"Frozen" goes everywhere**, as Crown named it: Water's slow reads "Slowed", its skip turn "Stunned" (the shared defaults); (3) **the other flavour names stay**: they are neither breaks nor DoTs. One plain naming scheme reads at a glance, as Honkai: Star Rail's "<Element> Weakness Break" and Octopath's plain status names do (both well liked for clarity).
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-12). Each is one line in `SkillEffectDisplayNames::GetDisplayName` if Crown wants otherwise. **Desktop review (2026-11-12):** an infused ability's DoT keeps its own name ("<Ability> Infusion", `ActiveSkillEffect.h`), and Water's Speed Debuff / Skip Turn lost "Frozen" / "Frozen Solid" and now read the plain "Slowed" / "Stunned". "Bleed" is still an FString literal (`SkillEffectDisplayNames.h`); it moves into the `WoRGameNames` LOCTEXT table when Crown names the physical caps.

### Q-170 — Action pips: the numbers
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170, D-088) · **Blocks:** — (B-170a)
**Question:** How many actions at fight start, per turn, at most, and how much does Reflex add?
**Options:** **A** start 1, +1 per turn, Max 2 base, Reflex adds +1 Max at each third of its range (2 → 5), hard cap 5 / **B** start 1, +1 per turn, Max 3 base, Reflex up to 6 (Divinity: Original Sin 2's AP ceiling).
**Recommendation (superseded by Crown's answer below):** A. Crown already set start 1, +1 per turn and base Max 2 (D-088). Octopath Traveler's Boost Points (start 1, +1 per turn, bank up to 5) are the best-liked banked-turn resource in recent turn-based RPGs; its cap of 5 keeps bursts readable. Reflex's share is read like the defense window's, so its gear and stones count with no new maths.
**Answer (superseded by Crown's answer below):** **A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Constants `ACTIONS_AT_FIGHT_START`, `ACTIONS_GAINED_PER_TURN`, `MAX_ACTIONS_BASE`, `MAX_ACTIONS_FROM_REFLEX`, `MAX_ACTIONS_CAP`, all `// TODO: tune`.
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-09, supersedes the default above where they differ):** start a fight with your **regain amount**. **Base Max Actions 2.** Max Actions come from **gear, evolutions and the like, not Reflex**: they give **action points**, 100 points = 1 action; items give e.g. +25 or +50 and they add up; only whole actions count (175 = +1), the leftover shows as progress ("Max 3 (+75/100)"). **Reflex sets the regain per turn:** 1, or 2 at a Reflex threshold, with room for 3 at a second threshold; the regain never fills past Max. **Confirmed by Crown (2026-11-09):** the second threshold is a real step at 0.8 of the Reflex range. Constants (`// TODO: tune`): `ACTIONS_REGAIN_BASE` 1, `ACTIONS_REGAIN_REFLEX_THRESHOLD_2` 0.5, `ACTIONS_REGAIN_REFLEX_THRESHOLD_3` 0.8 (share of the Reflex window range), `MAX_ACTIONS_BASE` 2, `ACTION_POINTS_PER_ACTION` 100, `MAX_ACTIONS_CAP` 5 (a safety ceiling only). Plan: `ActionPips.md` §2, §3.1.

### Q-170a — Action pips part 1: how action points and the bank are built
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-09; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170a) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The plan (`ActionPips.md` §2, §3.1) leaves the build details of part 1 open: how action points join the stat composition, whether gear carries them yet, how Reflex's share is read, what replicates and saves, and when the bank is first set.
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):**
- **Action points are their own additive layer set** (`FWoRActionPointLayers`: gear + evolution + transient + debug, never below 0), not a new `ESubStat` entry or GAS attribute. **This differs from the approved plan** (`ActionPips.md` §3.1: "a new stat in the stat composition, one new stat entry"); Crown can veto it, and the stat entry is then a follow-up. Stones fold into the gear layer (a socketed stone is gear), as the plan's "stones" source. Sub-stats are percentages bought with stat points and multiplied (Pattern P); action points are flat and add up (+25 +50 = 75), as Divinity: Original Sin 2's "+1 Max AP" gear and Pillars of Eternity's flat bonuses stack (sums players read at a glance). The plan already puts a GAS attribute later (§3.1).
- **No gear or evolution field yet.** No asset authors action points (§2), so the gear and evolution layers read 0 (`// TODO:`); a later job adds a `BonusActionPoints` line to `FEquipmentStatBonus` and the evolution data with its first authored item. `wor.Actions.AddPoints <N>` adds debug points for PIE.
- **Reflex's share counts Reflex gear:** (`CalculateReflexWindowBonus` × `ReflexWindowGearFactor`) ÷ `WINDOW_CAP_SECONDS`, clamped to 0..1, exactly as the defense window reads Reflex, so Reflex gear, stones and buffs lift the regain with no new maths (Q-170's own recommendation).
- **`CurrentActions` replicates** (as `CurrentEP`); Max Actions is computed where it is read until a source exists that a client may not hold (then it replicates like `MaxEP`, B-131). **Not saved** (plan §6): runtime, per fight (D-064).
- **The bank is first set when a fight starts, by the orchestrator (B-170c)**, through `ResetActionsForFight`; part 1 calls nothing in play (`wor.Actions.Reset` sets it for PIE checks).
- `SpendActions` refuses a cost above the bank (nothing spent); `LoseActions` floors at 0; `GainActions(N, bMayPassMax)` stops at Max unless it may pass, and never trims actions already over Max.
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Built in B-170a (`ActionPipsRules.h`, `UCharacterDataComponent`).

### Q-170b2 — Action pips part 2: how the rotation is built
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-09; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170b) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The plan (`ActionPips.md` §3.2) and Q-170d / Q-170f set the rotation's order. Some build details are left open: what an uneven side does when someone falls mid-round, whether pinned turns take a rotation seat, how first strike's opening round counts, and what the retired speed hooks do with the switch on.
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):**
- **Seats per round** = the larger side's living count at the round's start; the smaller side's rotation carries on across rounds (the deckbuilder, `WoR_TCG_Pivot_Design.md` §8 item 53: 2v3 plays A1 B1 A2 B2 A1 B3, then A2 B1 A1 B2 A2 B3). **A fallen combatant's own turn is purged; a smaller side's extra-seat turn passes to the next living teammate in the same place** (items 55, 56). The next round recounts the living.
- **Pinned turns don't take a rotation seat:** a bonus turn and a ritual's Execution turn are inserted (the deckbuilder's "a bonus turn inserts, a skip removes", §7c), and the holder keeps its own rotation turn. Under the speed timeline an Execution turn cost one slot of the caster's normal priority; in a fixed rotation there is no priority to cost, and the cast already cost its actions (B-170c). Every turn still burns pinned delays (ET-16 as built).
- **First strike's opening round is a round of its own** (`OnRoundAdded` fires at its start and again when the alternation starts), so the balance simulator counts it.
- **The switch is read once per fight**, at `InitializeCombat`: flipping it mid-fight changes nothing until the next fight (no half-switched fight).
- **With the switch on, `DelayNextTurn` (Wind's one-place delay) and `OnActorSpeedChanged` do nothing.** Wind's break takes an action instead, which lands with B-170d; until then the Wind break has no turn effect in rotation mode (switch on only; default play unchanged).
- **"No actions, no turn" (D-088) is not the rotation's job:** the rotation always gives the seat; the orchestrator skips the turn at 0 actions after the turn-start changes (B-170c, §3.3). Emerald on an enemy already removes their next turn through `SkipNextTurn`, which the rotation honours; Emerald's ally actions are B-170d.
- **The first-striking side also leads every later round** (Q-170f says the alternation starts with that side). Visible: a boss that strikes first acts twice in a row (its opening round, then B P1 …); a party that strikes first in 4v4 plays five turns in a row. Crown may prefer the other side to lead after the opening round.
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Built in B-170b (`Combat/TurnRotation.h`, `UTurnManager`).

### Q-032d — Per-impact defense for every spell shape: what an impact is, and what lands the leftovers
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-032d) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** B-032d widens per-impact defense (Q-031 B) from one-entry, HitCount-1 spells to every spell shape. What counts as one impact of a multi-entry or multi-hit spell, how is the damage cut, and what lands impacts that never get their own moment?
**Context:** `ActionExecutor.cpp` `ExecuteSpellAsync` (the shape gate), `ResolveSpellArrival`, `ApplyDamageAfterDefense`, `OnAsyncActionTimeout`, the Skill-end reconcile. `RealTimeDefenseRework.md` §8a already gives a spell's expected impacts as its arrivals summed over the cast entries; HitCount is the melee hit-frame count. The deckbuilder has no real-time defense (no rule to borrow).
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):**
- **An impact is what lands: one arrival** (a projectile, an AOE or Instant resolve), summed over the cast entries: a burst of 3 plus an AOE is 4 impacts, each defended on its own (one parry per impact). Expedition 33 asks for a parry on every hit of a multi-hit attack, one of its most praised systems; Sekiro's deflect per strike of a combo is the same idea. A HitCount with no arrival of its own would put two hits on one moment that one press can't answer.
- **For Crown to veto: the number of hits now follows the arrivals, not HitCount.** Each impact is one hit, so everything that fires once per hit changes count: on-hit Lifesteal, Burn and Double Hit, crit rolls, the minimum-damage floor (ED-22) and reflect. A spell with one cast entry and HitCount 3 goes from 3 hits to 1; a spell with N cast entries and HitCount 1 goes from 1 hit to N. Spells whose HitCount equals their arrivals keep their hits exactly.
- **The damage is cut the same way:** when HitCount equals the arrivals, the DamageSplit table gives each impact the slice the lumped path gave it, so nothing defended deals the same hits; otherwise the split is even (remainder on the first impacts, as a burst already does) and a warning names the spell, totals within rounding. `wor.Combat.PrintSpellImpacts` lists every loaded spell's plan for the editor check B-032b needs.
- **A spell's window stays open past the cast animation** (the animation-end close skips it): a projectile still flying keeps its own defense; its arrival, or the 8 s failsafe / timeout, closes it.
- **Support spells keep their lumped (timer-close) window**: they deliver no arrival (B-032b decides their window).
- **An ability whose montage has fewer Impact notifies than its HitCount (or none) lands the missing impacts when its animation ends**, each with its own defense, AI press and interrupt check (the Impact notify's own code). Every hit needs its own moment for a press, as in Expedition 33; with no notify, the end of the swing is the only moment the code has. Authoring the notifies is the real fix (ED-37d). An ability with a projectile cast entry is skipped (a projectile may still be flying).
- **A window that closes with impacts left** (the 8 s failsafe, an interrupted montage, the action timeout, a projectile that never arrived) **lands them one by one**, each with its own cast entry's (or hit's) no-damage / no-status flags, each slice reduced by the window's own outcome (a Guard pressed in it halves each, a Dodge stops them, none on a timeout); never Perfect, no luck dodge. Before, it re-cut the last impact's slice (too little damage).
- **A montage-less spell sends all its cast entries at once** (the balance simulator's path), as a montage-less ability lands all its impacts.
- **Behind `wor.Combat.PerImpactDefense`, default 1**; 0 is the lumped path, to compare in PIE until B-032b deletes it. (B-032b, 2026-11-14: deleted; per impact is the only path, and a support spell of several hits lands them at its window's close.)
- Not changed: an interruptable cast entry only interrupts when it is the target's one impact (multi-entry spells never could); the spell-ending Flawless Parry tally counts per impact as for melee.
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Built in B-032d (`SpellImpactPlan`, `UActionExecutor::ApplyRemainingImpacts`, `LandFallbackImpacts`).
**Answer: Crown, 2026-11-12:** **kept.** One defence window per spell impact (one arrival), and per-hit effects (Lifesteal, Burn, Double Hit, crit rolls, the minimum-damage floor, reflect) count arrivals, not HitCount. Confirmed as built. **Desktop review (2026-11-12):** a spell with no montage now sends its later cast entries at once (as the montage's Cast notifies do), support spells too, so a montage-less support spell's extra entries apply where they used to be dropped; the swing's end checks the action is still the same one before its Return leg (a fallback impact can finish it and, with action pips, start the next).

### Q-170c2 — Action pips part 3: how several actions per turn are built
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-09; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170c) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The plan (`ActionPips.md` §1, §3.3) and Q-170b / Q-170c set what costs actions and what ends a turn. Some build details are left open: when the first regain lands, when the cost is paid, what the AI and a stunned unit do with a bank, where an item's cost lives, and how free switching is read.
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):**
- **A combatant's first turn plays the fight-start bank, with no regain on top** (start 1, then +1 from the second turn). Octopath Traveler's Boost Points work this way (1 BP at the start, +1 each later turn), the best-liked banked-turn resource (Q-170). Regaining on the first turn too would start every fight with twice the regain.
- **The cost is paid when the action is accepted**, after its checks and before it plays, as Energy is (ActionExecutorCosts). An action that then fails (a miss, a fizzle) keeps its cost spent, like a thrown item that misses (ET-54).
- **An action cost is at least 1 (`ClampMin 1`, as the plan says).** Changed after review: a 0-cost skill could be repeated without limit in one turn (only Energy would stop it). A free quick skill (Divinity: Original Sin 2's 0-AP actions) would need its own once-per-turn rule first. Default stays 1. Data validation warns when a cost is above `MAX_ACTIONS_CAP` (no one could pay it). A cost above the user's Max Actions is unusable even when a gain has pushed the bank past Max (Crown's "above Max = unusable", read literally).
- **Items have no asset**, so their cost lives in the item table (`CrystalEffectTable::GetActionCost`): every item costs 1 until one is priced per (type, tier).
- **The AI takes one action per turn and banks the rest until B-170f** (the plan's spend-or-bank rule lands there). A Void break playing your turn does the same.
- **A stunned unit gets one action (Attack or Defend, D-001), then its turn ends**: a stun that let a full bank act would barely be a stun (the plan's "not stunned" gate before each prompt). At turn start a stunned unit is still asked, as built.
- **Switching is free with the switch on, read from `wor.Turns.ActionPips` itself** (not the fight's latched mode): a co-op client's loadout copy has no TurnManager fight to ask. Flipping the switch mid-fight changes only the switching rule until the next fight (a debug-only edge).
- **A bonus turn (Extra Turn) regains like any turn.** ET-15 keeps it "a whole bonus turn, as built", and a whole turn starts with the regain (Octopath's Boost Points come back on every turn the unit gets). Added at the desktop build's review; B-170d may revisit it with Extra Turn's text.
- **End Turn at switch 0** ~~ends the turn as a pass~~ is refused and the turn is asked for again (B-170e review: a co-op client with its own switch at 1 must not pass a turn in a normal fight; `ACombatOrchestrator::StartActionAsync` checks the fight's latched mode).
- **A win during a turn ends the fight at once** (the win check runs after every action, plan §3.3); the turn-end step doesn't run for that last turn.
- **"No actions" on the HUD** waits for the placeholder UI (B-170e); the skip is logged.
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Built in B-170c (`ACombatOrchestrator` action pips section, `USkillDataBase::ActionCost`, `FAction::GetActionCost`, `ULoadoutComponent::GetTurnSwitchRefusedReason`).

### Q-170d2 — Action pips part 4: how the action effects are built
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-09; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170d) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The plan (`ActionPips.md` §3.4) and Q-170e / Q-170f / Q-170g set the effects. Some build details are left open: how "now" and "next turn" are authored, what order same-turn changes land in, how low Max Actions can go, which Wind and Ice breaks take actions, and who counts as an ally for Emerald. (Named Q-170d2 because Q-170d is the rotation's order.)
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):**
- **Timing is the payload's Duration:** 0 = now, N = at the holder's Nth turn start from now (1 = next turn, 2 = the one after, which also carries Emerald's "in 2 turns"). One field, no new authoring field. A timed change waits on the holder (`UCharacterDataComponent`), not as a lasting effect, so it can land in step 2 of the turn-start order (after the regain, before the check). It can't be cleansed (as Divinity: Original Sin 2's Adrenaline AP debt, which nothing removes). Author **Value** (actions), not Magnitude; a permanent one is refused.
- **Same-turn changes land gains first, then losses**, so a loss is never wasted on an empty bank (Crown's turn-start order sums them before the check either way; this only matters at 0).
- **Every effect gain may pass Max** (Q-170g as answered); the waiting Wind loss doesn't need the flag.
- **Max Actions never drops below 1** (`MAX_ACTIONS_FLOOR`), so a default-cost action always stays usable; the safety cap 5 still holds. A lowered Max never trims the bank (as gains over Max stay banked). Darkest Dungeon and Octopath never let a debuff remove the basic action either.
- **Wind takes its action only on a new gust** (a same-source refresh doesn't, as its one-place delay didn't, D-012); **Ice takes its action on every break**, as its damage lands every time.
- **The break rows change with the fight's latched mode** (`TurnManager::IsRotationMode`), like the rotation; the effect text follows the live switch (text has no fight).
- **Emerald's side is the fight's teams:** a target on the other team is an enemy (loses its next turn); anything else, outside a fight included, is treated as an ally (gains actions), never skipped.
- **A turn removed by `SkipNextTurn` doesn't count down waiting changes** (Emerald on an enemy): it isn't a turn start, so nothing lands on it and a waiting Wind loss lands at the next turn the holder really starts. Intended: a loss always costs a turn the holder plays. Counting removed turns would need the turn belt to call into the character, which isn't a one-line change.
- **Authoring checks:** `UEffectDefinition::IsDataValid` warns when an action effect's Value is 0 or less (Magnitude isn't read), when it is Permanent, and when a Max Actions change has Duration 0.
- **Extra Turn's regain** stays as B-170c built it (Q-170c2: a bonus turn regains like any turn); nothing changes here.
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Built in B-170d (`UCharacterDataComponent` waiting changes, `BreakTable::ActionPipsRows`, `UItemExecutor::ExecuteBonusTurnEffect`).

### Q-170f2 — Action pips part 6: how the AI spends or banks
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170f) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The plan (`ActionPips.md` §3.6) says the AI acts while its best pick scores above a floor, otherwise ends its turn and banks, and that it may bank for a pick it can't pay yet. Left open: the floor, when banking for a pricier pick is worth it, what Easy does, whether a stunned or Void-broken AI spends several actions, and how the Emerald and the action effects are valued. (Named Q-170f2 because Q-170f is bonus turns and first strike.)
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):**
- **One pick per ask, then spend or bank.** The orchestrator asks the AI again while it has actions; the existing pick runs each time (the deckbuilder's Medium AI: one best move at a time, re-planned after each, `wor.core/Match/MatchAi.cs` `MediumPlan`).
- **The floor is 0:** a pick worth nothing banks (End Turn), anything worth something is spent. The deckbuilder's Medium AI leaves out "moves worth nothing" and then ends its turn. **Exception:** with the bank already at Max the AI spends anyway (banking would waste the regain, and an AI that never acts would stall a fight).
- **Banking for a bigger skill:** a pick that costs more actions than the bank holds (but no more than Max) is held; the AI banks for it when it is worth more than twice the best pick it can pay now (`AI_BANK_FOR_HELD_PICK_FACTOR` 0.5: one turn's wait, discounted). Saving AP for a big skill is the core of Expedition 33's and Final Fantasy X-2's AP turns, both well received. A pick above Max is never held (it can't ever be paid).
- **Picks by need are always spent:** heal, cleanse, Defend (it ends the turn itself and keeps the rest banked) and the fallback after a refused pick.
- **Easy** spends every action but, after its first action of a turn, stops early 30% of the time (the deckbuilder's Easy AI: `EasyStopPercent` 30). The roll is drawn from the battle random only when it can matter.
- **A stunned or Void-broken AI plays one action** (Q-170c2 as built): a stun leaves Attack or Defend, and a Void break's random turn is one random act (Final Fantasy's Confuse acts once).
- **An action change is worth one action of the affected unit's threat** (its best single action, the AI's existing per-turn threat estimate), plus on the AI's side, minus on the other; a **Max Actions** change counts half (`AI_MAX_ACTION_WORTH_IN_ACTIONS`, it only matters once the bank is full). The **Ice / Wind break's** action loss counts the same when the hit reaches the status score's trigger tier.
- **Emerald (Q-170f's rule):** on the foe, worth its lost turn(s) × its regain × its threat; on itself, the tier's actions × its own threat, discounted by delay with the existing `AIDelayDecay`; used when the better of the two beats the best pick (so D's "+1 now" for its 1-action cost is never worth it; A's "+2 now" is). The AI gives Emeralds only to itself or the foe, as before. **The old look-ahead is dropped in an action-pips fight** (`GetRescueExposureTurns` / `EMERALD_EXPOSURE_LOOKAHEAD`, which the plan §3.6 expected to keep): it priced *forcing* a foe's turn early, so a one-tick-lethal DoT killed before the foe's team could rescue it. Under Q-170f an Emerald on a foe *removes* its turn instead, so there is no early tick to secure and no rescue window to count; the lost turn is valued directly (its regain × its threat). Outside an action-pips fight the look-ahead runs as before.
- All numbers are named constants in `AI/AIActionPips.h`, `// TODO: tune`.
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Built in B-170f (`AI/AIActionPips.h`, `UAIDecisionManager`, `ACombatOrchestrator::CanContinueTurn`).
**Answer: Crown, 2026-11-12:** **bonus actions above Max Actions must be used that turn; whatever is above Max is lost at the end of the turn** (D&D 5e's bonus action is use-it-or-lose-it; Octopath Traveler's Boost Points never pass their cap, both widely understood). Normal actions still bank up to Max. **The AI spends bonus actions on the turn it gets them, so no Emerald look-ahead is needed** (it stays dropped in action-pips fights, as built). The rest of the recommendation stands. Built on B-170f: `ActionPipsRules::BankAtTurnEnd`, `UCharacterDataComponent::ExpireActionsAboveMax`, `ACombatOrchestrator::ExpireBonusActionsAtTurnEnd` (each of the holder's own turn ends, before the turn-end effects; not on an Execution turn), `AIActionPips::DecideSpendOrBank` step 1b (above Max: spend, Easy too). Specs: `WoR.Turns.MultiAction` (a bonus above Max expires at turn end; the bank up to Max carries over), `ActionPipsSpec`, `WoR.AI.ActionPips`. **This changes Q-170g's "the extra stays banked until spent" and Q-170f's "Emerald's actions may go over Max" (they still may, for that turn).**
**Build choice (reference-backed default, 2026-11-12; Crown vetoes):** a bonus given outside the holder's turn (an ally's Emerald, a Gain Action from someone else's skill) lasts until the end of the holder's next turn, so it is never lost before the holder could use it (D&D's bonus action belongs to your own turn).

### Q-170f3 — Actions above a lowered Max at turn end
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (B-170f review, after Q-170f2) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** Q-170f2 (Crown) makes whatever is above Max lost at the end of the holder's turn. When Max drops (a Max Actions Down, or a Max Actions Buff ending) below actions already banked, are those lost too? B-170d built "a lowered Max never trims the bank" (Q-170d2; PIE B-170d check 14).
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):** **yes, at the holder's turn end, the same rule:** the bank is never above Max once a turn ends. Crown's words name no source ("whatever is above Max"), and Octopath Traveler's Boost Points never sit above their cap, which players read at a glance; one rule is simpler than tracking which pips were bonus. The drop itself still never trims at once (Q-170d2 holds within the turn): the holder can spend them on its next turn, and only what is still above Max at that turn's end goes. The AI sees them as above Max and spends them (`DecideSpendOrBank` step 1b).
**Answer: Crown, 2026-11-12:** **when Max drops below the actions already banked, the extra ones become bonus actions that must be used that turn; whatever is left is cut at the end of that unit's own turn** (never at another unit's turn end). The extra pips show as **bonus pips with their own look** so the player knows they will expire. Built in `fix/Q-170f3-bonus-pips` (2026-11-12): the trim was already only at the holder's own turn end (`ACombatOrchestrator::ExpireBonusActionsAtTurnEnd(CurrentActor)`), now proven by `WoR.Turns.MultiAction`; the party card draws actions above Max as ◆ and says "N bonus, lost at turn end" (`ActionPipsText::MakePips` / `GetBonusActions` / `MakeActionsLine`). Earlier: built with Q-170f2 (`UCharacterDataComponent::ExpireActionsAboveMax` trims to the live Max). **Desktop review (2026-11-12):** a stunned or Void-broken holder gets one action (Q-170c2) and its turn ends, so its bonus actions go unused at that turn end; this follows the rule as worded (cut at the end of its own turn). PIE B-170d check 14 says so.

### Q-170e2 — Action pips part 5: how the placeholder UI shows actions
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170e) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The plan (`ActionPips.md` §3.5) asks for pips on the character panel, an End Turn button and key, each skill's cost, greyed costs and Max Actions with its progress. Left open: how the pips and costs read, where they sit on B-391's new HUD, which key ends the turn, and whether enemies show pips.
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):**
- **The deckbuilder first:** its Battle screen ended on **"ACT 2"**, a number beside Energy, because pips "don't scale to 6+ Actions" (`WoR_TCG_Pivot_Design.md`, "Actions are a number", Crown 2026-10-13), with **End Turn** on its action strip. Here Max Actions stops at 5 (`MAX_ACTIONS_CAP`) and the approved plan asks for pips, so the card shows **both**: filled pips for the bank, empty ones up to Max, then the number, as Expedition 33's AP pips (widely praised for reading at a glance): **"Actions ●●○ 2 · Max 3 (+75/100)"**, on each party card under EP. A bank past Max (Q-170g) shows all filled; past 8 pips only the number shows.
- **Max Actions with its progress** sits on the same line ("Max 3 (+75/100)", Q-170's format); at the cap just "Max 5". The stat screen's line (§3.5) waits for that screen (B-391 slice screens).
- **Party only:** enemy cards show no pips, as Expedition 33 shows only the party's AP (enemies keep B-391's bar-only card, Q-391).
- **Costs on every skill, spell and item button**, as Expedition 33 prints each skill's AP: "Fireball (2 actions)". The deckbuilder dropped cost labels for a hover preview on its card strip; this menu has no hover yet, so the label stays (the plan's own ask). Greyed with the reason (the plan; B-170c's two texts): "Needs 2 actions." (bank too low) or "Costs more actions than you can hold." (above Max). A spell already greyed by a broken source keeps that reason.
- **End Turn** is a main-menu button (after Defend), shown only in an action-pips fight; a stunned actor's menu stays Attack + Defend (D-001). Defend's tooltip says "Ends your turn."
- ~~**End Turn's key: Backspace / View (Touchpad), main menu only**, like the B-391 category keys. Backspace is XCOM 2's End Turn key; Space and letter keys stay clear in case the editor-authored Guard / Dodge context uses them; View is the one gamepad button combat leaves free. Code-built (`IA_Combat_MenuEndTurn`), no `IA_` asset.~~ (dropped by Crown, 2026-11-12: the key comes later)
- **A co-op client** reads the switch (`wor.Turns.ActionPips`) to know the fight's mode, since it runs no TurnManager; the server reads its latched mode. Replicating the mode on the turn state is a TODO (a mid-fight flip on a client is a debug-only edge).
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Built in B-170e (`ActionPipsText`, `UBattleHUDWidget`, `UCombatCommandMenuSubsystem::ShowActionCost` / `CreateEndTurnButton`, `ACombatPlayerController::GetMenuKeys`).
**Answer: Crown, 2026-11-12:** the turn **ends automatically at 0 actions**; an **End Turn button** ends it early (banking the rest); **a key for End Turn comes later** (when it lands it gets its `docs/Design/InputMap.md` row), so the Backspace / View key above is dropped; add a **setting to hide the End Turn button** (per player, saved with the other UI settings, default shown). Crown accepted that End Turn is refused outside action-pips fights while both turn systems exist. The rest of the recommendation stands. **Built (B-170e, 2026-11-12):** `FSavedSettings::bHideEndTurnButton` (false = shown, so older saves read the default: no save bump), `USaveSubsystem::IsEndTurnButtonHidden` / `SetEndTurnButtonHidden`, `ActionPipsText::ShouldShowEndTurnButton` (read on the machine that builds the menu, so a co-op client uses its own setting), console placeholder `wor.UI.HideEndTurn` until the settings screen (D-024); `IA_Combat_MenuEndTurn` removed from `ACombatPlayerController::GetMenuKeys` (the menu still takes End Turn as a main-menu key, so the later key is one row). Hidden, the turn still ends at 0 actions and on Defend.

### Q-170i — Action pips: the Reflex regain, Crown's rework
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown's ruling, 2026-11-12; stones and buffs reading confirmed by Crown 2026-11-12; other build details by the reference-backed default) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (B-170i) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The regain was 1 / 2 / 3 at Reflex window-share thresholds (Q-170). Crown reworked it: how is it built?
**Crown's ruling (2026-11-12):** **Reflex value** = the character's Reflex points, each at its own worth (a higher gear tier makes a point worth more), × the Body multiplier **including** external Body boosts. **Step** per +1 regain = `REGAIN_STEP` × the character's **natural** Body multiplier (own Body level only). **Regain = 1 + ⌊value ÷ step⌋**, never filling the bank past Max Actions. Levelling Body cancels out; only Reflex investment, point worth and external Body boosts raise it. Progress is shown ("Regain 2 (Reflex 4.1 / 6.0)"). The defence window keeps its own Reflex formula.
**Build details (reference-backed default):**
- **`REGAIN_STEP` = 15** (`// TODO: tune`): about a sixth of the 93-point stat budget, so a light Reflex build (~15 points) regains 2 and a focused one (~30) regains 3, as Crown asked.
- **The points** are the character's own `Reflex` plus the gear's `BonusReflex`. Gear points already carry their tier's worth: that is where the per-point tier conversion lives (`GetActiveStatBonus`, TierPowerScaling 3c).
- **External Body boosts** are everything `GetEvolutionModifiedBody` adds over the natural pillar: a slotted crystal's Body, gear Body percents, Body buffs and the run's over-cap points.
- **Reflex stones and Reflex buffs (percent) don't change the regain.** They are percent lifts on the window, not Reflex points, and the ruling names only points, their worth and Body boosts. They still widen the defence window.
- **The regain stops at the Max Actions safety cap (5)**, so the text never shows a regain no bank could hold. **Confirmed by Crown (2026-11-12):** 5 is the cap for now; later, bigger skills and Vessels will cost 2 or 3 actions. A negative value (cursed gear) gives the base 1.
- **The progress line** is a debug string in `wor.Actions.Print` until the battle HUD (B-170e) shows it as FText.
**Confirmed (Crown, 2026-11-12):** Reflex stones and Reflex buffs raise Reflex's other effects (the defence window) but **not** the action regain; anything that raises actions is its own separate action buff (GainActions / regain effects).
**Answer:** Crown's ruling, with the build details by the reference-backed default (2026-11-12). Built in B-170i (`ActionPipsRules::RegainForReflexValue`, `UCharacterDataComponent::GetRegainReflexValue` / `GetRegainReflexStep`).

### Q-170j — Action pips: the caps (cost 1–3, Max 5, bonus +4) and three skills a turn
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12; point 7 by the reference-backed default, Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (Crown, through the desktop operator) · **Blocks:** —
**Crown's ruling (2026-11-12):**
1. A skill's **action cost is 1 to 3**: small 1, medium 2, large 3 (`ACTION_COST_MIN` / `ACTION_COST_MAX`). A cost above 3 is read as 3 and data validation flags it as an error.
2. **Max Actions has a hard cap of 5:** Max = min(2 + ⌊gear action points ÷ 100⌋, 5), buffs included (`MAX_ACTIONS_CAP`, no longer a safety ceiling). With 5 a hero can use five small skills, or one large and one medium. Actions gained above Max (an S-tier Emerald, bonus actions) still work as ruled (Q-170f2 / Q-170f3); this caps Max itself.
3. **Bonus actions above Max are capped at 4** (`MAX_BONUS_ACTIONS`): a bank never passes 5 + 4 = 9, three large skills. A gain stops there; a bank already over it (a lowered Max) is not trimmed by the gain, only at the holder's turn end.
4. **At most 3 skills per turn** (`SKILLS_PER_TURN_MAX`): every action that costs actions counts (skills, spells, items, attacks, arming a ritual); End Turn, Defend and weapon switching don't. At the third the turn ends even with actions left; they stay banked within the caps (bonus actions above Max are lost at that turn end, Q-170f2).
5. Q-170i's regain cap of 5 stays (confirmed the same day): later, bigger skills and Vessels will cost 2 or 3 actions.
**Point 7 (reference-backed default):** Crown called the 3-skill limit "hidden". **Recommendation, built:** the player can see it: the acting character's card reads "Skills 2/3" from the first skill of the turn. A limit you can't see feels like a bug; Slay the Spire's Velvet Choker (at most 6 cards a turn) shows its count on the relic, which players read at a glance. Greying the skill buttons at 3 isn't needed: the turn ends at the third skill, so the menu never shows at 3.
**Answer:** points 1–5 Crown's; point 7 by the reference-backed default (2026-11-12). Built in `fix/Q-170j-action-caps` (`ActionPipsRules::ClampActionCost` / `BankAfterGain` / `HasSkillsLeftThisTurn`, `FAction::GetActionCost`, `USkillDataBase::ActionCost` ClampMax 3 and `IsDataValid`, `UCharacterDataComponent::SkillsUsedThisTurn` (replicated) / `NoteSkillUsed` / `ResetSkillsUsedThisTurn`, `ACombatOrchestrator::PayActionCost` / `CanContinueTurn` / `BeginTurnActions` / `ExpireBonusActionsAtTurnEnd`, `ActionPipsText::MakeSkillsUsedText`). Item costs are all 1 today (`CrystalEffectTable::GetActionCost`); the clamp covers them too.

### Q-170g2 — Action pips part 7: how Turn Speed is retired
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-09; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170g) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The plan (`ActionPips.md` §3.4) says data validation refuses the retired Turn Speed family (the D-056 pattern), and §3.5 says the stat screen's Turn Speed line goes. Left open: an error or a warning while the speed timeline still plays at `wor.Turns.ActionPips 0`; which authored fields count; and what "the stat screen" is, since no character sheet exists yet (`UI_ScreenInventory.md` 3.2). (Named Q-170g2 because Q-170g is the action effects.)
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):**
- **A warning now, an error in B-170h.** Until Crown's PIE flips the default, the speed timeline still reads Turn Speed, so the assets using it are live and work; an error would mark them invalid (and fail a validate-all run) before their replacement plays. The deckbuilder rejects a retired member once nothing plays it (`Lasts.WhileWatcherRuns`: kept, retired, the validator rejects it, its `docs/reference/08` rule 116), and D-056's error came only once Speed Buff / Debuff read nothing; Unreal itself deprecates with a warning one version before removing (`UE_DEPRECATED`). So: warn while the 0 path reads it; B-170h, which deletes the reads, makes the warnings errors.
- **Which fields:** every authored source that has data validation: the three effect types (`TurnSpeedBuff`, `TurnSpeedDebuff`, `ModifyTurnSpeed`) in any effect definition (skills, gear, evolutions and boss phases all author effects there), a skill's `StatScaling` on Turn Speed, a character's `TurnSpeed` points, and gear / evolution `BaseStatBonus.BonusTurnSpeed`. Not runtime data: gear rolls (`GeneratedStatBonus`) keep rolling Turn Speed until B-170h, because changing the roll table would change loot at 0; `EItemEffectType::BuffTurnSpeed` comes only from the already-retired Turn Speed stone (B-353) and no asset stores it; `FCrystalId::StoneStat` and `FFusionId::BonusStat` can hold `ESubStat::TurnSpeed` but are runtime instance data (a stone's or fusion's rolled target), like `GeneratedStatBonus`, so they aren't warned on either. **One authored gap:** a reaction trigger's `Effects` (`UReactionTriggerData::Triggers[].Effects`) can hold a Turn Speed payload, but that class has no data validation, so nothing warns; `wor.Data.ListTurnSpeed` lists it instead (adding validation to a new class is outside this job).
- **The enum entries stay visible** in the editor's dropdowns (not `Hidden`) until B-170h, so assets that still hold them show their value; each one gets the warning, and `wor.Data.ListTurnSpeed` lists them.
- **The stat screen:** there is no character sheet yet, so the retirement lands where Turn Speed shows today: the character print (`UCharacterDataDebug`) marks its Turn Speed lines retired, the Details panel names the fields "Turn Speed (retired)" / "Bonus Turn Speed (retired)", and the character sheet's spec (`UI_ScreenInventory.md` 3.2) drops Turn Speed for Max Actions and the regain. B-170e's placeholder adds the Max Actions line.
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Built in B-170g (`SkillEffectClassification::IsTurnSpeedFamily`, the `IsDataValid` warnings, `TurnSpeedRetireDebug`).
### Q-214a — Field skills part 1: the dash on the double tap, Enhanced Sprint as a toggle, the starting numbers
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-12; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (B-214a) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) What does the double tap do before a character picks a field skill? (2) How does Enhanced Sprint run: held, or on / off, and when is EP paid? (3) Which source gives which element? (4) The starting numbers.
**Context:** D-099 and `Design/FieldAbilities.md`: everyone has the normal dash (the only unlocked movement); one active field skill per character (a source, then a movement type); double-tap sprint uses it; Enhanced Sprint is faster by element (Lightning fastest, Wind second) and costs EP; EP has no regen in the field.
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** the dash is the default active field skill: the double tap dashes until another skill is picked. **B** a separate dash key.
- (2) **A** a toggle: double tap on, double tap again off, off by itself at 0 EP; it drains while on (moving or not) and the first second is paid on start. **B** only while sprint is held.
- (3) **A** innate = a Caster's element (other classes have none); weapon = the crystal in the weapon in hand (none when broken); ring = a Resonator's active adapter, another class's primary ring (the same reading as the parry colour, B-393).
- (4) **A** dash: free, 1 s cooldown, 1800 cm/s; Enhanced Sprint: 1 EP per second; speed over the normal sprint ×1.5 Lightning, ×1.4 Wind, ×1.25 the other elements (all `// TODO: tune`).
**Recommendation:** A, A, A, A. (1) Kingdom Hearts 2's growth abilities and Nightreign give every character a free basic dodge-dash; the design already makes the dash the one move everyone has, and B-214g's input list has no separate dash key (FieldAbilities, InputMap). (2) Warframe's channelled abilities (toggle on, energy per second, off at 0) are the standard drain model and widely used; paying the first second up front follows Honkai: Star Rail, where a missed Technique still spends its point (FieldAbilities appendix), so tapping it on and off is never free. (3) One reading of "the character's element" for the whole game keeps it learnable. (4) FieldAbilities §4: the passive bonus is modest; the big moment is the active skill; numbers are a starting point for play-testing.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-12). **Crown changed (2), 2026-11-12:** Enhanced Sprint drains EP **only while the character is actually moving at sprint speed**, not while standing still (still a toggle; started mid-sprint, the first second is paid at once). The rest stands.
**Notes for Claude:** (2) as changed is built in B-214a2 (`FieldSkillRules::IsMovingAtSprintSpeed`: ground speed above 600 cm/s, between the 500 walk and the 800 slowest sprint; each 1 s drain step checks it). Follows from Crown's rule (review 2026-11-12): turning it on while standing costs nothing until the character sprints; a jump or a dash faster than 600 cm/s counts as sprinting (moving at that speed); the step samples the speed once a second, so a second is charged or free by the speed at that moment. Built in B-214a (`UFieldMovementComponent`, `FieldSkillRules`, `FPartyMember::FieldSkill`, save v36). Left: a crystal source doesn't wear yet (the B-214 row says it does; slice with B-214b or B-214f); unlocks are B-214b (any built type with an elemental source can be picked until then).

### Q-214b — Field skills part 2: what an unlock is keyed by, which spell counts, and whether it can be lost
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-13; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (B-214b, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) Is an unlock per movement type, or per type and element? (2) How does the game know which spell is a type's aura spell? (3) Is an unlock lost when the spell is later levelled down, given away or dismantled? (4) Which characters' spells count? (5) Who does the placeholder picker edit?
**Context:** `Design/FieldAbilities.md` "Unlocks" and "Rank needed" (Crown, 2026-10-27): each type comes from one to three auras (Elemental / Enhancement / Manipulation); the elemental variant of the aura spell unlocks it at rank 3 (D tier) for simple types, rank 7 (S tier) for advanced, all listed auras needed; Teleport is Void only; "once unlocked, anyone with the matching source can use it (lightning speed → any Lightning source)"; the spell per type is Crown's table (not yet written).
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** per type **and element**: ranking the Lightning Enhancement Aura unlocks Enhanced Sprint for every Lightning source, not for Fire. **B** per type for every element.
- (2) **A** a field on the spell asset, `USpellData::FieldAura` (None by default); the spell's element is the variant. Crown marks the aura spells in the editor (editor step). **B** a separate table asset.
- (3) **A** never lost: the account keeps the best rank each aura reached per element. **B** recomputed from the spells held now.
- (4) **A** any spell any character holds in the store (and the owned list), and every rank-up (`LevelUpSpell`); a Generic or None spell never unlocks (the Generic version is the standard one, not a variant).
- (5) **A** the player's character on the map (the field pawn), from a "Field skill..." button on the party screen or `wor.UI.FieldSkill`; another member picks when it leads.
**Recommendation:** A, A, A, A, A. (1) The design's own example ("lightning speed → any Lightning source"). (2) The spell is the source of truth for what it is (CLAUDE.md "Source-of-truth queries go to the owning object"); one dropdown per spell is the least editor work. (3) Final Fantasy IX's ability mastery (learn an ability from gear, keep it after taking the gear off; widely praised) and every Metroidvania's movement upgrades (Hollow Knight's, never taken away): a movement unlock that could vanish would break map routes the player already opened. (4) "Account-wide" in the B-214 row. (5) Only the map pawn has field movement (B-214a); the CommonUI loadout screen (D-024) replaces the picker.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-13).
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-214b (`UFieldSkillUnlockSubsystem`, `FieldSkillRules::IsUnlockedByRanks`, `FSavedFieldUnlocks` with no save bump, `UFieldSkillPickerWidget`). Follows from it: B-214a's Enhanced Sprint is now locked until a Enhancement Aura of the source's element reaches rank 3 (`wor.Field.Unlock EnhancedSprint <Element>` for testing). Left: in co-op each machine keeps its own unlocks and the host checks a guest's pick against the host's (`TODO` for co-op accounts); Crown's spell table (which spell is each aura) is an editor step.

### Q-214c — Field skills part 3: how glide, the jumps, the air dash and the wall-run behave, and their starting numbers
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-13; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (B-214c, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
