**Question:** (1) How is each move used with the one double tap, and what does it cost? (2) How often can the double jump and air dash be used? (3) When does a wall-run happen and how long does it last? (4) The starting numbers.
**Context:** `Design/FieldAbilities.md`: Glide (slow fall, Manipulation Aura at D tier), High / double jump, Air dash ("hover, aim, dash"; Lightning a chain of blinks), wall-running at speed during Enhanced Sprint (Prototype, Warframe). EP has no regen in the field.
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** Glide: a toggle that starts only in the air, paid once, off on a second tap or on landing. High jump: on the ground a high jump, in the air one double jump; each paid. Air dash: in the air along the camera's aim, paid. **B** Glide and flight drain per second like Enhanced Sprint.
- (2) **A** once each per airtime: landing gives the double jump, the air dash and the wall-run back.
- (3) **A** automatic while Enhanced Sprint is on: in the air at sprint speed with a wall to the left or right, the height is held and the run follows the wall for up to 1.5 s, once per airtime; no extra EP (Enhanced Sprint's drain pays).
- (4) **A** glide 1 EP, gravity ×0.15, fall at most 250 cm/s, air control 0.8; high jump 1 EP, 1200 cm/s up, double jump 900; air dash 2 EP, 2000 cm/s; Lightning 3 blinks of 250 cm then a hover; wall-run 80 cm side check, 1.5 s (all `// TODO: tune`).
**Recommendation:** A, A, A, A. (1) Breath of the Wild's paraglider (open in the air, close by pressing again or landing; loved for exploration) and Hollow Knight's Monarch Wings (one double jump per airtime) are the best-known models; a one-off cost keeps EP (no field regen) for the moves that matter. (2) Hollow Knight's double jump and Mothwing Cloak dash, Celeste's air dash: once per airtime, back on landing — widely praised for readable platforming. (3) Warframe and Titanfall 2's wall-runs start by moving fast along a wall in the air with no extra button and last about a second and a half; Prototype's runs need speed. (4) A starting point for play-testing.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-13).
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-214c (`FieldMovementMoves.cpp`, `FieldSkillRules::CheckGlideStart` / `CheckJump` / `CheckAirDash` / `CanWallRun`). Moves run on the server (a client goes through `ActivateFieldSkill`'s RPC), so a guest may see a small correction. The air dash's "hover, aim" is the camera's aim at the moment of the tap (no aim pause yet); Lightning's blinks have no VFX yet. Follows from it: picking another field skill ends a glide; a move other than the active one is refused (`NotActiveSkill`); a guest's jump, dash and glide are server-only (no client launch like the dash's), so a correction per move is expected (`TODO`).

### Q-214d — Field skills part 4: how flight is used, what it costs, where the ceiling is, and what a no-fly zone is
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-13; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (B-214d, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) How is flight turned on and off, and does hovering cost EP? (2) Where is the height ceiling measured from? (3) What marks a no-fly zone and what happens on entering one? (4) How do effects lift the limits? (5) The starting numbers.
**Context:** FieldAbilities choice 4 (Crown, 2026-10-27): flight drains EP while flying, hovers when still, has a height ceiling and no-fly zones (boss arenas, some secrets); effects can raise these (an evolution's unlimited flight). The research notes there: limit flight's height or duration and ban it in some areas (Tears of the Kingdom's Ascend bans, Legends: Arceus' Braviary height limit).
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** a toggle on the double tap, as Enhanced Sprint: the first second is paid at once, then EP every second, hovering included (it is still flying); off on a second tap, at 0 EP (it falls), or when the source stops allowing it. **B** hovering free.
- (2) **A** a fixed height above the ground under the take-off point. **B** above whatever ground is below now (terrain-following).
- (3) **A** any volume with the actor tag `Zone.NoFly`: flight is refused inside one, and a flyer entering one drops (falls). **B** a dedicated volume class.
- (4) **A** `FFieldFlightLimits` on the field component: unlimited EP, no ceiling, ignore no-fly, extra ceiling; set by effects through `SetFlightLimits` (B-214h's evolutions, later items).
- (5) **A** 2 EP a second, 900 cm/s, strong braking so it stops and hovers with no input, ceiling 1500 cm (all `// TODO: tune`).
**Recommendation:** A, A, A, A, A. (1) Crown's ruling says it drains while flying; Warframe's Archwing and Genshin's wind gliding cost while airborne with no free hover; following Enhanced Sprint's toggle keeps one rule for the double tap. (2) A ceiling from the take-off point is how Legends: Arceus' Braviary and Tears of the Kingdom's Ascend limits read to players: "you can climb this much"; terrain-following would let a flyer climb a mountain by hugging it. (3) Tears of the Kingdom bans its traversal powers per area; an actor tag lets a designer mark any existing volume with no new asset. (4) The ruling's "effects can raise these limits". (5) A starting point for play-testing.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-13).
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-214d (`FieldMovementFlight.cpp`, `FieldSkillRules::CheckFlightStart` / `GetFlightCeilingZ`, `FFieldFlightLimits`). Earth's magnetism and Fire's Phoenix Rising are looks only, for later. The flight limits aren't saved: an effect sets them each time it applies (B-214h). Placing `Zone.NoFly` on a boss arena is B-214g's editor step. Follows from it (review 2026-11-13): flight lifts off at 600 cm/s; up / down is `AddFlightVerticalInput`, bound in B-214g to one axis, `IA_Field_FlyVertical` (Q-214g), `wor.Field.FlyVertical` until then; touching the ground doesn't end flight (it keeps flying low; a second tap lands); at the ceiling the rise stops and the pawn is only put back when 100 cm over it.

### Q-214e — Field skills part 5: how teleport, phase step, mark and recall, super launch and regroup work
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-13; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (B-214e, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) Where does each move go, and what stops it? (2) Mark and recall with one button? (3) Which party member does Regroup go to? (4) Costs and cooldowns.
**Context:** `Design/FieldAbilities.md`: Teleport (Void only, "one long vanish"), Phase / shadow step (through thin walls or bars), Mark and recall (drop a marker, snap back), Super launch (blast up, then glide), Regroup (teleport to a party member; co-op catch-up; not in or entering a fight). All unlock at rank 7 (B-214b).
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** Teleport: along the camera's aim up to 15 m, stopping short of a wall (it doesn't pass walls). Phase step: 4 m ahead to a free spot, whatever is between (a thin wall, bars); refused with no room there. Super launch: straight up fast, then a free glide from the top.
- (2) **A** the double tap places the mark (free) when there is none, and recalls to it (paid) when there is one; the mark is used up; it lasts for the level.
- (3) **A** the farthest other party member's field pawn (the one to catch up with), landing just behind it; refused with no one there or while a fight runs.
- (4) **A** EP: teleport 3, phase step 2, recall 3, super launch 3, regroup 3; cooldowns 2 / 1 / 1 / 2 / 5 s; paid only when the move happens (all `// TODO: tune`).
**Recommendation:** A, A, A, A. (1) Dishonored's Blink (aim, then appear there; never through walls) is the best-loved short teleport; Phase step follows Hollow Knight's Shade Cloak (passing what blocks a plain dash), checking only that the far side has room; Super launch is Tears of the Kingdom's Revali's Gale (up, then the glider), widely praised. (2) Tracer's Recall and Warframe's Transference anchor use one button to mark and return; a free mark keeps it from punishing setup. (3) Co-op catch-up in It Takes Two and Diablo IV's party teleport goes to the friend you've lost; the farthest one is that friend. (4) A starting point for play-testing.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-13).
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-214e (`FieldMovementAdvanced.cpp`, `FieldSkillRules::CheckAdvancedMove`). Every movement type is built now. Looks (Void's long vanish, Darkness' shadow path) are later. Regroup's "entering a fight" is covered by the fight check only (a fight opens its own level, so the field pawn is gone). Follows from it (review 2026-11-13): the teleport aim is level with the ground (the camera's pitch is ignored), and a teleport moving under 1 m is `Blocked` and not paid; Regroup goes only to a party member's or a player's pawn. Open for Crown if wanted: phase step has no wall-thickness limit (anything leaving room 4 m ahead is crossed, which may reach sealed rooms; mark such walls thicker than 4 m or add a `Zone.NoPhase` later).

### Q-214f — Field skills part 6: what a field hit does to the fight it starts
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-13; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (B-214f, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) Which enemies take the field hit's buildup, and how much? (2) Which source does the attack start on, and what if it has no element? (3) What is "held"? (4) Starting numbers.
**Context:** B-214 row: a field hit starts the fight with first strike and carries the hit's element status buildup in (Metaphor: ReFantazio); holding it with a Water source makes it Ice; the source is switchable; the weapon's physical type applies. B-185 already judges first strike on contact.
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** every enemy in the fight it starts (the struck team, and any team caught in the join window, B-184) takes the same buildup before the first turn, as if hit by the party's first member (resistances and immunities apply as for any hit). **B** only the enemy that was struck.
- (2) **A** the weapon by default (the swing is a weapon swing); switching steps innate → weapon → ring; a source with no element carries only the weapon's physical type; no weapon and no element: a first strike with no buildup.
- (3) **A** the input decides (B-214g's `IA_Field_Attack` hold trigger); held with a Water source the hit deals Ice (D-029's Water-deals-Ice rule).
- (4) **A** 25 buildup, reach 2 m (a 0.8 m sphere swept ahead, bodies only: an encounter's trigger sphere doesn't count), 0.6 s between swings (all `// TODO: tune`).
**Recommendation:** A, A, A, A. (1) Metaphor: ReFantazio's field strikes stun or weaken the whole group they start a fight with, and Persona 5's ambush gives the whole party the opening against every enemy; both are widely praised for rewarding the field approach. One encounter is one team on the map (B-184), so "the struck enemy" has no single counterpart in the fight. (2) A weapon swing is what the player sees; Metaphor's field attack uses the equipped weapon. (3) Matches D-029: Ice is Water's held form. (4) A starting point for play-testing.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-13).
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-214f (`FieldMovementAttack.cpp`, `UEncounterComponent::StrikeFromField`, `ABattleGameMode::ApplyFieldStrike`, `FFieldStrike` carried by `UTrialRunSubsystem` like the first strike). The weapon's physical type on the map (cutting vines, B-236) waits for the world reactions job. The attack source isn't saved (the weapon each session). **D-064 exception (review 2026-11-13):** `UTrialRunSubsystem::PendingFieldStrike` is a new field on a GameInstance subsystem; like `PendingFirstStrike` it only carries the strike across the level change and is cleared before the first turn, and it moves with that carry for co-op (D-065). The field attack has no air / flight restriction.

### Q-214g — Field skills part 7: flying up and down on one axis, and how the held field attack is read
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-13; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (B-214g, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) Fly up / down: two input actions (`IA_Field_FlyUp` / `IA_Field_FlyDown`, as Q-214d's notes said) or one axis? Which keys? (2) The held field attack: the asset's hold trigger (Q-214f's wording) or timed in code?
**Context:** D-063: unique action names so rebinding comes later. InputMap's gamepad B is Back everywhere; A is Jump; L3 sprint; LT aim; RB attack; LB switch source.
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** one 1D axis `IA_Field_FlyVertical`: Space / A up, Left Ctrl / **R3** down (B stays Back). **B** two digital actions.
- (2) **A** timed in code: press then release; held 0.4 s or more is the held attack (one action, no trigger setup; a release with no press is ignored). **B** a Hold trigger on the asset plus a second binding.
**Recommendation:** A, A. (1) Unreal's own flying templates and Minecraft's creative flight use one up / down axis (Space up, crouch down; on a gamepad A up and the right stick click down), well known to players; one action is one rebind row. (2) Breath of the Wild's and Genshin's charged attacks are a press-and-hold on one button released to fire; timing it in code keeps one action and lets the threshold be one named constant.
**Answer:** **A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-13). Replaces Q-214d's `IA_Field_FlyUp` / `IA_Field_FlyDown` note and Q-214f(3)'s "the asset's hold trigger".
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-214g (`AWoRHubController`). Space is also `IA_Field_Jump` (proposed): a jump does nothing in Flying mode, so both firing is harmless.

### Q-214h — Field skills part 8: what an evolution's movement bonus and augment are
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-13; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (B-214h, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) Which evolution counts? (2) How big is the always-on bonus? (3) What is "its augment of the field skill"? (4) A broken evolution?
**Context:** FieldAbilities choice 2 (Crown, 2026-10-27): evolutions give both a small always-on movement bonus (+10-20% sprint, `// TODO: tune`) and an augment of the active movement skill; Kingdom Hearts 2's forms. Choice 4: "an evolution might give unlimited flight".
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** the evolution on the character's own slot (`GetCharacterSlotEvolution`, the one that counts as the character's, B-357e).
- (2) **A** +15% sprint by default, per evolution asset (`FieldSprintBonusPercent`, 0-100).
- (3) **A** two parts, per evolution asset: the field skill's EP costs less (25% by default, each cost at least 1; Enhanced Sprint's drain and every move and flight), and flight limits it lifts (`FieldFlightLimits`: unlimited flight, no ceiling, no-fly ignored, extra ceiling; none by default). **B** a new movement type per evolution.
- (4) **A** a broken evolution gives nothing (as its other passives).
**Recommendation:** A, A, A, A. Kingdom Hearts 2's forms level "growth abilities" that make the movement you already have better everywhere (Quick Run, High Jump, Aerial Dodge, Glide), not new buttons; a cheaper field skill and lifted flight limits are that for this game, and the ruling names unlimited flight. +15% is the middle of Crown's +10-20%.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-13). **Every existing evolution asset now gives +15% sprint and -25% field EP** (the defaults); Crown changes them per asset.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-214h (`UEvolutionItemData` Field fields, `UFieldMovementComponent::GetSeatedEvolution` / `GetFieldEPCost` / `GetEffectiveFlightLimits`, `FieldSkillRules::GetEvolutionSprintMultiplier` / `ApplyFieldEPDiscount` / `CombineFlightLimits`). Follows from it (review 2026-11-13): the start checks accept the discounted cost; the bonus multiplies Enhanced Sprint's element multiplier (Lightning ×1.5 with +15% = ×1.725), it doesn't add; the bonus reaches the sprint the next time the pawn starts sprinting. In co-op a guest's loadout isn't replicated, so its own machine doesn't see the bonus and gets corrections (`TODO`).

### Q-233b — Sessions part 2: when a friend can drop in, what happens when one leaves, and invites before EOS
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-12; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (B-233b) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) Mid-run, when can the host seat a friend who joined? (2) What happens to a friend's character when it leaves (quits or loses its connection), in and out of a fight, and if it comes back? (3) How are friends invited before EOS exists?
**Context:** Rules 06 (rules review, 2026-10-26): "other players can be invited from rest spots mid-run (drop-in)". D-150: a joiner takes an AI seat the host picks; that seat's gear goes to the host; no unclaimed members in co-op. PA-37h: a dropped host's seats are played by the AI until it rejoins. Seats are fixed during a fight (Q-21-audit). The Null subsystem (LAN) has no friends list.
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** the session stays open (a host hand-over's rejoin needs it); a friend can arrive any time, but in a run the host seats a **new** friend only while the party is at a rest spot (rested there, not walked away); outside a run, any time. **B** close the session itself between rest spots.
- (2) **A** its members turn into **AI seats held for it** (in a fight the host commands them until the fight ends, then they turn AI); if the same friend comes back and the host seats it on its held seat, it takes its own member back with its gear, anywhere outside a fight; if the host gives that seat to someone else, it is a normal AI seat (D-150: its gear to the host) and the hold is gone. Holds last for the level (a level change forgets the hold; the seat stays AI). **B** its character leaves the party at once (an empty slot).
- (3) **A** the platform's friends list (Previous / Next, Invite) on a placeholder screen, from the hub (`wor.UI.Invite`) and the rest menu; on LAN the screen says to use Find / Join. EOS and Steam are B-233c.
**Recommendation:** A, A, A. (1) The ruling names rest spots; B would also block B-234b's rejoin after a lost host. (2) Baldur's Gate 3 and Divinity: Original Sin 2 (both praised party-based co-op RPGs) keep a leaving player's characters in the party under the host's control, and give them back when the player returns; this game's own PA-37h already plays a dropped host's seats with the AI. An empty slot (B) would break D-150's "every slot is a player's character or an AI seat". (3) Epic's online subsystem design: invites go through the platform's friends interface, which is empty on Null.
**Answer:** **A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-12). Crown may change any.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-233b (`RunJoinRules::CanSeatJoiner`, `URunStateComponent::HandlePlayerLeft` / `GetHeldFor` / `CanSeatNewJoinerNow`, `UWoRSessionSubsystem::ReadFriends` / `InviteFriend`, `UInviteWidget`). Left: holds don't survive a level change; a friend who returns with a different character on its held seat swaps it like any AI seat (its old member's gear goes to the host). Review (2026-11-12): a logout while the world closes or during a host hand-over (B-234c) changes no seat; a player with no unique net id leaves a plain AI seat.

### Q-234d — Host hand-over part 3: what the new host keeps after the run, and the join order across a travel
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-09; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-234d, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) When a handed-over run ends, when does the new host get its own party and pool back, and what of the run stays with it? (2) How is the join order kept across a seamless travel?
**Context:** Q-234b (2): the new host plays the old host's copy (party, armoury, run), keeps its own wallet and look, and never writes its own slot; `USaveSubsystem::AdoptCheckpoint` restores the copy's armoury on top of the new host's pool for the rest of the session. `AWoRGameState::HostOrder` was built up as players arrived, so a new level's GameState could swap two joiners (B-234b review TODO).
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** at the run's end (the trial's exit, after its save): in the hub that follows, the party and pool come from the player's own save as it was before the hand-over; everything else stays as played (the wallet with the run-end payout, the look, journal, hidden quests, curses and counters were its own all along); its own slot is written again from then on. **B** only at a restart (today). **C** also keep in the new host's pool what the old host's characters gained during the run.
- (2) **A** each player's join place on its PlayerState (server only), carried by `CopyProperties`; the order is rebuilt from the places. **B** carry the last order over in the hand-over subsystem (a game-instance subsystem; D-064 / D-103 put per-session player state on the PlayerState).
**Recommendation:** A, A. (1) Diablo III and Borderlands 2 keep each player's character and stash their own: playing in someone else's game never puts their gear in your stash, and what you earned yourself (gold, XP) goes home with you; leaving the party puts you back in your own game with your own character. The copy's gains stay in the copy (the old host keeps it as its checkpoint copy). C would hand one player the other player's characters' gear. (2) Unreal's `APlayerState::CopyProperties` is the engine's own place for per-player data across a seamless travel (it carries the score and the ids), and Epic's ShooterGame sample keeps each player's team that way. The deckbuilder has no co-op rule (it is a card model, `docs/reference/00-index.md`).
**Answer:** **A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown may change either.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-234d (`USaveSubsystem::EndHandedOverRun` / `ReturnToOwnSave`, `UPoolSubsystem::ResetStore`, `AWoRPlayerState::JoinSequence`, `WoRHostSuccession::OrderByJoinSequence`). Left as is: a new host that hands over again in the middle of the copied run stays on the copy until a restart (its run ends on another machine). **Review fixes (2026-11-09):** (a) the upgrade memory (D-097) the run raised is kept: memory is per definition, so the own save gets every entry the run took above what the copy held when adopted (the old host's earlier memory is not taken). (b) The own slot is written at the run's end, right after the exit save (played data, own party and pool, merged memory, no run), so a quit or crash before the hub loses nothing; the in-memory return still happens when the hub loads. (c) The return acts only in the hub; if the travel to it is refused, it is dropped (the own slot is already written, so a restart brings the player back). Known: after a refused travel the player keeps playing the copy and later saves go to the copy's slot only, so a restart loads the own slot as it was at the refusal (gains after it stay on the copy; the travel is retried by leaving again). (d) Personal loot taken after the hand-over (B-235c chests): gear is run-only and leaves at run end anyway (D-111); a spell or ability from a chest is learned by the copy's character (the old host's) and stays with the copy: moving it to one of this player's characters would need a rule for which one, so it isn't done. **Desktop review (2026-11-12):** the merged upgrade memory is read from the exit save's snapshot, so a raise made between the trial's exit save and the hub load is not carried to the own save (no raise can happen there today).

### Q-170b — Action pips: what a skill costs
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170, D-088) · **Blocks:** — (B-170c)
**Question:** D-088's discussion tried costs by tier (1/1/1/2/2/2/3, then F 1 … S 7), then dropped them. With banked actions confirmed, what does an act cost?
**Options:** **A** every Attack, Ability, Refraction and Item costs 1 action; Energy alone prices big skills / **B** by tier, F–D 1, C–A 2, S 3 (so Max Actions unlocks the big skills) / **C** F 1 … S 7.
**Recommendation (superseded by Crown's answer below):** A. The deckbuilder prices skills in Energy only and never in Actions (`docs/reference/06-bonuses.md`, "The turn economy"), and that rule comes first. Two prices on one skill (Energy and actions) is the kind of double gate Crown dropped as over-engineering (D-088, "Simplified"). Banking still matters: Divinity: Original Sin 2's carried-over AP is what made its turns feel tactical. B stays an easy later change (one function from tier to cost).
**Answer (superseded by Crown's answer below):** **A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09).
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-09, supersedes the default above where they differ):** every skill, spell and item has **its own action cost**, default 1, set per asset (big skills can cost 2 or more). **Energy still applies too.** A skill whose cost is above the unit's Max Actions is unusable (greyed). Plan: `ActionPips.md` §1, §3.1, §3.3.

### Q-170c — Action pips: what costs an action, and what ends the turn
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170, D-088) · **Blocks:** — (B-170c)
**Question:** Which commands cost an action, and when does a turn end?
**Recommendation (superseded by Crown's answer below) (default):**
- Attack, Ability, Refraction, Item: 1 action each.
- **Defend:** free, and ends the turn (the rest stays banked). Octopath Traveler's Defend also ends the turn and still lets BP build.
- **End Turn** (new): ends the turn, banking the rest (Divinity: Original Sin 2's "end turn" keeps unused AP).
- **Arming a ritual:** 1 action, and ends the turn (ET-16 already ends it).
- **Weapon / ring switch:** the first each turn is free; a second costs 1 action (Crown's own note on ET-19).
- **Row move:** one free per turn (D-096), unchanged.
- **Flee:** free; it ends the fight (D-091).
- The turn also ends at 0 actions; a turn that **starts** with 0 (after the gain) is skipped (Crown, 2026-11-09).
**Answer (superseded by Crown's answer below):** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09).
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-09, supersedes the default above where they differ):** the turn ends **at 0 actions, on End Turn, or on Defend**; unused actions bank. **Arming a ritual does not end the turn**; it just costs its actions (ET-16 changes). **Weapon switching is always free** (no free-first rule, never costs; ET-19 changes). The row move and Flee stay as in the default. Plan: `ActionPips.md` §1, §3.3.

### Q-170d — Fixed rotation: the order within a round
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170, D-088) · **Blocks:** — (B-170b)
**Question:** With Turn Speed gone, who goes when in a round?
**Options:** **A** the deckbuilder's turn belt: sides alternate (P1 E1 P2 E2 …); a smaller side fills the empty seats in turn, carried across rounds; within a side, party-screen order and enemy spawn slot / **B** sides alternate while both have members left, then the rest of the larger side in order (each combatant exactly once a round).
**Recommendation:** A. It is the deckbuilder's rule (`WoR_TCG_Pivot_Design.md` §7c and §8 items 53–56), which comes first. It also answers the classic "a lone boss loses to the party's action count" problem the way D&D 5e's legendary actions do (a boss acts between the heroes' turns; widely used and praised). **Cost:** a lone boss acts 4 times a round against 4 heroes, so boss damage per hit needs a tuning pass. Party first in round 1 unless the enemy struck first.
**Answer:** **A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown: if a lone boss acting once per hero feels wrong, B is a small change in one function.
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-09, supersedes the default above where they differ):** **approved as planned** (A): sides alternate; a lone boss acts after every hero; boss damage gets a tuning pass.

### Q-170e — Ice: which speed does it slow?
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170, D-088, D-151) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-088 first said "Ice slows Action Speed". D-151 split Action Speed and Spell Speed again, and D-090 / B-064a made Ice's break "a heavier Water", which slows both. Which stands?
**Options:** **A** both, as built (Action Speed and Spell Speed, plus Ice's damage) / **B** Action Speed only, so Ice differs from Water by more than its damage / **C** Spell Speed only.
**Recommendation (superseded by Crown's answer below):** A. It is the latest ruling and already in the game (`BreakTable`, `ICE_BREAK_SPEED_SLOW_PERCENT`), so no code changes. Final Fantasy's Slow slows every kind of action and players read it at a glance. With Turn Speed gone, a slow makes the holder's attacks and spells easier to defend against (wider defense windows) and slower to play out.
**Answer (superseded by Crown's answer below):** **A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09).
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-09, supersedes the default above where they differ):** **Ice** slows Action Speed and Spell Speed, **removes 1 action** and deals its damage. **Water** slows **Spell Speed only**. **Wind** slows **Action Speed** and still takes 1 action at the target's next turn. **Confirmed by Crown (2026-11-09):** Ice's action comes off **at once** (Wind's is the one Crown timed at the next turn). Constants (`// TODO: tune`): `ICE_BREAK_ACTIONS_LOST` 1, `WIND_BREAK_ACTIONS_LOST` 1, `WIND_BREAK_ACTION_SPEED_SLOW_PERCENT` (starts at Water's 30%). Behind `wor.Turns.ActionPips` with the rest. Plan: `ActionPips.md` §3.4; Rules SB-22, SB-23, SB-29 ("Coming with B-170").

### Q-170f — Bonus turns, Extra Action and first strike with action pips
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170, D-088) · **Blocks:** — (B-170b, B-170d)
**Question:** Does Emerald's bonus turn (D-052) become +1 action? Does first strike (D-069 / Q-185) become +1 action or stay "act first"?
**Recommendation (superseded by Crown's answer below) (default):**
- **Emerald (Wind's crystal), changed by Crown's Wind rule (D-088, 2026-11-09):** on an **enemy** it makes them **lose their next turn** in the rotation (the built skip, ET-13; S: the next two, Q-341b).
- **Emerald on yourself or an ally: actions, not a bonus turn (Crown, 2026-11-09).** Lower tiers give +1 action after a set number of the holder's turns; S gives 2 to 3 actions at once. Ladder (reference-backed default, all `// TODO: tune`): F +1 after 6 turns, E 5, D 4, C 3, B 2, A 1 (its next turn); S +2 to +3 now, rolled per crystal. Why: it reuses Emerald's own former delay table (F 6 to S 0, Q-341b), so the tier spacing is one this game already had; an immediate +2 echoes Divinity: Original Sin 2's Adrenaline (+2 AP now, a well-liked tempo tool), topped at 3 like Octopath Traveler's maximum boost. A delayed gain lands in step 2 of the turn-start order (ActionPips.md §1), before the 0-action check. The S gain may pass Max Actions for that turn (Q-170g). Replaces CC's earlier "keeps its bonus turn" reading.
- **The Extra Action effect becomes +1 action now** (what its name says; the deckbuilder: "an Action effect arrives in the pool at once").
- **First strike stays as built:** the striking side takes its whole first round, then the alternation starts with that side.
**Answer (superseded by Crown's answer below):** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09), except Emerald on yourself or an ally: **Crown, 2026-11-09: actions scaled by tier** (above; the ladder numbers are the reference-backed default).
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-09, supersedes the default above where they differ):** **keep both effects:** **Gain Action** (+1 action now, within the turn; the new `GainActions`) and **Extra Turn** (a whole bonus turn, as built). The existing **Extra Action** effects **become Extra Turns** (they keep their bonus turn; the name changes; ET-15 stays). **Emerald on yourself or an ally** (replaces the 6..1 ladder): F +1 action in 2 turns; E +1 next turn; D +1 now; C +2 in 2 turns; B +2 next turn; A +2 now; S +3 now. **Emerald's actions may go over Max** (for that turn only: what is above Max is lost at the turn's end, Crown 2026-11-12, Q-170f2). On an enemy, Emerald still removes their next turn. Constants (`// TODO: tune`): `EMERALD_ALLY_ACTIONS_GAINED` {1,1,1,2,2,2,3}, `EMERALD_ALLY_GAIN_DELAY_TURNS` {2,1,0,2,1,0,0} (F..S). First strike: as in the default. Plan: `ActionPips.md` §3.4.

### Q-170g — Effects that give or take actions
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170, D-088, Crown's note of 2026-11-09) · **Blocks:** — (B-170d)
**Question:** Which effect types, and can a gain go past Max Actions?
**Recommendation (default):** four new effect types:
- `GainActions` (+N, now or at the holder's next turn start);
- `LoseActions` (−N, same two timings, never below 0);
- `MaxActionsBuff` / `MaxActionsDebuff` (±N Max for N holder turns, D-051).
- Crown's "+1 now, −1 next turn" is one skill with two rows, exactly Divinity: Original Sin 2's Adrenaline (+2 AP now, −2 next turn), a well-liked tempo skill.
- Wind's break becomes `LoseActions` 1 at the next turn start (D-090's placeholder).
- **A gain "now" may go past Max for this turn;** (superseded: the extra now stays banked, see Crown's answer below) the bank is trimmed to Max when the turn ends, so "+1 action" is never wasted at a full bank (CC's call).
**Answer:** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09).
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-09, supersedes the default above where they differ):** **approved:** gain actions, lose actions (now or next turn), raise or lower Max Actions for N turns. A gain may pass Max and the extra **stays banked until spent** (follows Crown's "Emerald's actions may go over max"; the default's trim at turn end is dropped; confirmed by Crown 2026-11-09); the regain adds nothing at or above Max. **Changed by Crown (2026-11-12, Q-170f2):** the extra above Max lasts only until the end of the holder's turn, then it is lost; actions up to Max still bank.

### Q-170h — Reflex's two jobs, and Spirit's Turn Speed slot
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-170, D-088) · **Blocks:** — (B-170a, B-170g)
**Question:** Reflex widens the defense window today. Does it keep that job as well as setting Max Actions? And Turn Speed is one of Spirit's five sub-stats: what fills its place?
**Options:** **A** Reflex keeps both jobs; Spirit keeps four sub-stats (Turn Speed retired, its points inert, data validation flags them) / **B** Reflex sets Max Actions only, and the window loses its stat / **C** move Max Actions to a Spirit sub-stat.
**Recommendation (superseded by Crown's answer below):** A. One "quick" stat that acts and reacts more reads naturally, and none of Reflex's gear, stones or buffs need reworking. Spirit loses no value in practice: Max Energy now also sets the per-turn Energy refill (D-089 as changed, B-173). Crown already chose Reflex for Max Actions (D-088), so C is out.
**Answer (superseded by Crown's answer below):** **A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Watch in play: if Reflex becomes a must-have, split it (B).
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-09, supersedes the default above where they differ):** **approved:** Reflex = the regain amount + the defense window (Reflex no longer sets Max Actions, Q-170); Turn Speed removed from Spirit.

### Q-394 — Cast visual source: the cases D-170 leaves open
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-394, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-170 sets weapon / adapter-type default / Generic preference. (1) Which visual do a Refractor's innate spells and an evolution's spells show? (2) Where does the Generic "class preference" live, and what is its default? (3) Does the weapon's fixed visual cover its abilities and weapon-crystal spells too? (4) What plays before any art is authored?
**Options / as built (A):** (1) the adapter-type default, read from the worn adapter's type, **Ring** with none (D-170: "rings are the default"; casters share the one setting); a spell item keeps its own visual. (2) **class data**, one value on the presentation-defaults asset (`UImpactVisualDefaults::GenericCastVFXPreference`), default **Adapter**, applied to every spell a Generic casts while it wears both. (3) yes: attacks, abilities and weapon-crystal spells play `UWeaponData::CastVFX`. (4) the skill's own Muzzle VFX (today's look) whenever the picked source has nothing set. **B** (2) a per-character player setting (needs a menu and a save field).
**Recommendation:** A. Elden Ring and Dark Souls show a spell's cast through the catalyst that casts it (staff glow, seal glow), so the spell tool decides the look; Final Fantasy XIV's casters all share one cast circle per job rather than per item, the same as one default per adapter type. Keeping (2) as class data matches D-170's word "class" and adds no setting before the cosmetics work.
- **No adapter, not a Refractor (review):** the skill's own visual, not the Ring default, so enemies' innate casts keep their look.
- **D-170's per-character cast setting** (adapters "share the character's own setting with Refractors and Generic") is not built: it waits for the cosmetics work; today the adapter type's default stands in.
**Answer:** **A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown may change any part; a per-character setting (B) is one more input to `CastVisuals::Resolve`.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-394 (`Combat/Presentation/CastVisuals.h`; `UWeaponData::CastVFX`; `UImpactVisualDefaults::AdapterCastVFX`, `GenericCastVFXPreference`).

### Q-392 — The two guards: where the setting lives, what "equipping" resets, which montages
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-392, D-170) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** Where does the per-character guard setting live, when does equipping a weapon put it back to Weapon, and which montages are the two guards?
**Context:** Every Guard plays `UCosmeticsData::BlockMontage` today (B-202, `DefenseSystem.cpp` `PlayDefenseAnimation`). `UWeaponData::ParryMontage` and a per-weapon, unsaved `FWeaponLoadoutEntry::bUseWeaponParryAnimation` exist from before (only the obsolete `GetParryMontage` reads them). There is no weapon-type asset.
**Recommendation (default):**
- The setting is per party member (`FPartyMember::GuardStyle`, saved, save v32), read and set through the battle pawn's loadout (`ULoadoutComponent::GetGuardStyle` / `SetGuardStyle`). A pawn with no party record (an enemy) keeps its own, default Weapon.
- Choosing Unarmed remembers the weapon put away (its owned GUID). With any other weapon in hand the weapon guard is back, so equipping a weapon makes the weapon guard the default without a hook in every equip path. Putting the same weapon back in hand later keeps it put away. With no weapon in hand the guard is always Unarmed.
- The weapon guard is the weapon asset's `ParryMontage`, authored per weapon with its type's clip (AssetAudit "Weapon families"). The unarmed guard is the character's `Cosmetics->BlockMontage` (the body), and customising it means picking that asset. A weapon with no `ParryMontage` yet plays the unarmed guard. Reference: in Elden Ring and Dark Souls the guard animation follows the item held (one per weapon or shield type), and how you hold it changes only the look; players understand it well. The window doesn't change (D-170, Expedition 33).
- It is set from the console for now (`wor.Guard.Set`), and from the loadout screen later (D-024). There is no player-facing label yet; the screen adds its LOCTEXT names when it is built (D-062).
**Answer:** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). **Known (desktop review, 2026-11-12):** in co-op a guest's guard choice stays on its own machine (`UParty` is not replicated), so the host plays the default weapon guard for a guest's character until the choice goes through a server call with the party record's replication (TODO in `ULoadoutComponent::SetGuardStyle`, D-103). **Fixed (B-392b, Crown chose B on Q5, 2026-11-12):** a guest's choice now reaches the host (`AWoRPlayerState::ServerSetGuard`), which applies it to its copy of the guest's fighter and its party record. Choosing Unarmed is refused when the weapon in hand has no persistent id.
**Answer: Crown, 2026-11-12:** **kept** as built (the per-member guard setting, Unarmed remembers the weapon put away, the weapon guard is the weapon's `ParryMontage`, the unarmed guard the character's `BlockMontage`). The co-op note above (a guest's choice stays on its machine until the D-103 server call) is fixed by B-392b.

### Q-393 — Landing a parry: what counts, which element, which priority
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-393, D-170 corrected) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-170 (corrected) says weapons always spark, a parry-effect cosmetic per guard plays on a landed parry coloured by the element behind it, unarmed with no element shows nothing, and a loadout setting picks the priority between sources. What counts as a landed parry, where do the cosmetic and the setting live, and which sources count for which guard?
**Context:** a successful Guard plays the defender's `UCosmeticsData::GuardSparkVFX` and a Perfect Guard its flash and sound (B-202, B-369). There is no weapon element field: a weapon's element is its crystal (`FRuntimeAttachedItem::GetElement`). `ESpellElement::Generic` means "inherit" (no colour of its own).
**Recommendation (default):**
- **A landed parry** is any successful Guard, normal or Perfect (D-084: Guard is the parry). The weapon guard's spark plays on a normal Guard; a **Perfect Guard keeps its flash in the spark's place** (B-202), and the parry effect plays on both. The unarmed guard has no spark.
- **The cosmetic** is two slots on the character's cosmetics (`UCosmeticsData::WeaponGuardParryEffectVFX` / `UnarmedGuardParryEffectVFX`, soft references), empty until art: with none authored, play is the spark as before. It is tinted through the `CoreColor` / `EdgeColor` user parameters with the element's palette colour (`ElementColors`), as skill VFX are.
- **The sources:** the weapon's crystal counts **only for the weapon guard** (the unarmed guard has put the weapon away); the character's own magic is a Refractor's element, else its adapter's crystal (a Resonator's active adapter; another class's primary adapter). None, Generic and a broken crystal give no colour, so a weapon with no crystal sparks only and the unarmed guard shows nothing.
- **The priority setting** (`EParryElementPriority`: Weapon / Magic) is per party member, beside the guard (`FPartyMember::ParryElementPriority`, saved, **save v33**), read through the loadout. It decides only when both sources have an element; the other fills in when the first has none. **Default Weapon:** the thing that parried colours it. Set from the console for now (`wor.Guard.SetParryPriority`), from the loadout screen later (D-024); no player-facing label yet (D-062, as Q-392).
- Reference: Sekiro's deflect is one readable spark for every weapon (widely praised for clarity), and Elden Ring's weapon buffs (fire, lightning, holy) colour the weapon you hold, so the element shows on the thing in use. Here the spark stays the same and the element tint on the parry effect carries the identity, coming first from what parried.
**Answer:** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). **Known (desktop review, 2026-11-12):** as with the guard (Q-392), a co-op guest's priority stays on its own machine; the host picks the visual, so a guest's character uses the default (Weapon) until the setting goes through a server call (TODO in `ULoadoutComponent::SetParryElementPriority`, D-103). **Fixed (B-392b, 2026-11-12):** it reaches the host through `AWoRPlayerState::ServerSetParryPriority`.
### Q-391 — The placeholder battle HUD: layout, enemy HP, statuses and menu keys
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-391) · **Blocks:** — (B-391 ships with it)
**Question:** The new plain C++ battle HUD (VisualGameplayPlan step 1) had to pick where things sit, what enemies show, how statuses look and which keys the menu uses. Keep these?
**Context:** `UI/Combat/Battle/BattleHUDWidget.cpp` (`BattleHUDStyle`), `BattleCommandMenuWidget.cpp`, `ACombatPlayerController::GetMenuKeys`. References: the Expedition 33 study (`docs/analysis/MaelleCombatPresentationStudy.md` §7 and §11), FeelGuide rows 5 and 13.
**Options / what was built:**
1. **Layout:** turn order down the left edge (current actor first, then the next six; E33's left column of about eight cards); party cards bottom-left; enemy cards top-right; command menu bottom-right; a **Statuses** button top centre. E33 puts the party bottom-right and the menu beside the acting hero; the placeholder keeps the menu fixed bottom-right, so the party moves left. The menu beside the hero (study U5) comes with the CommonUI HUD.
2. **Enemy HP:** a bar, **no numbers** (E33 §11: normal enemies show a small HP bar, no numbers). Party cards show "HP x / y" and "EP x / y".
3. **Statuses:** small text chips on each card ("Burn 2", "Haste x3": turns left, stacks), and one **Statuses** button that opens every combatant's statuses in full (name, element, turns, stacks, description), per FeelGuide 5. Icons replace the chips with the art pass.
4. **Menu keys (main menu only, one key per category, two presses to act: FeelGuide 13; E33's menu rows are each a face button and a word, §7):** Attack **1 / A**, Skills **2 / X** (Abilities, else a weapon resonance), Spells **3 / B** (Refractions, else a breakthrough or ring resonance), Defend **G / Y** (built, B-093), Items **4 / D-pad Up**, Flee **5 / D-pad Down**, Switch weapon **6 / D-pad Left**, Change ring **7 / D-pad Right**. B stays Back everywhere except the main menu, where there is nothing to go back to. Picking inside a submenu or a target list is a click for now; gamepad navigation there comes with the CommonUI menu (D-024).
5. **Switch:** `wor.UI.NewHUD` 1 (default) builds the new HUD; 0 builds the old WBP HUD, kept until Crown's PIE pass.
**Recommendation:** all five as built: they follow the E33 study and FeelGuide 5 and 13, and every value is one named constant or one key row (`docs/Design/InputMap.md`).
**Answer:** all five, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Damage numbers follow Crown's Q-111 rule (element colour, size and brightness by resistance, grey when immune) in B-391b.

### Q-391b — Damage numbers and the Broken Darkness card: the build details
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-391b) · **Blocks:** — (B-391b ships with it)
**Question:** Crown's Q-111 rule fixes what a damage number means (element colour, Physical white; weak bigger, brighter, a glow; resisted smaller, dimmer; immune grey and small). The build needed the numbers behind it, and a few details the rule doesn't name. Keep these?
**Context:** `UI/Combat/Battle/DamageNumberView.h` (every value one named constant, `TODO: tune`), `BattleHUDWidget.cpp`, `BattleHUDView::FormatAbsorbLine`. The deckbuilder has no damage-number rule (its reference is a card model with no match presentation), so the reference is Expedition 33 (`docs/analysis/MaelleCombatPresentationStudy.md` §11: numbers at the hit point, about 1-2 s, several stack upward, small green "+" heals, a dark outline).
**What was built:**
1. **Size and brightness are linear in the element multiplier**, clamped: at the 50% resistance cap a weak hit is 1.5x size and lifted halfway to white with a glow (a wide outline in its own colour); a resisted hit 0.65x (clamp) and half as bright; within ±0.05 of 1 is neutral. A crit adds +0.25 size on top. Genshin Impact's element-coloured numbers (Crown's Q-111 model) and E33's size-by-strength.
2. **Immune** is a multiplier at or below 0: grey, 0.6x. No hit can reach it today (resistance caps at 50%), so the style is ready for an immunity source; a hit that took 0 for any other reason (fully warded, buildup only) shows **no number**, as E33 shows none for a miss.
3. **Heals** are green "+N" at 0.85x (E33). Sent from `USkillEffectManager::BroadcastHealing`, so every reported heal shows on every machine.
4. **Motion:** at the top of the target (its collision top; the exact impact point isn't in the hit event), rising 60 units over 1.5 s, fading over the last 40%, each further number on the same target starting 30 units higher (E33's ~1-2 s, stacking). A pool of 16; the oldest is reused when all are up.
5. **Darkness** keeps its pale outline (D-106); every other number has E33's dark outline.
6. **Broken Darkness card:** the EP line reads "Absorb x / y" (the old panel's "Abs", spelled out), and past max ", overload z%", tinted white / yellow / orange / red at the old panel's thresholds (`CombatConstants::OVERLOAD_*_THRESHOLD`).
7. **Split:** the status buildup bar, the weapon / loadout name and tooltip and the durability header go to B-391c, to keep B-391b at M.
**Recommendation:** all seven as built (references above; not lore). Crown tunes the numbers in PIE.
**Answer:** all seven, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown may change any.

### Q-391c — The buildup bar, gear line, durability header and the absorb cue: the build details
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-391c) · **Blocks:** — (B-391c ships with it)
**Question:** Bringing the old HUD's buildup bar, weapon name and durability header to the new HUD, and adding an absorb cue, needed a few choices the rulings don't name. Keep these?
**Context:** `UI/Combat/Battle/BattleHUDView.h` (`CRACK_START_FRACTION`, `CRACK_COUNT`), `BattleHUDWidget.cpp` (`BattleHUDStyle`), `BattleCommandMenuWidget.cpp`, `DamageNumberView.h` (`SHOW_ABSORB_CUE`, `ABSORBED_SCALE`; now `SHOW_NEGATED_CUE`, `NEGATED_SCALE`). The deckbuilder has no HUD rule (its reference is a card model, no match presentation), so the references are Crown's Q-111 A, the old HUD itself, and other games below.
**What was built:**
1. **Buildup bar on every card**, under the HP bar, tinted as the old panel (the last hit's element; a Broken Darkness attacker darkens it; physical neutral). Party cards add "Status x / y"; enemy cards show the bar only, as their HP (Q-391).
2. **Cracks (Q-111 A):** clean below half, then **four** thin dark marks at 50 / 62.5 / 75 / 87.5% appear as the bar passes each, so it "cracks more and more" toward the break. The **shatter** and the dimmed shards during the break wait for the art pass; the break's status chip shows it today. Octopath Traveler's shield count and Honkai: Star Rail's toughness bar breaking, both praised for reading at a glance (Q-111's own references).
3. **Gear:** a party card's line reads "weapon · ring" (the Resonator's active ring, else the primary ring); hovering the card shows "Loadout / Weapon / Ring". To make the hover work, party cards now take the mouse; enemy cards still let clicks through.
4. **Durability header:** the old header's rule and labels ("AD x / y" ring first, then "WD x / y"; none without a crystal), under the menu title. One fix: a Generic / Caster ring reads its primary ring (the old header showed "AD:0/0" there since B-304 made `GetActiveRing` Resonator-only).
5. **Absorb cue:** a hit that took 0 on a character in Broken Darkness form floats a small **"Absorbed"** in the hit's colour (0.75x), instead of nothing. Persona 5 prints a word (NULL / DRAIN / REPEL) where a hit does no damage, and its readable battle feedback is widely praised; Expedition 33 shows nothing for a miss, which is today's behaviour. One switch (`DamageNumberView::SHOW_ABSORB_CUE`, default on): **Crown may veto it** and it goes back to no number. The hit event doesn't say why it was 0, so a ward stopping a whole hit on Broken Darkness reads "Absorbed" too. Two Broken Darkness checks are in play, each matching its old source: the cue asks the target's `UCharacterDataComponent::IsBrokenDarkness()` (the replicated flag), the buildup tint asks the attacker's `UBrokenDarknessManager::IsInBDForm()` (the manager's transform flag, as the old panel). They can disagree for a born-in-Broken-Darkness character before its manager mirrors the flag (`InitializeBornBrokenDarkness`), or on a client where the manager's flag isn't replicated.
6. **Combatants that join mid-fight:** not built. Nothing in the code adds or announces a combatant mid-fight (no summons exist); when a summon system lands, it should broadcast a join event and the HUD add a card from a spare pool (TransBuffer rule).
**Recommendation:** all six as built (references above; not lore). Crown tunes sizes and colours in PIE.
**Answer:** all six, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown may change any; the absorb cue is one switch.
**Answer: Crown, 2026-11-12:** all six kept, with one change: the cue text is **"Negated", not "Absorbed"** ("Absorbed" reads as Broken Darkness's energy absorption); **on by default**. `LOCTEXT("Negated", "Negated")` in `DamageNumberView::GetNegatedLabel`; the code names follow the word (`EDamageNumberTier::Negated`, `SHOW_NEGATED_CUE`, `NEGATED_SCALE`, `IsNegatedHit`, `MakeNegatedStyle`).

### Q-435 — Same-element stacking: spells too, or abilities and attacks only?
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14; replaces the reference-backed default A) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-435, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-174 says "an infused action uses every same-element source". A spell is origin-bound to one source (`GetAllowedInfusionSourcesForSpell`, D-110), and the battle HUD plan shows a spell's one lit source tab. Should a spell also stack (a free ring or weapon-crystal spell would then pay a stacked innate's full spell EP, and HP at L2)?
**Context:** `UActionExecutor::ResolveSameElementInfusionSources` (`ActionExecutor.cpp`); cost in `ActionExecutorCosts.cpp`.
**Options:** **A** abilities and attacks stack; spells keep their one origin source (as built). **B** spells stack too: every same-element source pays its own cost on a spell as well.
**Recommendation:** A. It keeps this game's own ruling (a spell is bound to where it is slotted, D-110) and the HUD plan's one lit tab. With B, a ring spell that costs 0 EP today would quietly start costing a full innate spell's EP. No outside game was found that settles this exact case, so the recommendation rests on the existing ruling.
**Answer:** **B** (Crown, 2026-11-14; replaces the default A): spells stack too, under D-174's rule: a spell stays locked to its origin source's element (adapter, weapon crystal, innate or evolution); every other source the character has with that element joins the cast, each paying its own cost (so a free adapter spell starts costing innate EP when innate shares the element), and each extra source adds +25% buildup, same cap. **Exception:** a Reality or Broken Darkness character may infuse a spell with their own element **or** its source element; whichever they pick, the same stacking applies.
~~**A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-14).~~
**Notes for Claude:** abilities and attacks built in B-435; spells are **B-435b**.

### Q-435b — Spell stacking: only when infused? and a Broken Darkness evolution in the stack
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-13; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (B-435b, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) Q-435 B says spells stack; does an uninfused (L0) cast stack too, so every normal cast of a free ring spell by a same-element Caster costs the innate's EP? (2) A Broken Darkness character's evolution, stacked on a spell, is free at the EP layer (its cost is wear, which only its own origin path charges): does it still join?
**Context:** D-174 / ED-51: "an infused action uses every same-element source". Abilities stack only when infused (B-435). Crown's Q-435 B example: "a free adapter spell starts costing innate EP when innate shares the element".
**Options / as built:**
- (1) **A** only an infused spell (L1 / L2) stacks, as abilities: an L0 cast keeps its one origin and its old cost. **B** every cast stacks.
- (2) **A** it joins (+25% buildup) and pays at commit through the evolution source's own commit path (L2 HP); no EP, as when it is the origin. **B** it doesn't join.
**Recommendation:** A, A. (1) The ruling's own words are "an infused action"; Final Fantasy XIV's and Expedition 33's resource-boosted casts are opt-in, so a plain cast never costs more than it shows; one rule for spells and abilities is easier to learn. Crown's example still holds for every infused cast. (2) Keeps BD's evolution rule (wear, not EP) unchanged rather than inventing a second price.
**Answer:** **A, A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-13).
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-435b. Follows from it: a BD caster who picks Innate on an evolution spell now hits with their own element (Darkness), not the evolution's (Q-435 B's "their own element"). Left: the battle menu's ring and weapon spell lists lock the source, so a BD / Reality caster can't yet pick Innate on those from the UI (only the Breakthrough list cycles sources); a UI follow-up.

### Q-402 — Side-on Defence view: which side, how wide, and what "no shake" covers
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-402, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** Crown's Q2 (2026-11-09) made the Defence view a low side-on two-shot, steady, no shake (study R10, R10b, R11). Still open: (1) which side of the fight the camera stands on; (2) how a wide shot for several targets differs from the two-shot; (3) when shake and the FOV punch are held, and what happens to them afterwards.
**Context:** `FCombatCameraRules::ComputeDefenceView` / `SteadyForDefence` (`Combat/Camera/CombatCameraTypes.cpp`), `UCombatCameraDirector::IsViewerDefending` / `PlayShot`.
**Options / as built (A):** (1) the **attacker's right** (the weapon hand of most rigs), swung 20° toward the defenders' backs so they sit nearer, the camera at about hip height looking a little up; one constant flips it (`WeaponSideSign`). (2) the **same rule**, stood back until every defender and the attacker fit, with more padding and a longer minimum (700 cm against 450 cm). (3) held while the viewer defends: the Defence hand-off is pending or showing, or a viewer character's defense window is open; **dropped, not replayed**: the first shot after the combo has its punch again. The view is set once per enemy action, so the angle only changes between actions. **B** (1) the side the turn view looks from; (3) replay the held shake when the combo ends.
**Recommendation:** A. Expedition 33's defend shots (Painted Maelle, 5:38–5:52, 6:10–6:31; study §1) keep the attacker's sword in view from the side with one fighter near the camera, and its whole-party attacks (Part 2, 3:23) hold every hero in one wide side-on shot; its parries get text and a flash while the camera stays put, with no delayed shake (R11). FeelGuide 3 ("no pause") and Sekiro's deflects point the same way: a late shake after the combo would land on nothing.
**Answer:** **A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown may change any; every number is a named constant `TODO: tune`.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-402. Cutting between side angles inside a long combo (study §1, "a few clean cuts") is not built: one angle per enemy action.
### Q-405 — Boss framing and kill shots: the details the row leaves open
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-405, study R13/R14) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** B-405 builds the low boss camera and the two kill shots. Six details aren't in the row: (1) which ranks count as "boss"; (2) a fight with a boss and minions; (3) which views get the height; (4) when the kill shot starts and how it delays the rewards; (5) what "breaks apart" means in code; (6) camera movement off.
**Options / Recommendation:** Expedition 33 (study Part 2 §9 and §12: the Dualliste boss vs the field fight; its boss staging is widely praised):
1. **Boss and Mini-boss** get the low camera and the Boss kill shot; **Normal and Elite** stay at eye level with the Normal shot (Elite is a beefier normal fight, Q-224). An enemy's own data (`UCharacterData::EnemyCameraHeight`) can override its rank: Low for a tall normal enemy (E33 shot its golem low for size), EyeLevel for a small boss.
2. The turn view is **low while any living enemy is low**; once the boss falls, its minions are framed at eye level again.
3. **The turn view only** (centred-wide and over-the-shoulder). Not-your-turn, the static view, Defence and the skill shots keep their framing; the Tactical side-on view is an overview and keeps its height. Normal fights keep today's turn view exactly (the "eye level" of the row; B-157 / B-398 tune it).
4. It starts **when the killing action completes** (the win is checked there) and **holds the fight open**: the rewards, results and return to the world come after it (FeelGuide 6). The kill shot is chosen by the **last enemy down's own rank**, not the fight's. Normal: wide and high from the party's side, darkened (saturation 0.35, vignette 0.8), 1.5 s. Boss: close, low and to one side, looking up, 5 s, breaking apart at 2 s. No slow motion (it would slow gameplay timers). All `TODO: tune`.
5. **One Niagara effect** on the camera director (`BreakApartEffect`, soft, D-057) spawned at the boss, then the boss is **hidden**. Empty: the boss stays as it fell. A per-boss effect on `UBossData` can come later if bosses need their own.
6. **Movement off** (D-078): the static view holds instead, but the hold, the darkening and the break-apart stay (they are pacing, not camera motion).
**Answer:** all six as recommended, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown: change any in PIE (`docs/PIE/B-405.md`).
### Q-398 — Turn-view setting: the over-the-shoulder layout, and where the setting is kept
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-398, study R1, Q1) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** Crown's Q1 answer makes the turn view a player setting (Centred Wide default, D-074; Over-the-Shoulder option). How exactly is Over-the-Shoulder framed, where is the setting kept, and what happens to the third style?
**Context:** `FCombatCameraRules::ComputeTurnView` (`Combat/Camera/CombatCameraTypes.cpp`); the old Over-the-Shoulder sat a fixed 260 cm back and 90 cm right, so the character's screen spot moved with the distance to the enemies. No settings save or settings screen existed (D-024).
**Recommendation (default):**
- **Layout:** the camera stands behind and to the **right** of the acting character, aimed along **its own** line to the enemy centre, which sits mid-frame; the character lands in the **lower-left third**, back to the camera (about x 28 %, y 80 % of the screen). The offsets are parts of that distance (back 0.35, right 0.25, up 0.2; never scaled below 500 cm), so every party member at any range gets the same layout. Reference: Expedition 33's turn view, the same for every hero in the Maelle, Dualliste and field fights (study §1, §9, R1 [confirms]): a widely praised combat presentation where the player always knows where "us" (bottom-left) and "them" (middle) are.
- **Kept:** in the player's save slot (`FSavedSettings`, D-059 explicit struct, **save v34**), stays the player's own on a host hand-over, read at the start of every fight; set from `wor.Camera.Style` until the settings screen (D-024). Reference: Expedition 33 and console RPGs generally keep camera options in the game's settings, persistent across sessions, not per fight.
- **Tactical** stays reachable through the same setting (D-074 (2) offered it); the settings screen may show only the two Crown named.
- The player's saved setting overrides a value typed in the orchestrator's Details panel.
- A boss's lower, looking-up turn view (R13 as changed) is not in this job.
**Answer:** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown judges the look in PIE (`docs/PIE/B-398.md`); the offsets are `TODO: tune`. **Crown, 2026-11-12 (PIE B-398 check 14):** kept the default: Over the Shoulder also goes low and looks up against bosses and mini-bosses (B-405).
- **Known (desktop merge, 2026-11-12):** against a boss or mini-boss, B-405's low height (`FBossFramingRules::ApplyHeight`) is applied on top of Over the Shoulder too (B-405's own PIE check 3), so the hero can leave its lower-left third in those fights. Kept as built; Crown may veto (PIE B-398 check 14).
### Q-403 — Shot-pattern tuning: the rush, the impact two-shot and the held spread
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-403) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The row (study R5, R6, R8) leaves four build choices open: which skills rush, where the impact camera stands, how big the normal-hit punch and shake are, and when the spread is "still".
**Context:** `FCombatCameraRules::ComputeShot` and the `CombatCameraShots` constants (`CombatCameraTypes.cpp`); the director plays them (`CombatCameraDirector.cpp` `PlayShot`).
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):**
- **Rush on melee only.** A melee ability's wind-up becomes a 0.25 s rush in (from 420 cm back to the 200 cm wind-up end) with motion blur 0.8 on the camera; spells (melee-warping spells included, as the row says "spells don't") and ranged skills keep the 1.2 s slow push. With camera movement off (when cinematics still play), the slow push (study R15). Expedition 33's confirm rush (study §1, 5:33.8, 6:52.7) is the reference: the cut from still to fast is what makes the press feel strong (study §2 pattern 3).
- **Impact two-shot:** the camera stands out to the side of the caster-target line, 120 cm toward the caster (three-quarter front of the target), 40 cm above the fighters' centres, looking at their middle; it pulls out with their gap so both stay in frame at any style (the framing check now asks for the caster too). Expedition 33's normal hits (study §1, 5:35.4; pattern 4: "close enough to feel, wide enough to read").
- **Smaller punch and shake:** FOV punch 5 (crit 10, was 8 / 14), shake 0.25 (crit 0.6, was 0.6 / 1.0). Crits stay bigger (FeelGuide 4).
- **Spread holds still:** no shake and no FOV punch at all, even on a crit; nothing replaces the pull-out until the next turn, the Defence hand-off or another action. Our prompts are on the HUD (screen space), so "timing prompt kept on screen" holds; anything shown beside a target stays in frame because every target does. Expedition 33's area skill (study §1, 6:53–6:56) holds still so the whole effect fits.
- **The impact's FOV punch is cut from 8 / 14 to 5 / 10** (normal / crit). R6 says "keep the small FOV punch"; B-371's numbers were never tuned in PIE, so this is a tuning step, listed for Crown to keep or undo.
**Answer:** the recommendation, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). All numbers `TODO: tune` in Crown's B-157 PIE pass.
**Notes for Claude:** the rush doesn't follow the dashing caster yet (the camera ends 200 cm behind where the caster started); if it reads badly in PIE, follow the caster during the rush as a follow-up.
**Answer: Crown, 2026-11-12:** **keep the softer FOV punch: 5 / 10** (normal / crit; was 8 / 14). Confirmed. The other three choices stand as built; the numbers stay `TODO: tune` for the B-157 PIE pass.

### Q-407 — Short signature-spell cameras: how long, whose sequences, how the cut looks
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-407, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** FeelGuide 9 (Crown, 2026-11-09) keeps player and team spells less cinematic than a boss's: "short, no long sequences; signature shots stay brief" (study §9, R7). Still open: (1) the cap; (2) whose sequences are capped; (3) what happens at the cap; (4) how the validator tells a boss's skill from a player's.
**Context:** `SignatureCameraCap` (`Combat/Camera/SignatureCameraCap.h`), `UCombatCameraDirector::PlaySequence` / `HandleSignatureCapReached`, `NotifyValidation::CheckSignatureSeconds` / `GetValidationReportString`.
**Options / as built (A):** (1) **2.0 s** (`MaxSignatureSeconds`, `TODO: tune`; 0.05 s tolerance for frame rounding). (2) **any caster who is not an opposing combatant** (the player and co-op teammates); enemy casters run to the end, since only bosses and named attacks get a sequence (Q3). (3) **cut back as if it had finished**: the sequence stops and the view blends to the turn view (0.6 s); a pending Defence hand-off (B-370) takes the view instead; skip-after-hit and the next turn still cut earlier. The skill's animation and hits are not touched. (4) a skill some `UBossData` phase adds (`AddedSkills`) is a boss's; **every other skill is treated as a player or team skill** and gets a warning line (never a failure). **B** (1) 3 s; (3) a fade or a whip-cut instead of the blend; (4) a per-skill "boss sequence" flag.
**Recommendation:** A. Expedition 33's player signature skill (Maelle, 5:59–6:03, study §1) runs about 4 s with timed presses; Crown asked for less than that, so half. Final Fantasy X's long summon sequences are a commonly cited pacing complaint once seen a few times, while Persona 5's critical-hit cut-ins last about a second and stay well liked; both point to short, repeatable shots for anything a player sees every fight. The blend back is the same one every shot pattern already uses (B-371), so no new look.
**Answer:** **A**, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown may change any; every number is a named constant `TODO: tune`.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-407. A boss's base-loadout skills are not in `UBossData`, so the validator may warn on them; the warning says "fine if only enemies use it". The runtime cut doesn't depend on that list: it reads the caster's side.

### Q-399 — Calm menus: which menus step the camera in, and what the step is
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-399, study R2, R3) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** The row says opening Skills / Items steps the turn camera closer and lower, cancel steps back, and nothing moves while choosing. Our menu also has spell-source sub-menus, a direct Attack → target picker, an Allies / Enemies step, and several actions per turn. Which of these move the camera, and how far?
**Context:** `UCombatCommandMenuSubsystem` (depths Main / Submenu / TargetCategory / TargetSelection); `FCombatCameraRules::ComputeTurnView`; `UCombatCameraDirector`.
**Recommendation (default):**
- **One step, for any sub-menu** (Skills, Items, every spell source and school list): depth 1. Deeper menus (a school's spell list) hold that one step. Reference: Expedition 33's skill list (study §1 "Skill list open": one step closer and lower, then still while browsing), a widely praised turn presentation.
- **A target picker keeps the depth it was opened from:** a skill's picker stays stepped in, Attack's picker stays at the main view; the Allies / Enemies step likewise. So picking a target never moves the camera (R3; one enemy in Expedition 33: no move while picking). Several enemies get B-400's target cut instead.
- **The step:** in toward the acting hero by 0.25 of the camera's distance and down by 0.3 of its height, still aimed at the same point, in every turn-view style; blended over 0.3 s (study estimate), then still. All `TODO: tune`.
- **Only the viewer's own turn view moves:** with camera movement off (static view, R15), during a skill's cinematic or a held shot, the depth is remembered but nothing moves.
- **Closing the menu (an action is picked) moves nothing:** the action's shots take over; the next open sends its depth, so a second action in one turn (B-170c) that reopens on the main menu steps back out (a skill's shot still held stays put; B-401 brings that back to the turn view). The step is the acting character's own: an action started by anyone else (an Execution turn, a ritual firing) resets it to the main view.
**Answer:** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown judges the feel in PIE (`docs/PIE/B-399.md`).

### Q-400 — Target cut: what the camera shows while picking among several targets
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-400, study R3 as changed in §9) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** With two or more targets, the row says the picker cuts to a front medium shot of the highlighted target on each press, back to the turn view on cancel, the skill's shots on confirm; Centred Wide keeps "a highlight and a small re-aim". What exactly do the shots look like, what counts as a press, and what about ally targets and confirm?
**Context:** the target picker is a list of buttons with no highlight concept before this job; `UCombatCommandMenuSubsystem`, `FCombatCameraRules::ComputeTargetView`, `UCombatCameraDirector`.
**Recommendation (default):**
- **Over the Shoulder: a cut to a front medium shot** of the highlighted target, from the side of the fight it faces (an enemy from the party's side, an ally from the enemies' side), a three-quarter view a little above eye level; the body plus room for its HP bar fills about 0.6 of the screen's height (min 250 cm away). Reference: Expedition 33's field fight (study §9 / §12: "jumps to a front medium shot of the first target … cycling cuts to that enemy", one cut per press, no blend), praised for making each target readable.
- **Centred Wide / Tactical: a small re-aim** (0.35 of the way toward the target, from the same place, 0.15 s blend): §9 says Centred Wide needs only a highlight and a small re-aim.
- **One target: no move** (§9 R3).
- **What highlights:** the first valid target when the picker opens; then a target button's hover or keyboard/gamepad focus (existing menu navigation, no new key), or `wor.Camera.TargetCycle`. Confirm is unchanged: the button pressed is the target picked.
- **Confirm:** the camera cuts back to the turn view and the action's camera runs exactly as before B-400 (its wind-up starts from the turn view). **Cancel:** back to the turn view (cut in Over the Shoulder, short blend otherwise), at the menu's depth (Q-399).
- **Only the viewer's own turn view moves:** camera movement off, a cinematic or a held shot: nothing moves (R15).
- Numbers are first guesses, `TODO: tune`. A world marker on the highlighted target (Expedition 33 shows the target's HP bar) is not in this job: `OnTargetHighlighted` is ready for it.
**Answer:** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown judges the feel in PIE (`docs/PIE/B-400.md`).

### Q-406 — Per-boss cinematics: the details the row leaves open
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-09; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-406, study R9/R17/R18, FeelGuide 9) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** B-406 builds the opt-in boss opening and phase cinematics and the named-attack shot. Details the row leaves open: (1) where the phase sequence lives; (2) what holds the fight and for how long; (3) settings and headless fights; (4) two phases at once; (5) what the sequences bind; (6) a skip key; (7) the named attack's reach; (8) what is not built.
**Options / Recommendation:** Expedition 33's boss opening and phase change (study Part 2 §9: hero shots, a HUD-free cinematic, back to a wide shot with the HUD; widely praised staging), FeelGuide 9 (opt-in, on trial):
1. **On each phase** (`FBossPhase::CinematicSequence`), so a memory's skip or added phase keeps its own; the opening on `UBossData::OpeningSequence`. Both soft (D-057), empty = none; a mini-boss's preset phases have none.
2. **Turn 1 waits for the opening** (the orchestrator's async wait); **a phase cinematic holds the next action request** (no menu, no AI, so no defence timing until the HUD is back, R17). An action already playing when the phase is reached finishes under the cinematic. **Cap 20 s** (`BossCinematicMaxSeconds`, `TODO: tune`; the study's phase change is ~15 s).
3. **Skill cinematics setting applies** (D-078: camera movement off skips them unless Cinematics is On). **No local viewer** (headless specs): nothing plays, the fight starts at once.
4. **The newest phase's cinematic replaces a playing one**; the hold continues.
5. Tags **"Boss"** (the boss) and **"Party"** (the local combatants), like the skill sequences' Caster / Targets.
6. **No skip key yet** (follow-up B-406b, with its `InputMap.md` row; E33 lets you skip cutscenes). Until then `wor.Camera.BossCinematic stop`.
7. **An enemy's named attack** (`FSkillCameraTuning::bNamedAttack`) opens on a ~1 s front medium shot of the enemy instead of the over-the-shoulder wind-up (R9, Crown's Q3). Any enemy can carry it; a party skill ignores it. A skill's own `CameraSequence` still wins.
8. **Not built (B-406b):** a hero shot on every boss turn (Q3 reads "bosses and big attacks"; built for flagged attacks only); a fade between cinematic and fight (author it in the sequence); a held Execution (ritual fire) turn (built in B-406b); a skip key (built in B-406b); the real CommonUI HUD binding (the native `UCombatHUD` hides now; a Blueprint `OnCombatStartedUI` override must bind `OnCinematicHudHidden` itself); **co-op**: only the server's local viewer decides whether a cinematic plays, so a listen-server phase cinematic holds clients who never see it (the client view waits on B-228b). Known limit: under a phase cinematic only the next **action request** waits; that actor's turn-start effects (damage over time, Broken Darkness overflow) still resolve and the turn strip updates while the HUD is hidden.
**Answer:** all eight as recommended, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown: judge the trial on one boss in PIE (`docs/PIE/B-406.md`).

### Q-406b — Boss cinematics, the rest: skip key, ritual hold, hero shot, CommonUI
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-406b, from Q-406 (6) and (8)) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** (1) which key skips a boss cinematic, and how; (2) in co-op, does a client's skip end only its view or ask the server; (3) is an Execution (ritual fire) turn held by a phase cinematic; (4) a front hero shot on every boss turn; (5) the CommonUI HUD's binding.
**Options / Recommendation:** skippable cutscenes are standard practice and widely expected (Expedition 33 lets you skip its cutscenes; most console RPGs skip on Start / Options, PC on Space or Esc). `docs/Design/InputMap.md` defaults:
1. **Tab (keyboard) / Menu, Options (gamepad), on press**, action `IA_Combat_SkipCinematic` (unique, D-063), built in code in the B-093 menu-keys context like `IA_Combat_Menu*`. No confirm and no hold: a skip costs nothing in play.
