Not Space (review): it is the most common dodge key and Slate's Accept, and the skip doesn't consume input, so mashing it could also dodge or confirm. **Checked:** no `InputMap.md` row and no code uses Tab or Menu in combat (Enter / Esc / Backspace / 1–7 avoided). **Not checked:** the editor combat context (Guard / Dodge, any pause), which can't be read here; PIE must confirm neither skip key is on it (`docs/PIE/B-406b.md` step 1), else move the skip key (one InputMap row). It calls `UCombatCameraDirector::FinishBossCinematic` (via `ACombatPlayerController::SkipBossCinematic`), which ends it exactly as its end or the cap does: HUD back, then turn 1 or the held request.
2. **Skip locally.** The skip ends this machine's view only; a client's press doesn't ask the server, whose hold ends on its own end or the 20 s cap. On a listen server the host's skip ends the hold for everyone (the host's view is the one that holds). Revisit with the client view (B-228b): a "both ready" skip, as co-op games often do, would ask the server.
3. **Yes, held like an action request**: `FireScheduledExecution` waits while a boss cinematic plays and fires when it ends, so no ritual hit or defence timing lands under the hidden HUD (R17). As with action requests, the turn-start step still runs first.
4. **Not built yet (B-406c).** Q3 reads "bosses and big attacks"; B-406 built it for flagged named attacks only. Whether every boss turn earns a hero shot is for Crown's B-406 trial (`docs/PIE/B-406.md` step 11). If it does, the hook is `UCombatCameraDirector::HandleActorTurnStarted` (a boss's turn start, a ~1 s front shot like `ECameraShotMoment::NamedAttack`), or `FCombatCameraRules::OpeningMomentFor` for every boss skill.
5. **Waits on the CommonUI screens (D-024; B-406c)**, which don't exist yet. `UCombatCameraDirector::OnCinematicHudHidden(bool)` stays the contract: the native `UCombatHUD` binds it today; the CommonUI battle HUD binds the same delegate when it lands.
**Answer:** all five as recommended, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown: check the skip in PIE (`docs/PIE/B-406b.md`); judge (4) with the B-406 trial.
**Answer: Crown, 2026-11-12:** **kept.** The skip is on **Tab / Menu** (press, no hold), local; a ritual fire waits for a phase cinematic; (4) and (5) stay with B-406c. Confirmed as built.

### Q-404 — Wide beats and the party handover: when, how long, and when the HUD steps
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-404, study R12, R16, §9) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** R12 puts a short wide beat between turns and R16 blends from it to the next hero with the turn strip and the active card moving on the same frame. When exactly does a beat play, how long are its parts, and which moment does the HUD follow?
**Context:** our turn start comes on the same frame as the end of the last action (`ACombatOrchestrator::OnActionCompleted` → `AdvanceToNextTurn`), so the beat has to sit at the start of the new turn. The camera director (`Combat/Camera/CombatCameraDirector.cpp`) already blends shots on a short timer (B-371); B-401 owns several actions inside one turn (B-170c).
**Recommendation (default):**
- **When:** only between turns: someone acted since the last turn start, the turn passes to **another** actor, and the new view is a hero's Turn view. No beat on the first turn, between actions or turns of the same actor (B-401), with camera movement off (the static view, R15), or before an enemy's turn (its watch view is already the wide). Reference: Expedition 33 goes wide after an action or an enemy turn and then to the next hero (study §9, Boss 3:25.9–3:26.9, Field 2:32–2:34); it never resets the camera between a single hero's moves (R4).
- **How long:** 0.3 s into the wide (the NotYourTurn wide, from the next hero's side), 0.2 s hold, then a **0.5 s blend** behind the next hero (`TODO: tune`). Reference: the same clips, "within about 0.5 s it blends (not cuts)". The whole change takes about 1 s, inside the pace FeelGuide rule 2 asks for.
- **HUD moment:** the director's new `OnTurnHandover(From, To)` fires once per turn start **on the frame the blend to the next hero starts** (after the beat); the HUD binds it for the turn-strip step and the card lift + ring. Reference: Expedition 33 lifts the card and steps the turn column at the moment the camera starts its move (study §9, F4 [confirms]).
- **Interruptions:** an action (or another turn) that starts during the beat runs the handover at once (the event still fires exactly once); the action's own camera then plays. The one exception to "once per turn start": a fight that ends during the beat (won, lost or fled) drops its handover on purpose, as the HUD is going away. A `wor.Camera.Handover` preview never fires it. The Defence hand-off (B-370) and cinematics are never delayed by a beat.
- The menu still opens at turn start (`OnActionRequested`), during the beat; holding it until the handover is a HUD change for B-105.
**Answer:** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown judges the feel in PIE (`docs/PIE/B-404.md`).

### Q-401 — No reset between actions: how the camera comes back for a hero's next action
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-09) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 (B-401, study R4) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** with several actions per turn (B-170c), what does the camera do between two actions of one hero's turn: when does it come back, to what, how fast, and where does it not?
**Context:** B-170c's orchestrator asks the same actor again (`OnActionRequested`) after each action while actions are left; `OnActorTurnStarted` fires once per turn (Q-170c2). Shot patterns (B-371) hold their last shot until something else takes the view; B-404 puts a wide beat only between turns of different actors.
**Recommendation (default):**
- **When:** at the re-ask after an action, i.e. when the hero's menu opens again; not at the turn start's own ask, not when a refused pick is asked again. The signal is the existing `OnActionRequested`, no new event.
- **To what:** the **same** turn view of the same hero (the transform the turn started on, in the player's turn-view style). No wide beat and no handover between actions; only a turn change does the full move (B-404). Reference: Expedition 33 settles straight back behind the same hero between that hero's moves and only goes wide at the handover (study R4, §9 "Party handover").
- **How fast:** one **0.35 s** blend from where the last shot ended (`TODO: tune`), shorter than the handover (0.5 s) since the camera is already close; reference: the same clips, the menu is back almost at once, and FeelGuide's pace rule.
- **Where not:** camera movement off (the static view stays, R15); a signature sequence or Defence hand-off still playing (its end shows the turn view, as before); enemy turns (the AI acts once a turn until B-170f; the menu event is player-only).
**Answer:** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-09). Crown judges the feel in PIE (`docs/PIE/B-401.md`).

### Q-397b — Hidden quests masked by default (screen-share spoilers)
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12; the mask-again-on-close and notice parts by the reference-backed default, Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (Crown, through the desktop operator) · **Blocks:** —
**Crown (2026-11-12):** hidden quests are masked by default so screen-sharing does not spoil them. In the journal each found hidden quest shows as "Hidden Quest 1", "Hidden Quest 2", … numbered in found order; its name and step stay masked until the player presses **Reveal** on that entry.
**Recommendation (reference-backed default):** Discord's spoiler tag: the text is covered until clicked, and the reveal lasts only while it is open. So: (1) a revealed quest is **masked again when the journal closes**; (2) a hidden quest's **notices are masked** too: "Hidden quest found. Open the journal to view it." on discovery, and, built the same way, "Hidden quest updated. …" on a step and "Hidden quest complete. …" on its finish (B-397 had named the quest and its next goal in those). A mask number counts every found hidden quest, finished ones too (a finished one reads "Hidden Quest <n>: done"), so a number never changes.
**Answer:** as above, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-12). Crown may change the close behaviour or the notices. **Flag answered (Crown, 2026-11-12):** the end memory a hidden quest earns is masked too: "New memory found. Open the journal to view it." (a main or side quest's end memory still reads "New memory: <title>"). `wor.Quest.Print` (debug) still names everything.
**Notes for Claude:** placeholder UI: one "Reveal Hidden Quest <n>" button per masked quest under the Quests page text (`UMemoryJournalWidget::RevealQuest` / `Close`); no key, so no InputMap row. The real CommonUI journal puts Reveal on the entry itself (D-024). Text is placeholder `LOCTEXT` (`HiddenQuestNotice`, `MemoryJournal` namespace). PIE `docs/PIE/Q-397b.md`.

### Q-096 — Payout passives: who carries them, how they stack, when they're read
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (B-096, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** B-073 left one run-end payout multiplier for "passives"; which items carry the passive, how do several add up, and when is it read?
**Context:** `UTrialRunSubsystem::PayoutMultiplier` (B-073, `TrialRunSubsystem.h`) was never raised; evolutions are the asset-backed passive carriers (`UEvolutionItemData`), attached to the character slot, weapon, adapter or apparel (`ULoadoutComponent::GatherAttachedEvolutions`).
**Recommendation (default):**
- **Carrier:** a number on the evolution, `RunPayoutBonusPercent` (e.g. 10 = "+10% run-end payout"). It counts **wherever it is worn** (any place, any charge level), because it is not a combat effect, so D-147's "gear passives only at L2" doesn't apply. A broken evolution gives nothing (as its other passives).
- **Stacking:** the party's bonuses **add up** (+10% and +15% = x1.25), **capped at x2.0** (`RunRewards::MAX_PAYOUT_MULTIPLIER`, `TODO: tune`). Reference: Path of Exile's "increased Item Quantity" and Diablo II's Magic Find add up across gear (additive, well understood by players); Diablo II's diminishing returns on MF and Hades' capped Fated Authority / Fated Persuasion show a cap keeps a farming build from breaking the economy.
- **When read:** at the end of each fight, won or lost (after breaks), from the fighters' gear, so the run's end pays by what the party wore in its last fight. Simple and visible; swapping gear at a rest spot after the last fight doesn't count. ~~A resumed run starts at x1.0 until its next fight (the multiplier isn't saved).~~ Moot: there are no saved runs (D-172, Crown 2026-11-12).
- **Difficulty / leave early / boss bonus** apply on top, as before (B-073, B-187).
**Answer:** the default, by the reference-backed default (2026-11-12). Crown may change the cap, the stacking or where it counts.
**Notes for Claude:** applied in `RunRewards::SumPayoutBonusPercent` / `PayoutMultiplierForBonus`, `UTrialRunSubsystem::RefreshPayoutMultiplier`, called from `ACombatOrchestrator::FinishCombat`. No evolution asset sets it yet (an editor step).

### Q-098 — Weapons stop giving spells: what happens to the spells weapons already hold
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (B-098, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-027 says weapons never give spells. Weapon crystals already offer spells today (Resonate Weapon), and weapons in assets and saves hold assigned / picked spells. Are those spells deleted, moved, or kept unread? Does the player get anything back?
**Context:** reads: `FWeaponLoadoutEntry::GetAllSpells` / `GetSpellSlots` / `ReplaceSpell`, `FCombatCapabilities::BuildFrom` (Resonate Weapon), `ULoadoutComponent::GetWeaponResonateSpells`. Data: `FWeaponInventoryEntry::AssignedSpells` / `SpellPicks` / `bSpellSlotsActive`, `FWeaponLoadoutEntry::AssignedSpells`, saved in `FSavedGear` / `FSavedLoadout`. The crystal already keys the weapon's abilities' element (B-080).
**Recommendation (default):**
- ~~**Kept unread, not deleted:**~~ (superseded by Crown's answer below: deleted from saves, save v37) the rule switches every read off (`WeaponSpellRules`, `wor.Loadout.WeaponSpells` 0); the fields stay in data and saves, so old saves load and nothing is lost before Crown's PIE. The fields, the switch and the old-path specs go in one sweep (Sweep row 48). No save version change.
- **Nothing to refund:** spells are learned per character (B-316b) and stay learned; the player can put them on an adapter or evolution, which still give spells. The weapon crystal's job becomes the abilities' element (infusion source, B-080).
- **The menu:** Resonate Weapon shows only for augment-stone abilities.
- Reference: Final Fantasy IX and Final Fantasy Tactics moved spells off weapons onto the character's own learned list without taking anything away (equipment teaches, the character keeps); Monster Hunter's elemental weapons add element to attacks rather than spells, the role D-027 gives a weapon crystal.
**Answer:** **Crown: deleted** (2026-11-12), replacing the default's "kept unread". A weapon's spells are dropped from saves: not saved, and a loaded weapon holds none (save v37; older saves are wiped, D-060; no migration, no refund). The runtime fields and the `wor.Loadout.WeaponSpells` comparison switch stay until Crown's B-098 PIE, then go with the old code (Backlog B-411). **The weapon's spell slots become ability slots** (Crown, 2026-11-12): a weapon whose gem / evolution had N spell slots has N more ability slots, counted while it is attached, broken or not; the weapon's own tier and stone slots are unchanged. Those gem slots take only abilities with **no element (None)**, **Generic** (inherits the crystal's element) or **the crystal's own element**: abilities have no element field yet (each takes the crystal's element, B-080, so all fit today), so the check comes with that field in B-410.
**Notes for Claude:** survey command `wor.Loadout.ListWeaponSpells` (owned weapons still holding spells). Loadout validation (`LoadoutRules.cpp:193-223`, the clean-up in `LoadoutComponent.cpp:1477`) still checks weapon spells and can clear an unlearned or mismatched one: the only way the kept data still changes; harmless (it was never offered) and swept with the fields. `TryAssignWeaponSpell` refuses while the switch is off. `CreateAndConfigureLoadout` still writes spells onto a weapon-primary loadout (no C++ caller; unread now): drop that branch in the sweep.

### Q-424 — Air-burst dash: when the camera follows, and when it cuts
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-13; Crown vetoes) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (B-424) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the light hit dashes in with a warp over its airborne frames only (`analysis/ShowcaseAnimAnalysis.md`, "Travel without stretching"). How does the B-403 melee rush fit the dash, and when is the burst too fast to follow?
**Context:** `ApproachWarp::Measure` gives the burst (warp distance minus the clip's own travel, over the window); front-row bursts are ~2,100 cm/s, back-row ~3,700 (the analysis). `FCombatCameraRules::FollowApproachBurst`, `UCombatCameraDirector::StartShotPattern`.
**Recommendation (default, built):**
- **The rush rides the burst:** the camera holds on the rush start until the burst, then rushes over the burst window to the wind-up framed at the striking spot. Reference: Expedition 33's melee rush moves with the hero's ~0.2 s close-in (study §14, well received); Persona 5 / Metaphor / Star Rail follow a short stylised dash with a fast camera.
- **Too fast: hold, then cut** as the burst ends, at **3,500 cm/s** (`ApproachWarp::CAMERA_CUT_BURST_SPEED`, `TODO: tune`): those games cut rather than follow a long leap; the travel is never chased on screen.
- **A ritual lead-in keeps the old rush** (its own timing comes first); the camera reads the montage at play rate 1.0 for now (`TODO`: the action's Action Speed).
**Answer:** as the recommendation (reference-backed default; CLAUDE.md "Approve by default, Crown vetoes"). Crown changes it after the B-424 PIE pass if it reads wrong.
**Crown's PIE (2026-11-13):** smooth, but too fast for a starting character; 0.24 s is the dash at max speed. The burst time is now `wor.Combat.ApproachBurstSeconds` (default 0.45 s, the hop slowed inside the window to fit; 0 = the clip's own); the rush and the cut read it. Crown picks the base from 0.35 / 0.45 / 0.55; speed-stat scaling is an IDEA row.
**Notes for Claude:** `Combat/Actions/ApproachWarp.h/.cpp`, `wor.Debug.ApproachWarp <MontagePath> [cm] [rate]`, the `[Warp]` and `[CombatCamera] Rush timed` log lines.

### Q-239 — Item slot upgrades: whose pouch, which vendor, what price
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 (B-239, cloud) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-097's slot upgrades were written for one party inventory; since D-117 each character has its own pouch. Whose slots does a purchase raise, which hub vendor sells it, and what are the numbers?
**Context:** `FPoolPouch::SlotCount` (`PoolSubsystem.h`, saved in `FSavedPouch`), `InventoryConstants::ITEM_SLOTS_START` 10. No vendor screen exists yet; the hub shop window is a Blueprint (`UMerchantShopSubsystem::OpenForMerchant`).
**Recommendation (default):**
- **Whose:** one chosen character's pouch, +1 slot per purchase (follows D-117: each character carries its own).
- **Price:** hub Gold (`ECurrencyType::Prisms`, D-159 naming), **200 + 100 per slot already bought** for that pouch (200, 300, 400, …), **cap 20 slots** (all `TODO: tune`). Reference: Breath of the Wild's Hestu, one NPC who sells each extra slot for a rising price (well liked; the rising cost keeps it a long-term goal); Monster Hunter's and Diablo III's stash expansions likewise cost more each step.
- **Where:** the **Spiritualist** (crystals and stones, which are what a pouch carries). Until the real CommonUI vendor screen exists the placeholder opens with `wor.UI.ItemSlots`; hub only (refused during a run, like the other hub services).
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-12):** the **Jeweler** sells pouch slots, not the Spiritualist (the placeholder and the Jeweler's merchant name say so). Currency and price curve as the default: hub Gold (`ECurrencyType::Prisms`), 200 +100 per slot, cap 20; host-only in co-op for now.
**Notes for Claude:** `Inventory/ItemSlotUpgrade.h/.cpp`, `UPoolSubsystem::AddItemSlot`, `UItemSlotUpgradeWidget`. Co-op: **host-only (Crown)**: `ItemSlotUpgrade::TryBuy` refuses a wallet without authority ("Only the host can buy item slots.") and the placeholder greys out Buy for a guest. Not spec-covered (headless specs are standalone, so always authority): PIE check 10.

### Q-428 — A player who leaves a run: what happens to the Prisms they dropped
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-13) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (audit, B-428) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** each player drops their own run Prisms on a wipe (ER-12b). If a player leaves the run without extracting, including a host whose leaving hands hosting to a friend, what happens to the Prisms they dropped on the map?
**Options:** **A** split them equally among the players still in the run · B they stay on the map for no one · C they go with the leaver.
**Answer:** **A** (Crown 2026-11-13). The leaver's dropped Prisms are split equally into the remaining players' wallets (the remainder to the host) and the drop is removed. Reference: Deep Rock Galactic's team minerals are shared by the whole team, so nothing gathered is lost when someone drops out.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-428 (b), next to the hand-over re-key (a). Rules PA-37i.

### Q-437 — Ability Stones in an old save when the stone is removed
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-13) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 (D-175, B-437) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** B-437 removes the Ability Stone type. What happens to Ability Stones (owned, socketed, in a loadout) in a save made before it?
**Options:** **A** dropped: the save version is bumped and an older save is wiped (D-060), no migration · B refunded: each stone turns into its dismantle essence (Skill essence at its tier, ER-31) on load.
**Answer:** **A**, by the reference-backed default. Before the first public build every save version bump already wipes older saves (D-060), and Crown chose the same for weapon spells (Q-098: deleted, no refund). Live games refund removed items (Warframe and Destiny 2 hand back currency for sunset or removed gear) only because players own those saves; B is the path once there are public saves. Crown may change it.
**Notes for Claude:** B-437 bumps `WoRSaveVersion::LATEST`; no loader code for the old type.

### Q-441 — Can the home chest be used during a run?
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-441) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the code refused putting **gear** in the chest or taking it out during a run (Q-347), but let **pouch stacks** go to storage and back. The approved inventory mockup greys Store at a rest spot with "Home chest: in the hub only". Which is it?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change it): **the chest can't be used during a run, for stacks too.** Store and Take are greyed with "The chest stays at home until the run ends"; Give between members still works. It is what the approved mockup draws and what Q-347 already says for gear ("a run has only what it brought"); extraction games keep the stash at home (Escape from Tarkov, Dark and Darker), and Diablo's stash is in town only. What a run finds still goes to the pouch, else to storage (Q-322): that is unchanged.
**Notes for Claude:** `WoRInventoryVerbs::GetRefusal`. The old `wor.UI.Storage` placeholder (`UStorageWidget`) still moves stacks in a run; it goes with the D-124 sweep.

### Q-445 — Selling: what a vendor pays, and which vendor buys what
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-445, D-177, D-193) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** vendors now buy items (D-177). (1) What does a sale pay? (2) Does each vendor buy only its own kind of thing? (3) What happens to "Convert to Gold"? (4) Are evolutions sold?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **(1) Half the tier's buy price, in the currency of the place** (Gold in the hub, Prisms in a run): F 12, E 25, D 50, C 100, B 200, A 400, S 800. It is the price the duplicate's "Convert to Gold" already paid (Q-319), so one rule covers both. Selling back at about half is the genre's habit (Diablo, Skyrim, Final Fantasy shops), and Hades and Slay the Spire sell back at a fixed share too.
- **(2) Any of the three vendors buys anything that can be sold.** Elden Ring, Persona 5 and Expedition 33 let any shop buy any item; a "wrong shop" refusal only adds walking. Dismantling is the service that is split by vendor (D-193).
- **(3) Convert has no tab:** it is read as replaced by Sell (the same price, the same result). The duplicate screen's Convert button stays until the vendor screen (B-456).
- **(4) Evolutions are not sold yet.** They have no tier buy price to halve; the Spiritualist dismantles them. A sale price for them is a later question.
- **What is refused:** a loadout's primary (swap first), a piece from home while in a run (only what the run gave is sold there), and an evolution in use is not dismantled.
**Notes for Claude:** `UEconomyService::GetSellPriceForTier`, `SellGear`, `SellCrystal`; `WoRVendorServices` (`Shop/VendorServices.h`). Rules ER-32b.

### Q-459b — Play together: friend status, seats and the session row
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-459b, D-200, D-181) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup shows each friend's presence (online, in another game, offline) and portraits by the seats; the friends list the code reads gives names only, and LAN has no list.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **A friend row shows the name and "invite", or "invited · waiting" once invited while the screen is open.** Presence comes when the online layer reads it. Deep Rock Galactic and It Takes Two list friends with an invite action and mark one invited until they answer.
- **With no friends list (LAN) the screen says how a friend on your network finds the session**, and the lit row starts on the seats.
- **The seats only show who plays each** ("You (host)", a friend by name, "AI seat", "AI seat · held" for a friend who left); who plays a seat is changed on the party screen and by a friend joining (the join request card, B-459c).
- **The last right-hand row is Host a session / Stop hosting**; hosting reopens the level for friends, as the placeholder did. Rows answer a second click, not the first.

### Q-459c — The join request card: where, which key, what it shows
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-459c, D-150, D-200) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup puts the card in the host's corner with "Hold View to answer"; View is the proposed Map key and End Turn in a fight, and keyboard had no key.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **A small card in the top-right corner while a request waits; it never stops play** (mouse and keys stay with the game). Elden Ring's summon notice and Deep Rock Galactic's join prompt sit in a corner and are answered when the player chooses.
- **Hold View (Tab on keyboard) for half a second to answer.** A hold leaves a tap of View free for the Map; the key is only mapped while a request waits and isn't eaten. Not in a fight: seats are fixed there, and the card says to answer after it. Destiny 2 and Halo put "hold to" on shared buttons the same way.
- **Answering: Up / Down light a seat, A gives it, X refuses, B lets go.** The first request waits first; the card says how many more wait.
- **Each seat shows the AI character's name and "AI seat · give"** (or "AI seat · held · give" for one held for a friend who left). The element icon by the seat comes with the HUD art.

---

### Q-459a — Host-lost card: the next host's name, and what B does
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-459a, D-200, D-181) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup names the next host ("Kestrel is taking over as host.") and offers B "Leave to the Void Realm"; once the host drops, the old world and its player names are gone, and leaving a dropped run for the hub isn't built.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **The card reads "You are taking over as host." or "The next player is taking over as host."** The join order keeps only ids; names come when it carries them. Monster Hunter: World and Deep Rock Galactic's host migration both show a plain "migrating host" message, not a name.
- **B is "Stop waiting", only while this machine waits on someone else** (never on the new host, whom the others wait on). It stops the search and leaves you where you are; your run waits at its last checkpoint and `wor.Session.Find` still works. A clean "leave to the hub" from a dropped run is its own job.
- **Four pips:** the host left, the old session left, the new host up, rejoined (the card closes). Planned hand-overs read "Handing over host".
- **The rest menu's "Hand over host" asks first** ("Hand over host?", the next host by name from the live player list); it refuses a guest, a host alone, or a fight, with the reason.

---

### Q-458 — Party screen: the rows the mockup shows that the code no longer has
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-458, D-200, D-181) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup shows "When your fighters fall", Y "Swap order" and a friend's name under "Played by"; B-346 retired the fall setting, the party has no reorder, and seats have no friend names until the co-op screens.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **"When your fighters fall" is left out.** B-346 (Q-21-audit) made an AI seat play on with nothing handed over, so the row would do nothing. Showing only settings that change something is the usual rule in well-received menus (Persona 5's and Fire Emblem's party menus hide what doesn't apply).
- **Y "Swap order" waits for a party reorder.** The slot order is read across the code (the leader is slot 0); a reorder is its own job. Until then the screen hints only A and X.
- **"Played by" reads "You (leader)", "You" or "AI"** (no level, D-200); a friend's name comes with B-459's seats.
- **A on a member toggles you / AI; the leader is refused with a reason; X steps the member to the next start cell** (a held cell swaps, as the placeholder did). Divinity: Original Sin 2's formation and Baldur's Gate 3's party line-up both move one member at a time with the others giving way.
- **A on "Invite" opens the existing invite screen** after closing the menus, until B-459's Invite screen replaces it.

---

### Q-444 — Spirit of {character}: when a character counts as met, where the screen opens
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-444, D-184, D-181) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup shows unmet characters as "???", but the code has no "met" record; and the screen has no menu row yet.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **A character counts as met once their meter has moved or you own or wear one of their pieces.** Until then the row is "???" with no pieces. Hades' codex and Monster Hunter's monster list stay blank until the first encounter, then fill in; this uses what the code already tracks instead of a new record.
- **Their pieces show by name once you own or wear them; the rest are "?"; the unlock at 100% names them all** (the reveal, CharacterForms.md §1). Mortal Shell's shells reveal their lore the same way, piece by piece.
- **A on your own form takes a skin off; A on an unlocked form wears it; Y switches the base body.** Refusals (locked, in a fight, not at a rest spot) show in amber with the code's reason.
- **The screen opens with `wor.UI.Forms` for now;** its "Spirit of {character}" row comes with the rest menu (D-184). The old placeholder is `wor.UI.FormsOld` until your PIE pass.
- **Left for later:** the form preview picture, the pieces as icon tiles (a list of names now).

### Q-453f — Element letters on colours: which letters, where
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14; **confirmed by Crown, 2026-11-14**) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-453f, D-190) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** what tag each element gets, and which colour-only signals get it first.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **Two letters per element** (Fi, Wa, Ea, Wi, Li, Da, Lt for Lightning, Vo, Ge; "?" for the unknown element), so Light and Lightning differ and Water and Wind too. Colour-blind modes in Hearthstone and Slay the Spire add short text or symbols beside colour, widely praised; two letters fit beside a damage number.
- **First where colour alone tells the element in a fight:** damage numbers ("120 Fi") and the status buildup line. Physical hits share one colour, so they get no tag. The menus mostly name the element already; their remaining colour swatches are a follow-up.


**Crown, 2026-11-14:** confirmed for now, but Crown wants **element icons that appear with the damage**. That is B-294b (the element gem beside every damage number). The two-letter tags are the stand-in until B-294b's icons land; whether the letters stay beside the icon is decided then.

### Q-453e — "AI finishes my turn when I idle": how long, how much, and co-op
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14; **confirmed by Crown, 2026-11-14**) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-453e, D-190) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** how long is "idle", does the AI take one action or the turn, and what happens to the next turn and to co-op guests?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **30 seconds with no choice in the battle menu** (any selection, Back or target change restarts it), **off by default**. Final Fantasy XIV and Dota 2 hand an idle player's turn or hero to the AI after a grace period, and turn timers in online tactics (Hearthstone's 75 s, Into the Breach has none) sit in that range; 30 s is generous for a single-player turn.
- **The AI plays the rest of that turn** (its remaining action pips), **then the next turn is the player's again**, unlike "Let AI Play", which hands the member over for good.
- **Co-op guests later:** the host can't see a guest's menu input yet, so the clock runs only in a game with no guests (TODO in `ACombatOrchestrator::StartIdleTurnClock`).

### Q-453d — Text size: what grows, and by how much
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14; **confirmed by Crown, 2026-11-14**) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-453d, D-190) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-190 approves "text size" but not its steps or whether only the text grows.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change it): **three steps, Normal / Large / Larger (about 15% each), and the whole UI grows with the text** (panels, icons and text together, so nothing overflows its box). Marvel's Spider-Man and The Last of Us Part II offer text size steps; Elden Ring and Baldur's Gate 3 scale the whole HUD and menus, which keeps layouts intact. Growing only the text would need every hand-built screen's boxes to stretch; the HUD-wide scale doesn't.

### Q-453c — Hold to confirm off: what confirms instead
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14; **confirmed by Crown, 2026-11-14**) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-453c, D-190, D-176) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** with "hold to confirm dangerous actions" off, what confirms a quit or a start over?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change it): **the confirm step stays and a press on it confirms** (two presses in all), so D-176's "strong confirm" is kept without a hold. The Last of Us Part II and Forza Horizon offer "hold to press" toggles as accessibility for players who can't hold a button; both keep the confirm screen.

### Q-453a — The screen shake setting: how many steps
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14; **confirmed by Crown, 2026-11-14**) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-453a, D-190) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-190 approves "screen shake" but not its values.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change it): **Full (default), Reduced (half), Off.** The Last of Us Part II's camera shake slider and Celeste's / Hades' screen shake options are among the most praised accessibility settings; three steps fit a left / right row. Only the camera shake is scaled: a shot's field-of-view punch and motion blur are camera movement, which B-453b's switch covers.

### Q-440d — The Skills page: how it is reached, which lists it shows, what X does
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-440d, D-181, D-145) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup draws a Refractor's innate spells by school but not how the page and its lists are reached, what the other classes see, what Change lists, or what X does to a slot.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **LB / RB (Q / E) turns between Gear and Skills; Left / Right picks the list** (a school, then each weapon and adapter). The shared menu controls already give LB / RB to page tabs (UIScreens); Expedition 33's character menu turns its Skills / Weapons pages the same way, widely liked for speed.
- **A Refractor's school tabs are the schools it holds a spell in, counting learned spells**, so a school can be started from an empty tab. Six slots a school, the points bar on the 24-point budget less the pillar discount (`SpellPoolConstants.h`).
- **Every class sees each weapon's skill slots and each adapter's spell slots** on the same page (UIScreens #2); a Refractor's school tabs come first.
- **A lists what the member has learned** (a weapon also lists its own built-in skills), not what is already in the list. An innate spell over the points stays greyed with the reason and the points after in amber, as the gear picker does. Expedition 33's skill screen (pick from learned skills into a fixed number of slots, the point cost shown) is the model.
- **X takes a skill out in one press:** nothing is lost (an innate spell stays learned; a replaced built-in comes back), so no second press as for gems (Q-440c). X on a weapon's own built-in is refused: Change puts another in its place.
- **Left for later:** the lists as tab plates (a text line now), R3 Details, a mouse click on a list or page tab, a Broken Darkness Refractor's element pools (its rule check still applies).

### Q-440e — Saved loadouts on the loadout screen: keys, names, lore ones
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14; **confirmed by Crown, 2026-11-14**) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-440e, D-111) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** UIScreens #2 says the five saved loadouts open from the loadout screen, but not what each key does there, what a new one is called, or what happens to a lore (the character's own) loadout.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **On the v2 screen the list opens from Y Options, "Saved loadouts"** (UIScreens #2, B-479; the first build put it on Y of the old screen, which v2 uses for Options). **In it A uses the lit loadout and goes back to its entries, X duplicates it, Y renames it, B goes back to Options.** The last line, "New loadout", makes an empty one while there is room. Destiny 2's loadouts screen and Diablo IV's wardrobe both keep use / save / rename on face buttons, widely used and liked for switching builds quickly.
- **A new loadout is "Loadout N"** (the first free number); a copy is "<name> copy". Names are trimmed and at most 24 letters (`WoRLoadoutSaved::MAX_LOADOUT_NAME_LENGTH`), like Destiny 2's short loadout names.
- **A lore loadout can be used and duplicated but not renamed** (it is read only, D-111); the copy is the player's own (B-311). Lore ones don't count toward the five.
- **No delete here** (not in the job; `ULoadoutComponent::DeleteLoadout` exists): a full list says to rename or change one.
- **Two loadouts may share a name** (Destiny 2 allows it too); only a new one's "Loadout N" skips names in use.

### Q-440f — Changing apparel on the loadout screen: what the list shows, how a piece comes off
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14; **confirmed by Crown, 2026-11-14**) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-440f, D-164) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the job says an apparel row picks an owned piece or takes one off, but not which pieces are listed, whether taking one off asks first, or what a lore loadout does.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **On the v2 screen apparel is its own tab** (LB / RB; UIScreens #2, B-479) that works like Weapons: A on a worn piece or on the Add apparel frame opens the list of pieces (the first build did this with A / X on the old screen's rows).
- **The list shows the character's own pieces** (the store's owner is that character), less those already worn in this loadout; a piece with a stone in its socket is listed but refused. Gear is a view over the store by owner (CL-09), as the weapon list already works.
- **A Remove on the EQUIPPED line takes a piece off in one press**: nothing is lost (it stays owned), unlike a socket's gem (Q-440c asks twice because that destroys it). Elden Ring and Diablo IV unequip armour in one press.
- **A lore loadout can't change apparel yet**: its apparel isn't saved until B-380e, so a change would be lost on reload (the same guard as `wor.Loadout.WearApparel`). The screen says to duplicate it.

### Q-440c — The socket picker: how a socket is reached, what it lists, what taking a gem off costs
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-440c, D-181, D-141, CL-28b) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup draws the picker for an empty crystal socket but not how the D-pad reaches a socket, what fills a filled one, or how taking a socketed gem off is shown when the code destroys it (CL-28b, the debit model).
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **Left / Right moves across the lit row: the row, its crystal socket, its stone socket.** Diablo IV's and Path of Exile's item screens reach sockets the same way, with no new button. A on the row still changes the piece (B-440b); A on an empty socket opens the socket picker.
- **The crystal socket lists the gems the member carries (highest tier first) and its owned evolutions; the stone socket lists carried stones.** Each line shows what it adds to the budget; over the budget, a second S-tier ("S-tier: one per loadout") and an evolution in use stay greyed with the reason (the mockup; UI_Theme §5).
- **A filled socket is emptied first (X); there is no swap in one step.** Taking off follows the existing rule: a gem and the stone come off together and are used up (CL-28b), an evolution goes back with wear. **The first X says exactly that; the second X does it.** A press-again confirm is how Diablo IV's salvage and Elden Ring's discard ask before something is lost, and it needs no new key. Whether taking a gem off should give it back is Crown's to change (a rule change, not this screen's).
- **Apparel's socket** comes with the apparel picker, **B-440f**.

### Q-440b — Changing gear on the loadout screen: what the picker offers, what X does to the first slot
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-440b, D-181, D-116) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup shows A Change and X Unequip but not what Change lists, what X does to the one slot every loadout must keep (D-116), or what happens in a fight.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **The picker lists the owned weapons or adapters the place takes, not ones already in this loadout; each line shows the budget after it in amber.** A line over the budget, or a piece another member wears, stays greyed with its reason (UI_Theme §5; Expedition 33's Pictos screen shows every piece with its cost and greys what doesn't fit). A swap that leaves an over-budget loadout no worse is allowed, so an old loadout can be fixed one piece at a time.
- **X on the first slot:** a Generic's second weapon moves up into it; with nothing to move up it is refused ("Every loadout keeps a weapon or adapter here. Change it instead."), as D-116 refuses giving a primary away. Diablo IV and Elden Ring let you swap a main weapon but never leave the character with no weapon slot filled.
- **Which weapon is in hand doesn't change when the other one is swapped;** "Put … in hand" is the first line of the other weapon's picker (no new key).
- **No gear changes during a fight** (the loadout is in use; Expedition 33 and Final Fantasy X change gear only outside the action menu's turn).
- **Apparel** is changed in its own piece, **B-440f**.

### Q-461 — The inventory's filter row and Sort: which filters, which sorts, how the keys reach them
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-461, D-181) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the approved mockup draws one filter row on the Gear tab (All, Weapons, Adapters, Crystals, Evolutions, Apparel) and "Y Sort". Four things it doesn't say. (1) The built screen keeps crystals and stones on the Pouch tab (B-441), so a Crystals filter on Gear would always be empty: which filters does each tab get? (2) What does Sort sort by? (3) LT / RT and LB / RB are taken: how do the keys reach the filter row? (4) Evolutions and apparel can't be given or stored yet (Q-323): what do their tiles do?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **(1) Each tab's row holds All and only the kinds that tab can hold.** Gear: All, Weapons, Adapters, Evolutions, Apparel. Chest: All, Weapons, Adapters, Crystals (crystals and stones). Pouch: no row (it holds stacks only). A filter that can never show anything is a dead button; Baldur's Gate 3 and Diablo IV only offer filters for what the bag can hold. A filter with nothing in it right now says "Nothing of this kind here."
- **(2) Y steps through three sorts: Kind (as the tab lists them), Tier (highest first), Name (A to Z).** The line under the tiles says which ("Sort: Tier"). Elden Ring's inventory cycles its sort on one button the same way; Baldur's Gate 3 sorts by type, value and latest. The sort is kept across tabs and characters and starts again at Kind each time the screen opens.
- **(3) Up from the top row of tiles moves onto the filter row; Left / Right changes the filter at once; Down, A or B goes back to the tiles.** The mouse clicks a filter. It is how a D-pad reaches a row of tabs above a grid in Diablo IV and Persona 5's menus, and it needs no new button. A new tab starts on All.
- **(4) An evolution or apparel tile shows its details and lists Give and Store greyed, with "Evolutions can't be given or stored yet"** (or "Apparel ..."): the screen's own rule is that a verb that can't run is greyed with its reason, never hidden (`UIScreens.md` #4). The **E** mark on them means an evolution in use, or apparel a loadout wears.
- **The place name top right** uses the game menu's own words for where you are ("Void Realm · The hub"; in a run, the trial and its variation and difficulty).
**Notes for Claude:** `WoRInventoryScreen::GetFilters`, `GetNextSort`, `BuildPage(World, Member, Tab, Filter, Sort)`. When Q-323 is built, the Chest row gains Evolutions and Apparel.

### Q-463 — The journal screen: what it does where the mockup needs things the game doesn't have yet
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-463, D-181, D-196, D-198, D-199) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the journal's look and its secret quest rules are decided (`UIScreens.md` #6, D-198, D-199). Six things the mockups show need data or screens that don't exist yet. (1) A "Track" on a quest: nothing stores a tracked quest or shows it on a HUD. (2) A ready conversation says "talk to Garrick in the Void Realm", but NPC talk isn't built (B-471), and today conversations are played in the placeholder journal. (3) "Talk to V" is not a journal page any more, and V's hub talk isn't built either. (4) Memories show a NEW mark, a replay window and where they were found; lore shows its kind and place: none of that is recorded. (5) A finished quest's reward: rewards have no player-facing words. (6) Which characters get a Memories sub-tab.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **(1) Track is left out of this job** (no hint is shown for it); **B-463b** builds it: one tracked quest, saved, its step on the game menu and the trial HUD. The Witcher 3 and Expedition 33 track one quest at a time (`UIScreens.md` #6).
- **(2) A ready conversation is played in the journal for now:** A plays it one line at a time; with the last line it moves to **Played**, where the whole talk can be re-read. So nothing that works today stops working. When NPC talk exists (B-471) the talk is played by talking to the character, as Expedition 33's camp talks are, and the journal's A becomes "Show where".
- **(3) The hub's journal station still opens the old placeholder journal**, because it is the only place V's lines can be heard until B-471. The game menu's Journal opens the new screen.
- **(4) Memories and lore show what the game records:** the title, whose memory it is, and the text; newest first; "3 found". No NEW mark, no replay, no place or kind until those are recorded (B-463c).
- **(5) An unfinished quest says "Reward: shown when the quest is done"; a finished one shows no reward line** until rewards have words to show (B-463c).
- **(6) Memories has "Everyone" and one sub-tab per character who has a found memory**, by name. A character with none isn't listed, so the tabs never tell you who you haven't met or what you haven't found (pillar 10; the "???" rule of D-181).
**Notes for Claude:** the steps' tick and arrow are plain marks until the icons come with the art pass. The reader's text scrolls on the right stick and Page Up / Down (`InputMap.md`).

### Q-463b — Tracking a quest: which quests, where it shows
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-463b, Q-463 point 1) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the journal mockup has a Track; what can be tracked, and where does it show before the trial HUD (B-465) exists?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **One quest at a time, an unfinished main or side quest; A tracks it, A again stops.** Tracking another moves the track. The Witcher 3 and Expedition 33 track one quest at a time.
- **A secret quest can't be tracked:** its step would show on a shared screen, which D-198 masks.
- **It shows as "{quest}: {current step}"** in the game menu's Journal preview and top right in a trial (the hub shows none), and is saved. It goes when the quest finishes.
- **No marker in the world** for the step (none of the steps has a place to point at yet).

### Q-463c — Journal details: when NEW goes, how a place is named, how lore is grouped, what the reward line says
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-463c, Q-463 points 4, 5) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** Q-463 left the NEW mark, the place found, the lore's kind and a finished quest's reward until the game records them. How should each work?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **NEW goes once the entry has been shown in the reader** (lit in the list), and stays while it is lit; it is saved. The Witcher 3's and Hades' codex mark an entry new until it is viewed. A save from before this build shows nothing NEW.
- **The place is "{trial}, {variation}"** (as the game menu names it), or **"Void Realm"** in the hub, kept when the memory or lore is found. A memory reads "{who} · found in {place}", a lore entry "{kind} · {place}" (the mockups).
- **Lore kinds:** Inscription (the default, as the placeholder's "Weathered inscription"), Note, Overheard, Item description, set per lore object in the editor.
- **Lore sub-tabs: Everything, then each place something was read in**, in the order first read there; no tab for a place with nothing read (pillar 10, as Memories' character tabs).
- **A finished quest ends "Reward: {names}."**: items and skills by name, a curse given ("the curse {name}") or lifted ("{name} lifted"); titles are left out until titles exist. An unfinished one keeps "Reward: shown when the quest is done." (Mass Effect and Expedition 33 show a quest's reward once it is earned.)
- **Left out:** the memory replay window (waits for memory scenes); "found by {friend}" on lore.

### Q-466 — The Gate: what's built before the art and co-op steps
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-466, D-191, D-192) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the Gate's look and rules are decided (D-191, D-192). What do the gates, the locks and the parts with no data yet do?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **D-192's mapping holds:** a gate is a `UTrialData`, a trial is one of its variations. **The gates are every trial asset with a map**, in a fixed order; the door's own gate opens lit. Any trial door in the hub opens the same Gate.
- **A trial is locked by a quest:** each trial can name a quest (Unlock Quest Id) and the line shown while locked (Unlock Hint, Crown's words); it opens when that quest is finished. Locks of other kinds ("after clearing Past on Hard") wait until the game records clears. Hades' and Hollow Knight's locked doors say what opens them; D-191 asks for the same.
- **A gate with no trials asks for the difficulty only** (as the door did before).
- **Order of choice:** A on a trial picks it and moves to the difficulty, A there moves to Enter (the mockup's "gate, then trial, then difficulty"); Left / Right change the difficulty from any row, as a settings row does.
- **X opens the party screen, Y the loadout**, over the Gate (Back returns), as drawn.
- **Left out until their data exists:** the Party Combat Tier line (the hub has no fighters to read a tier from), "{friend} is ready" in co-op (no ready step), and the hub gate's glow colour (an editor / VFX step).
- **The run line** reads "To leave a trial with your rewards, extract at a rest spot." The mockup's "can't be saved" is left out: a run in progress is saved and resumes from the hub (Q-208).

### Q-467 — The title screen: where it lives, what Continue and New Game do before the new game screen
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-467, D-202) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the title's look and rows are decided (D-202, `UIScreens.md` #9, #10). Five things the code needed are not. (1) There is no title map: where does the title sit? (2) What does Continue do? (3) What does New Game do after the hold, before the new game screen (B-469) exists? (4) Join a friend and Credits have no screen yet (B-459, B-470). (5) The Continue line shows hours, and the save has never counted time.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **(1) The title sits over the first level, once per launch; no title map.** Hades opens its menu over the live House scene and Hollow Knight's title is a screen over the game, not a separate place. It needs no new map or game mode. In the editor's PIE it does not open by itself (it would be in the way of every test): `wor.UI.Title` opens it.
- **(2) Continue closes the title.** The save is already loaded when the first level starts, and a saved run resumes from the hub by itself (Q-208), so the level under the title is where the save is.
- **(3) After the 2-second hold the save is deleted, and the new game begins the next time the game starts.** Starting it at once needs the party, pool, journal, quests and forms in memory reset, which is the new game screen's job (B-469, its row now says so). The screen says so in plain words. Nothing is written for the rest of that session, so the old game can't come back.
- **(4) Join a friend and Credits are listed and say they aren't built yet**, as the settings screen does for rows not built (B-452).
- **(5) The save counts time played from now on** (`FWoRSaveData::PlaySeconds`, no version bump: older saves start at 0). Under an hour it reads in minutes. Hollow Knight's and Elden Ring's Continue show the save's place and time (`UIScreens.md`).
**Notes for Claude:** B-467b is the backdrop's editor step. B-469 owns the fresh start in place (built: Q-469; point 3 now opens the new game screen after the hold).

### Q-469 — The new game screen: who the pick starts as, which elements, how the keys move
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-469, `UIScreens.md` #11) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the screen's look is decided (mockups `front/new_game/`). What does a pick start as, are all nine Refractor elements open (`UIScreens.md` open point 16), and how do the keys move between the parts?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **A pick starts as one of the lords** (the character assets already in the game: the Generic lord, the Resonator lord, a Refractor lord per element). The list is `NewGameCharacters` in `DefaultGame.ini`; the screen takes the one whose class (and, for a Refractor, element) matches. The born Broken Darkness lord is never a start. A class's identity and its gear come from its lord, as Hades' and Elden Ring's starting classes come with their kit.
- **All nine elements are open at the start**, as drawn: Pokémon's starters and Elden Ring's classes are all open on the first screen. Earning Void, Reality or Broken Darkness later would be a change to the start; say if you want it.
- **Keys:** Up / Down move through the class cards (the class follows), Up from the top card reaches Body, Down from the last reaches Begin. Left / Right pick the body; Right from a Refractor's card goes into the element grid (it stands to the right), Left from its first column comes back. A moves on to the next part, and starts the game on Begin. B goes back to the title.
- **Begin starts the game in place:** everything in memory goes back to a new game's, the party is the picked lord alone, the slot is written with it, and the hub opens with the player as the picked character. The title's New Game opens the screen with no save too (nothing to lose there).
- **Left out:** the body previews (grey boxes until an editor step), the mouse, and the heading and opening (Crown's; "placeholder heading" stays).

### Q-476d — The Skills tab: where Innate spells sit, the evolution, the tab order and X
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-476d, UIScreens #2-3) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup shows a Refractor's Innate spells as an entry and the tabs Weapons | Skills | Apparel | Cosmetics on LB / RB; where does Innate spells sit, does the character's evolution get an entry, how do LB / RB turn now that there are three built tabs, and what does X do on the Skills tab?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **Innate spells is one entry, after the gear entries**, its slots the school rows on points (the mockup's `7_skills_refractor.png`). Persona 5 and Expedition 33 list a character's own skills after the equipped gear's.
- **The character's evolution has no Skills entry:** it adds no skill slots (its passives show on the Weapons tab).
- **LB / RB turn Weapons | Skills | Apparel in a ring** (RB right, LB left; Cosmetics joins later), the chips' order (B-482), as Elden Ring's and Expedition 33's bumper-paged menus wrap around. This changes B-440f's "either key turns" (two tabs).
- **The Skills tab looks like the Weapons tab** (desktop, 2026-11-14): an entry's row keeps its weapon-type icon, socket badges and ACTIVE; "Skills by entry" sits above the list (the mockup's line; cells are the Weapons tab's); a skill in the box uses the B-487b header; the box follows the mouse in the slots and the picker (as B-484). One screen, one look, as Expedition 33's and Persona 5's menu tabs keep one row style.
- **X switches a skill's full and short effect text, shared with the Weapons tab's view** (B-479c: the screen remembers the view); the box beside the list stands in for the mockup's open-in-place row (the B-479c layout).

### Q-476c — Loadout v2's Add gear window: where sockets live, Remove all gear, the remembered view
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-476c) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** building the rest of the v2 Weapons tab raised five small points the mockups don't settle.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **Sockets stay on the piece they are in.** Swapping an entry's main hand (or adapter) brings the new piece's own sockets; the old crystal stays on the old weapon, nothing is destroyed. Path of Exile and Diablo IV keep socketed gems in the item when you swap gear, and players rely on it to keep builds on spare weapons.
- **A socket change goes in at once** (A Pick, Y Confirm on that socket's list), through today's socket rules: the pouch pays, a full socket is emptied first and the gem and stone come off together and are used up (CL-28b), said first, A again to do it. The window's other slots (main hand, off hand, adapter, evolution) go in together on Y Confirm. A new entry's sockets are its piece's own; A Edit on the entry changes them once it is in.
- **Remove all gear keeps the first entry** (D-116: every loadout keeps a weapon or adapter there); everything else comes out, sockets stay on their pieces. It asks first (A twice), as the mockup's "Asks first".
- **The full / simple view is remembered while the game runs**, not saved across launches yet (TODO with the settings save). Expedition 33 and Persona 5 keep a menu's sort and view for the session.
- **The layout follows UIScreens' B-479c** (list left, box right, X Simple / Full), approved after B-476b drew the full-width B-479b list; B-476b's list is replaced here.

### Q-477 — Weapon sets: what the first code part decides
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-477) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-209 to D-211 set the sets' rules. Four smaller points the code needs before the screen (B-476) and the editor step (B-478) land.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **A new weapon is `SingleOnly` until the editor step sets it** (`EWeaponHandling`), so nothing pairs by accident before B-478 marks the swords and daggers that may.
- **A pair's weapon Power is the main hand's**; the off hand adds its stat and resistance lines (D-210 "both weapons' stats"). Monster Hunter's Dual Blades carry one attack value for the pair, and players read it at a glance; adding two Powers would double a pair's base damage over a two-handed weapon.
- **The sockets are the main hand's**: an off hand's own crystal or stone is not read (D-210: the sockets belong to the entry). An off hand that is no longer owned leaves its main hand alone; a main hand that goes takes the set with it.
- **A bad pair is cleared to its main hand alone** by the loadout check (a sword with a dagger, a single-only weapon, a pair on a Resonator or Refractor). A dual-only weapon on its own is dropped like an unowned one (D-116 refills the primary).

### Q-480 — Entries in the code before the Add gear window
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-480) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-210 sets the entries. Where does the code keep a Generic's adapter beside two weapons, and what does the loadout check do with a loadout that breaks a D-210 rule (an old save, an authored loadout)?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **A Generic's adapter entry sits in the same adapter list as a Resonator's** (one at most), so the save needs no new field; with no adapter primary it is the Generic's adapter in use.
- **A broken rule is fixed at the next check, not refused silently:** a second adapter on a Generic is dropped (its adapter entry first, then a weapon if still over the budget); an evolution on a Resonator's weapon or a Generic's adapter is taken off and goes back to the inventory free (an evolution on gear is never consumed). Each drop says "Removed: ..." as the other loadout drops do (D-116 style).
- **Two stones:** a stone goes in the stone socket first, then in an empty crystal socket. The screen offers a stone for the crystal socket only once the stone socket holds one, so what the screen shows is where the stone lands. Path of Exile's sockets take any gem colour the socket allows and its players stack the same support twice for more; stacking two of one stat here follows that.

### Q-456d — The Spiritualist's tabs: one piece or everything, whose curses, Merge's layout
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-456d, D-193) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the approved mockups for Repair, Curses and Merge differ from the code in four places. (1) Repair shows "A Repair this" and "X Repair all", but the paid repair is all or nothing (ER-37). (2) Repair lists unworn pieces with "—". (3) Curses says "Sera holds it", but curses are held by the player, not by one character. (4) Merge has two columns (the crystal, then the tier to make).
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **(1) A repairs one piece, X repairs everything; both ask first (D-016).** Per-item repair beside "repair all" is the long-standing habit of RPG blacksmiths (Diablo II and III, Dragon Age: Origins), and it is what the approved mockup draws. The price of each piece is unchanged (its tier times its missing share).
- **(2) Only worn pieces are listed.** Nothing to do on an unworn one; Diablo's repair lists only what needs it.
- **(3) The tab lists the player's curses**, with the curse's own text in the row. Curses are the player's (ER-12i, Q-442).
- **(4) One list; Left / Right picks the tier to make, starting at the highest the crystals reach.** Same choice and panel as drawn (uses, makes, change back, price), one column fewer for a pad: Monster Hunter's melding and Diablo's gem cube pick the result in one list.
- **Co-op:** only the host can pay (a guest's rows are grey with "Only the host can buy for now."), as the shop and the item slots (B-427, Q-239).
**Notes for Claude:** `WoRVendorScreen::BuildRepairPage`, `BuildCursesPage`, `BuildMergePage`, `RunService`, `RepairAll` (`UI/Menus/VendorScreenModel.h`); the refusals are the services' own (`GearRepair::GetRefusal`, `UCurseSubsystem::GetClearRefusal`, `UEconomyService::GetMergeRefusal`). Rules ER-37, ER-28.

### Q-456b — The Buy tab: which vendors use it, how the cart is bought, how a line is taken back
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-456b, D-193) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-193 names the tabs of three vendors, and the Buy mockup shows a cart. (1) What do the other vendors (Combat Master, Spell Shop) open? (2) Does Purchase ask first (D-016)? (3) The mockup has no key to take a line back out of the cart: which? (4) A cart can hold lines for different members, but a purchase is made for one member at a time.
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **(1) Every vendor opens the same screen;** a vendor D-193 doesn't name has the Buy tab alone. `UIScreens.md` #15 already calls the shop "every vendor", and one shop screen for every merchant is the genre's habit (Elden Ring's merchants, Expedition 33).
- **(2) X buys the cart at once.** The cart with its total and "After purchase" is the question. Baldur's Gate 3 and Divinity: Original Sin 2 trade a whole offer on one button with no second confirm. D-016's ask-first stays on Sell and Dismantle, which act on a single row.
- **(3) Left takes one back; Right (or A) adds one.** Left / right for quantity is the common shop control (the buy-quantity step in Elden Ring and Monster Hunter). Mouse: right-click a row.
- **(4) One member's lines are bought together, all or nothing; the members one after the other.** The whole cart's cost is checked against the wallet first, so a later member is refused only if something else is in the way (no room); the screen then says who and why, and what was bought before stays bought.
**Notes for Claude:** `WoRVendorScreen::GetTabs`, `AddToCart`, `RemoveFromCart`, `PurchaseCart` (`UI/Menus/VendorScreenModel.h`); the refusal words are the purchase's own (`UEconomyService::GetPurchaseRefusal`). Leaving the screen drops the cart with no warning. Rules ER-54.

### Q-442 — The character sheet: which number does a sub-stat row show?
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-442) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the mockup's sub-stat numbers were drawn as examples. Does a row show the stat's **points**, or the **value the fight uses**?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change it): **the value the fight uses right now**, gear and stones included, in its own unit: a stat that multiplies reads **"x1.48"**, a stat that is a share reads **"9%"**. Elden Ring's status screen shows the derived numbers (attack power, defence, resistances) beside the attributes, and Expedition 33's attribute page shows what each point gives; players read those, not raw points. **Luck** reads as a multiplier ("x1.00"): in code it is a score its users scale, not a chance. **Curses** are held by the player, not by one member, so every sheet lists them. Each resistance bar is full at its own cap (75% for All, 50% for the others).
**Notes for Claude:** one table in `CharacterSheetModel.cpp` (`MIND_STATS`, `BODY_STATS`, `SPIRIT_STATS`). "Where it comes from" (the layers) is the R3 view, not built yet.

### Q-451 — What counts as a run's "upgrades"?
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-16) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (D-189, B-451) · **Blocks:** — (B-451 builds with the reading below unless Crown says otherwise)
**Question:** D-189 keeps "the run's upgrades" on extracting or beating the boss and drops them when the party runs out of wipes. Which things are upgrades?
**Context:** what a run can leave behind today, besides the currency payout: the **gear upgrade levels** it raised (the upgrade memory, D-097); **items found** in it (to home storage); **memories** found; **quest steps** done (hidden quest steps stay, B-377); the run's **pillar levels** always end with the run (D-102).
**Options:** **A** upgrades = the gear upgrade levels and the items found; memories and quest steps are story records and are kept whatever the ending · **B** everything the run gave (A plus memories and quest steps) is lost when the party runs out of wipes ("their story ended") · **C** only the gear upgrade levels.
**Recommendation (my reading):** **A**. D-188 lists "upgrades, memories, quests" as separate things in the payout line, so upgrades are not the memories or quests; and losing a found memory would break the journal's "found is found" (pillar 10). Crown's "their story ended" may mean B: this touches the story, so it is Crown's to say.
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-16):** A. Memories and quest steps found in a run are kept whatever the ending: "you can keep them, since they've been found". Out of wipes loses the found gear and items and costs tier (D-219), and pays a quarter of the currency.

### Q-238b — At run end, do pouch finds move to home storage?
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-04 (B-238b) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** RunStructure §6 says items found in a run are kept "to home storage" (D-097). Since B-203 a find already stays, in the finder's pouch (or storage when the pouch is full). Should run end move the pouch finds to storage?
**Answer**, by the default (Crown may change any): **yes.** When a run ends any way but a quit (win, extract, out of wipes), the items found in it that sit in a pouch go to home storage; what was carried before the run stays in the pouch. Reference: Darkest Dungeon empties the expedition's pack into the Hamlet on return (loot is banked, the party re-packs for the next expedition), widely liked for the "bring it home" moment and a clean start each run. A quit still takes the finds back (Q-439b).
- **Counted as Q-439b:** per kind, for the whole party; pouches give first.
- **Shortcut:** a find that already went to storage (a full pouch) still counts, so an item carried from before the run may go to storage in its place. Nothing is lost.
- **Not covered:** a co-op guest's own pouch (as Q-439b).
**Notes for Claude:** `UPoolSubsystem::StoreRunFoundItems` (`Pool/PoolRunLayer.cpp`), called by `UTrialRunSubsystem::ExitTrial`; spec `WoR.Pool.RunLayer`.

### Q-450 — Does the rest menu's "Revive boss" stay?
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-23: **B**) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (D-187, B-450) · **Blocks:** B-589
**Question:** D-187 says no enemy rank is shown to the player; D-182 (the same day) names the rest menu's row "Revive boss". Keep it, or reword?
**Options:** **A** keep "Revive boss" (it names what you just beat, after the fact, so nothing is spoiled) · **B** name the opponent ("Revive the Ash Warden") · **C** a rank-free word ("Revive the guardian").
**Recommendation:** **B**: Elden Ring and Hades name the foe itself and never its rank, and D-187 already asks for the opponent's name on the wipe card. A stays until Crown answers.
**Answer (Crown):** B, name the opponent ("Revive the Ash Warden"); built by B-589.

### Q-408 — A game closed in the middle of a run: lost, or resumed once?
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14; **confirmed by Crown, 2026-11-14**: A, closing the game mid-run counts as a quit) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-408, D-172, D-176) · **Blocks:** — (B-408a, which waits for Crown's agreement to the survey anyway)
**Question:** today the run is saved at every fight exit and rest spot, and a restart resumes it. That save also carries the co-op hand-over (B-234b) and the only record of what the run changed in the store (`docs/analysis/NoSavedRunsSurvey.md`). With no saved runs (D-172), what happens when the game closes mid-run (quit to desktop, crash, power cut)?
**Answer**, by the default (Crown may change it):
- **A: it counts as a quit (D-176).** On the next start the run is restored only to settle it: home pieces go back, found items are taken back, nothing is paid, run recruits leave, and the player is in the hub. Escape from Tarkov counts leaving a raid as "missing in action" (the gear is lost), and Dark and Darker treats a disconnect the same way; players accept it because it keeps extracting meaningful, and it closes the "close the game instead of quitting" loophole.
- B (not taken): crash recovery, resuming once from the last checkpoint. Tarkov lets a player reconnect to a raid still running, and Returnal's lack of a mid-run save was its most criticised flaw until its Suspend Cycle patch. But it would go against D-172's "no saved runs".
**Notes for Claude:** B-408a builds A. The co-op hand-over keeps resuming: there the run is still being played.
### Q-447 — The trial NPC who sells pillar points: who, where, and the price
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-447, D-183) · **Blocks:** B-447
**Question:** D-183 moves "buy a pillar point" from the rest spot to an NPC found in the trial. (1) **Who is this NPC?** A character of the world (V, a recruit, a new face) is lore, so it is Crown's. (2) Where does it stand: at every rest spot's area, once per trial map, or at random spots? (3) Does the price stay `WoRLevelDrops::TryBuyPoint`'s run Prisms?
**Options (lore, no recommendation):** (1) A: V (D-079: V is the guide and every hub NPC); B: a recruitable character met in the trial; C: a new character Crown names.
**Reference-backed defaults for the non-lore parts (used once (1) is answered):** (2) once per trial map, near a rest spot, marked on the map, like Hades' Charon shop appearing in fixed chambers per region (widely liked as a reliable stop); (3) the price stays as it is (D-156's Prisms).
**Notes for Claude:** the NPC is a placed actor in the trial maps (a `ATrialMapFeature`), so placing it is an editor step after the code. The code (the NPC's actor and its placeholder buy UI, the rest spot's level screen without "buy") can be built as soon as (1) is answered.
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-14):** (1) It rotates between the trial's NPCs: each run, one of them carries the pillar-point stock, and the player has to find which one. (2) The trial's NPCs stand at rest spots that are not tied to a boss (Crown, 2026-11-14). Each NPC stays at one rest spot for the whole run, so once the player has found the seller, they come back to it there. Which NPC sells changes between runs, not during one. (3) The price stays D-156's Prisms. Note: the rotation means the seller is not marked on the map; only the NPCs are.
### Q-449 — The trial NPC who extracts the party: who, where, and whether it charges
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-449, D-186) · **Blocks:** B-449
**Question:** D-186 adds an NPC in the trial whose talk extracts the party at the half share. (1) **Who is this NPC?** Lore, so Crown's. (2) Where does it stand? (3) Does it charge on top of the half share?
**Options (lore, no recommendation):** (1) A: V (D-079); B: the same NPC as Q-447's point seller; C: a new character Crown names.
**Reference-backed defaults for the non-lore parts (used once (1) is answered):** (2) at most one per trial map, away from the boss's rest spot so it is a real second way out, like Escape from Tarkov's paid and conditional extracts beside the free ones; (3) no extra charge: the half share already is the price (D-186, RunRewards::LEAVE_EARLY_SHARE).
**Notes for Claude:** a placed actor in the trial maps (`ATrialMapFeature`), so placing it is an editor step; the code (its actor, the extract through `UTrialRunSubsystem` as the boss rest spot's Extract does since B-448) can be built once (1) is answered.
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-14):** (1) A: V extracts the party. (2) and (3) take the defaults above: at most one per trial map, away from the boss's rest spot, with no charge beyond the half share.

### Q-439b — A quit run's found items: how they are counted
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-439b, Q-439 point 2) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** a quit run takes back the items found in it (Q-439 point 2: Escape from Tarkov and Dark and Darker take what you picked up in a failed raid). Nothing marks one crystal as "found", so how is it counted?
**Answer**, by the default (Crown may change any):
- **Per kind, for the whole party:** the game counts each item kind when the run begins (every pouch and storage); on a quit, what is held above that count is taken back. What was owned before always stays in number.
- **An item used or sold in the run is not charged again:** use one owned potion and find one, and the quit takes nothing (you end with what you began with).
- **Pouches pay first, then storage.** So where an owned item sits can change: if your pouch was full and the find went to storage, the quit takes one from the pouch and the stored one stays.
- **A recruit's starting items are not finds** (D-097: items are never thrown away): after a quit they are in storage, as after any run end.
- **A save from before this build** has no count, so a run resumed from it treats everything held at the resume as owned.
- **Not covered:** a co-op guest's own pouch (only the host can quit; the guest's items are on the guest's machine).
**Notes for Claude:** `UPoolSubsystem::TakeRunFoundItems`, `GetRunStartItems` (`Pool/PoolRunLayer.cpp`); rules ER-65b. An exact "who found what" needs a count per pouch (the `shortcut:` comment there).
