### Q-439 — The game menu: pausing, what a quit run takes, quitting with no title screen
**Status:** ANSWERED (reference-backed default, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-439, D-176) · **Blocks:** — (B-439b for point 2)
**Question:** (1) does opening the game menu pause the world? (2) "Quitting loses everything" (D-176): what is taken? (3) there is no title screen yet: what does "Quit to title" do?
**Answer**, by the reference-backed default (Crown may change any):
- **(1) Nothing pauses**, solo or co-op. Elden Ring, Nightreign and Monster Hunter keep the world running in menus because they are online games, and this one is co-op (D-103); the CommonUI base already states that no screen pauses. A solo-only pause (Persona 5, Expedition 33) can come later if wanted.
- **(2) The run's whole payout is lost** (the tally, the boss's Mentor Tokens), on top of what every run end already takes (the run's Prisms, run-only gear, the run's pillar levels). Extraction games (Escape from Tarkov, Dark and Darker) also take **what you picked up** in a failed raid; here that is the pouch items found in the run, and nothing records which those are yet, so **they are kept for now**: job **B-439b**. **Built (B-439b, 2026-11-14):** the pool counts each item kind when the run begins, and a quit takes back what is held above that count (whole party, pouches first, then storage).
- **(3) In a run, Quit ends the run and returns to the hub** (with nothing). **Outside a run, it saves and closes the game.** Both go to the title screen once it exists (UIScreens #9).
- **A co-op guest can't quit the host's run** from this menu ("Only the host can end the run"); a guest leaves through the rest menu's Leave (ER-09e).
**Notes for Claude:** `UTrialRunSubsystem::AbandonRun`, `WoRPauseMenu::Quit`. Rules ER-65b.

### D-213 — The player-text audit is approved in full
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, chat 2026-11-14, relayed by the Operator: "3 YES APPROVE") · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (`docs/Audits/PlayerTextAudit.md`) · **Blocks:** — (applied by B-491)
**Answer (Crown):** the audit's wording is the game's wording. The rules it sets, for every player-facing text from now on:
- **"Worn" is not used for two things.** Gear in a loadout is **Equipped**; wear on gear is **Damaged** (or Broken). "Nothing needs repair."
- **No debug words on a player screen:** no "see the Output Log", no console commands, no "wallet", "armoury", "primary".
- **One word per thing:** Back (Cancel only in a confirm box), Select, Leave, Buy / Sell, Equip / Unequip, EP, Spell (Skill = spell or ability), Ally / Enemy / the target, Adapter, Form, Chest, Defence, "X Up / X Down" for buffs and debuffs, DoT, \<Element\> Break, Negated / Barrier. One wording for a shared refusal: "Not during a fight.", "Not enough \<currency\>.", "Couldn't \<verb\> that.", "\<Name\> isn't here."
- **A button doesn't name the place the screen already shows:** Leave, Enter, Hand over host.
- **Interact prompts are the key, the verb and the thing:** "E  Open · Chest", "E  Rest" (Skyrim's prompt; the hub mockup's "X Talk · Blacksmith").
- **Lore is untouched:** story, dialogue, quest and journal text, names, flavour lines.
**Left open by the audit itself** (it gives no single answer; Crown's call when it comes up): Secret quest or Hidden quest (the code says Secret); Perfect Dodge / Perfect Guard beside Flawless Parry; the New Game heading (lore).

### Q-245 — What a new character picks goes into its inventory (D-245): the three open points
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-18 (D-245) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Where each pick lives today (read from code):** the picks are saved and kept on the party member's record; the player is rebuilt from them on every load (`WoRPlayerBuild::Build`). The picked weapons (Generic) or two empty adapters (Resonator) are the template's StartingGear: minted into the store at the first hub draw, owned by that character, worn (D-223), and listed on the inventory's Gear tab. The picked crystals are banked into its pouch. No spell is picked on the new-game screen: a Refractor's innate spells come from the class table into its innate list. The class kit's StartingSkills (D-224) went into the run's list only, at trial entry.
**Built (D-245):** the class's StartingSkills are now the new character's own known skills from the start (`WoRPlayerBuild::MakeInventory`), so they show in the hub. Only characters made after this get them.
**The three open points, with the defaults built:** (a) **Worn at once, or only owned?** Gear is worn at once (D-223, unchanged). Skills are known, not put in a slot (D-224, Q9): the player slots them. (b) **Its own inventory, or the shared pool?** Its own: gear by owner and skills by character, which is how the store already keeps them (D-219, D-221). The shared chest is unchanged. (c) **Is the trial-entry kit made from the inventory?** No, unchanged: a run starts on the class kit with the picks laid over it (D-221, D-228 (a)), minted as its own copies, and the hub's starting pieces stay at home. So in a run the player holds the kit's sword while the hub's sword of the same kind waits at home. Reference: Hades and Slay the Spire give a new character its starting kit as its own from the first screen, and each run then starts from a fixed loadout rather than from what was collected.
**For Crown, if (c) should change:** making the run's kit reuse the owned starting pieces (no second copy) touches D-221's rule that the kit is per run and never sold or dismantled. Say so and it becomes its own job.
**Not changed:** a Refractor's innate spells stay innate (not known skills), so they can't be dismantled or levelled like a learned spell. **Only the player's own new character** gets the starting skills: a co-op guest's skills are its own save's (and are handed to the host's copy and back, B-564), and a hub recruit keeps its fixed row. **A consequence:** the starting skill is now an owned skill, so a lost run treats it as D-224 treats any owned skill (its hub copy can drop a tier), where before it was a run-only find.

### Q-590 — The turn-order hexagons' small points
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (B-590, from D-264) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-264 sets the hexagons' sizes and colours. What shows without portrait art? Are bonus turns marked? Where does the column sit next to Intel? How does Turns shown step?
**Answer (defaults):**
1. **The picture (Crown's answer, 2026-11-23, after B-590 QA 1):** face snapshots. The hexagons had shown item icons, because the test characters' Portrait field points at item art. Now, when the fight starts, one close-up picture is taken of each fighter's face from its 3D model, from in front, and kept for the fight. After QA 3 (Crown wants the mockup `I_hex_row_small.png`'s look, cropped from Intel's view): the picture is taken from the side Intel's camera stands on for that fighter, framed on the head, shoulders and upper chest, in the lit arena (the other fighters left out). The plain dark hexagon with the same coloured edge shows only when that capture fails. `wor.UI.Print` names each face.
2. **Bonus turns:** not marked; the next hexagon is simply the same character again. The old list's green "(bonus)" stays in `wor.UI.Print`.
3. **The current turn:** violet whatever its side. Cyan and rose are for the next turns.
4. **Column:** the current turn on top, the rest centred under it, and Intel under the column as under the row.
5. **Turns shown:** stops at 1 and 6 rather than wrapping round, as a count does. A save from before this (none set) reads 6.
6. **Sizes:** gaps of 4 px at 720p; edges of 2.7 px (current) and 2 px (next). QA 1 thickened them from 2 px and 1.3 px, which were hard to see on a bright sky.

**Reference:** Honkai: Star Rail's action order shows an extra turn as just another portrait in the line, with no badge, and marks only the acting character. Expedition 33's turn cards are small portraits with no names. The deckbuilder has no turn strip.

### Q-588 — Position's keys and the picker's look
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (B-588, from D-263 points 1 and 2) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-263 makes one Position command with a Front / Middle / Back picker. Which key opens it, how are the rows picked by keys and pad, and how is the current row marked?
**Answer (defaults):** (1) **Position's key: 4 / D-pad Up**, both free since B-436g (D-258); its hint shows the key like Defend's. (2) **The picker is a sub-menu titled "Position"** with three rows, Front Row, Middle Row, Back Row, top to bottom, then Back; as in every sub-menu only Back stays a corner hint, so the other hints hide. (3) **The rows take the main menu's first three keys:** 1 / A Front, 2 / X Middle, 3 / Y Back (the keys that press the first three rows there), and a click. (4) **The current row is greyed "You are here."**; a row the move is refused for is greyed with why. (5) Picking a row moves there at once (no confirm) and returns to the main menu with Position greyed "Already moved this turn."; Back leaves without moving.
**Reference:** Final Fantasy's Row command is one command on the battle menu (D-263 cites it). The keys follow this menu's own layout (1-3 / A X Y already press its first three rows), so there is nothing new to learn. The deckbuilder has no rows.

### Q-436b — The active card's small points
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (B-436b, `Design/BattleHUDRefinePlan.md` decision 2) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** the plan's decision 2 (2026-11-13/14) predates D-254 and D-261. What of it is still built, and what do its open values become?
**Answer (defaults):** (1) **No class line on a fight's card.** The plan's centred "Darkness Refractor" line is the line D-254 removed ("we also dont need spirit of generic body of nobody"); what a character is stays in Intel and the Character sheet. (2) **No status icons on a party card** (D-261: they are in Intel). (3) ~~The tier beside the name on the party's cards ("Garrick (C)")~~ **Changed by Crown, 2026-11-23** (after B-436b's QA): "We don't need the tier on names". **No tier letter on a party card's name;** an enemy's tier stays at the bottom of its marker (B-436c). (4) **Low HP is under 30%** of max, alive only: the HP bar turns a hot pink-red and its opacity pulses between full and 45% once a second (`TODO: tune`); a downed card stays dimmed, without the pulse. (5) **The darker colours** apply wherever the card is drawn, so the hub and trial strip get them too, and the white numbers' one-pixel dark outline (no shadow, no backing box).
**Reference:** Expedition 33's party cards show name, HP and AP bars with no class text; Final Fantasy VII Remake and Persona 5 turn a low HP gauge red and flash it so danger reads at a glance; Persona and Final Fantasy X put a character's level by the name. The deckbuilder's card shows HP only.

(2) **Above the head,** 40 cm over the body's top (`TODO: tune`), following the camera. ### Q-586 — Intel: how enemy details are learned, and its keys
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (B-586, from D-261) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-261 shows "?" for what isn't learned and names no key. What fills a "?", and what opens Intel?
**Answer (defaults):** (1) **A hit teaches its own type:** hitting an enemy with an element or a physical type shows that line's weakness or resistance; the rest stay "?". (2) **Kept for that enemy type until the run ends,** as Kit Sight's reads are (Q-572 point 4). (3) **Never hidden:** an enemy's bars and Effects, and everything about your own party. (4) **Keys:** V / R3 opens and closes Intel in a fight (`UIScreens.md`'s Details, `IA_UI_Details`); a click on a card opens it on that character's tab; Q / E and LB / RB move along the tabs while it is open, as in every tabbed menu.
**Reference:** Final Fantasy XIII's enemy screen fills in as you fight that enemy, and Octopath Traveler 2 shows a weakness once you hit it (https://progameguides.com/octopath-traveler-2/octopath-traveler-2-weakness-order/). The deckbuilder has no rule for it: it isn't a match engine.
**Built (B-586), with these build defaults (Crown may change):** only elements are listed under Weak to / Resists (a physical type carries no damage multiplier, Q-145, so (1)'s physical lines have nothing to show); a neutral element is never listed, it only leaves the "?" line; the enemy's HP number stays hidden though the picture shows one (Q-391); a heal or a hit on the party teaches nothing; what is learned is not in the save, so loading a run mid-way starts it blank again; while Intel is open every combat menu key stands down and Back closes it.

### D-271 — Ice infusion from a Water source; the infusion HP backlash scaled and cut by Efficiency
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-24) · **Raised:** 2026-11-24 (Crown, during the weapon glow change) · **Blocks:** B-593, B-594
**Crown:** Ice is not an element; it stays Water's damage type, but a Water source can infuse Ice. The infusion HP backlash stays tied to EP, gets its own tunable multiplier, and Efficiency also cuts it.
**Ruled (Ice):**
- Ice stays a **damage type of Water** (ED-04): no Ice crystal, adapter or Refractor.
- **New:** where a character has a Water source (weapon crystal, adapter, evolution, innate), the infusion picker also offers an **Ice** infusion from that same source. It deals Ice (the Ice hit kind: Ice resistance alone resists it) and fills the Ice bar.
- An Ice infusion **costs more** than a Water infusion from the same source: × `ICE_INFUSION_COST_MULTIPLIER` (placeholder, `// TODO: Crown tune`).
- On a skill marked **Deals Ice**, an Ice infusion costs the normal (Water) amount, no extra. On a plain Water skill it costs the higher amount.
- Ice has its own row in the default infusion glow list (`IceInfusionGlowVFX`), scaling with the level like every glow (built on `feature/weapon-glow-trail`).
**Ruled (HP backlash, ED-32):**
- HP lost = (infused EP before Efficiency ÷ max EP) × max HP × `HP_BACKLASH_MULTIPLIER` × (1 − Efficiency) × (1 − Resistance).
- `HP_BACKLASH_MULTIPLIER` placeholder **0.15** (`// TODO: Crown tune`). At 1.0 a level-2 Heavy Attack already kills a 119 HP character; 0.25 lands level 2 at about 15-30% of max HP, and Crown went lower still.
- Efficiency's cut on HP is **capped at the stats-only max** (`EFFICIENCY_MAX`, 50%) even with gear; EP keeps its 90% gear ceiling. Resistance still applies.
- **D-035 stands:** an infused ability keeps the x1.5 EP surcharge (`INFUSION_ENERGY_MULTIPLIER`). A same-day proposal to drop it was withdrawn.
**Build defaults (Crown may change):** the Ice multiplier scales whatever the source pays for that infusion (EP, the L2 HP backlash, or crystal wear for a free source). Its placeholder value is the builder's pick in B-593, named and marked TODO.

### D-270 — Single targets are picked on the field, not from a list
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-23) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (Crown, B-582c QA) · **Blocks:** B-582c
**Crown:** no need to show the target's name row in the panel while targeting; the marker and the enemy's card above it already show it.
**Ruled (new battle HUD):**
- A single-target pick (Attack, a skill, an item, on one foe or one ally) draws no target name rows or their section headers. The panel keeps its heading and summary, and the weapon / infusion lines on 2 / 3 (D-268).
- **W / S** move the marker to the previous / next target, wrapping, the camera following as hover; on a pad **D-pad Up / Down** do the same, in a target pick only (on the main menu they stay Position / Flee).
- **D**, Enter / Space and 1 again (Attack) confirm the marked target (D-266, D-267).
- **Mouse:** the pointer over a fighter marks it; a left click on the fighter, or on its card (the marked enemy's floating card, an ally's own card in an ally pick), picks it. Outside a pick a card click inspects, as before.
- A group pick (all foes, self) keeps its one confirm row and wording.
- **The floating enemy card loses its tier line** (Crown chose this over also dropping its HP bar, which is the only place an enemy's HP shows). It keeps the HP bar, buildup bar and status line.
**Build defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** before this, neither mouse route existed: the floating card showed only on the marked enemy and a click on it opened Intel, and the fighters took no pointer events. Only the marked enemy has a floating card (D-263), so other enemies are hovered or clicked on their bodies. Honkai: Star Rail and Expedition 33 pick targets on the field with left / right or up / down and no list.

### D-269 — WASD moves through the battle menu
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-23) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (Crown, after D-267's hold) · **Blocks:** B-582c
**Crown:** said yes to WASD navigation in the battle menu.
**Ruled (new battle HUD):**
- **W / S** move the highlight up and down the rows of whatever list is open (main menu, skill kinds, a skill list, items, the target rows, the Position picker), wrapping at the ends. On target rows the highlight moves the target marker and camera, as hover does.
- **D** goes forward: it presses the highlighted row, the same as its number key or a click. On a target row that attacks or uses the skill, the same as Enter / Space.
- **A** goes back one step, the same as Backspace. On the main menu it does nothing.
- **Q / E** stay off the target step (B-592's X / Y = 2 / 3 panel and R / T cover weapon and infusion there). They would step a list's tab row, but the new HUD has none (skill kinds open as their own list), so nothing is bound yet.
- WASD never moves a fighter or the camera in a fight. While Intel is open the menu keys stand down as before (Intel keeps Q / E; A closes it, as Backspace does).
**Build defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** a list always has a row lit, so D works at once: Back re-lights the row you left it on (after a number key, its first row); any other list, and each new turn, opens on its first enabled row; a target step opens on its highlighted target (B-582, QA 2026-11-23). Disabled rows and section headers are skipped. A key moves one row per press (no repeat while held). On the old battle HUD W / S / D do nothing, and move input is blocked there too. Expedition 33 and Persona 5 move a menu cursor up and down with wrap and confirm / back on two face buttons; WASD as arrows is the PC norm (Baldur's Gate 3's controller-style menus, Divinity: Original Sin 2).

### D-268 — A compact target panel with X / Y toggles
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-23) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (Crown, mockups `docs/Design/screen_mockups/combat/hud/refine_v4/`) · **Blocks:** B-592
**Crown:** the target step (Attack, a skill, an item) starts small; X shows the weapon, Y the infusion. Later notes: no "Size x…" line and no backlash text in the target panel; the HP cost shows only as a ghost on the caster's HP bar.
**Ruled (new battle HUD):**
- The panel starts small: the heading ("Attack · choose a target") and one dim summary of the current choice ("Hammer · Lightning · Lv I"; "No infusion" at level 0). No weapon, element or level lines by default. The target rows stay under it (the only way to pick a target until a target-cycle key exists).
- **X / 2** shows only the weapon line (Attack with two weapons); X again switches to the next weapon and stays in the step. It follows the switch rule of 6 / L3 (a switch is free any number of times, B-170c), so X keeps cycling.
- **Y / 3** hides the weapon line and shows the infusion lines (element, then level); R / T (LB / RB) change them as before; Y again closes them. Back closes an open line first.
- A spell bound to one element (any spell but a breakthrough one, or a breakthrough with one source) shows no element line and no element in the summary: Y opens the level line only. Attack, weapon skills and breakthrough spells with a choice keep the element line.
- Corner hints: "2 Weapon / 3 Infusion" (keys), "X Weapon / Y Infusion" (pad), "Next weapon" / "Close" while open, "Level" / "Element" for the infusion keys only while the infusion line is open. Only what applies shows: no Weapon with one weapon, nothing for an item.
- In a target step 2 / 3 / X / Y never open Skills / Items (B-588's Position picker pattern).
- No "Size x…" or backlash line in the panel. The HP-cost ghost on the caster's HP bar is its own job (B-592b): nothing predicts a cost before commit yet.
**Build defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** the summary leaves out the element when it can't be chosen (one source); a chip click picks that weapon, element or level, as the keys do; Back closes an open line before leaving the step. Expedition 33's skill screen keeps the extra choices folded behind one button each, and Persona 5's target step shows only the action and target.

### D-267 — Enter and Space confirm in the battle target pickers
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-23) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (Crown, on B-582) · **Blocks:** B-582b
**Crown:** "enter should be confirmation also space bar".
**Ruled:** In the new battle HUD, Enter and Space pick the highlighted target in any single-target picker (Attack, a skill, an item), the same as clicking its row or the Attack picker's second 1. They do nothing on the main menu, in the Position picker, on a group pick (Self / All), while Intel is open, or while a Guard / Dodge window is open on this machine. No gamepad change (A is already the Attack picker's second press). This replaces D-266's note that Enter doesn't confirm. Tab gets no battle-menu binding (Crown, 2026-11-23: "Q/E, no Tab"); it stays the boss-cinematic skip.
**Build defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** a group pick (one labelled confirm row for Self / All) isn't on Enter / Space yet; it is still a click. Expedition 33 and most PC turn-based games (Divinity: Original Sin 2, Baldur's Gate 3) confirm with Enter and Space alike.
**Held (Crown is redesigning, 2026-11-23):** keyboard row movement (W / S), value change (A / D) and Q / E target cycling. A first build is parked on `feature/B-582c-menu-nav-hold`, not for merge. Redesigned as D-269 (B-582c).

### D-266 — Quick attack: 1 twice
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-23) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown, B-582's idea) · **Blocks:** B-582
**Crown:** chose the quick attack as pressing 1 twice in the battle menu.
**Ruled:** (1) The Attack target picker (1 / A on the main menu) opens on this fighter's last attack target in this fight; when that target is dead or there is none, on the first target as before. (2) In the Attack picker, 1 again (A on the pad) attacks the highlighted target, the same as clicking its row; the highlight can still be moved in between. (3) The last target is remembered per fighter for one fight only. The Position picker is unchanged (there, 1 is Front Row). Enter doesn't confirm: the new battle HUD has no confirm key, picking is a click or now 1 / A in the Attack picker (Q-391's open gap).
**Reference:** Expedition 33 keeps the last target selected and confirms with the same button that opened the attack; Persona 5 and Final Fantasy X also remember the last target.

### D-265 — Remove the battle HUD's Status button
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-23) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (Crown) · **Blocks:** B-591
**Crown:** "Remove it" (the top-centre Status button).
**Ruled:** The button goes. Intel opens from a party card click and with V / R3, which is what the button did since B-587 QA, so nothing is lost. The old all-status list it used to open is no longer reachable in play.
**Reference:** Expedition 33 opens character details from the character, not a separate HUD button.

### D-264 — The turn order becomes a row of small hexagon portraits, with two settings
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-23, on the turn-order mockups) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (Crown) · **Blocks:** B-590
**Crown:** asked for a nicer turn visual; kept the hexagons of the approved `refine_final/1_main_menu.png`; "they should be smaller ... they are not huge" (Expedition 33's turn cards); picked the small row (mockup I); "maybe we create options for it"; "make an option for how many turns ahead ... the max 6".
**Ruled:** (1) The text list at the top right becomes a row of hexagon portraits: the current turn bigger (about 56 px at 720p) with a violet edge, the next turns smaller (about 40 px), border cyan for the party and rose for enemies, no panel, no names. (2) Two settings on the settings screen: **Turn order layout** Row (default) / Column (down the right side), and **Turns shown** 1 to 6 (default 6, counting the current turn); 6 is the most, down from today's 7.
**Reference:** Expedition 33 and Honkai: Star Rail show the turn order as small portraits; Final Fantasy X as a column. The deckbuilder has no turn strip.

### D-263 — One Position command picks any row; the enemy's HP shows on the target only
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-23, on B-587's play shots) · **Raised:** 2026-11-23 (Crown) · **Blocks:** B-588; moves B-436c up
**Crown:** "the enemy health bar should be on the target enemy rather than permanently showing which frees up space for us and gives the turn order room to breath"; "merge step back and forward into a single button called position which lets us chose where we want to go on the grid"; asked whether Position may jump to any row or keeps the one-row step, Crown chose **A: any row**.
**Ruled:** (1) **Position** replaces the Step Forward and Step Back hints: one hint; choosing it opens a small picker of the three rows (Front / Middle / Back) with the current row marked, and the other corner hints hide while the picker is open. (2) **Any row, free, once per turn** (it doesn't end the turn): this changes PA-32's one-row step (D-096). Refused off-turn, while stunned and after the turn's move, as before; push and pull skills keep their one-row moves. (3) The enemy's always-on card (top right) goes: its HP shows on the highlighted target only. That is B-436c, already planned (`BattleHUDRefinePlan.md` piece 3a), now next after B-587; it doesn't need B-436b.
**Reference:** Final Fantasy's Row command (one command for the party's row); Expedition 33 shows an enemy's HP on the target. The deckbuilder has no rows.

### D-262 — Intel shows a close-up of the character, with the panel on one side
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22, after B-586's play) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown) · **Blocks:** B-587
**Crown:** "we can improve the observation list ... make it so the observation when activated does a close up on the character and is on one side", with Final Fantasy XIII's enemy screen (the creature large in the middle, its details down both sides) and Expedition 33's item target (the camera cuts to a front shot of the character being targeted).
**Ruled:** when Intel opens, and on every tab change, the fight camera cuts to a front close-up of that tab's character; the panel moves from the centre to one side so the character stays in view. The panel's content and keys stay as B-586 built them. Closing Intel gives the camera back to the turn view.
**Defaults (Crown may change):** (a) the panel sits on the right, the character framed in the left two-thirds; (b) a cut, no blend, as B-400's target view; (c) the close-up reuses the target view's front shot (`FCombatCameraRules::ComputeTargetView`), a little nearer; (d) with Camera Movement Off the camera doesn't move and only the panel moves to the side.
**Reference:** Final Fantasy XIII's Libra screen and Expedition 33's target close-up, both from Crown's shots.
**Built (B-587), with these build defaults (Crown may change):** the close-up is the target view's front shot at 0.75 of its distance, never under 2 m from the character's middle, in every turn-view style; the panel fills the right third of a 16:9 screen; it shows only on your own turn view (an enemy's turn, an action's shots or a cinematic hold the camera, the panel still opens); a new turn view while Intel is open (the next party member's turn, Movement back On) shows the open tab's close-up again.
**Parked (Crown):** the command menu shown next to the acting character, as Expedition 33's item list: "id rather test that out when everythings in place". Not a job yet.
**Additions (Crown, 2026-11-23, after B-587's agent QA):**
- **One title, not a row of tabs.** Crown: "could we have it as just one name then Q/E lb rb to switch we can also include arrows to indicate theres more". So the panel's top is one line, "◀ Q  Broken Darkness Lord  E ▶" (LB / RB on a pad), a small "Party" / "Enemy" label above it (yours reads "You"), the enemy's name in the enemy colour. The arrows show only when there is more than one character, and a click on one steps too; a click on a card still opens that character. Replaces D-261's "one row of tabs".
- **V / R3 cycles.** Crown: "create a binding to flip between ally and enemy i.e cycle first click ally second click enemy third click off and hold to off aswell we can also make an option for them to choose their preference". So a tap opens Intel on the party (the featured character), the next tap jumps to the first enemy, the next closes; holding it closes at any time. Settings > Gameplay > **Intel key: Cycle / Open and close**, default Cycle, saved like the turn-view style.
- **Nothing covered.** While Intel is open the command menu, the corner hints plate and the enemy cards hide (none of them acts while Intel is open); the turn order stays, and the panel sits on the right under it. **Reference:** Expedition 33 hides the action UI on its target close-up.
- **Build defaults (Crown may change):** a hold is 0.5 s, and a tap acts on release so it can be told from a hold; on an enemy's page (reached by Q / E too), or with Open and close, a tap closes; with no enemy left, the second tap closes. The footer names what the next tap does.

### D-261 — An Intel panel for the party and enemies; the B, S, D squares leave the player's card
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22, on the third draft) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown, on the approved menu drawing) · **Blocks:** B-586; the status badges of B-436b
**Answer (Crown):** "yes though i think the tabs should be merged into one for simplicity also seras tabe or whoever our character is would say you"
**Ruled:** Intel is built as the third draft (the points below), the B, S, D squares leave the player's cards, and: **one panel with one row of tabs**, the party first and then the enemies (read from "merged into one"; both sides are capped at 3, so at most six); **your own character's tab reads "You"** (its heading keeps the character's name; another co-op player's tab keeps the name). The picture is `Design/screen_mockups/combat/hud/refine_final/13_intel.png`; its numbers are examples.
**Crown:** "im also thinking we remove the MBS for players i dont think theres a need that would be for somewhere else matter of fact i think ff13 does something well where they have Inspect Enemy Intel which we could have something similar i.e it allows us to see our parties intel buffs debufss etc"
**Brain's reading (Crown said yes to the draft built on it):** "MBS" is the row of three squares under the card's bars. In the drawing they are B, S, D: example status badges (buffs and debuffs), not Mind, Body, Spirit. The draft takes them off the player's card and puts both things in Intel, so either meaning has a place.
**Proposed:** (1) B-581's inspect grows into an Intel panel in this game's own menu frame, with a tab per character. (2) A party member's page: bars, Spirit / Body, Pillars, Stats, Effects with turns left. (3) An enemy's page: bars, weaknesses, resistances, Effects; what isn't learned yet shows "?".
**Changes (Crown, on the first draft, 2026-11-22):** "mind, body, spirit should just read as pillars", "buffs and debuffs should just be called effects", "we should also have a stats one to see how the stats have been affected undeer pillars". So: the headings read **Pillars** and **Effects** (one list, on both pages), and a **Stats** section sits under Pillars.
**How Stats shows it:** the Character sheet's eleven stats; one that an effect changed is green (better) or red (worse) and reads "before → now", where "before" is without the fight's effects. Checked 2026-11-22 by reading the code: `GetStatLayers` can give that "before" (the layers composed with `TransientFactor` at 1) for ten of the eleven; Resistance isn't in it and needs its own route.
**Amounts (Crown, on the second draft, 2026-11-22):** "the effects should show us by how much the effects are impacting them". So every effect reads name, amount, turns left, on both pages: a stat effect its percent ("+50%"; Stats shows the result, 12% → 18%), damage or healing over time its HP a turn ("-7 HP a turn"), and an effect with no number what it does in short (Stun: "can't act"; that wording is proposed).
**The cost:** with no badge on the card a debuff, a stun included, isn't seen until Intel is opened.
**How an enemy's details are learned, and the keys:** Q-586's defaults.
**Reference:** Final Fantasy XIII's enemy info screen opens on one button in a fight and fills its unknown entries as they are learned (https://www.gamerguides.com/final-fantasy-xiii/introduction/tutorials/libra-screen). The idea only, not its layout or look.

### D-260 — The action pips move to the command menu, which is one frame with Act attached on top
**Status:** ANSWERED, the place, the grouping and the look (Crown, 2026-11-22) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown) · **Blocks:** B-436g
**Answer (Crown):** "im thinking why dont we have it on the action menu instead?"; then, shown a drawing with the pips above Attack: "this works though i think we should group the command menu in one frame then we can have the acts above it attached but somewhat seperate if that makes sense maybe show me some other menus to see how it looks i.e the skills items etc"
**Ruled:** (1) B-436g moves D-259's pips from the card to the command menu. (2) The menu's rows share one frame, and Act sits on top of it, attached but set apart.
**Until B-436g:** the card shows the pips (B-581), so no build lacks an action count.
**The look (Crown, on the drawings: "yes i like how it looks also for skills we dont need the population number thats what seting the skills is for"):** Act as a narrower tab on the frame's top edge, on every sub-menu too; "Skills 1/3" in that tab; a cost drawn as pips at a row's right end; the same frame for Skills, Items and targeting. The pictures are `Design/screen_mockups/combat/hud/refine_final/11_menu_frame.png` and `12_menu_frame_submenus.png`; their names, costs and item counts are examples.
**No skill counts:** the Skills kinds show their names only, since the loadout is where skills are set. Read as that count alone: an item's count and "Skills 1/3" (skills used this turn) stay as drawn.

### D-259 — The fight card's actions are pips: "Act: ●●○"
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown, from the fight card's screenshot) · **Blocks:** B-581
**Answer (Crown):** "and the actions shoudld we not just turn them into pips? i.e Act: * * * for example"
**Ruled:** the card reads "Act: ●●○": a filled pip for each action held, an empty one up to Max, and no numbers. This replaces D-254's "Act: 0/2" and is part of B-581, before the command menu (D-258).
**Defaults (Crown vetoes):** (a) actions above Max are one ◆ each ("Act: ●●●◆"), as inspect draws them (Q-170f3). (b) Past 8 pips (`MAX_PIPS_SHOWN`) the card falls back to the number ("Act: 9/3"). (c) "Skills 1/3" still joins once a skill is used (Q-170j). (d) Inspect is unchanged: pips, the number, Max and progress.

### D-258 — The command menu is built next: the main menu only, ahead of the skill screens
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown: "also should we not create the proper command menu?") · **Blocks:** B-436g
**Answer (Crown):** "Yes, menu next", to the brain's proposal:
1. B-436g (the main menu of `refine_final/1_main_menu.png`: Attack, Skills, Items as big buttons) moves ahead of B-436b to f and follows B-581; the camera rules (B-583) come after it.
2. The skill screens stay parked (B-436e, f, h; B-582).
3. Flee, End Turn and Step Forward / Back are small corner hints like Defend (this answers the plan's open point 2).

**Defaults, from building it without the skill screens (Crown vetoes):**
- (a) Skills opens today's lists: straight in with one kind, else a short list of the kinds the character has.
- (b) Switch weapon and Change ring are corner hints too, with today's keys, until B-436i and the skill screens replace them.
- (c) On a gamepad RB is Defend on the main menu only; past it RB stays infusion level until B-436j moves that to LB. So the level can't be preset from the main menu on a gamepad.
- (d) No new keys for End Turn or the steps (End Turn's key "comes later", Crown 2026-11-12): their hints are clicked, and Flee keeps 5 / D-pad Down.
- (e) A button shows only the key of the device in use, as every other menu does (B-481).

### D-257 — The status bar looks like HP and EP; a filled slot is its icon, then its name
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown) · **Blocks:** B-585
**Answer (Crown):** "status shouldnt say status" · "the status bar should be similar to the ep and hp bar" · "just put 0/max (whatever the value is)" · "also shouldnt the icon be before the text and also we dont need it to say off hand augment etc when its been attached"
**Read as:** (1) the build-up bar has no word and no line under it: it is as tall as the HP bar, with current/max on it ("0/100"). This replaces B-503m's quiet "Status" label. (2) In the loadout a piece's own icon comes before its name, and a filled slot has no slot word beside it.
**Defaults (Crown vetoes):** (a) a small party card shows the number too, as its HP bar does (the mockup had a thin bar with none). (b) An enemy's bar matches its HP bar, so no number (Q-391). (c) "etc" covers every filled slot, Main hand included; an empty slot keeps its word. (d) An entry's socket badges stay after the entry's name. (e) The top-centre Status button keeps its word.

### D-256 — The silver frame means slotted: a loose crystal is the gem alone; Iolite gets its icon
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown) · **Blocks:** B-584
**Answer (Crown):** "illodfite should have the same icon as the rest also as crystals they shouldnt have the silver fram around them since that means they are sloted"
**Read as:** a crystal in no socket (pouch, shop, socket picker) is drawn without the frame; in a socket it keeps it. Iolite (Reality) gets a diamond like the other crystals.
**Defaults (Crown vetoes):** (a) an evolution follows the same rule, since it uses the same badge. (b) An adapter keeps its frame (D-229), and so does an innate element on the hub's chips. (c) Iolite's gem is the Void gem tinted Reality's indigo (#5A2ED4, D-024).

### D-255 — Camera rules: a limit up and down, and less control once an opponent is engaged
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22: less control at engagement); the numbers and the still character are defaults Crown can veto · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown) · **Blocks:** B-583
**Answer (Crown):** "also we need to investigate camera rules as idk it seems like its not production quality also when we engage with a opponent the camera is still controlable so it can fly around everywhere which i dont mind it having some control but not as much as weve given it".
**Found in play:** in the hub and trial the pitch limits are the engine's -89.9 / +89.9; looking up takes the camera to the floor under the character. In a fight the turn and attack views did not answer to the mouse (defence and other shots not tried).
**Found in code:** nothing limits look or move during the 2 second window after contact. The shards are a still picture over the live camera. How far the camera turns in that window was not conclusive in play.
**References:** [Pignole, third-person camera design](https://gamedeveloper.com/design/third-person-camera-design-with-free-move-zone): a capped pitch, and collision that comes before every other rule. [Buehler, common camera problems](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/third-person-camera-view-in-games-a-record-of-the-most-common-problems-in-modern-games-solutions-taken-from-new-and-retro-games): Batman Arkham Asylum frames and centres the camera when a fight starts. Neither gives numbers, and no readable source on Expedition 33's camera limits was found, so every number below is ours, worked out from this game's own camera.
**Defaults (Crown vetoes):** (a) Hub and trial look at most 10 degrees up and 55 down. With the 550 arm, the 70 socket and a standard capsule, 10 up puts the camera about 60 cm off flat ground; about 16 is the floor. (b) From contact to the fight the look moves at most 30 degrees either side and 10 up or down from where it pointed, inside (a). (c) The engaged player's character stops taking movement input for that window; allies can still join. (d) If the fight doesn't start, all of it is given back. (e) The fight's own camera stays as it is. (f) Distance, field of view, lag and speed of turning are unchanged.
**Risk:** 10 up is tight for a tall enemy or a ledge. If play shows that, raise the camera's pivot and then the limit; don't raise the limit alone.

### D-254 — Fight cards: the party row, no Spirit / Body in a fight (click to inspect), "Act: 0/2"
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22), the row's position included · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown, from a screenshot of a fight) · **Blocks:** B-436a, B-581
**Answer (Crown):** "question why is the players like this  it doesnt match our design where they wre horizontal in the bottom right also it shouldnt show spirit of body off etc in matches thats more of an options detail i.e click a player and inspect"; then "actions could be act we also dont need max 2 we could just have a value i.e act: 0/2".
**Ruled:** (1) the party shows as a horizontal row, as designed. (2) No Spirit / Body lines on a fight's cards; they are details shown by clicking a player to inspect. (3) The actions line reads "Act: 0/2".
**Changes:** D-241 for the battle card (it put both lines there), D-216's "battle: everything" by those two lines, and the card's wording from Q-170f3 / Q-170j / ET-18. The rules behind them are unchanged.
**Why the game differed (found 2026-11-22):** B-436a built the row on 2026-11-13 on `feature/battle-hud-refine` (`541c9c97f`), then waited for Crown's PIE pass and was never merged; `dev` kept the B-391 placeholder, and D-241 added the two lines to it on 2026-11-17. That branch also holds the newest `refine_final` mockups and plan; the copies in `dev` are older.
**Position (Crown, 2026-11-22: "As the picture"):** as the newest `refine_final/1_main_menu.png`: the acting character's big card bottom left, the others in a row to its right along the bottom. This settles the "bottom right" in the first message. **Defaults (Crown vetoes):** (b) The action-point progress ("+0/100") moves to inspect. (c) Actions above Max keep a mark, as Q-170f3 asks: "Act: 3/2 ◆", explained in inspect. (d) "Skills 2/3" still joins the line once a skill is used, as Q-170j asks. (e) Inspect reuses the Status panel, for the clicked character. (f) replaced by Crown the same day, below.
**Amended (Crown, 2026-11-22, from a screenshot of the trial screen):** "we also dont need spirit of generic body of nobody"; then, with a picture of another game's character page: "an example of where it would go is somewhere like or player profile".
**Ruled by that:** the hub and trial cards drop both lines too. They belong on the player's profile, which is the existing Character sheet (B-442); the picture says where they go and is not a layout to copy.
**Defaults for the amendment (Crown vetoes):** (g) the game menu's party list drops them as well. (h) The title's save slot keeps them (Crown's D-241). (i) A list keeps them under the name only when one player has two characters in it, to tell the two apart. (j) No new profile screen.
**Process:** under D-251 the B-436 pieces this fight needs pass by agent QA against the mockup; they no longer wait for Crown's PIE pass.

### Q-581 — How inspect opens and closes, and what the party list shows instead (D-254, built in B-581)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (B-581, from building it) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-254 says a click on a player inspects it in the Status panel, and that the party list drops Spirit / Body. It does not say how inspect closes, what the Status button does while one character is inspected, or what the party list's line reads then.
**Answer (defaults):**
1. **A click on a party card opens the Status panel on that character alone.** A click on the same card closes it; a click on another party card switches to that one. Enemy cards are not inspected (their HP is hidden by Q-391; targeting shows it, B-436c).
2. **The Status button is always the view of everyone.** While one character is inspected it switches to everyone; pressed again it closes.
3. **Inspect lists, in order:** the name, Spirit of ... and Body of ..., the full actions line as it was on the card (pips, the number, Max with the action-point progress, the turn's skills, and "N bonus, lost at turn end" above Max), then each status in full.
4. **Replaced by D-259 (Crown, the same day):** the card draws pips and no numbers ("Act: ●●○"), one ◆ for each action above Max ("Act: ●●●◆"), as inspect does. (This default was one ◆ on a numbers line, "Act: 4/2 ◆".)
5. **The party list reads the element and the class under every name,** a player character's included, as a recruit's already did (the class alone with no innate element). The Character sheet keeps Spirit of ... / Body of ....
**Reference:** Expedition 33 and Persona 5 open a character's detail from its own portrait or card and close it with the same input or Back; a second press on the same thing closing it is the common toggle. From memory, not looked up. The deckbuilder has no inspect.
**Not built, say if wanted:** a key or pad button for inspect (comes with B-436h); closing inspect by clicking empty ground.
**Built:** B-581.

### Q-579 — The potions' price, and what can't be done with a potion (D-253, built in B-579)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (B-579) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-253 gives the potions' strength and says they are sold for less than a tier F Sapphire or Citrine. It doesn't give the price, or say whether a potion can be sold, dismantled, merged or socketed.
**Answer (defaults):**
1. **A potion costs half a tier F crystal's Prisms price, and no essence.** Today that is 12 Prisms, against 25 Prisms and 10 Elemental Essence for a tier F Sapphire or Citrine. The price follows the tier F price if that changes.
2. **A potion can't be sold or dismantled.** Its price has no essence in it, so dismantling one would turn Prisms into essence. The vendor says "A potion can't be sold or dismantled".
3. **A potion can't be merged and fits no socket.** It has one strength, so there is no tier to merge up to (D-253 says no tiers and not socketable).
4. **One stack per potion.** A potion made at any tier is stored as the same Health Potion or Energy Potion.
5. **It can be used on any living target, an enemy too,** as every other item can. The fight menu asks Allies or Enemies as it does for the others.
6. **The AI's energy item search finds the Energy Potion as well as a Citrine.** Nothing was changed for that: it looks for an item that gives energy.
**Reference:** Final Fantasy and Expedition 33 sell their basic potions and tints for the common money only, cheaper than their rarer restoratives (from memory, not looked up). The deckbuilder has no potion.
**Not built here:** which merchants stock potions. That is written in each merchant's asset, and this job changed no asset. The five merchants that already sell crystals should get the two rows: `DA_Merchant_Blacksmith`, `DA_Merchant_Jewler`, `DA_Merchant_CombatMaster`, `DA_Merchant_Refractor`, `DA_Merchant_Evolutionist` (the list in `MerchantShopSystem.md`, not read from the assets). Until then potions come from the test kit.
**Built:** B-579.

### Q-580 — What exactly death takes, and what can land on the dead (D-253, built in B-580)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (B-580, from building it and its review) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-253 says death takes buffs and debuffs, and that what is part of the character stays. Building it needed the edges.
**Answer (defaults):**
1. **Part of the character is a permanent effect from its own gear** (weapon, adapter, apparel, the innate evolution; a boss's or a gate's passives). Everything else goes: every timed effect, every effect a skill or an item gave even if it has no end, shields, wards and barriers, and action changes waiting for a turn.
2. **A timed buff the character's own gear gave goes too, and a revive does not give it back.** A gear or reaction effect written with a number of turns is a buff; one that should always be there has to be written as permanent.
3. **Nothing lands on the dead.** An effect that would not survive a death is refused on a dead character, and so is status buildup. So the debuff a killing blow carries, an on-hit burn and its buildup do not show on the corpse.
4. **Every item but a Sapphire is refused on a dead character, and is not spent.** D-253 ruled this for the Healing Stone; it is the same for all, so a mis-click on a corpse costs nothing. A Sapphire is refused on a dead enemy.
5. **Sapphire's death-save takes every buff the holder has,** another Last Stand from a skill included. A permanent Last Stand from gear stays. Another Last Stand's own save takes nothing and is exactly as it was.
6. **A debuff carried by the very hit that triggered Sapphire's save lands after the save,** so the saved character keeps that one: the save clears what was held at that moment.
7. **The hit that kills is still reflected** (Q-032 stands): the target's reflect is read before the damage.
**Reference:** Final Fantasy: a knocked-out character loses its buffs and most status effects, and a Phoenix Down brings it back with none. Pokémon: fainting clears stat changes. Both from memory, not looked up. The deckbuilder has no rule for a death that takes effects.
**Not built, say if wanted:** giving timed gear effects back on a revive; a cap on what one save can clear.
**Built:** B-580.

### Q-436a — Which party card is the big one when no party member is acting (D-254, built in B-436a)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (B-436a, from building it) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-254 puts the acting character's big card bottom left. It does not say which card is big on an enemy's turn, or before any party member has acted.
**Answer (defaults):**
1. **On an enemy's turn the big card stays on the party member that acted last,** so the cards do not move while the enemy acts. If that member is knocked out on an enemy's turn, the card stays big (dimmed) until another party member acts.
2. **Before any party member has acted, the first party member's card is the big one.**
3. **A small card shows the name, the HP bar with its number and the thin status bar,** as the picture and the plan's decision 2. EP, actions and status text are on the big card and in the Status panel.
**Reference:** a party panel that holds still on the enemy's turn is the usual way in turn-based fights (Persona 5's party column and Final Fantasy X's party list do not move when an enemy acts). From memory, not looked up. The deckbuilder has no party row.
**Built:** B-436a.

### Q-436g — The command menu's small points: greyed rows, Back, what a row shows, the frame's width (D-258, D-260, built in B-436g)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (B-436g, from building it) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-258 and D-260 (and the two pictures) set the menu. Building it left small points they don't settle.
**Answer (defaults):**
1. **A row that can't be used stays in its place, greyed, with its reason under the name** ("No items to use.", "Needs 3 actions."), so Attack, Skills and Items never move.
2. **Back goes one step** (a skill list to its kinds, the kinds to the main menu, a target screen to where it came from) **and is a corner hint, not a row.** It does nothing on the main menu. On the keyboard Backspace and Esc both work; the hint shows Backspace.
3. **Only a greyed row shows a description.** The kinds show no counts (D-260), the schools no "N spells", and the ring's break-chance line and Defend's "ends your turn" are no longer on screen (Defend and End Turn are hints).
4. **The frame is the full card's width as it is today** (`BattleCardStyle`, also the hub's and trial's card). The pictures draw the card bigger; that is the card's own piece (B-436b), and the frame follows it. Names wrap rather than overflow.
5. **The keys use the gamepad's letters** (A, B, X, Y, LB, RB, D-pad): the pictures' "R1" is RB. Only the device in use shows, switching live.
6. **The infusion controls stay small clickable rows at the foot of the target list,** with their keys ("Infusion: Weapon crystal Lv 1"), as picture 12. They become the tab rows in B-436j / B-436k.
7. **A row turns violet under the mouse and for the highlighted target.** There is no keyboard cursor on the rows yet (the rows take no focus, Q-391).
8. **Names are white;** the schools' colours on their names are dropped, as the pictures.
9. **Items 4 and D-pad up are free** now that Items is 3 / Y (InputMap).
**Reference:** Persona 5 and Final Fantasy X keep an unusable command in its place, greyed, and Expedition 33 shows each skill's cost beside it; a back button that steps one list at a time is the norm in both. From memory, not looked up. The deckbuilder has no command menu.
**Built:** B-436g.

### D-253 — Potions; death takes buffs and debuffs; the Healing Stone counts in a Sapphire revival
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown, answering Q-577 and the Sapphire question) · **Blocks:** B-577, B-579, B-580
**Answer (Crown):** "okay lets add potions and enegy potions which will be less powerfull then sapphire and citrine crystals also healing stone can only be used on alive as when you die you loose buffs and debuffs while healingstone ffect is included with sappire revival you still loose all buffs and debuffs including the healing stone one"
**Ruled:** (1) a health potion and an energy potion, each weaker than Sapphire and Citrine. (2) The Healing Stone is used only on the living. (3) Death takes all buffs and debuffs. (4) The Healing Stone's boost counts in a Sapphire revival, and is then lost with the rest. Sapphire keeps its death-save and revive.
**Read from Crown's words (veto if wrong):** (a) "Sapphire revival" is both the revive and the death-save: each is raised by the Healing Boost held at that moment (for a revive, the moment of death; it is remembered for that one number only), then buffs and debuffs are gone. Last Stands from other sources are unchanged. (b) What is part of the character is not a buff and stays: permanent gear and innate effects, and auras still being given.
**Defaults (approved by the reference-backed default; Crown vetoes):** (c) Health Potion 25% of max HP, Energy Potion 25% of max EP: one strength, no tiers, no element, no status build-up, not socketable; under Sapphire's 30% revive and the weakest Citrine's 30%. (d) The stone boosts the Health Potion, not the Energy Potion. A character who can't gain EP from a Citrine gains none from the potion. (e) Both are fight items (picked, at most 10 a fight), used on the living, sold by the merchant for less than an F Sapphire or Citrine, and in the test kit. (f) The AI's low-HP heal becomes the Health Potion. (g) Death empties status bars too, as a fight's end does (ET-53). (h) Sapphire revives dead allies, not dead enemies.
**Reference:** Expedition 33 keeps separate Healing, Energy and Revive Tints, the Healing Tint starting at 30% (https://bo3.gg/games/articles/how-to-get-all-tint-upgrades-in-clair-obscur-expedition-33 and https://maxroll.gg/clair-obscur-expedition-33/guides/getting-started-in-clair-obscur-expedition-33-beginners-guide, from search summaries). 25% sits under that because Crown wants potions weaker than the crystals. The deckbuilder has no potion rule; its Revive is a fixed 1 HP, where this game stays at 30%.
**Found in the code (read, not played):** death clears no effects today; and a Sapphire revive can't happen in a fight, because `CombatCommandMenuSubsystem::ResolveTargets` lists only the living and `ActionExecutor::ValidateAction` refuses a dead target. Both are B-580.

### D-252 — The Healing Stone increases the amount healed instead of giving HP
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (Crown) · **Blocks:** B-577
**Answer (Crown):** "lets also change what a healingstone does lets make it so it increases the amount healed rather than gives you health".
**Defaults (approved by the reference-backed default; Crown vetoes):** (1) it is still used up in a fight on any target and gives that target the existing Healing Boost (`HealingReceivedBuff`): the healing it receives is increased. No HP on use, and still inert when socketed. (2) Size: today's tier numbers read as percent more healing (F 15, E 20, D 25, C 30, B 35, A 45, S 60). (3) Length: the shared consumable curve (F and E 4 turns, D to B 3, A and S 2). (4) The AI stops using it as its low-HP heal. No new AI scoring.
**Reference:** Final Fantasy XIV's Mantra and Krasis are timed boosts to the healing a character receives (from memory, not looked up). The deckbuilder's reference has no Healing Stone rule.
**Consequence:** nothing in the game uses this stone as a heal any more, and milestone 1 planned it as the likely potion: Q-577.

### Q-577b — Do Healing Stones add up?
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (B-577, from the review) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-252 says what one Healing Stone does. It doesn't say what a second one does while the first still lasts.
**Answer (default):** as every other item effect already does (ET-39, ET-41, ET-42). The **same tier from the same user refreshes** it: the turns start again, the percent does not grow. A **different tier, or the same tier from another user, adds its own percent** (F and E together: 35%). Nothing was built for this; it is what the effect rules give, and a spec holds it.
**Reference:** Final Fantasy XIV: two different healing-received buffs on one target both count, and the same buff applied again only refreshes (from memory, not looked up).
**To know:** the largest reachable boost is one user with every tier (15 + 20 + 25 + 30 + 35 + 45 + 60 = 230%), at the cost of seven turns and seven stones. Say if a cap or strongest-only is wanted.
**Built:** B-577.

### Q-575 — The fight-item pick (D-250 part 1): what can be picked, where a recruit's items go, and the pick in the save
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (B-575) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-250 rules no carry limit and a pick of up to the item slots. It doesn't say whether a stone can be picked, where a leaving recruit's items go now there is no storage, what happens to the pick when a stack runs out, or how the pick is saved.
**Answer (defaults):** (1) **Anything with a use can be picked (corrected 2026-11-22, Crown: "Didn't we make stones items too?"):** every gem, the Healing Stone and every stat stone (D-097: crystals and stones are consumables). Only the Durability Stone can't, because it is attach-only. The same stack can still be socketed instead. The first draft said gems only; that was wrong. **This adds to dev:** before B-575 a party member's fight list hid every stone, so a carried Healing Stone could not be used in a fight. Reference: Elden Ring's quick pouch takes any item that can be used. (2) **A leaving recruit's items go to the host** (the party leader), the same place D-250 sends an old chest. (3) **A stack that runs out leaves the pick;** the next new kind takes the free slot. A kind that comes back after running out counts as new: it is picked by itself again while a slot is free, even if it was unpicked by hand before. (4) **A bought slot starts empty,** for the player to fill. (5) **Ten a fight is counted per fight:** each fight brings up to 10 of a picked item again while more is carried. (6) **The pick is saved with the pouch** (save version 45), hand-made changes and pick order included. Q-250 (Crown: B): no converter; a save from before is wiped with the usual warning. (7) **An apparel socket's old crystal** goes back to the piece's owner (was storage, Q-380d).
**Not changed (say if wanted):** the evolution limit of 5 counts worn evolutions too, so it is a rule on owning them, not a carry room. The chest and the old Store / Take buttons still work until part 3.
**Until part 2's screen:** the pick is changed with the console command `wor.Inventory.SetFightItem` (numbers from `wor.Inventory.PrintSlots`).
**Built:** B-575.

### Q-250 — D-250 changes the save: convert the old save once, or wipe it as D-138 says?
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-22: B, start fresh) · **Raised:** 2026-11-22 (the brain, starting checkpoint 2) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-250 part 1 saves the fight-item pick, so the save goes from version 44 to 45. D-138 (Crown, 2026-11-03) says a bump wipes older saves and no migration code is written until the first public build. D-250's default says an old save's chest contents go to the party leader. Both can't hold.
**Options:** **A** convert once, 44 to 45 only (the chest to the leader, the pouch stacks picked): an exception to D-138, and the rule stands after it. **B** follow D-138: bump the version and the minimum together; every existing save starts over, with the warning.
**Recommendation:** A, so Crown's characters survive. Reference: Stardew Valley carries old saves into its big updates. B is less code.
**Correction:** the brain told the Code Writer to build A earlier without asking Crown. That go is withdrawn until Crown answers; nothing of it was built.
**Decided by the reference-backed default (Crown vetoes), not part of the question:** a picked item keeps the existing cap of 10 per item in a fight; the rest stays carried. Reference: Elden Ring's held and stored counts.
**Answer (Crown):** "It's fine if it affects waves because it's still in development" ("waves" read as saves, the question asked). **B:** D-138 stands. The version and the minimum go up together, older saves are wiped with the warning, no converter is written.

### D-250 — No chest: each character carries everything; one Inventory list; only the fight items are limited
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-20: "sounds good") · not built · **Raised:** 2026-11-20, from Crown's PIE look at D-249 piece 1
**Answer (Crown):** "we could merge gear and pouch ... since crystals and stones count as gear", then "remove the chest and just allow them to carry as much as they want since they can only bring in a set amount in combat". So: (1) the Inventory screen is the character tabs, then **one Inventory list** per character (weapons, adapters, crystals, stones, evolutions, apparel, items), sorted by the filter chips; Gear / Pouch / Chest stop being tabs. (2) **No chest and no carry limit** out of a fight. (3) **The fight items are the only limit:** each character picks up to its item slots (10; the Jeweler still sells more, CL-13, B-239) to take into fights, shown as a counter on the list. (4) Give between characters stays.
**Approved by the reference-backed default (Crown vetoes):** an old save's chest contents go to the party leader (dropped by Q-250, 2026-11-22: old saves are wiped instead); the chest code is hidden and flagged OBSOLETE, deleted only after Crown's PIE pass; runs are unchanged (ER-64: a run sees only what it brought, the rest waits at home). Reference: Elden Ring (carry everything, a 10-slot quick pouch for fights), Expedition 33, Persona and Final Fantasy (no storage boxes); players like not juggling storage.
**Changes:** D-249 (the Chest tab and Give / Store piece 3 become Give only); ER-64b and the Q-441 chest rules (no chest to refuse); the pouch's carry role (CL-13) becomes the fight-item pick. Also in the job: pictures for the types that show text (Greatsword, Shield, Gun).
