### D-247 — Bows and guns use no ammunition: the arrow or bullet is part of the attack
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-18: "agreed") · not built · **Raised:** 2026-11-18, after D-246 was dropped
**Answer:** no arrow or bullet items and no ammunition count. A bow's or gun's attack plays its own draw-and-shoot animation and flies an arrow or bullet model to the target, the way a spell flies its bolt. Effects on the shot come from what the weapon already carries: the crystal or stone in its socket and infusion (a fire crystal in a bow gives fire arrows).
**Reference:** Expedition 33, Octopath Traveler and Persona keep ranged attacks free of ammunition; well liked for keeping turn-based fights about choices, not stock.
**Today:** test bows (`DA_Weapon_FalconerBow`, `DA_Weapon_MechanicalBow`) and guns (`DA_Weapon_HunterGun`, `DA_Weapon_HunterGun2`) exist with no Weapon Attack, so they use the unarmed attack. **To build:** a bow attack and a gun attack (animation, projectile model, hit timing).

### D-246 — Ammunition is an augment: arrows and bullets go where stones go
**Status:** DROPPED (Crown, 2026-11-18: "actually ignore that idea theres no point") · nothing built · **Raised:** 2026-11-18, while planning a weapon test set after the D-242 grip work
**Answer (Crown):** first: "bow main hand arrow off hand then we can add some effects to it" and "so arrow becomes a weapon type and so does bullets". Then changed: "we can make arrows and bullets an augment type i.e where stones go". So **arrows and bullets are a kind of augment**, set in a Bow's or Gun's stone socket like an augment stone, and they are where ammunition effects sit. They are **not** a weapon type and not held in the off hand.
**Today:** augment stones sit in a weapon's stone socket (`AttachedItem` kind AugmentStone, `ECrystalType` stone sub-types); `EWeaponType` has Bow and Gun; no bow, gun, arrow or bullet data or model is in the game yet.
**Open:** which effects ammunition carries and how they stack with the weapon's; whether ammunition is used up or kept; whether a Bow takes only arrows and a Gun only bullets, and whether they can also take normal stones; whether ammunition shows on the model (a quiver?).

### D-245 — What a new character picks at the start goes into its inventory
**Status:** ANSWERED in outline (Crown, 2026-11-18) · built 2026-11-18 (starting skills owned from the start; Q-245 holds the defaults), not yet seen in PIE · **Raised:** 2026-11-18, from Crown's PIE pass of a new Generic
**Answer (Crown):** "when we pick our starting gear/spells we should put it in inventory on a new character". So the weapons, rings and spells picked on the new-game screen (D-228) are **owned by the new character and show in its inventory** from the first moment in the hub, not only worn or only handed over at trial entry.
**What happens today (log, 2026-11-18):** the new character's inventory starts with 0 weapons, 0 rings, 0 spells; its starting gear is minted and worn (D-223, 2 pieces) and the hub pawn "draws the shared armoury"; the picked weapons and the starting spell are given when a trial is entered (D-221 class kit, D-224 run skills). The Code Writer is to report exactly where each pick lives today before anything is changed.
**Open (Crown):** whether the picks are also worn at once or only owned; whether they sit in the character's own inventory or the shared pool (D-219 / D-221); whether the trial-entry kit is then made from the inventory.

### D-244 — A melee weapon's basic attack goes to the enemy; a leap is the look wanted later
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-18) · not built · **Raised:** 2026-11-18, from Crown's PIE pass of the Generic in the trial
**Answer (Crown):** asked whether basic attacks should run to the enemy or stay in place: "yeah for melee weapons at least". And: "ideally i would like a leap for the movement but thats for another day". So a **melee weapon's basic attack travels to its target**; ranged weapons and spells are not ruled on. The travel is a **leap** in the end, not built now. This changes the old default that a standard attack stays in place (CLAUDE.md, Animations): in place is now the default only where no travel is ruled.
**What Crown saw:** the Generic's sword attack (`DA_Sword_Attack_LateralCut`, montage `AN_KwangAttack_C`) played on the spot. The Code Writer's findings: the only travel today is Motion Warping, and only a montage with a warp window on `WarpTarget` moves; the Kwang montage has none. The dash, strike and hop back Crown passed on 2026-11-13 (B-424, `DA_SAS_Attack_LightHit`) belonged to the old baked `DA_Equipment_Generic_SAS`; since the class kits (D-221 / D-223) the Generic holds `DA_Weapon_Sword`, whose attack is LateralCut. A sword and shield pair attacks as the main hand alone (B-515).
**Options on the table (Code Writer):** (A) data: make the three LightHit montages play on the shared skeleton (D-242) and set `DA_Weapon_Sword`'s Weapon Attack to `DA_SAS_Attack_LightHit`; (B) data: a warp window and an Impact notify on the Kwang montage, which only moves if the clip has root motion there; (C) code: a step-in and step-back for any melee basic whose montage has no window. Reference: Expedition 33 and Persona 5 both send the attacker to the target for a melee strike and back again; widely liked for making hits read.
**For now the roll (Crown, 2026-11-18):** "only if it looks good"; "for now just keep the roll we used"; and "we don't want to stretch to leap, let's just leave it for now, we use the roll until I find the one I like". So option A is the one built: the B-424 Light Hit travel (the step, the roll or hop to the enemy, the strike, the way back) that Crown passed on 2026-11-13 goes back on the sword, and Crown looks at it before the sword is switched. The existing clip is **not** stretched or dressed up to pass as a leap. The leap waits until Crown finds a clip they like; nothing is built for it.
**Open:** which leap clip, when Crown finds one; whether enemies follow the same rule.

### D-243 — A weapon skill multiplies the weapon's Power; it no longer adds its own damage to it
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-17: "yes go with multiplied") · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 · **Blocks:** the numbers of the six skills per weapon (D-242)
**Answer (Crown):** an attack or ability used through a weapon deals **the weapon's part times the skill's multiple** (standard attack x1, a heavy skill x2), like Expedition 33's skills against a weapon's Power, "but we'd use lower, controllable numbers": Power stays on the present ladder (F 40 to S 192, D-101). **This changes D-101 / ED-43**, where the weapon part is added to the skill's own damage (the deckbuilder's flat weapon bonus), so the two games now differ here on purpose.
**Why:** added, a strong weapon drowns the skill out (at S, Power 192, a 40-damage skill is 232 against a standard 212); multiplied, a x2 skill is always double a standard attack, and the player reads it at a glance. References: Expedition 33 (weapon Power with scaling grades, skills as multiples); Monster Hunter's motion values (every move a percentage of the weapon's attack).
**Not decided / to be planned before code:** how the skill's tier power (F x1.00 to S x4.80), the tier gap (ED-11), infusion's share and status build-up sit with the multiple; turning the 225 existing weapon skills' base damage (all single hit, single target, no effect; basics about 20, abilities 20 to 95) into multiples; the tests that hold the added maths. Spells are not affected (no weapon part). Not built; a job of its own after the trial pool stages.

### D-242 — Placeholders first; the Paragon animations are the templates for each weapon type
**Status:** ANSWERED in outline (Crown, 2026-11-17); nothing built · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** "with bodies and everything else we just need placeholders, to see how it would look, find issues we may come across, and standardise themes, code etc." And: "we should use the Paragon assets as templates for the future, which we can tune when we get our own assets; we just have to plug and play". So each weapon type gets a **template** built from what the project already owns (a weapon model from the owned packs, attack animations from the matching Paragon hero, already fitted to Manny), set up so that final art later drops into the same template without code changes. **To look into first:** "how dual weapons and sword and shield would work" under such templates.
**Pairs (Crown, 2026-11-17):** option B of the shortlist's section 5: a pair that has a real animation set of its own (sword and shield, two swords, two daggers, two guns, two maces, gauntlets) gets a **named pair template**; the rest (two axes until their clips are fixed, mixed pairs) use the main hand's template with the off hand carried. **A pair can use the main hand's moves as well** as its pair moves. **"Don't forget we will be turning these into skills that the player can choose from"**: the moves in a template, single or pair, become skills the player picks, so a template is a source of skills, not a fixed move list.
**The standard attack of a pair (Crown, same day):** "we should use the main hand, unless the shield has an override; that way we can add sword and shield attacks". A pair's standard attack is the main hand's (D-215), **unless the off-hand piece carries an override**, which then replaces it. So a shield (or any off-hand piece) can bring its own paired attack, and a pair with no override simply swings the main hand. (The code already has an unused `OverrideAttack` on the weapon entry, `FWeaponLoadoutEntry.h:53-57`; whether it fits is for the build.)
**The general rule (Crown, same day):** "essentially all weapons will have a universal default, but we will also include an override". Every weapon type has **one universal default** (its template: the standard attack and stance every weapon of that type uses), and **any single piece can carry an override** that replaces the default for that piece, alone or in a pair.
**Paragon skins and weapons as cosmetics (Crown, same day):** only the animations are on this PC; the free hero packs "come with different weapons and skins, so that can come under our cosmetics aspect". A hero pack's skins and weapon looks are candidates for the Cosmetics side (the mirror's third option, D-234), not for gear. What the packs hold is to be checked online before any is added.
**Paragon is the reference (Crown, same day):** "the reason I want to switch to Paragon is because they have been tried and proven"; "so if we can get them to work, all we need to do is tweak everything else to fit". **How a Paragon hero holds its weapon is the standard**; every weapon model (the StylizedWeapons ones included) and every body is lined up to it once, not the other way round. This replaces the draft rule in `Design/GearAndBodyStandard.md` that took the StylizedWeapons pattern as the standard. The grip cannot be measured from this PC (the Manny copies of the animations carry no weapon bones): one free hero pack has to be added and its weapon's place in the hand measured first.
**Rulebook questions answered (Crown, same day; `Design/GearAndBodyStandard.md` section 11):** (1, 2) when both pieces of a pair carry an override, **the off hand's wins**, and that is how a pair gets its own attack: "for example dual blades", the second blade brings the two-blade attack. (4) **Rings in a fight are a glow, not a model**, and **each finger stands for one slot** ("we should set each finger to represent a slot"), so a Resonator's five adapters are five fingers. (5) An unlocked body **keeps its own look**; it does not take the element colour. (6) A weapon that is put away is **hidden**. (7) Each template starts with **a small first set: one standard attack and three skills**; more are added later. (8) **A scythe can simply be a staff**: "we don't need to type it as scythe if that's the case, or maybe we just use the same skills but change the defense point". So a scythe shares the staff's template and skills; what may differ is the defence it strikes (brain's reading: the physical damage type, Slash against the staff's Impact; to confirm). The spear borrows the staff's moves the same way until it has its own. Question 3 (does every shield bring the paired attack) is still with Crown.
**Scythe, made clear (Crown, 2026-11-17; replaces the brain's damage-type reading of point 8):** the scythe starts from the staff's movements, but each move is **looked at for the scythe's shape**: "because the scythe has a blade it could look different"; "a wide swing with a staff would have a later delay than a scythe, as a scythe has more reach". So the two share the animations, and **the moment the hit lands (the "defense point", when the defender must react) is set per weapon type**, not once per animation. A longer-reaching weapon connects earlier in the same swing. Standard: a shared move carries its hit moment per weapon type that uses it; the same goes for the spear borrowing staff moves.
**Attack hand types (Crown, 2026-11-17; answers rulebook question 3 with "it depends"):** a shield may become a main weapon one day, so the shield question is not every-shield or set-up-only. Instead **each attack says which hand it uses: main hand, off hand, or dual (both)**. A piece brings the attacks that fit where it is held; a pair's own attack is a dual one. **Flipping (Crown, same day): mirror it.** One set of moves; a move made for one hand is mirrored by the engine when the other hand uses it, with one mirror setup per skeleton. How a mirrored move looks with a shield on the other arm is part of the attach-and-watch check.
**Hero packs read (2026-11-17; Kwang, Greystone, Sun Wukong added by Crown, kept out of git):** the weapon is **baked into each hero's body mesh** and driven by bones; no pack has a separate weapon mesh. Skins: Kwang 6, Greystone 5, Wukong 7. Kwang and Wukong hold the weapon on `weapon_r` under `hand_r`; Greystone uses his own `sword_bottom` bones and carries the shield on the forearm. **Crown: "don't bother for now"** about cutting a weapon out of a hero. So for placeholders our weapons come from StylizedWeapons, the heroes are used as whole bodies (Body of, cosmetics) and as the pattern for how a weapon is held, with **Kwang's hold as the reference** (brain's pick; Wukong matches it, Greystone does not). Not yet read: the offset of Kwang's `weapon_r` from `hand_r` (the connection has no bone tool). All three heroes' animations sit on their own skeletons and need retargeting to Manny.
**Six skills per weapon (Crown, 2026-11-17; replaces point 7's three):** each weapon type's template starts with **one standard attack and six skills**, picked from the hero pack's own moves (Kwang for the sword, Greystone for sword and shield, Sun Wukong for the staff). The picks are being chosen with Crown; the packs' attack and ability lists were read the same day (names and lengths only, nothing watched, hit moments not readable through the connection).
**The holds, measured (2026-11-17, `wor.Debug.PrintBoneOffset`, reference pose; location x, y, z then pitch / yaw / roll):** Kwang `weapon_r` from `hand_r`: -10.144, 5.902, 2.104; 16.348 / 51.134 / 109.024 (`weapon_l` from `hand_l` is its mirror; `weapon_backMount_r` from `spine_02`: -28.904, 2.131, 57.391; -90 / -158.321 / 0). Sun Wukong `weapon_r` from `hand_r`: -8.099, 3.863, -0.617; 17.026 / 59.274 / 109.680, within about 8 degrees of Kwang, so the two share one hold. Greystone `sword_bottom` from `hand_r`: -6.960, 2.908, 0; 0 / 0 / -10.651 (blade along +Z, a different convention); his `shield_inner` from `hand_l`: -14.486, -0.819, -0.160; 0.632 / 3.236 / 81.473. **Kwang's hold matches neither of our sword sockets** (Manny `hand_r_sword` -3.7, 2.65, -6.47; 10.9 / 89.24 / -67.42; Quinn's rotation -29.74 / 163.24 / -174.02): about 11 units apart, and the rotations are not like for like because Kwang's blade runs along the bone's -Y and the StylizedWeapons along +Y. So lining up to Kwang means a new socket on Manny and Quinn at Kwang's numbers, with the weapon turned for the axis; not done, and to be looked at by Crown in the editor.
**One sword hold (Crown, 2026-11-17: "we should use one sword type if anything"):** every sword is held one way, **Kwang's**, the sword in a sword-and-shield pair included. Greystone's own hold is not used; his moves are played with the sword in Kwang's hold, and how that looks is part of the attach-and-watch check.
**The grip on Manny, set by Crown (2026-11-17, "I am happy with that"):** one skeleton socket **`hand_r_grip`** on `hand_r`, fitted by eye in the mesh editor with the Beastslayer Sword and the twin-sword idle: location (-4.008, 3.069, -5.631), rotation (180, -20, 180) as the Details panel shows them. It landed beside the old `hand_r_sword` location (-3.7, 2.65, -6.47), not at Kwang's raw numbers: Manny's hand is not Kwang's, so what is kept from Paragon is the method (one hold per body, every weapon made to match), not the numbers. **The rule that follows (Crown):** the grip is set once per body, and **each weapon is then made to fit it**, so a weapon is only referenced, never rotated per use: "not all weapons will be in the same position" (the Adventurer's Spear sits backwards in the same grip). The **Beastslayer Sword is the reference weapon**; a weapon we use is copied into our own folder, its pivot moved to the handle and its direction turned to match the reference, and the game uses that copy (the vendor pack stays out of git). Only weapons in use are fixed. The old per-type sockets (`hand_r_sword`, `hand_r_axe`, `hand_r_greatsword`) stay until the new path is proven in PIE. Not done: Quinn's grip, the left-hand grip, any weapon fix, and code that attaches to `hand_r_grip`. Found on the way: the bodies and animation packs sit on about five skeletons, so a body's preview list shows only its own pack's animations; "one skeleton for every body" is an open point for the standard.
**One skeleton for every body (Crown, 2026-11-18, built):** `SKM_Manny_Base` now sits on Quinn's skeleton (`/Game/Imports/Big_Pack_Magic_VFX/Demo/Characters/Mannequins/Meshes/SK_Mannequin`), so Manny and Quinn share one skeleton and every animation on it plays on both. Manny's 89 bones all match by name and parent; its seven old hand sockets (`hand_r_sword`, `hand_r_axe`, `hand_r_greatsword`, `hand_l_sword`, `hand_l_axe`, `hand_l_sas`, `hand_l_greatsword`) are kept as mesh sockets with their old values until the attach code moves to the grip. Crown's `hand_r_grip` (on `hand_r`: location -4.008187, 3.069104, -5.630992; rotation 180, -20, 180 as Details shows it) is a skeleton socket on that one skeleton, so both bodies have it. Crown's PIE check: the Manny body shows, holds its weapon and reaches the trial fight. **Open:** the skeleton file is in a git-ignored vendor folder, so the grip socket exists only on Crown's PC; `hand_l_grip` is not made; the sword-and-shield attacks sit on other pack skeletons and the pool sword-and-shield basic has no montage; Quinn's mesh has none of the seven old sockets, so weapons hang at the hip on that body until the attach code uses the grip.
**The left grip, the shield points and the attach code (Crown, 2026-11-18):** Crown made `hand_l_grip` by eye, a skeleton socket on `hand_l` of the shared skeleton: location 4.15874, -7.489597, 2.571151; rotation -50, 0, -40 as Details shows it (pitch 0 / yaw -40 / roll -50). Both bodies read it. Crown also ruled two shield points, `hand_l_shield` and `hand_r_shield`, one per hand; **neither is made yet**. The code now attaches a right-hand weapon to `hand_r_grip`, an off-hand weapon to `hand_l_grip` and a shield to `hand_l_shield` or `hand_r_shield`, falling back to the older per-type socket (a shield: `hand_l_sas`) with a warning when a body lacks the socket; `wor.Weapons.UseGrip 0|1` switches between the two and `wor.Weapons.PrintSockets` lists what each hand uses. Crown, on seeing it: the Beastslayer Sword "fits perfectly" on `hand_r_grip` and another weapon "doesn't sit as nicely", so the Beastslayer Sword is the example and "everything else needs to be positioned to fit it". This supersedes the line above that says `hand_l_grip` is not made.
**Facts (`docs/research/asset-shortlist.md`):** 32 Paragon heroes' animations are on disk, none of the hero characters; they cover ten of the twelve weapon types with confidence (no real spear, no scythe). Weapon models are in StylizedWeapons (painted: swords, axes, daggers, hammers, polearms, staves, shields, bows, fist weapons) and an untextured base-mesh pack (scythes, spears, guns). The licence allows commercial use in Unreal games, not the Paragon name or logo; to be read at the source before release.

### D-241 — Spirit is what a character is; Body is how it looks; duplicates are allowed
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-17); built (`feature/D-241-spirit-body`) · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** the two words swap places. **Spirit** is what a character is, its element: the page is titled "Spirit of" and the choices under it are just the element names; a character reads **"Spirit of Fire"**. **Body** is the appearance (what D-234 called the spirit): "instead of spirit for appearance it should be body, so Body of Orthos etc." So the mirror's screen, the game menu row and the new-game row are Body ("Starting body"); Male Nobody and Female Nobody are bodies; unlocked looks are bodies ("Body of Orthos"). **A character is shown as two lines: "Spirit of Fire" and "Body of Orthos"**; "for the name we should use their body selection, then we can show their class".
**Duplicates (Crown, same day, reverses D-229's no-duplicates rule):** "we should allow duplicate characters to be made, which I'll explain at another date". A player may make more than one character of the same kind.
**A third line, Mind (Crown, same day):** "Mind of could be based on what skills they have / which character they lean towards". So a character's identity is the three pillar words: **Spirit of** (its element), **Body of** (its look), **Mind of** (what its skills lean towards). This makes the shared words with the pillars deliberate. How "Mind of" is worked out, and what it can read, is Crown's to define; nothing is built for it.
**Kept from D-229:** the player's own card reads "You"; another player's shows their gamer tag; the 12 recruits keep their names ("Fire Spirit").
**Defaults (Crown may change):** a Generic's and a Resonator's first line is their class name, since they have no element; Broken Darkness reads "Spirit of Broken Darkness" and Reality "Spirit of the Unknown" is not used: Reality reads "Spirit of Unknown" by the name table. Body and Spirit are also two of the three pillars (Mind, Body, Spirit); **Crown confirmed the double meaning is intended** ("yes, there would be double meaning", 2026-11-17).
**Built (2026-11-17, `feature/D-241-spirit-body`):** (1) duplicates: D-229's one-of-each-kind refusal is removed (`WoRNewGame::IsSameKind`, `ReadMadeCharacters`, `GetKindRefusal`, the Begin card's reason, Begin's refusal and the join path's); the most slots stays the configurable 12. (2) Body: the game menu row "Body" (was "Spirit", its preview "Wear Male Nobody, Female Nobody or a body you have unlocked."), the mirror screen titled "Body · {character}" (was "Spirit of {character}"; "Body of {name}" is a look's name, so the screen uses a dot), an unlocked look's row and the wearing line "Body of Orthos", "Your own body.", "Only you and the Spirits change body here.", the new-game row "Starting body". Male Nobody and Female Nobody keep their names. (3) Spirit: the new-game element list is titled "Spirit of" with the plain names under it; `WoRPlayerBuild::GetSpiritName` / `MakeIdentityLines`: "Spirit of Fire" over "Body of Male Nobody" under a player's card name (the hub and battle cards), on the pause menu party and the character sheet (the class line), and on the title slot ("Spirit of Fire" as its label, "Body of Female Nobody" over the run's levels). A slot's name, in the delete prompts, is its body ("Delete Female Nobody?"). The body shows on the viewer's own character only: another player's body isn't known on this machine, so its card shows the Spirit line alone (shortcut). (4) You and the gamer tag kept. Not changed: code names, assets, save fields; the console help text; the recruits' names. **Pickers (`fix/D-241-picker-names`):** the give, inventory, storage, item-slot and level-drop member lists and the co-op invite and join seat rows name a member as the cards do (`WoRPlayerBuild::GetMemberListName`: You, the gamer tag, a recruit's own name); when one player has two characters in the list, the identity lines go under each name. The join request's "with Fire Refractor" (the joiner's character) keeps the kind name. The level-drop line reads "Pillar levels to place (You): 2" (was "{name}'s ...").

### D-240 — Innate spell places grow with pillar points: three to start, one more per point
**Status:** ANSWERED in outline (Crown, 2026-11-17); not built · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** "maybe we revamp the system in relation to Mind, Body and Spirit for the innate pool, since 24 is a lot: maybe we start them off on 3, then they get an additional one per pillar point". A character with innate spells has **3 innate places to start and gains one more for each pillar point**, up to today's full 24 at 7 / 7 / 7. **Which spell fills a place:** "it's handled like spells: they get it, it goes to their inventory, they decide to equip it": innate spells are gained, held in the inventory and equipped into the places by the player, not handed out in a fixed order.
**It is the point budget that grows (Crown, same day, after the brain read the code):** today's rule is not a count of places but a **budget of 24 points** (each innate spell costs its tier, F 1 to S 7; at most 6 per school; a discount per high pillar; rules CL-34 to CL-36). Crown picked: **the budget starts at 3 points and gains 1 per pillar point, up to 24**; the tier costs, the 6-per-school cap, the discount and the element rule stay. Read "places" above as points. **Broken Darkness (Crown, same day):** it starts with **the same 3 points**; **each pillar point is worth 2, and the last point of each pillar (the seventh) is worth 3**. That is 3 + three pillars of (6 x 2 + 3) = 48, today's full Broken Darkness budget, shared across its pools as now.
**Notes:** the innate pool is at most 6 spells in each school (`MAX_EQUIPPED_SLOT_POOL`). A wipe takes pillar points (D-237), so it would take innate places with it; what happens to the spell in a lost place is open. Crown also asked for the enemy kit to have **its own limit for innate spells**, separate from the skill limit, "since it's much bigger than the rest of the pools".

### D-239 — Each character keeps the loadout you last left them with; a swapped-out member keeps their gear
**Status:** ANSWERED in outline (Crown, 2026-11-16); the reading below is to be confirmed · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** "the characters you took into the trial, the last loadout you gave them is what they take out of the trial too, so it's kind of like a mix and match system: I could take you one run then leave you the next, and you'd have the gear I last left you with. Then it resets if, one, you go into a run with them and, two, you pick them up within the run." And for a member replaced mid-run: "the one you traded for keeps their current loadout ... instead of giving back the character's loadout when replaced, the character just keeps them, so you need to unequip the gear if you want to give it to the new recruit".
**Brain's reading (to confirm):** (1) every character has their own worn record, and it stays theirs between runs whether or not they are taken along. (2) A character's loadout resets to their class kit at the two moments they join a run: when a run starts with them in the party, and when they are picked up inside a run (D-221's trial start, per character). (3) When a member is swapped out for a recruit, their gear is **not** handed back to the shared stock: they keep wearing it. To give a piece to the new recruit, the player takes it off first.
**On the reset (Crown, same day):** "you can still get your own version of the weapon, gear etc. from the pool". So the reset on joining a run stands: the kept loadout is the character's in the hub, and inside a run each character gets their own pieces from the trial's pool (D-225). **Point 2 confirmed (Crown):** "they will start from scratch as well": a character who joins a run, at its start or picked up inside it, starts from scratch like the player: class kit, base levels, no run skills. **In the hub too (Crown, 2026-11-16):** a member in the hub keeps "all of the gear from their last run", whether or not they are in the party: "for hub secrets we can still form a team". So nothing is handed back when the party changes in the hub either.
**Built (B-552, in dev, 2026-11-16):** (2) a character picked up inside a run wears its class kit, reset and taken on the run (`UTrialRunSubsystem::StartRecruitOnKit`). (3) a piece worn by a character out of the party (benched, or swapped out mid-run) stays theirs: nobody else can take it until it is taken off. Choices in Q-552.

### Q-572 — Seeing a kit (stage 8): what is built, and what waits on Crown
**Status:** PART ANSWERED (the rule built with reference-backed defaults; four points OPEN for Crown) · **Raised:** 2026-11-18 (B-572, trial pool stage 8) · **Blocks:** the always-on variation and the real input
**Built with defaults (Crown may change):** (1) **A skill carries it:** a new field on every skill, Kit Sight (None / Field / Always On); a character has the strongest among the skills it knows. (2) **The field use** needs the skill, an enemy within 15 m, and 10 EP (`FIELD_EP_COST`, to tune). It shows the run's rolled kit for that enemy, the one it fights with. (3) **A team:** the card shows the encounter's first enemy; the others are written to the log until the card can page. (4) **The run remembers** the type read (cleared when the run ends). (5) **Host only** for now: a co-op guest's use needs its EP paid on the host. (6) **The owner of the always-on variation can use the field one too.** (7) **The card shows the enemy as if fought alone, from where it stands:** a fight that catches a neighbouring encounter as well, or starts across a zone border, rolls a different kit, so the card can differ from that fight. Rolling with the fight's caught set is the fix once the map can know it. Reference: Persona's and Final Fantasy's Scan / Libra cost a little to reveal one enemy and then stay known for that enemy type; players use it on new enemies and bosses.
**OPEN for Crown (not built):** (a) **The key** for the field use. Default to propose: the interact key while looking at an enemy that is beyond its engage range; it needs its InputMap row. Today it is the console command `wor.Trial.InspectEnemy`. (b) **How the always-on variation shows gear** as the player gets close: a small label over the enemy, or the card. This is a look decision, so nothing is drawn yet; the rule (who has it, 8 m) is in code. (c) **What a remembered type shows** on other enemies of that type: default, its always-has pieces only (`MakeCard` with the shared part), shown the same way as (b). (d) **The two skill assets** (their names, tiers, where they are found): authored in the editor once (a) to (c) are settled; `wor.Trial.KitSight 1` or `2` stands in for testing.
**Built:** B-572 (the rule and the field use through the console).

### Q-571 — The merchant's shelf (stage 7): which pool, which kinds, and a short side
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-18 (B-571, trial pool stage 7) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** TrialPoolDesign §2 rules a trial vendor's shelf half its own list, half the trial's pool (a named tunable). It doesn't say which pool a vendor in a zone with its own loot tag reads, whether a vendor type limits the pool kinds it shows, what a pool piece costs, or what happens when one half has too few lines.
**Answer (defaults):** (1) **The trial's pool** (the trial's own tag), not the zone's: a vendor's shelf is rolled once per run from the run seed, wherever it stands. (2) **Any vendor shows any pool kind** the pool can give (weapon, adapter, spell, ability, evolution, prebuilt find); the vendor types' specialities stay in their own half. (3) **Price and tier as any stock line:** a pool piece is sold at its own tier for the shop's tier price, and bought like the vendor's own stock (a known skill is still refused, ER-20). (4) **A short side leaves its places to the other;** a trial with no pool shows the vendor's own list as before. (5) **No repeats:** a pool line the vendor lists itself is not offered. (6) **The share is `TRIAL_POOL_SHARE` (0.5).** Reference: Hades' Charon and Slay the Spire's merchant mix a fixed shop category with picks from the run's reward pool, rolled once per visit or run; players read the shop as a second chance at a drop they missed.
**Built:** B-571.

### Q-570 — Duplicates (stage 6): which kinds ask, what Replace does to the old one, and a known skill
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-18 (B-570, trial pool stage 6) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** TrialPoolDesign §6 rules three choices (Replace, Add, Convert to essence) for a drop the player already has, for everything but utility items. It doesn't say what happens to the replaced one, how a kind that can't be held twice (a skill) or is normally held in twos (an adapter) is treated, or where the choice is made.
**Answer (defaults):** (1) **The drop is added first** and the prompt comes after, on the trial map: Add (keep both) is the no-loss default (Q-565), so a prompt that is never answered loses nothing. (2) **Weapons ask.** A duplicate is another copy of the same weapon asset, at any tier. (3) **Replace** puts the new one in the old one's loadout places; the old one stays in the inventory, unworn: taking gear apart is the Blacksmith's, in the hub, never on a run (D-177). (4) **Convert** dismantles the new one for Gear essence (the tier's dismantle yield); this is the one place essence is made on a run, D-225's ruling over D-177, for the dropped copy only. (5) **Adapters don't ask:** two of one adapter is the normal case and they differ by what is socketed (B-530, D-223). (6) **Crystals stack** as before; a stack is dismantled at the Blacksmith (D-177), which already covers the ruling's utility dismantle. (7) **A known skill converts** to Skill essence at the drop, with no prompt: a character can't hold a skill twice, and its tier is the asset's own, so there is nothing to replace or keep. (8) **ER-20 stays:** a shop still refuses a skill the buyer knows, since buying it could only turn money into essence. Reference: Diablo III and Borderlands add the pickup first and let the player salvage after; Slay the Spire and Hades turn a reward you can't take into currency rather than a second roll.
**Differs from the ruling (Crown's call):** the ruling lists adapters, crystals and skills under the three choices; built as (5), (6) and (7) above. Say if any should get the prompt.
**Where the list lives:** the waiting duplicates are run state on `UTrialRunSubsystem` (they cross the level change back to the map), not per-fight state (D-064); cleared on trial entry and exit, not saved. A duplicate from the run's last fight is cleared with the run and keeps both. **Shortcuts (TODO):** evolutions and apparel get no prompt (apparel isn't dropped yet); a prebuilt find of a weapon already held keeps the older notice, with no prompt; a co-op guest's duplicate keeps both (its prompt needs the choice sent to its machine, D-103); a skill held as a spare to give a teammate needs skills to be countable.
**Built:** B-570.

### Q-569 — Drops (stage 5): the crystal's share, the tiers of what wasn't worn, and who gets one
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 (B-569, trial pool stage 5) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** TrialPoolDesign §6 rules one drop of any kind per player per won fight, 3 to 1 toward what the enemies used, at the worn tier; it doesn't say how a crystal counts against the pool's assets, what tier a piece nobody wore comes at, a skill's tier, or who a "player" is in the party.
**Answer (defaults):** (1) **The crystal** is one entry beside the pool's things (weight 1, or 3 when any enemy in the fight socketed one); drawn, it is a crystal of any element leaning to the trial's (Q-566's weights) at the zone's tiers. (2) **A piece nobody wore** comes at a tier even across the zone's range; a worn one at the tier worn (the first enemy's, when two wore it). (3) **A skill** comes at its own tier. (4) **One per player character:** the host's and each co-op guest's character get one each; a recruit or an AI seat gets none. (5) **Seeded** by the run, the fight's encounters and the player, so a retried fight drops the same (as Q-568's kits). Reference: Diablo III and Borderlands drop what an enemy carried more often than the general table and roll it once per player ("personal loot", widely liked for ending loot fights in co-op); Slay the Spire and Hades seed rewards per run so a reload can't reroll them.
**Shortcuts (TODO):** apparel is not given yet (`TrialLoot::CanGrant`); a boss's first-defeat extra drop from its always-has list is not built (needs a saved record of first defeats); a duplicate takes today's path until stage 6; the Q-518 stopgap fill stays until Crown's PIE pass of B-568.
**Built:** B-569.

### Q-568 — The roll (stage 4): the seed, how full a kit is, and where its pieces go
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 (B-568, trial pool stage 4) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-225 says each enemy's kit is rolled from the run seed inside its limits, but not what else the seed holds, how many pieces it takes, or how they are worn.
**Answer (defaults):** (1) **The seed** is the run's seed, the fight's map encounters, the enemy type and which of that type it is (the 1st, 2nd, 3rd bandit): a retried or resumed fight meets the same kits, two bandits in one fight differ, and the same bandit in another fight differs. Reference: Slay the Spire and Hades seed each run so a reload gives the same outcome (save-scumming doesn't reroll). (2) **How full:** the open part fills every place its limits leave (unset: its class's, so a Generic with nothing set wears 2 weapons and 1 adapter), each pick even across what fits, the same piece allowed twice (two Adapter Rings). Each tier is even across its range. Reference: Diablo's monster gear and Borderlands' enemies carry full loadouts scaled to the area. (3) **Crystals:** each adapter gets a crystal from the trial's crystals (the lean of Q-566, inside its element limit and the socket's type); weapons stay empty (§8's examples count weapon slots by the weapon's tier). (4) **Skills:** the always-has ones first, then ones its gear can use, in a seeded random order; each goes on the first piece that holds it by the player's slot rules, up to that piece's slots and the per-piece limit (B-567c). (5) **Worn:** the first weapon is the primary and in hand, the second the Generic's second weapon; adapters in the adapter places (an adapter-only kit: the first adapter is the primary). A Caster keeps its innate spells. (6) A fight with no pool (outside a trial, or a trial with none) keeps the authored inventories. (7) **A Refractor enemy** (innate element, brain for Crown 2026-11-17): its class rule holds, so it wears one item (a weapon or an adapter) beside its innate spells, which it keeps from its authored loadout. Innate spells **don't count** against the skill limit: they have their own (most innate, a point budget; unset, D-240's budget, B-567c). Its element limit is the kit's Elements list: set to Fire, it takes only a Fire crystal and Fire spells; unset, a Fire Refractor may take a Water crystal (any element, like the player).
**Shortcuts (TODO):** evolutions and apparel are not rolled or worn yet (an always-has one is skipped with a log); a shield is worn as a main weapon (the loadout's own check decides); a co-op guest's copy of an enemy is not rolled yet (B-230). The roll counts items, weapons and adapters but not the class budget (a crystal costs 1): the loadout's own fight-start check drops adapters over it, as for the player. The roll matches skills to gear by weapon type and element only; a skill a slot rule refuses (wield mode, a weapon that can't hold it) is logged as "found no slot".
**Built:** B-568.

### Q-567 — The enemy's parts (stage 3): what counts against which limit
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 (B-567, trial pool stage 3) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-225 sets the always-has list and six limits but not how they meet: do always-has pieces count toward the limits, what "which kinds" covers, and how a kit tier range meets the zone's.
**Answer (defaults):** (1) The limits count the **whole kit**, always-has pieces included ("most gear 1" with an always-has sword leaves no room for another; B-567b: the limits merged, Crown, five now), and the class limits always hold on top: the player's rules, exactly (D-225), the same way the player's loadout counts every piece worn. An always-has list the class can't carry is a data warning, not cut. (2) **Most gear** counts weapons, adapters and an evolution together (the class's item count; a Resonator's is its 1 weapon and 5 adapters, 6); apparel has its own place and doesn't count, as for the player. (3) "Which kinds" is two lists in the Details panel, one per enum: the kinds (weapon, adapter, evolution, apparel) and the weapon types; a third list holds the elements (for its crystals and spells); empty is any. An evolution is allowed when its kind is listed (or the list is empty). An always-has piece of a kind the list leaves out is a data warning. Until evolutions and apparel are rolled (Q-568 shortcut), a kinds list of only those takes nothing from the pool. **The old values don't carry over:** the two removed numbers and the switch are dropped on load, so a kit authored before B-567b is re-authored (only `DA_Enemy_Generic` had one). (4) A kit tier range is cut to the zone's range; if the two don't meet, the enemy takes nothing from the pool. An always-has piece keeps its given tier wherever it stands (note 5: the zone print warns). (5) ~~Most skills unset is 3~~ (Crown, B-567c): most skills is **per piece**, unset every slot the piece has; innate spells have their own limit, **most innate**, a **point budget** (CL-34's costs: each spell its tier, less the pillar discount), unset D-240's budget from the enemy's own Mind, Body and Spirit levels (3 + 1 per point, at most 24; Broken Darkness the same 3, each point worth 2 and a pillar's seventh worth 3, up to 48). The enemy keeps its authored innate spells (and Broken Darkness pools) in order while they fit; one that doesn't fit is skipped and a cheaper one after it may still fit. A spell kept as innate is not also put on an adapter. Old values don't carry over: the old Most Skills is dropped on load (no enemy had it set).
**Built:** B-567.

### Q-566 — The trial pool (stage 2): which crystals, and how much the lean is
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 (B-566, trial pool stage 2) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** D-225 says a trial gives "every element's" crystals leaning to its own; the design's example 5 says "all five". Which crystals, and the two lean numbers.
**Answer (defaults):** ~~the five base elements~~ **every element's crystal** (brain for Crown, 2026-11-17, D-225 "every element's crystals": the five was too narrow): Garnet, Sapphire, Citrine, Emerald, Amber, Opal, Onyx, Amethyst, Iolite. **Quartz is left out:** it is no element's, and no trial pickup gives it today (a merchant may stock it as a consumable). The trial's own element comes up **3 times** as often as each other, and its tiers lean up (+1 weight per tier above the zone's lowest; other elements even across the zone's range). A Generic trial leans nowhere. Both numbers are named tunables (`TrialPool::OWN_ELEMENT_WEIGHT`, `OWN_ELEMENT_TIER_STEP`). Reference: Hades' god pools and Slay the Spire's class card pools weight what the area or character is about without shutting the rest out; Diablo's area loot tables raise the odds of a theme, not exclude others. The trial's pool tag is `Source.Trial.<Element>` (`Source.Trial.Fire` first).
**Built:** B-566; every element in B-566b.

### Q-565 — The trial pool’s look (stage 1): closing the duplicate prompt, and its words
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 (B-565, trial pool stage 1) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Question:** the design (TrialPoolDesign §6) gives the three choices for a duplicate drop but not what Back does, nor how the kit card and the dropped line read.
**Answer (defaults):** Back on the duplicate prompt **keeps both** (Add): closing it never loses the drop or the piece held. Reference: in Diablo III and Path of Exile, closing a window never destroys an item; a cancel that keeps everything is the safe default. The dropped line reads "Dropped: Arming Sword (D), empty" (a prebuilt piece "built", D-231; a skill or a crystal no word), on the fight’s results under the turn count, one line per thing. The kit card lists name, class with its Mind / Body / Spirit, each piece as "name · tier · slots", then the skills; no rank word (D-187). The convert’s essence number is made up until stage 6.
**Built:** B-565 (stage 1, built unseen; previews only).

### Q-561 — A co-op guest's own character: where it is built and kept
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-17 (B-561, stage 5) · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (brain, 2026-11-17, from the defaults proposed):** a guest joins from the in-game menu with one of its own character slots (read from its own save files) or a new character, the same list the title shows (D-229). It sends its picks; the host builds the character from the host's class table under a guest name of its own, never the host player's (`WoRPlayer_Guest_<name>_<class>_<element>`). The guest's character lives in the guest's own save; the host's save keeps its picks only for its seat in the host's game. Reference: Diablo III and Borderlands keep each player's hero in their own save and bring it into the host's game.
**Crown, 2026-11-17 (changes two defaults):** a guest "keeps what they earn, as they have played a game". When the guest's time in the host's game ends, what its character earned goes back into its own save for that character, by the solo run-end rules: currencies by the run-end rules (each player's own wallet is already paid in full, D-046 / D-103, so the guest keeps its wallet's change since it connected; Gold stays run-only, B-178), kept levels by D-237, gear kept by D-219 and skills by D-224 (gear and skills: B-564). Only a settled host save counts (no run open): leaving or the host quitting mid-run is a quit, keeping only the wallet's change. A new character made to join is saved to a free slot of the guest's own when the join is sent.
**Defaults in the build (Crown may change):** the guest's word is trusted for its picks (co-op trust TODO); two guests with one name, class and element share one character; a guest back under the same name with other weapons or crystals keeps its first build; a save from before B-554 (no picks) can't join; after a join is sent the guest's screen closes and the host's answer shows as the seat; joining with every slot full is refused for a new character; the new character's slot is written when the join is sent, so a refused join still leaves it on the guest's title (as if made there); spending in the host's game comes off the joined character, as in its own game; the wallet's change goes to the character joined as, never the slot in play's balance copied in. **Built:** B-561 (the host side), B-562 (the game menu's Join the party), B-563 (the guest keeps its wallet's change and kept levels; a new character gets a slot); B-564 (the found gear and evolutions its run kept, as hub gear in its own save: owned, not worn, never sold, as D-221's hub gear; the skills it learned, a skill it already has keeps its own copy: this is the run-end hand-back into the guest's save, where nobody is there to choose; the trial pool's three duplicate choices (D-225) happen earlier, when the skill drops).

### D-238 — The hub's recruits are built the same way as the player
**Status:** ANSWERED in outline (Crown, 2026-11-16); planned with D-228, not built · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** deleting the old Lords (removal list group 2)
**Answer (Crown):** "we need the 12 recruits, but they would be similar to our character, no? So we would just set their element and that's pretty much it". The 12 walk-up recruits stay in the hub, but each is **the one shared character plus fixed picks** (D-228), not its own character and inventory asset: its class and, for a Refractor, its element; the class table gives the rest (stats, kit, innate spells).
**Fresh start and names (Crown, 2026-11-16):** the recruits "start fresh, the same as our classes do": no evolution or other gear carried over from the old Lords. They are no longer called Lords: each is a **Spirit**, named by its element or class ("Fire Spirit", "Water Spirit", "Generic Spirit", "Resonator Spirit" and so on).
**Reality's recruit** is called **Unknown Spirit** (Crown, 2026-11-17: kept).
**Their look, and the player's (Crown, 2026-11-17):** the 12 recruits "will look how they look now, to an extent": each keeps **its colour, standing for its spirit element or class**, and "you can put a spirit over them" (a spirit worn from the mirror shows instead). The same goes for the player: "when you make a character you should gain the spirit colour on your character, like how they are now": a new character's body takes the colour of its element or class until a spirit is worn over it.
**Their spirits (Crown, 2026-11-17):** "for their spirits they will be similar to us: they will have male and female [Nobody] as well as any others we unlock". A recruit chooses from the same spirit list as the player: Male Nobody, Female Nobody, and every spirit the player has unlocked. **The class numbers:** "the class numbers on old Lords should be reset to 0": every class and element starts even, with nothing carried over from the Lords.
**Defaults (Crown may change):** a recruit's picks are one small row (name, class, element, spirit, and a colour for its box or body); it starts on its class's F-tier kit like a new player. This changes D-228's "named party members keep their own character assets" for these twelve; a character with its own story, look or fixed gear can still have its own asset.
**Built (B-557, stage 4b first half, built unseen):** the recruit rows are `FPlayerPicks` on the class table (`Recruits`, flagged `bRecruit`), filled with the 12 defaults by **Fill Recruits**: Generic Spirit (Sword and Shield), Resonator Spirit (Garnet and Sapphire), a Spirit per element, and Broken Darkness Spirit, each on Male Nobody until its Body is set. A placed recruit finds its row by its old asset's class (a Refractor: and element), so the placed actors are unchanged and there is one Generic and one Resonator recruit; it is built like the player under its own name and keeps its picks (saved, built again on load, laid over its trial kit). A Lord already in the party from an older save stays itself. A recruit's name is an authored `FString`, like `UCharacterData::Name` (TODO: D-061 when names become `FText`). Not yet: a recruit wearing spirits, and the colour on the body (B-558).
**Built (B-558, stage 4b second half, built unseen):** a recruit's spirit is on its party member (`SpiritForm`, `SpiritBody`, saved). The Spirit screen steps through the party (Z / C, LT / RT); a recruit wears from the player's unlocks by the player's rules (hub or rest spot, not in a fight). On its starting body with no spirit worn it shows its Blueprint's own look, which is its element or class colour (the default: no colour code, the Blueprint's material is the colour); the other Nobody body is the plain body. Its fight pawn and its hub body are dressed. Not yet: the player's own body colour (needs a coloured Nobody material per element or class: an editor look first), a recruit's spirit turning on that spirit's bound-gear bonus, co-op.
**Built (B-560, built unseen):** the body colour. With no spirit worn, the player and each Spirit recruit are the plain Nobody body with every material slot's `Tint` set to the character's colour (`ClassColors`: a Refractor its element, Broken Darkness its black, a Generic and a Resonator their class colour); a worn spirit shows its own materials. A recruit standing in the hub is its row's starting body in its colour (B-558's own-Blueprint look is replaced).
**Chest light (Crown, 2026-11-17, `feature/D-238-chest-light`):** the light in a body's chest takes the same colour as its Tint, for the player and the recruits, and is cleared with it when a spirit is worn. It is set on the materials' `LogoLayer0_Color`, `LogoLayer1_Color` and `LogoLayer2_Color` (`WoRSpiritLook::CHEST_LIGHT_PARAMETERS`); which one is the chest light shows only in PIE, so all three are set until Crown names it.

### D-237 — Run levels are kept in the hub, the way gear is
**Status:** ANSWERED in outline (Crown, 2026-11-16); the reading below is to be confirmed before it is built · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** the title slot's levels (D-229), one-player stage 3
**Answer (Crown):** "run levels should work the same way as gear does: we keep it in the hub". This changes D-102 (a run's Mind / Body / Spirit levels ended with the run).
**Brain's reading (to confirm):** the levels a character ends a run with are theirs in the hub afterwards, by the gear rules of D-219: kept on a boss win, an extract and out of wipes; a quit puts back what they had before the run. As with gear (D-221), the next trial still starts from the class's base levels; the hub keeps the last run's. The title slot shows these (D-229). **The cost (Crown, 2026-11-16):** "one random pillar point per death; even though we punish their gear they can just change it". Each death in the run takes **one point from a random pillar** (Mind, Body or Spirit) off the levels the character keeps. **"Or maybe two random points"** (Crown, same day): the number is one named tunable, set to **2** for now (each point rolled on its own, so both may land on one pillar or on two). **Where it happens (Crown, same day):** "not in the hub, in the trial; the hub is just the last points you had in the trial". The points are lost **in the run, at the death**, so the rest of that run is played with fewer; the hub simply keeps whatever levels the character had when the run ended. This replaces the default below that took the points off at run end. Defaults (Crown may change): a death is a party wipe; a pillar never drops below the class's base level; the points come off the kept levels when the run ends; a quit still puts back the pre-run levels with no further loss; the roll uses the run's seeded random.
**Built (B-555, built unseen):** a boss win, an extract and out of wipes keep the levels the run ended with (`FPartyMember::Kept*`, saved; `UTrialRunSubsystem::KeepRunLevels`), replacing the last run's; a quit keeps the ones from before it. The hub adds them (`UCharacterDataComponent::SeedWorldStats`); a trial starts without them. The title slot shows them. A wipe takes `DefeatRules::WIPE_POINTS_LOST` (2) run points from each member at once (`TakeWipePoints`), each from a pillar rolled on the run's seed among those with run points; a pillar's over-cap points go first; only run points are taken, so nothing drops below the class's base (the defaults above, approved by the reference-backed default). Over-cap points and waiting levels are not kept (they end with the run, as before). This replaces D-160's no-points wipe; the Prisms still drop.

### D-234 — An appearance mirror in the hub; the two base bodies are Male Nobody and Female Nobody; Enter picks on the quick strip
**Status:** ANSWERED in part (Crown, 2026-11-16); two points open · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** (1) **Appearance mirror:** "like the shops, we should have an appearance mirror that will allow us to change our cosmetic": an object in the hub (a box for now, as the shops are) that opens the appearance screen. (2) **The first two entries** of the spirit list are **Male Nobody** and **Female Nobody**, "who would be Manny and Quinn" (Crown's names). (3) **Quick strip:** **Enter selects**; the "Click Pick" hint goes ("click is intuitive, people will just know"); the click still works.
**Answered (Crown, 2026-11-16):** the new-game screen **keeps the choice**, and it is called **Starting spirit** (Male Nobody / Female Nobody); it can be changed later at the mirror. The appearance mirror is **the same mirror as the loadout presets' mirror** (D-221): one mirror. **Its three options (Crown): Spirit (meaning appearance), Presets and Cosmetics.** "Spirit" is the player-facing word for the appearance screen.
**Built (B-539, in dev, 2026-11-16):** point 3: Enter selects the lit piece on the quick strip; the hint says Enter Select; the click still picks.
**Built (B-541, in dev, 2026-11-16):** points 1 and 2: Male Nobody / Female Nobody in the name table, first in the Spirit list (A wears one: any form off, that base body); the new-game row is Starting spirit; the game menu row Forms is now Spirit; the hub mirror (a box, E: Open, Mirror) opens the Spirit screen. Only rows that work show: Spirit alone today, so the mirror opens it directly; with Presets (D-221 stage 3, hook in `AHubMirror::Interact`) it becomes a row menu. The loadout screen's Cosmetics tab is only a greyed chip ("That tab comes later"), with nothing behind it.
**Fixed (B-542, in dev, 2026-11-16):** the player's pawn always wears its spirit (Male Nobody used to leave the Blueprint body on). Default (Crown may change): in a fight, the pawn the player controls (the leader) wears the spirit too, as Female Nobody already did; the player's character is their spirit (D-228).

### D-233 — Every screen works with the mouse as well as keys and a pad
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-16); a standing rule · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** "we should allow mouse clicks; it's a PC and console game". Every menu and screen can be used with the mouse (hover lights a row, a click picks it, the wheel scrolls a list), as well as the keyboard and a gamepad. A new screen ships with all three; existing screens that are keys-only (the title and the new-game screen first) get the mouse added. **Crown, same day, on the remaining keys-only screens: "we should allow the mouse"**: the rest are done in order (the Equipment screen's wheel first).
**Built (B-538, in dev, 2026-11-16):** the title and the new-game screen (hover, click through the confirm path, the cursor shows). Every other screen and what the mouse does there: `InputMap.md`, "Screens and how they are reached", the Mouse column.
**Built (B-553, in dev, 2026-11-17):** the rest of the screens: the Equipment wheel, the Recruit and Join request cards, hover on the Character sheet, the Gate and Play together; Credits, the Rest spot and the battle HUD needed nothing for the mouse. Every player screen now takes the mouse (`InputMap.md` screens table). Left open: the Rest spot's old panel has no Back key and nothing focused for the keys and pad, and the battle HUD's gamepad inside a submenu (Q-391).

### D-232 — The title is its own level
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-16) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** "should we not make a new level which is the menu, and it takes us there, that way it's separate". The game starts in a small **menu level** that holds only the title (Continue, Load; D-229); choosing a character travels to the hub. The title no longer opens over the hub.
**Defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** the menu level is the game's default map and the map the editor opens on, so pressing Play shows the title; pressing Play with the hub or a trial level open goes straight into that level on the last played character, for quick tests. Reference: a separate front-end map is the standard Unreal set-up (Lyra's front end, and most shipped games), so the hub never loads before a character is chosen.
**Built (B-537, in dev, 2026-11-16):** `AWoRFrontEndGameMode` (no pawn) opens the title in the menu level; Continue and Begin travel to the hub; the game menu's Quit outside a run comes back; Play in the hub or a trial with no save goes to the menu level, not the Character Template. The menu level counts once `GameDefaultMap` is not the hub, so it switches on with the editor step (sheet B-537).

### D-231 — A prebuilt adapter or weapon shows its crystal in the first slot
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-16) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** "if a prebuilt adapter is used, augment slots disappear": "the prebuilt comes with a crystal, so we shouldn't allow an additional crystal to be attached". A prebuilt adapter (bought or found already built, D-226) shows no augment slot and takes nothing more.
**Changed the same day (Crown):** "maybe the best way to do it is a prebuilt adapter shows its crystal in augment slot 1": the slots do not disappear; the prebuilt's crystal sits in the first slot, filled. "Then we can decide if the crystal is removable or not": whether a prebuilt's crystal can be taken out is **a setting on each prebuilt**, chosen when it is made (brain's reading; default not removable). **Weapons too (Crown):** "same would apply for weapons": a prebuilt weapon shows its crystal in the first slot, with the same removable setting. **Second slot (Crown):** usable, "but only for stones, since it comes with an element crystal already": a prebuilt piece's second slot takes an augment stone, never another element crystal.
**Built (B-532, in dev, 2026-11-16):** the flags on the owned piece, set by the find grant and the starting gear, read by one rule (CL-28d); small cases in Q-532.
**Sockets come off one at a time (Crown, 2026-11-16, changes Q-532's default):** "removing one shouldn't clear the other": on every piece, taking out the crystal leaves the stone and taking off the stone leaves the crystal. What is taken out is still destroyed. So a fixed prebuilt's stone can come off; only its built-in crystal is locked.
**Removal and restore (Crown, 2026-11-16, changes the default below):** a prebuilt's **normal crystal is removable by default**; taking it out destroys it, **unless it is an evolution**, which goes back to the player. Where a crystal **is locked to its piece**, a broken one (durability run out) is **restored in place**: "you need to repair it by using a crystal of the same type, which would tie into our merge logic, i.e. an S tier crystal would require x lower crystals". There is **no separate repair system or screen** for it ("maybe for the Jeweler"): the piece's crystal slot shows a **Restore** option when it is broken.
**Restore amounts (Crown, 2026-11-16):** the merge logic is "combining crystals to a higher tier"; restoring uses "something similar for fixing durability, if we assume that the correlating tier is 100%". So one crystal of the **same type and the same tier restores 100%** of the durability; a lower-tier crystal restores the share it is worth by the merge ratio (if N of a tier merge into one of the next, one of them restores 1/N).
**Breaking, restoring and the word (Crown, 2026-11-16, final for now):** (1) an **unlocked** crystal that runs out in combat is **destroyed**, as today. (2) A **locked** crystal that reaches 0 **stays in its piece and must be restored** before it works again. (3) A crystal that **still has some left can be restored** (topped up) with same-type crystals at the merge ratios; so Restore is offered on any socketed crystal below full, and on a locked one at 0. (4) The player-facing word is **wear**, not durability. **Crown, later the same day:** the number stays as it is ("40/60" means 40 left) and **the word is not shown beside it** ("we wouldn't show the text wear, since we would tell them what it is"); the word is kept for help text and the Wear Stone's name. (5) **Hard requirements on gear:** a piece can be set to accept only one crystal type ("i.e. Emerald can only be attached"); **confirmed by Crown the same day**: a piece can be set to accept only one type of crystal, so when its crystal is taken out or destroyed only that type can go back in. Default (Crown may change): it is a setting any weapon or adapter can carry, and a prebuilt can set it too; with none set, the piece takes any crystal as now.
**Restore built (B-534, in dev, 2026-11-16):** points 1 to 4 as above: a locked gem at 0 is kept at fight end; the worn crystal's socket list has a Restore line; the player text says wear. Small cases in Q-534. Point 5 is the next job.
**Built (B-551, in dev, 2026-11-16):** the wear number shows alone ("40 / 60", no word beside it) on the loadout screen's detail box, the socket choices and the inventory tiles. The word stays in help text and the Wear Stone's name; the vendor's repair page keeps its Wear line. Where the player is told what the number means today: nowhere directly; the repair page says "Gear wears in fights. Repair restores it", and taking an evolution off warns it comes back "with some wear (it breaks at none)". Crown to decide where to explain it.
**Point 5 built (B-540, in dev, 2026-11-16):** Accepted Crystal Type on the weapon or adapter asset (a prebuilt uses its asset; not saved per piece); only that gem goes in; the add window greys the rest with the reason; stones and evolutions are not limited (CL-28e). Starting gear and found loot obey it too (a wrong gem leaves the socket empty). Defaults (Crown may change): one type per piece, not a list; a gem already socketed in a saved piece before the setting was added stays until taken out.
**Defaults (Crown may change):** the adapter remembers that it came prebuilt; its own crystal stays as built and cannot be swapped; an adapter the player built from an empty ring keeps its augment slot. A starting ring handed out with its crystal (the Earth Ring) counts as prebuilt.

### D-230 — The loadout screen gets an Inventory tab, first in the row
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-16) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** "we should have an inventory tab, which should be first": the tab row reads Inventory, Equipment, Skills, Apparel, Cosmetics. It shows what the character owns and is not wearing, so spares can be seen and swapped in ("we should add what they can't equip to inventory so we can change it").
**Edit on a worn entry (Crown, 2026-11-16):** Edit opens the same choice as "+ Add equipment" (a weapon or an adapter). Choosing **weapon** opens the entry's weapon window with what is there, to edit or replace it; choosing **adapter** replaces the entry with an adapter (and the other way round for an adapter entry).
**Stacking (Crown, 2026-11-16):** "since they are the exact same we could stack them": pieces that are exactly the same show as one row with a count ("Adapter Ring x5"); choosing the row takes one. Default: exactly the same means the same piece and tier with an empty socket, no skill picks and the same wear; anything built or changed keeps its own row (D-223's tier-then-number rule still tells those apart). It is a display stack: each ring stays its own item.
**Defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** grouped as Weapons, Adapters, Evolutions, Crystals; hovering a piece fills the same details box as the Equipment tab; choosing a piece offers to wear it (the Equipment tab's add flow, reused). The screen still opens on the Equipment tab. Reference: Destiny 2's and Breath of the Wild's inventory pages sit beside the worn gear and equip straight from the list.
**Built (B-543, in dev, 2026-11-16), Crown's addition:** the inventory screen's tabs read Gear, Loadout, Pouch, Chest; Loadout opens the Equipment screen for the same character. Adapter tiles show the adapter icon. The loadout screen's own Inventory tab stays (it alone can wear a piece from the list).

### D-229 — Character slots at new game; an adapter's icon is the frame with its crystal
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-16); slots are planned with D-228, not built yet · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** (1) **Character slots:** "we should have character select slots, so when we create a new game we pick a slot". Each slot is its own game with its own character (body, class, picks, name; D-227, D-228). Default (Crown may change): three slots, as Destiny has; the title shows the slots, an empty one starts a new game, a filled one continues it. (2) **Adapter icon:** an adapter shows **the crystal's icon inside the frame**; with nothing socketed the frame's centre is empty. This replaces the text-only tile on the quick strip and the lists.
**The title and the save list (Crown, 2026-11-16, replaces the three-slot default):** the title has **Continue** and **Load**; there is no New Game entry and **no 2-second hold**. **Continue** resumes the last played character. **Load** shows the characters (one save file each); **New game sits under the last entry** of that list. The **most save files** is "the max classes we can have, i.e. Generic, Resonator and each of the Refractors", held as **one configurable value** Crown may change later. **The slots show on the title itself (Crown, 2026-11-16, replaces the Load list):** "instead of showing New Game we just show the character slots", as Destiny's character select does: **the slots sit on the right of the title**, each filled one showing its character; **the next empty slot shows a + sign** ("if the first two were populated, the third would show a +"); picking a filled slot continues that character, picking the + starts a new one. The highlighted character is shown on the left (default, as Destiny does; a preview box until the bodies have previews). Reference: Destiny 2's character select. **What a slot shows, and the figure (Crown, 2026-11-16):** a slot shows only the character's **name** and the **Mind / Body / Spirit of the last run they had** (D-237). **The character figure on the title is wanted**: the highlighted character stands on the right, in its spirit and holding its gear, and the slots move to the left; until it exists the title stays centred. It is its own stage after one save per character exists (one-player stage 3). **Stage 1 look approved (Crown, 2026-11-16):** "the title looks good". The "your last run was lost" line is not wanted back. **No duplicate characters (Crown, 2026-11-17):** "we can't have duplicate characters unless they are players". A player can have **one character of each kind** (one Generic, one Resonator, one Refractor of each element, Broken Darkness and Reality included), which is why the slot count is the number of class choices; the new-game screen does not offer a kind already made. The same kind twice can only happen when **two players** each bring theirs in co-op. Then they are told apart by **the player's user tag** ("if they were players we'd use their user tag"), shown with the character's name. **How a player's card reads (Crown, 2026-11-17, replaces the tag in brackets):** the player's **own card says "You"**; **another player's card shows their gamer tag as its name** ("there's no point showing their character name in game, just show their gamer tag"); what the character is goes on a detail line under it, as **"Spirit of ..."** (for example "Spirit of Fire"). Recruits keep their own names ("Fire Spirit"). **Join a friend is not on the title** (Crown): "it would be in game, i.e. via the menu"; joining is reached from the in-game menu only. Defaults (Crown may change): with the list full, the New game row is not offered; deleting a character is a row action with a yes / no confirm in place of the hold.
**Built (B-546, stage 1, look only):** the title is all centred (Crown's change, 2026-11-16: nothing beside the slots yet): the name, the slots, then Settings, Credits and Quit as bottom buttons (Quit with them, last). R / Y or a right-click deletes a filled slot after a yes / no that opens on No; the + over today's one save asks yes / no too. Most slots: `[WoR.Title] MaxCharacterSlots` in DefaultGame.ini, unset = 12 (Generic, Resonator, and the Refractor once for each of the nine elements and Broken Darkness). The Hold to confirm setting (B-453c) no longer touches the title. A slot shows only two things (Crown, 2026-11-16): the character's name and the Mind / Body / Spirit levels of its last run, read from the save's run (`FSavedRun::RunLevels`); a run's levels end with it (D-102), so with no run in the save each shows a dash. The character figure, when it comes, goes on the right and the slots move left (Crown, 2026-11-16).
**Built (B-554, stage 3a, built unseen):** one save file per character: the first slot keeps the old file name `WoR_Main`, the others are `WoR_Main_1`, `WoR_Main_2` and so on. The last played slot is remembered in GameUserSettings (`[WoR.Save] LastSlot`) and loads at start. The title lists every filled slot in slot order, then the + while there is room. Continue on a slot loads that character (the one left behind stays as last written); the + starts the new character in the first free slot and leaves the others alone; delete removes only that slot's file. The start-over yes / no is no longer reached (kept, OBSOLETE). Save version 44: older saves are wiped with the D-060 notice.
**Built (one of each kind, 2026-11-17, `feature/D-229-one-of-each-kind`):** `WoRNewGame::IsSameKind` / `GetKindRefusal`: a kind this player already has a slot for stays in the new-game lists, and the Begin card reads "You already have a Fire Refractor.", dimmed; Begin refuses it in code too (and a new character made to join). A Generic's weapons and a Resonator's crystals don't make another kind. The default most slots (12) is one per kind. Not refused: a guest's character of the host's kind (B-561 / B-562 check nothing of the kind) and a recruit Spirit of the player's kind in the party (the party refuses only the same character twice). **How a player's card reads (built 2026-11-17, `feature/D-229-card-names`, replaces the tag in brackets):** `WoRPlayerBuild::GetCardName`: the viewer's own character reads **You**; another player's reads that player's gamer tag (its `PlayerState` name: the Null subsystem today gives the computer's name, EOS / Steam later the account's display name; "Player" with none); a recruit or an enemy its own name. Each machine decides You for itself; a player character nobody has claimed yet reads You only when the viewer is the only player, else "Player" until the claim arrives (the battle cards re-read their names each turn). Under the name, `GetDetailLine`: the plain kind name ("Fire Refractor"); the "Spirit of ..." wording waits on Crown's Spirit / Body rename. Used by the hub and battle party cards, the turn order and the turn title ("Your turn"), and the pause menu's party. There is no combat log on screen today. The title slot keeps the kind name. Two-player parts unproven.

### D-228 — One player character: body, class and a class pick are choices, not separate character assets
**Status:** ANSWERED in outline (Crown, 2026-11-16); a plan comes before any code · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** the stage 6 deletes of the old player characters
**Answer (Crown):** "every player would have the same bases in a sense, only enemies would have different"; "we have one Manny and one Quinn; if we have different data assets for classes, would we not have to make multiple versions instead of just having one". So the player is **one character**, not one data asset per class and element. (1) The player is always the picked **body** (Manny / Quinn) plus the picked **class**, in the hub and in trials; Character Template is never the player's character once a game has a leader. (2) **New-game picks by class:** a **Refractor picks an element** (the element choice shows only for a Refractor); a **Resonator picks their starting ring**; a **Generic picks their starting weapon**. (3) The picks are locked for the game (D-227).
**Shape (brain's proposal, to be planned):** one shared Player asset for what every player has; the choices saved on the player; one class table for what a class changes (stat budget, hub start, trial kit, innate spells by element). Enemies and named party members keep their own character assets. Reference: Destiny builds one character from a class pick; Elden Ring's and Dark Souls' new game picks a starting class and a starting gift.
**Broken Darkness and Reality (Crown, 2026-11-16):** they "come under Refractor": they are choices inside the Refractor's pick, beside the elements, not classes of their own and not separate characters.
**The picks (Crown, 2026-11-16):** (a) the new-game pick **also sets the trial kit**: every trial starts on what was picked. (b) A **Generic picks two weapons**: "that way they could pick to have two weapons or dual wield / sword and shield". (c) A **Resonator picks two starting prebuilt rings**. (d) This is "for the finalised version; we still need to prep full loadouts for our testing" (D-227): test starts stay full.
**Plan questions answered (Crown, 2026-11-16; `Design/OnePlayerCharacterPlan.md` section 7):** (Q2) a sword and a shield are **separate weapons** for the pick: a Generic's two picks are two weapons, so sword and shield uses both ("one day we will look into shield mains"). This is the pick only; for skill slots the pair still counts as one weapon (D-224). (Q6) the player **types their own name** at the new-game screen. **Changed by Crown in PIE, 2026-11-17: the typed name is removed** ("no need when the characters have names; also it clashes with Female Nobody" on the screen). Default (Crown may change): a character is named by what it is, its class and element ("Fire Refractor", "Generic", "Resonator"), on the title slot and the party card. **Built (2026-11-17, `feature/D-228-kind-names`):** `WoRNewGame::GetCharacterName`; a hub recruit keeps its row's name.
