Defaults (Crown may change): two characters of the same kind in two slots read the same, told apart by their place and their last-run levels; an older save's typed name is kept in the file and ignored; a co-op guest's build key is now the joining player's online name (`FPlayerPicks::GuestKey`; an older save's guest without one keeps its typed name as the key). **Resonator's start, changed (Crown, 2026-11-16, replaces (c)):** not two prebuilt rings but **two empty starting adapters and a bundle of crystals**: "they choose the crystals they want and they'll get like 6 of each". The player sockets them. Default (Crown may change): they choose two elements, all F tier. **Count (Crown, same day): 3 of each**, not 6 (a named tunable). **Tier:** the two picked weapons and the two picked rings (and the rings' crystals) all start at **F tier**. Q1, Q3, Q4, Q5 and the portrait go with the plan's defaults (approved by the reference-backed default).
**Built (B-554, stage 3a, built unseen):** Begin builds the player from the picks (`WoRPlayerBuild`). It copies the shared `DA_Character_Player` and takes the typed name, the picked class and the Refractor's element (Broken Darkness included) from the picks, and the world levels, action speed and portrait from the class table. The asset's own name and class are never read. The picks are saved with the party (`FSavedPartyMember::Picks`), and the player is built again from them on every load. Action speed 0 in the table (the Resonator and every Refractor row) is the normal speed: no points, ×1.0, the animations at their own pace. Until stage 4 the built player carries the matching old start character's inventory.
**Built (B-556, stage 4, built unseen):** the picks give the starting gear, minted once at F tier: a Generic's two weapons (a weapon and a shield are one hand set, the shield in the off hand; two other types take both weapon places), a Resonator's two empty adapters and 3 F-tier crystals of each picked type, a Refractor nothing beyond its innate spells. The weapon per type and the adapter are set on the class table (`DA_Loadout_ClassDefaults`, Picks). The innate spells and Broken Darkness pools come from the class table (the stage 3 shortcut through an old Lord's inventory is gone). A trial starts on the class kit with the picked pieces laid over it (a Generic's weapon places, a Resonator's first two adapter places; only the pieces change, the kit's skill picks and socketed crystal on those places stay; a class with no kit row keeps what it wears). Two of one weapon type (dual wield) can't be picked yet: the list takes each type once. **B-559:** the new-game list offers only the weapon types with a weapon under Pick Weapons (no hard-coded list); crystal types all stay.

### D-227 — A character's class is locked for the whole game; dev test sets are not limited by the final rules
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-16) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** (1) **Class lock:** "I want to have the classes like Destiny, where you're locked into it for the game": a character's class (Generic / Caster / Resonator) is chosen once and does not change in that game. (2) **Dev test sets:** the starting rules (D-221, D-224) describe the finished game; "right now we are in dev, so we should consider these things but are not limited for testing". A test start may hold more than the final kit. (3) **The Generic's test start:** Arming Sword and Round Shield in hand, the Earth Ring kept, **plus one separate empty Adapter Ring**, Gale Force (for testing only), the two axes as spares. (4) **The Generic's trial kit** starts with the **sword and shield** (one weapon for slots, D-224), not the sword alone. (5) **Tier in brackets:** the Equipment detail reads "Adapter (S)" and "Crystal (B)", not "Adapter tier S".
**Full test loadout (Crown, 2026-11-16, replaces point 3):** "for Generic we should use a full loadout since we are testing": every gear place is filled. Sword and shield in hand; the Hand Axe and Bearded Axe as the second weapon; every remaining place a ring, each with a different crystal, **one of them a prebuilt one** (the Phoenix Fire Ring); Gale Force; one empty Adapter Ring and the crystal stock as spares. **Skill slots stay empty** ("it will overcomplicate things").
**Reference:** Destiny 2 ties the class to the character for its whole life; a second class is a second character.

### D-226 — Merchants sell prebuilt adapters too; a named adapter shows its name; a ring's slots come from its crystal
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-16) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** nothing
**Answer (Crown):** (1) **Merchants** can stock **prebuilt adapters** (a crystal or evolution already in the socket) as well as empty ones: "we should be able to sell prebuilt ones as well". (2) **Names:** an adapter with no name of its own is named by its socket, as in D-223 ("Fire Ring", "Phoenix Fire Ring", "Adapter Ring" when empty); an adapter that **has its own name shows that name**, with its stats: "an adapter called King's Ring with stats would show". (3) **Slots:** "the slots come from the crystal": a ring's spell slot count reads the **socketed crystal's tier** (an F ring with a B crystal has B's slots), not the average. This changes D-224 for the slot count only: **skill power still uses the built average**, and the ring's stat lines stay on the ring's own tier. (4) **Details box:** instead of "Skill tier D (F + B)" show two lines, **Adapter tier** and **Crystal tier**.
**Defaults (Crown may change):** an empty ring keeps its own tier's slot count, as now; an evolution in the socket counts as the crystal; a prebuilt adapter's price is the adapter's plus the socketed piece's.

### D-225 — A trial's pool: what it gives is what its enemies use
**Status:** ANSWERED in outline (Crown, 2026-11-16); built after the asset overhaul's cast stage · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 · **Blocks:** the trial pool jobs
**Answer (Crown):** each trial has a **gear and skills pool** that "basically says what you can earn from the trial", and **the enemies use that pool**: "random weapons, abilities and skills, maybe we add some restrictions". The pool holds weapons, adapters, crystals, evolutions, skills **and apparel**. (1) **Normal enemies roll from the pool**, inside limits; how much an enemy gets (how many skills, what tier) **is based on total pillar points**. (2) **Mini bosses and bosses** are not full presets: they have **skills they will always have** ("this boss must have these three skills"); anything beyond that may still be rolled. (3) **Slots need not be filled**: "just because we have 6 slots doesn't mean we have to use all of them". (4) **Drops:** what an enemy is using has a **higher chance to drop** than the rest of the pool. (5) An enemy is rolled **once, when the run starts**. (6) **Seeing what an enemy has** is not free: it is a **field skill / an evolution's field skill**.
**Defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** each enemy type keeps its shape (its class, weapon types and elements limit what it may roll), so enemies stay readable; gear is rolled first, then only skills that gear can use (D-224); the roll uses the run seed (not BattleRandom, which is one stream per fight and is not sent to co-op clients; TrialPoolDesign §5, corrected 2026-11-17) so co-op players and a retried fight see the same enemy. References: Hades' region pools and Slay the Spire's act pools (one pool gives an area its identity); Persona's and Expedition 33's fixed boss signatures.
**Whose pillar points (Crown, 2026-11-16):** the **enemy's own**: "for example 1 1 1, we might expect them to have 1 equipment with 3 skills or something". An enemy's Mind / Body / Spirit levels, authored on its template, set how many pieces and skills it rolls and at what tier; the exact table is tuned later (a named table with // TODO values). This replaces the Q-518 stopgap enemy fill (built in B-518) when built (`Design/AssetOverhaulPlan.md` §7).

**Plan questions answered (Crown, 2026-11-16; `Design/AssetOverhaulPlan.md` questions 18 and 19):** **apparel** on enemies is rolled and drops like weapons. **The merchant** sells from the trial's pool **and** from a **merchant pool of its own**: things unique to merchants that do not drop from enemies. **Guaranteed pieces, for every enemy (Crown, same day; answers questions 15 and 16):** "we need a way to have guaranteed skills and weapons; we should be able to do this with all enemies, and if we don't provide anything it just grabs from the pool". Any enemy, not only a boss, can carry an **always-has list of skills and gear**; what the list does not fill is rolled from the pool (a boss's fixed skills plus a roll); an empty list means the enemy is rolled entirely from the pool. Elites need no rule of their own: they are enemies with a list. **"Build a bear" (Crown, same day):** "let's say we have an enemy type but we give them a sword, so when you encounter them they will always have a sword; then we can set some parameters for what they can grab from the pool, i.e. additional gear". So an enemy type is built in two parts: **what it always has** (the list above) and **what it may grab from the pool** (its parameters: how many more pieces, which kinds, and how many skills). Default (Crown may change): where an enemy sets no parameters, its own pillar points decide the amounts (the pillar table), so a plain enemy needs nothing authored. **Drops (Crown, same day; answers question 17):** what an enemy was using is **3 times likelier to drop** than the rest of the pool (a named tunable). Default (Crown may change): always-has pieces can drop too, and a boss drops one of its always-has skills the first time it is beaten. **The fallback table (Crown, 2026-11-17; answers question 14):** version **A** of `Design/TrialPoolDesign.md`: the piece's tier decides the skill count, so a small enemy with an F piece has 1 skill and grows into more; any enemy can still be given more by hand. Crown: "though I guess we should map out what the levelling would look like": how an enemy's pillar points grow across a trial, and what each step gives it, is to be mapped out before the table's numbers are set. **The lowest enemy (Crown, same day):** "for 1 1 1 I think it can be weapons or adapters, it's either or": a 1 1 1 enemy has **one piece, a weapon or an adapter**, not both; "7 7 7 is anything and everything". The steps between are being mapped (the brain first proposed each pillar running its own part; replaced by the ladder below).
**The ladder (Crown, 2026-11-17: "yes, that makes sense"):** ladder 3 of `docs/research/enemy-kit-scaling.md`. The **average of the enemy's three pillars, rounded down, picks one of seven rows** (how much it carries: pieces, top tier, skills); **its highest pillar sets the lean** (which kind of gear and skills come first). Only 7 7 7 reaches the top row. The row numbers are one named table to tune. An enemy is set by the three numbers on its own data, plus its always-has list and parameters where wanted.
**Limits, not a ladder (Crown, 2026-11-17, replaces the ladder and the fallback table above):** "we are not giving them F tier weapons only, they would get any tier: think of an adventure, you met a low level character with a high level sword". "Do we need these restrictions, or do we just need limits instead: they can take whatever they want from the pool, but we can provide gear and skills that they will always have; then for whatever they get from the pool we put a limit on, i.e. can have more than one weapon? max skills, etc." So an enemy is: **what it always has**, plus **whatever it takes from the pool at any tier the pool holds, inside its limits** (how many weapons, adapters and skills, and the like). An enemy's pillar points no longer decide what gear or tier it may carry. Crown also asked that the limits cover a Resonator's five adapters and every weapon combination, and that a one-weapon enemy has more than one skill so the fight is not dull. **The limits (Crown, 2026-11-17: "those make sense; we should have them default to any"):** each enemy can set, and each is optional: the most weapons, the most adapters, the most skills, which kinds it may take (a weapon type, an element), a tier range, and whether an evolution is allowed. **Unset means any**: with no limits an enemy wears what it was given and fills the rest of what its class can wear from the pool (so a Resonator enemy can carry five adapters, and every weapon combination its class allows can turn up). **Enemies follow the player's rules (Crown, same day):** an enemy's skills are capped by its gear's slots, exactly as a player's are. "Though we have given free rein, we might make some defaults for some enemies, i.e. provide a low rank enemy with a D class weapon; it will be up to us to set the limit; it should just follow our rules in the background if not, plus it puts everyone on equal footing." So a dull one-skill enemy is fixed by giving it a better piece, not by breaking the slot rule.
**Shared by the template, rolled per enemy (Crown, same day):** "we might make a bandit template, which they would all share the same skills to an extent; for example if we use an E weapon then we could fill one slot, then let them all have different skills for their second slot". What the template gives (its always-has gear and skills) is the same on every enemy of that type; what is left open is **rolled separately for each enemy**, so three bandits share their weapon and first skill and differ in the rest.
**How much drops (Crown, same day):** **one piece per won fight, for each player**; a boss always drops. What the enemies were using stays 3 times likelier to be that piece. **A dropped piece arrives empty** (no crystal, no skills; crystals and skills are their own drops), **unless the piece was set up as prebuilt**, which drops prebuilt. It drops at the tier the enemy wore.
**Crystals in a trial (Crown, same day):** a trial can give **every element's** crystals, "but leans more to its element crystal, i.e. you have a higher chance of getting higher tier crystals": the trial's own element comes up more often and is likelier to come at a higher tier (the weights are named tunables).
**A dropped skill the player already knows (Crown, same day):** "you get essence, remember": **duplicates are allowed** ("since everything is tiered"). The player chooses one of three things: **replace the one they have with it**, **add it to the inventory** ("in case you want to give it to a teammate"), or **convert it to essence**. There is no second roll. (This replaces the brain's first note that it simply paid essence.) **It applies to everything in the inventory** (weapons, adapters, evolutions, skills, apparel), **except utility items**, which stack as now. **Utility means the crystals and stones used up in a fight** (Crown, 2026-11-17, confirming the brain's reading), so crystals and stones stack and are not offered the three choices; "though we should have the option to dismantle utility as well": a utility item can be dismantled for essence.
**The merchant's shelf (Crown, same day):** a merchant has **its own pool** and "would also be able to draw from the wider pool": the shelf is split **half and half** between the merchant's own things and the trial's pool (the split is a named tunable).
**Seeing an enemy's kit (Crown, same day):** both forms exist, as **different variations of the skill**: one **used in the field** on an enemy before the fight (it costs EP, and what that enemy type carries is remembered for the run), and one that is **always on** once owned (enemies show their gear as the player gets close).
**All of the design's small questions are now answered** (2026-11-17); `Design/TrialPoolDesign.md` is to be brought in line with this entry before anything is built. The story question (why enemies carry gear you can win) is **answered by Crown (2026-11-17): "we don't need a lore reason for it; the reason is the trials are a simulation"**.
**Gaps closed after the rewrite (Crown, 2026-11-17):** (1) the one drop per won fight is **one thing of any kind**: a weapon, an adapter, a crystal, a skill (or apparel, an evolution), not a piece plus separate chances. (2) **Skills with no limit set:** an enemy fills **a small default number of its slots, 3** (a named tunable), not every slot; an enemy's own skill limit raises or lowers it. This is the one limit whose unset value is a number, not any. (3) **Each zone of a trial has its own tier range** for the pool: the first zone holds only the low tiers and later zones open the higher ones, so enemies and drops get stronger deeper in; a weak enemy with a strong piece is still possible, inside its zone's range. The ranges follow the zones' existing tier guide and are tunable. (4) **A boss's first defeat gives two things** (Crown: "first defeat give two things for a boss"): **one thing guaranteed from its presets, of any kind** (its always-has list: a skill, a weapon, an adapter and so on; Crown: "I meant presets in general, i.e. skills, gear, adapter... so essentially it's guaranteed") and the normal one drop; later defeats give the one drop. (5) Defaults not objected to: enemies' own crystals lean to the trial's element like the drops; the see-the-kit skill remembers an enemy type's shared part for the run and shows the full kit of the enemy it is used on. `DA_Ring_Adapter_C` goes with the old Generic inventory that still holds it (removal list group 3), not before.
**The trial asset lists what each rest spot has (Crown, 2026-11-17):** "we have the pool, but for the rest spot purchases should we not have somewhere on the trial asset where we add what they have? So it would say: trial pool, Blacksmith rest, etc." The trial's asset holds, beside its pool, **a list for each rest spot service** (D-214: the Blacksmith's anvil, the Spiritualist's ritual tree, the Jeweler's pedestal, the pillar shrine, the campfire, and V's rest) saying what that rest spot offers in this trial. **V's rest can give things that are not goods**: "things like a new spirit unlock, so you interact with him and gain a new body". **Their names (Crown, same day):** each rest spot reads with a possessive: **Blacksmith's Rest, Spiritualist's Rest, Jeweler's Rest, V's Rest** ("let's add 's ... whichever is the correct grammar"), on the trial asset and in what the player reads. **The shelf (Crown, same day):** a rest spot's shelf is **half from the trial's pool and half from that trial's list for the rest spot**; the trial's list takes the place of the merchant's own global list in a trial. Things on a rest spot's list that are not goods (V's spirit unlock) are given, not shelved. **How many (Crown, same day):** a rest spot sells **8 items**: "half of them gear and half of them crystals / stones, i.e. for healer and blacksmith": four gear, four crystals or stones, on the Blacksmith's and the healer's shelf. The count and the split are named tunables. **Apparel is included** (Crown): it counts on the gear side with weapons and adapters. **Corrections (Crown, same day):** "healer" was the **Jeweler** (autocorrect), so the two shops meant are the Jeweler's Rest and the Blacksmith's Rest. **Where each half comes from:** the **gear** half comes from the trial's pool and from the trial's list for that rest spot; **crystals and stones are available throughout and need not be listed**: they are drawn from every element with the trial's lean, and one that IS put on the list has **an increased chance of appearing**. **What each rest spot sells (Crown, same day):** every shop shows both halves, 8 items, four on the gear side and four on the crystal-and-stone side, each from its own kinds: **Jeweler's Rest: adapters, and crystals. Blacksmith's Rest: apparel and weapons, and stones. Spiritualist's Rest: evolutions, and crystals and stones.** **Skills (Crown, same day):** "the Spiritualist should sell Refractions, and the Blacksmith should double and sell skills, so we should change the Blacksmith's name for rest to Weapon Master's Rest". So the **Spiritualist's Rest also sells Refractions** (spells), and the Blacksmith's rest spot is renamed **Weapon Master's Rest** and **also sells weapon skills** (abilities). Default (Crown may change): the skills are their own lines beside the 8, four of them, a named tunable.
**The limits merged (Crown, 2026-11-17, replaces the six):** "why don't we just merge most weapons, most adapters and evolution, since the max really is the Resonator aspect; also their class will determine certain conditions"; "so it essentially becomes their slot count, which is validated by their class". An enemy's limits are now five: **most gear** (one number, its slot count; the class decides which kinds fit and caps it), **most skills** (unset: 3), **which kinds** it may take (weapons, adapters, evolutions, apparel, and which weapon types), **which elements**, and a **tier range**. Unset still means any. Whether an evolution is allowed is no longer its own switch: it is one of the kinds.
**Skills are limited per piece (Crown, 2026-11-17, replaces the total of 3):** the skill limit is **how many skills each piece may hold**, not a total for the enemy ("with B it's dependent on their gear, simpler to manage"). **Unset means as many as the piece can hold** (its slots by tier, a weapon's attached crystal included), not 3: "I think it should be B, makes it unpredictable". For reference the most a piece holds is 12 on a weapon with a crystal and 6 on an adapter; a Generic can reach 30, a Resonator 42. **Innate spells get their own limit**, separate from this one; by default an enemy's innate places follow D-240 (three, plus one per pillar point).
### D-224 — A run starts with no skills; weapons carry no skills; a ring's tier is the built average
**Status:** ANSWERED in part (Crown, 2026-11-15, on the asset overhaul plan, D-223); two points open below · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** the plan's skill and tier parts
**Answer (Crown):** (1) **A ring's tier:** showing the parts ("C (ring F · crystal S)") "is pointless, we should build the average": the average of the ring's tier and its crystal's is the adapter's real tier, built into how it plays, not only shown (answers Q-223a; replaces "display only"). (2) **Skills at a run's start:** "they should start the run with no skills / abilities". (3) **Weapons carry no skills:** "we should remove skills from the weapon, there's no need; that's what building presets is for". A weapon piece has no built-in skills and holds no skill picks; the skills are part of the preset / what is worn. This reverses D-145 (picks on the item) in favour of Q-085 A (picks are the loadout's), and makes the plan's "grey an unlearned pick on a given piece" question moot.
**No built-in skills on any gear; skills name what they need (Crown, 2026-11-15):** "remove preset skills from weapons, adapters and evolutions… we will have skills that require the evolution, weapon or adapter instead". Gear carries no skill lists at all (a weapon's `presetAbilities` and `defaultSpells`, a ring's spells, an evolution's spells). A **skill is its own thing and states its requirement** (a weapon type, an adapter, an evolution, an element); it can be used when the worn gear meets it.
**Where a run's skills come from (Crown, 2026-11-15, answers (a)):** "you buy them / defeat enemies for them". A run may give "one or two as a starting skill, i.e. a generic bolt", or there may be **trial skills** that change the start: "start with this spell in a trial, increase starting spells, etc." (a trial-side track; the same idea as the starting-gear track noted in D-221).
**Answers on the plan's revision 4 (Crown, 2026-11-15):** (Q3) the averaged tier does **not** lower the ring's own stats: "you would still get the ring's stats, the skills you use just wouldn't be as powerful". The ring's stat lines stay on the ring's own tier; the average drives how strong the skills cast through it are. (Q4) **Weapons average with their crystal too**, "and it would affect the status build-up". **Only the elemental side** (Crown, 2026-11-15): the weapon-and-crystal average drives the elemental (infused) strength and the **elemental** status build-up; **physical** attacks and physical status build-up are not affected and stay on the weapon's own tier. (Q7) Conjured (locked) rings become ordinary empty rings, "but you can find prebuilt rings, i.e. built with an evolution crystal or a normal crystal": a found ring may come with its socket already filled.
**More answers (Crown, 2026-11-15):** (Q8) a sword with a shield **counts as one weapon**, "as if it was one weapon, i.e. like a sword": the shield adds nothing to the D-211 slot average. (Q9) **the starting bolt is in the inventory**: "you would have to attach it yourself"; nothing is pre-attached. **Class starting kits, to be settled later, first shape:** a Refractor starts with nothing ("since they have innate"), a Generic with one weapon, a Resonator with two rings. (Q6, by that) innate spells stay: "no skills at the start" means no learned or bought ones. ((b)) **Yes: a character keeps the skills found in a run, "similar to their other equipment"**: found skills follow the found-gear rules of D-219 / D-221.
**The Broken Darkness pool (Crown, 2026-11-15):** "the pool is just saying we have these empty spaces to learn spells, so it might not even need to be attached to the character". A pool is capacity (empty places where spells are learned), not a list of spells the character is born with; **nothing is pre-filled in it** ("we wouldn't add presets to it"). The plan says whether it needs to be authored on the character at all (the asset dump shows `bDSpellPools` empty on every inventory).
**Found skills follow the equipment rules (Crown, 2026-11-15, the plan's questions 10 to 12):** (10) **only the skills that are slotted are kept**; "everything else returns to the pool, like gear". (12) a kept skill "would follow the same rules as equipment" on a lost run: what D-219 does to worn gear (found things are not kept out of wipes; what is kept or owned takes the tier loss) applies to slotted skills too. (11, agreed by Crown) a kept skill is for the hub and its presets; a trial still starts on the class kit with no skills, and the trial mirror is where a preset's skills are bought back. **Default that follows (plan, Crown may change):** a skill's own-tier power reads the learned copy's tier, not the asset's; today only the tier gap reads the copy, so the drop would boost the skill instead of costing (`Design/AssetOverhaulPlan.md` §5.3).
**Open, for Crown:** nothing blocking; the exact contents of each class kit are tuned later.
**Notes for Claude:** D-111 (learned skills stay on the character) and D-145 are affected; the plan says which mechanics change when the averaged tier is the real one (today the crystal's tier alone decides spell slots, output and wear; the ring's decides its stat lines and dismantle).

### D-223 — The character and gear data assets are rebuilt in the new format, not adapted
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15; brings D-221's stage 4 forward and widens it) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** the asset overhaul jobs
**Answer (Crown):** "instead of using the old ones we should overhaul it to match what we want to achieve. This is where the tweaks and problems are coming from: we're trying to make an old format fit our new one, when we should really be removing and updating, creating new characters if need be." The character templates, their inventories and starting loadouts, and the test gear are **rebuilt in the new format** (D-220, D-221, D-222): new assets are made, the old ones are deleted, and the code stops reading the old layout rather than converting it.
**Why (the evidence):** `Audits/CharacterAssetDump.md`: every loadout is still authored as primary / secondary slots; crystals are baked into weapons; weapon spell lists and old ability lists are filled; two characters are identical; stats are placeholder 3s; nine weapons have no attack or stance; names are misspelled.
**The first clean gear set (Crown, 2026-11-15):** two weapon types, as **four separate pieces: one sword, one shield and two axes** (the sword and shield are two pieces held together, the axes a pair of two pieces); **five rings**; **two evolutions**. **A ring is built like a weapon**: "you have an empty ring adapter, then you select the crystal you want to attach": a ring carries no element of its own; its element is the crystal in its socket.
**A ring is named by what is in it (Crown, 2026-11-15):** "with the adapter we will say the crystal / evolution type, example Phoenix Ring, Void Ring". An adapter's shown name is built from its socket: an evolution gives its name ("Phoenix Ring"), a crystal gives its element ("Void Ring", "Fire Ring"), then the adapter's form (Ring). An empty one is **"Adapter Ring"** (Crown, 2026-11-15: "Ring Adapter… or Adapter Ring for simplicity"), so the form word always comes last, as in "Phoenix Ring"; the name is worked out, like the held weapon type, wherever the adapter is named (the quick strip, the field strip, the Equipment screen).
**A ring's tier (Crown, 2026-11-15):** "the tier comes from the overall ring and crystal attached": an adapter's shown tier is worked out from the ring's own tier and the tier of what is in its socket. It is **the average of the two** (Crown: "or average even"), rounded down as D-211 does for a pair of weapons; an empty ring shows its own tier.
**Two pieces with the same name (Crown, 2026-11-15, replaces Q-516's number-only default):** the **tier** tells them apart first (the tier badge beside the name: a C "Lightning Ring" and an F "Lightning Ring"); **only if the tier is the same too** is a number added ("Lightning Ring", "Lightning Ring 2").
**The two evolutions' names (Crown, 2026-11-16):** **Gale Force** (Wind; in a ring it reads "Gale Force Ring") and **Phoenix Fire** (Fire; "Phoenix Fire Ring"). The weapon piece names in the plan (Arming Sword, Round Shield, Hand Axe, Bearded Axe) stand unless Crown changes them.
**Notes for Claude:** the order is a short asset plan first (the new authored shape, the list of characters and kits to make, what is deleted), Crown's OK on the list, then the code reads the new shape only, then the editor worker makes the assets and removes the old ones. Old saves are wiped (D-060).

### D-222 — The field picker is a quick strip, not a wheel
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15; replaces the wheel's shape in D-217 / D-218, not its rules) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** the round wheel is too compressed. The picker becomes a **quick strip like Breath of the Wild's**: holding RB opens a row of the character's equipment; **left / right moves along the pieces** (weapons, adapters or rings, the innate, the evolution), each shown by **its icon** (the icons assigned to weapons and rings, the icon table); **up / down cycles the source** (the element) of the piece under the cursor. The source is **not drawn**: it is written as text under the row, as the piece's name and the source in brackets, e.g. **"Excalibur (Fire)"**.
**The text and the frame (Crown, 2026-11-15, after the mockup; replaces the bracket form above):** the name line is the name alone, and the source goes on the second line after the type, with a dot: "**Storm Ring**" then "**Ring · Lightning**"; "**Flame Sword and Void Shield**" then "**Sword and Shield · Fire**". With no source the second line is the type alone. **One frame goes around the icons and the text together.**
**What stays:** every rule of D-217 and D-218: tap RB attacks, the hold opens it and it stays up, picking confirms, the pick is the weapon in hand and the parry, the names (a pair reads "Flame Sword and Void Shield"; the held type is worked out from the pieces), Reality's "(R) Fire", Broken Darkness's "Dark Fire", Water's Ice.
**Defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** on a pad the D-pad or left stick moves (left / right pieces, up / down source); on keyboard A / D or the mouse wheel moves along the pieces, W / S changes the source, a click picks. The piece in hand is marked. Reference: Breath of the Wild's and Tears of the Kingdom's quick-select row, liked for showing every item at full size without a menu.

### D-221 — The loadout is restructured: one "wearing now" per character, presets at a mirror
**Status:** ANSWERED in outline (Crown, 2026-11-15); the staged plan comes back for Crown's OK before building · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** the restructure jobs
**Why:** two independent audits (`Audits/LoadoutAlignmentAudit.md`, `Audits/CharacterStateAudit.md`) and the asset dump (`Audits/CharacterAssetDump.md`) show the loadout is still stored the old way (a primary slot, a secondary slot, a ring list, a shown flag; a lore loadout rebuilt from the asset on every map; no record of which loadout is in use). Crown: "I think the old way is outdated".
**Answer (Crown):** (1) Each character has exactly one **"wearing now"**: the equipment list (each piece with its sockets and skills), which piece is ACTIVE, the apparel and the wheel's pick. It travels with the character between maps and is saved; the template gives the starting gear once. Every reader (the Equipment screen, the wheel, the strip, the fight, the parry, the run rules) reads it. (2) **Presets are separate**: saved copies of "wearing now", made and put on at a **mirror**. The Equipment screen only edits what is worn; it no longer switches loadouts. (3) **The mirror is in the hub (free) and at a point in the trial, where changing costs**: "in the trial you pay a cost to gain that gear and it's free in the hub". (4) A **"last used gear" preset** is kept automatically, and **the last used gear is the starting gear in the trial** as well (Crown: "your last used gear becomes your starting gear… in the trial"). It includes found gear kept from the last run (D-219, "Starting gear").
**Crystals and evolutions in a preset (Crown, 2026-11-15, replaces the first draft's "not what is in the sockets"):** a preset **does** remember its crystals: when it is put on, "a crystal will be attached if you have it in inventory". **Evolutions are treated like the equipment**: they are part of the preset and are "provided for with the gear".
**Defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** a remembered crystal that is not in the inventory leaves the socket as it is, with a note; a crystal already in the socket is only replaced when the preset's crystal is there to go in (the replaced one is destroyed, CL-28b, so the mirror says so before it is confirmed). A preset that names a piece no longer owned leaves that slot empty with a note. The trial mirror's price **is based on the tiers of the items** it gives (Crown, 2026-11-15); by default it is paid in the run's Prisms (D-156's run currency), each piece priced by its tier and summed, tuned later. References: Monster Hunter's equipment sets at the item box, Final Fantasy XIV's gear sets.
**A default loadout at the trial's start (Crown, 2026-11-15, answering the plan's Q1 and Q5; this replaces "the last used gear is the starting gear in the trial" above and D-219's "Starting gear"):** found gear kept after a run is **not owned for good**: "it's just to have in the hub". "We make a default loadout that it resets to when you begin a trial, so everyone has the exact same starting point, and leave with their achievements." So a trial always starts on the default loadout; what a run ends with is worn in the hub; the trial mirror is where gear from a preset is bought back, priced by tier. **Q5:** delete `DA_Character_Template` (it duplicates `DA_Character_GenericLord` today and would drift after the changes); the played test character moves to the remaining asset (an editor step).
**The trial's start, found gear and presets (Crown, 2026-11-15):** (1) A trial always starts on **class-specific defaults**, "maybe F tier gear". (2) **Found gear is for the hub**, and is only kept when the run's conditions are met: **an extract or beating the boss**; a wipe does not provide it. (3) **Mid-run, at the trial mirror, a preset can be saved** of what is worn, found gear included; the found gear in it only counts once it is kept under (2). (4) **Presets are kept.** (5) Idea to design later: "something that allows us to change starting gear as well, maybe a trial-specific one where you can get all your trial-related skills": a trial-side place or track that changes the starting defaults.
**Defaults taken for the plan's other questions (Crown may change):** the trial mirror charges only for what it changes (Q4); on a lost run only the pieces drop tier, not their crystals or evolutions (Q6); on a quit the pieces worn at that moment that the player still owns lose the tier (Q7). Q2 no longer applies.
**More answers (Crown, 2026-11-16):** the **trial mirror is a type of rest point** (D-214: a themed object that is the service). The **Mind / Body / Spirit line** is left off the field cards (D-216): "you can check it in the menu". **Infusion wear lands on the crystal** ("that's what has durability"), not the ring. The defaults taken in D-217 to D-225 are agreed, and enemies "would roll what they have access to" (D-225).
**Stages:** (1) the one "wearing now" record, stored with the character; (2) every reader on it; (3) the mirror and presets (placeholder UI first); (4) the old fields deleted from code and data assets. Saves below the new version are wiped (D-060). **The staged plan:** `Design/LoadoutRestructurePlan.md` (2026-11-15, agreed by Crown 2026-11-15; answers in its §7).

### D-220 — The loadout's Weapons tab is the Equipment tab
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15; renames part of the v2 loadout screen, D-210) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** the tab is called **Equipment**, not Weapons, and its add row reads **"+ Add equipment"**. Choosing it first asks **"Add a weapon" or "Add an adapter"**, then lists the pieces. The list is not organised by weapon: the first slot is not "the weapon slot"; every entry (weapon or adapter) is a piece of equipment.
**Notes for Claude:** the tabs read Equipment / Skills / Apparel / Cosmetics. The class limits are unchanged (a Generic: 3 pieces, at most 2 weapons and 1 adapter). Player-facing text says "adapter"; a ring is one form of adapter.

### D-219 — What you finish or leave a run wearing is what you wear in the hub
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** "the equipment you finish or leave the run with is the equipment you have in the hub". The loadout as it stands at the end of a run (finished or extracted) carries into the hub: the ACTIVE weapon, the wheel's pick (D-218), what is in each socket, the rings and apparel. The hub does not put back the loadout the run started with.
**A quit is the exception (Crown, 2026-11-15):** "if you quit then the progress doesn't count, so they would be how they were before the run". A quit run puts the loadout back to how it was when the run began, as it takes back the items found (Q-439). Finishing and extracting carry the run's loadout into the hub.
**Out of wipes (Crown, 2026-11-15):** it carries over too: "it still counts as a complete run". Only a quit (and a game closed mid-run) restores the run-start loadout.
**Gear found in the run (Crown, 2026-11-15; refines D-111 / ER-65b):** only what is **equipped** at the end is kept; found gear left in the inventory goes back as before. The kept found gear is worn in the hub, and is **lost when the next run starts**. Crown's reason: hub secrets can then be found with the gear's movement and abilities from the last run.
**Equipped crystals (Crown, 2026-11-15):** a found crystal that is in a socket at the end is kept with its gear, on every carrying ending (out of wipes included), and the player may change it in the hub. Like the gear, it is lost when the next run starts.
**Starting gear (Crown, 2026-11-15, changes the two lines above that say kept gear is lost when the next run starts):** "whatever you finished, left or died with becomes your starting gear". The gear worn when a run ends by a finish, an extract (leaving early) or running out of wipes is the starting gear of the next run, found gear and socketed crystals included: it is **not** removed when the next run starts. A quit still puts back the run-start loadout (its progress does not count).
**A wipe costs tier (Crown, 2026-11-15):** "if you wipe with gear you lose two ranks from the tier… to stop people from just keeping S tier". Gear carried out of a run lost to wipes drops tier ranks, not below F: two, or "maybe even 3" (Crown). It is one named, tunable number; it starts at 3 (S to C) and is tuned in play. **Which gear and when (Crown, 2026-11-15):** "everything you were wearing, and only when the run ends or if you quit the game mid run": every piece worn (owned gear included, not only found gear) drops, once, when the run is lost to wipes, and also when the player quits mid-run or closes the game mid-run, so quitting cannot dodge the cost. A single wipe that does not end the run costs nothing; a finish or an extract costs nothing.
**Out of wipes pays a quarter (Crown, 2026-11-16):** "let's give them the 25% currency since they are losing 3 tiers". The run's currency payout on running out of wipes stays at a quarter (ER-65d), now confirmed.
**Notes for Claude:** built by B-503t. Found *items* (crystals and the like) keep their own rule (stored on a finish or extract, taken back on a quit).

### D-218 — The wheel's pick is what the character holds, and their parry
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** (1) Picking a **weapon** on the field wheel (D-217) **changes the weapon in hand**: the character shows and swings that weapon. Picking an **element source** (the innate, an evolution, an adapter) **hides the weapon**. (2) **Whatever is picked is the character's active parry** in the fight: "whatever you use is what your active parry will be". The parry priority setting (B-392 / B-393, `EParryElementPriority`) already decides which element a parry uses; the wheel's pick now drives it.
**The wheel is a toggle (Crown, 2026-11-15):** a hold opens it and it stays on screen; the mouse works on it (cursor, hover, click); the centre shows only the result ("Sword", "Fire Sword").
**A Generic and the pulse (Crown, 2026-11-15, answers Q-503s B):** "a Generic would only use the pulse if they had an adapter". So a Generic's **ring** pick keeps the weapon in hand, infused ("Lightning Sword"); only an **adapter** pick (or the innate / an evolution) hides the weapon and throws the pulse.
**Q-503s answered (Crown, 2026-11-15):** **A, yes**: a weapon picked on the wheel becomes the loadout's ACTIVE weapon, so the fight starts with it in hand and the loadout screen shows it as ACTIVE (one truth). **C, yes**: the wheel drives the guard: a weapon pick guards with the weapon and the parry takes that weapon's element (a plain weapon: a spark only); an element-source pick hides the weapon, guards unarmed, and the parry takes that element (Ice, "(R) Fire", "Dark Fire" included). The guard style and parry priority settings stop being player choices; they stay as the fallback for members never picked on the wheel (AI seats) and as debug commands.

### D-217 — The field attack strip: the weapon type above the card, LB changes its infusion
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15; changes D-215 point 3 and B-503h's chip) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** the separate field attack chip with the weapon's name goes. Instead a **strip above the character's card** names what the field attack is: for a weapon user the **weapon type** ("Sword"), and when infused the element first ("Fire Sword"). **LB (held) changes the infusion**: it starts plain ("Sword") and cycles the character's available sources. A Refractor or Resonator has no weapon line: the strip just names the source element in use ("Fire" for a Fire Refractor, or a Resonator whose main ring is Fire), so adapters and rings need no names here. The weapon is the loadout's ACTIVE entry; LB no longer cycles weapons, rings and evolutions as separate entries (D-215 point 3).
**LB, press and hold (Crown, 2026-11-15, replaces the default below):** **pressing** LB (R on keyboard) changes the **source**; **holding** it changes the **element** within that source. Crown's words: "pressing it changes source, holding it changes the element, since elements from innate, adapters and evolution cannot cycle sources; also for Reality and BD we should make it so you can cycle sources, so for BD when it's Darkness they can hold LB to cycle their dark sources (which we will base on whatever other gear they have), same for Reality". Read as: a source with one fixed element (innate, an adapter, an evolution) has nothing to change on a hold; a Broken Darkness character on Darkness, or a Reality one, holds LB to cycle which of their other gear the Darkness / Reality is drawn through.
**The LB wheel (Crown, 2026-11-15, replaces the press / hold split above):** **holding LB opens a small wheel** of every source and element the character can use (the weapon plain, each infusion source, and for Reality and Broken Darkness their "(R) Fire" / "Dark Fire" steps as entries); the stick picks, on PC the mouse wheel scrolls it, and **letting go confirms**. A **quick tap** still steps to the next entry. Reference: the weapon wheels of Doom Eternal and GTA V, liked because the whole set is visible and the place is never lost. The names on the wheel are the strip's names (Q-503l).
**The wheel on RB, loadout entries on it (Crown, 2026-11-15, after the v2 mockup; replaces the LB wheel's contents):** the wheel moves to **RB (held)**. Its slices are the character's **loadout entries** (the weapon, each ring, the evolution, the innate and so on), not elements. The **left stick** moves around the wheel; **D-pad left / right changes the element** of the entry under the stick (where it has more than one: Reality's "(R) Fire", Broken Darkness's "Dark Fire", a weapon's infusion); letting go confirms. **The played character's card is always the first card** in the strip, so it needs no highlight.
**RB, tap and hold (Crown, 2026-11-15):** **tapping RB attacks; holding RB opens the wheel** to change the source. The attack stays on RB (it does not move to RT). Keyboard: left mouse attacks; hold R for the wheel, the mouse picks a slice, the mouse wheel or A / D changes the element.
**Ice (Crown, 2026-11-15: "for water we make ice part of its cycle, like how BD and Reality change"):** the old *held* attack (hold 0.4 s: Water becomes Ice, B-214f) can no longer live on a hold of the attack key, since a hold is the wheel now. Ice becomes an element choice on the Water entry (D-pad left / right), which is what the hold was for.
**Defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** the swing or pulse takes the chosen element's colour. Trial only: the hub shows no strip, as it shows no HP (D-216).

### D-216 — One party card everywhere; what it shows depends on where you are
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** the party cards in the field are the battle screen's party cards ("should basically be our battle one"). What they show: **hub**: no HP, no EP, no status (who is in the party only); **trial**: HP and EP only; no status build-up bar (Crown, 2026-11-15: it is always set to 0 after a battle, SB-18, so it would never show anything); **battle**: everything (those plus the fight-only parts). The field attack's chip (D-215) sits after the cards in the same style.
**The hub too (Crown, 2026-11-15):** "because we have a loadout in the hub we can use R, attack etc. within the hub". The field attack and the wheel work in the hub as in a trial (D-219 keeps the run's loadout there; hub secrets use it).
**Default that follows (Crown may change):** the strip above the played character's card shows in the hub as well, since it is the only sign of the wheel's pick; the hub card itself still shows the name only (D-216).
**Notes for Claude:** one card class with a show-level (Hub / Trial / Battle), not three widgets. Built by B-503j (B-503i was the visual fix of the field strip, and hid the hub's HP bar). Note: SB-18 (D-013) empties the status bars after every fight, so the trial's build-up bar reads empty between fights unless that rule changes.

### D-215 — The field attack: one swing per weapon type, a pulse for casters, LB cycles the whole loadout
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15; extends B-214f and the B-214g source switch) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** (1) The field attack is the weapon's **standard attack**: one shared swing per weapon type, the same swing as the battle's basic hit without the dash (e.g. `AM_SAS_LightHit`'s strike), so the field and the fight match. (2) **Resonators and Refractors** throw an **energy pulse** instead: a burst from the hand that flies a few metres and engages, in the source's element colour, like Expedition 33's ranged field burst. (3) Out of battle, **LB cycles the field attack through the whole loadout**: each weapon, ring, the innate element and each evolution, in loadout order (replaces B-214g's Innate → Weapon → Ring switch).
**Defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** a weapon entry uses that weapon's swing; a ring, innate or evolution entry uses the pulse in its element (for any class), since only a weapon has a swing. Reach: swing about 2 m (as today), pulse about 7 m, tuned in PIE. The cycled entry shows as a small icon and name near the party strip for 2 s (Expedition 33 and Tales of Arise show the field action this way). The choice is saved per character.

### Q-552 — D-239's smaller choices (pouch and skills on a swap, co-op seats, reaching the gear)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-552, D-239) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-239 says a swapped-out member keeps their gear. Three small cases are left. None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the default (brain, 2026-11-16):
- **(a) Pouch and skills stay with the swapped-out member too** (Crown: "the one you traded for keeps their current loadout ... you need to unequip the gear if you want to give it to the new recruit"). So the player moves things over by hand before the swap. A party member who steps out already kept its pouch and skills; nothing changed for it. **Exception kept:** a recruit found in this run who steps out has left for good, so it still frees what it wore and its skills go to the host, as when the run ends (D-152). Otherwise those pieces would stay locked with someone the player can never reach again.
- **(b) Co-op:** a player taking over an AI seat still takes that seat's gear to the host (D-150 point 4). Unchanged.
- **Reaching a benched or swapped-out member's gear:** **in the hub**, walk up to them, invite them back, take the pieces off on the Equipment screen, then dismiss them. **Inside a run, you can't:** a member who stepped out is not in the party, and no screen opens them. So take pieces off *before* the swap (Crown's own words). They get them back when they rejoin in the hub.
- **Known limits:** a quit does not put back the old record of a recruit picked up mid-run (only members who began the run get theirs back, D-219). A wearer mark left over from an older save or a dismissed member now holds its piece; only run recruits, who see the whole armoury, can notice, since owners see their own pieces.
- **Not done:** a recruit picked up mid-run keeps its skills as before (it starts with none of its own run's). The "base levels" part of starting from scratch waits for stage 3's levels.

### Q-534 — Restore's smaller choices (which crystals, overshoot, cost, where)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-534, D-231) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-231 sets Restore's amounts (the same tier fills it, a lower tier by the merge ratio); a few small cases are left. None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default:
- **Amounts:** one crystal restores value(its tier) / value(the gem's tier) of full durability, from the merge values (`GetCrystalValue`: F 1, E 2, D 4, C 8, B 16, A 32, S 96). So one tier down restores a half, two down a quarter, and an A a third of an S (an S is worth three A).
- **Which crystals:** only the gem's own type. The **cheapest are spent first**, lowest tier up, and only as many as needed. Reference: Monster Hunter's and Diablo's crafting take the lowest-grade materials that satisfy a recipe first.
- **A higher tier than the gem** may be spent and restores it in full; **the extra is lost** (no change back). Lower tiers are tried first, so this only happens when nothing smaller is held.
- **Short of crystals:** what is held is spent and the gem comes back part way. Nothing held: Restore says which crystals it needs and spends nothing. A share too small to add a point spends nothing.
- **No money:** Restore costs the crystals only, no Prism fee (merging has one; restoring is already paid in crystals). The paid repair (ER-37) is unchanged and still brings a worn or kept broken gem back for money.
- **Where (D-231 final):** on **any socketed gem below full**, and on a **locked** gem at 0 (an unlocked one at 0 is destroyed at fight end, ER-35). Out of a fight only. On the Equipment screen, the crystal socket's list shows a **Restore** line under the gem saying what it spends ("Garnet (C) back to 100% for 1 Garnet (D)"); A on it restores. A on the gem itself still takes it out as before, so an unlocked worn gem can still come out. No new screen.
- **A kept broken gem in a fight:** the piece stays worn. The gem gives no element (so no element key, no Amp) and its spells stay listed but greyed (D-083); a ring holding it is skipped when the active ring swaps. Its stone still counts.
- **The word (D-231 point 4):** the player text says **wear**; the number keeps its meaning (what is left, out of the max). Changed: the **Wear** detail line ("40 / 60") on the loadout screen's details box (the worn piece and each socket choice), the inventory screen's tile details, and the Spiritualist's repair tab ("40 of 60", its state column a percent left, or "broken"); the **Durability Stone** shows as **Wear Stone**, and its description says wear. Not changed, no word to change: the battle HUD's "AD 3 / 5" and "WD 2 / 4" (adapter and weapon, D-107) and the old command-menu header ("AD:3/5"). **For Crown:** with the word wear, "40 / 60" can read as 40 worn; say if the number should flip to show what is used.

### Q-532 — D-231's smaller choices (a fixed prebuilt's stone, its evolution, older saves)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-532, D-231) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-231 settles the rules; three small cases are left. None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default:
- **A fixed prebuilt's stone:** ~~cannot come off~~. **Changed by Crown (2026-11-16): "removing one shouldn't clear the other".** On every weapon and adapter each socket comes off alone, and what comes out is destroyed (B-533). So a fixed prebuilt's stone comes off; only its built-in crystal stays.
- **An evolution prebuilt** (a find with an evolution, B-522b) follows the same setting. Crystal Removable is **on by default** (Crown, D-231 "Removal and restore"; B-533), so B-522b's check 4 works as before: the evolution goes back to the owned evolutions.
- **Dismantling a fixed evolution:** refused too. An evolution fixed in a prebuilt can't leave it by being dismantled (`RemoveEvolutionInstance`), or the lock could be got round for essence.
- **A fixed crystal that wears to nothing:** ~~stays, broken, and is mended~~. Corrected (B-533): today a broken gem is destroyed at the end of the fight (ER-35, `CrystalBreak`), fixed or not; only an evolution is kept broken. **Built (B-534):** a broken gem locked to its piece is now kept broken at fight end and restored in place (Q-534). Crown's Restore (D-231 "Removal and restore") is the planned answer; it needs the fight-end break to keep a fixed prebuilt's gem, as it keeps evolutions.
- **Older saves:** saves below version 43 are wiped (D-060), so no older save can turn a piece prebuilt with a stone or crystal already on it. A find whose socket couldn't be filled (full pouch) arrives as a plain piece, so a crystal the player sockets later is never locked.

### Q-527 — D-226's smaller choices (ring slots, tier lines, named adapters, prebuilt stock)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-527, D-226) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-226 settles the rules; a few small cases are left. None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default:
- **Which name field is "its own name":** the asset's existing `Name` (no new field). It counts as the adapter's own unless it is empty, the class default ("Unnamed Equipment") or "Adapter Ring" itself, so the clean set's empty adapter keeps being named by its socket. Diablo's uniques show their own name, plain items their base and affixes; the same split.
- **A weapon's detail lines** follow the adapter's: "Weapon tier" and "Crystal tier" replace the averaged line there too, so both kinds read alike.
- **The crystal line** shows only while the socket holds a gem or an evolution.
- **A prebuilt piece for sale** is priced as its piece plus a gem's or an evolution's shop price; if both are set, the gem goes in (as Q-522b). Buying one with an evolution for another member is refused, as any evolution is (Q-323). A weapon can be stocked prebuilt the same way.

### Q-525 — Settling a run's skills: the smaller choices
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-525, D-224 questions 10 to 12) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** Crown ruled that only slotted skills are kept and that they follow D-219's worn-gear rules. Some smaller cases are left. None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default (D-219's own rules for worn gear; Monster Hunter and Diablo keep what is equipped and drop the rest):
- **"Slotted"** means a pick in a slot of a worn weapon or adapter at the end. Innate spells, Broken Darkness pools and the evolution's spells are not gear picks: they are neither settled nor lowered, as a lost run lowers only the pieces and not their crystals or evolutions (D-221 Q6). An innate spell that is also picked into a gear slot is a slotted pick and takes the drop. Only skills in the run's list are settled: a pick naming a skill the run never had can't be cast, so it costs nothing.
- **A skill the hub already knew, bought back in the run** is the hub's one copy: the hub copy stays and takes the tier drop; the run's fresh copy goes.
- **The kit's starting bolt** counts as found: slotted at an extract, it joins the hub list.
- **A full hub list** (the learned-skill cap): a found skill with no room is not kept (counted in the log line).

### Q-523 — A run's skill list: what a kit member keeps at the start
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-523, D-224 stage S3) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** "a run starts with no learned or bought skills; innate spells stay" (D-224). Which skills count as innate, and who is reset? None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default (Hades and Slay the Spire: every run starts from the same fixed kit, and only what the character itself is keeps across runs):
- **Kept at the start:** the spells the worn record names apart from gear picks: the innate spells, the Broken Darkness pools and the character's own evolution's spells (the kit change already leaves those three in place). Also any picks the kit itself authors, so a kit authored with picks still casts; Crown's Q9 says the kit has none (an editor step).
- **Reset:** only a member who starts on its class kit. A member with no kit keeps what it wears and knows. A resumed run is not reset again.
- **Until S3c (B-525):** every ending puts the hub list back as it was, so a skill learned in the run is lost on every ending for now. S3c keeps the slotted ones.

### Q-522b — Prebuilt finds: the smaller choices
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-522b, Crown Q7) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** a prebuilt find names a piece and a gem or an evolution (`Design/AssetOverhaulPlan.md` §3 "Found pieces"). What if both are set, what if the socket can't be filled, and does a second copy of a piece still raise the duplicate choice? None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default (Diablo and Path of Exile drop socketed items as one item, already filled; a full inventory never loses the item itself):
- **Both set:** the gem goes in (the socket holds one); the editor's tooltip says the evolution is for when no gem is set. Data validation can flag it later.
- **The socket can't be filled** (a full gem pouch, or 5 evolutions already): the piece still arrives, empty, and a warning is logged; a gem that went into the pouch stays there. Nothing is lost.
- **A second copy** of a piece raises the usual "Gold or dismantle" choice (D-115), as any found piece does.

### Q-521 — Pieces start empty: what keeps its baked crystal until stage 6
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-521, D-224 stage 3) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the plan (`Design/AssetOverhaulPlan.md` §3 step 2) stops `CreateFromWeapon` / `CreateFromRing` copying the asset's baked crystal. Those factories also build every enemy's and every current character's gear from their old inventory and loadout assets, which have no other place for a crystal until stages 4 and 5 replace them. Taken literally, every enemy would lose its spells overnight. None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the project's own rule (build new alongside old, remove old paths only in stage 6, CLAUDE.md "Incremental") and the plan's intent ("a piece starts empty unless the find says what is in its socket"):
- **Starts empty:** anything new to the player: bought (and shown in a shop), found or dropped, given, a class kit piece, a piece restored from a save (its socket is the save's).
- **Keeps the baked crystal until stage 6:** only the old authored layout: an inventory asset's `Weapons` / `Rings` and an authored loadout's asset fallback (`CreateFromAuthoredWeapon` / `CreateFromAuthoredRing`, marked OBSOLETE). Stage 6 deletes them with the asset field.
- **Prebuilt finds** (Crown, Q7) come with stage 4: they reuse the starting gear's `Crystal` field.

### Q-520 — Skills name what they need: the smaller choices (stage S2)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-520, D-224 stage S2) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the plan (`Design/AssetOverhaulPlan.md` §5.2) says "`IsMet(Skill, FWornGear)`" and "the spells get `Kind = Adapter` or `Evolution`", but also "a spell with nothing set needs an adapter or an evolution with a spell source, as today". What does the rule check against, how do skills read before the editor pass sets them, and what do plain spells get? None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default (Path of Exile's skill gems and Monster Hunter's weapon-type moves check the item the skill is socketed into, and show the need on the skill):
- **The piece the skill sits in:** skills sit in a piece's slots (S1), so the rule checks that piece (a weapon by its main hand, an adapter, or the evolution seat), not anything else worn. An axe skill in the sword's list is not met even if an axe is worn elsewhere.
- **Not set yet (Kind "None"):** the old fields decide: an ability needs a weapon of its `RequiredWeaponType`; a spell with `RequiredEvolutionCrystal` needs that evolution (now read for the first time); any other spell needs any spell source. So nothing changes for abilities until the editor pass.
- **The editor pass:** abilities get `Kind = Weapon` with their type; spells with an evolution get `Kind = Evolution`; **other spells stay `None`** (any spell source, as the plan's "as today" line says), so an evolution seat still casts plain spells.
- **In a fight:** an unmet weapon pick is not listed; an unmet ring or evolution-seat spell is greyed with what it needs ("Needs: Phoenix Fire."), as a broken source is (D-083).
- **The Equipment screen** doesn't offer a skill the piece doesn't meet (the plan's "offered only with an axe worn").
- **Left reading the old field (a follow-up, not the plan's four readers):** the enemy fill (`WornGear.cpp`, Q-518, replaced by D-225's roll), `FAbilityCollection`'s by-type lists and the pool's weapon-type filter (`PoolAccessors.cpp`). They agree with the rule as long as the editor pass copies each ability's `RequiredWeaponType` into its requirement.
- **Editor-pass check:** list the spell assets that set `RequiredEvolutionCrystal` (now enforced); none was expected.

### Q-519 — The built tier's smaller choices (stage 2b)
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-519, D-224 stage 2b) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the plan (`Design/AssetOverhaulPlan.md` §4.1) splits an infused hit's status build-up into "the physical bar" and "the element bar", but a target has one status bar, filled by one number per hit with the hit's kind. And which tier does an infused ability's elemental share read when it is infused through the caster's own element (Innate) or an evolution? None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default (Monster Hunter's element and status read the weapon's element value whatever the move, and players read one bar per status there too):
- **Build-up stays one number:** an infused hit (it has an element) is an elemental hit, so its whole build-up reads the weapon's built tier; an uninfused (physical) hit's reads the weapon's own tier. There is no second bar to split into.
- **Damage splits:** the uninfused part on the weapon's own tier, the infusion's share above x1 on the built tier (as the plan says); a x1.00 charge adds nothing elemental.
- **Any infusion source:** the elemental share reads the weapon's built tier whatever the source (crystal, innate, evolution); the weapon is what the skill is used through.
- **Unchanged here:** a weapon crystal's wear, for its spells and infusions alike (the plan's "keep" row; only a ring cast's wear reads the built tier); a weapon's skill and spell slot counts (the plan's "keep": the crystal already adds its slots, CL-20); the evolution seat's spells (its own tier); the action-tier fallback (safety path, the weapon's own tier).
- **Small changes that follow from the rule:** an evolution in a ring's or weapon's socket now gives that piece's spells a channel (the built tier; before, none, x1.00); a broken gem still counts until it breaks at turn end, as the channel always read it.
- **The skill's own-tier power** reads the player's copy's tier now (the S3 default, §5.3), so stage S3's lost-run drop costs; an unowned skill (an enemy's) reads the asset's.

### Q-518 — Enemies' skills once gear has none
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the brain, the reference-backed default, 2026-11-16; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-16 (B-518, D-224 S1 part 2) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** with no skills on gear (D-224), today's enemies lose the skills their loadouts gave them through the old merge. What do they fight with until stage 5 gives them their own? None of this is lore.
**Answer:** an enemy's weapon or ring with no picks fills from that enemy's known skills, in order, up to its slot count: a weapon with the abilities it can hold (its type, wield mode, a gem slot's element), a ring with a spell source with the spells of its element (not the innate ones). Enemy-origin characters only; marked `// TODO:` and removed in stage 5. Fights stay testable (DA_Enemy_Darkness and DA_Enemy_Generic fill 5 abilities, DA_Enemy_Resonator 2). Code: `FCombatLoadout::FillEmptyPicksFromKnown` (`WornGear.cpp`), run from `UInventoryComponent::InitializeFromInventoryAsset`.

### Q-516 — The empty adapter's smaller choices
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-516, D-224 stage 2) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** with one empty ring asset for every ring, how do two same-named rings read, when can the add window socket a ring, and which ring does a spell both rings pick read? None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default:
- **Same names (replaced by D-223 "Two pieces with the same name", built by B-516b):** the tier tells them apart first (the Equipment list's tier badge; the quick strip's tier letter); a number only where the name and the tier both repeat. The first default was: "Lightning Ring", then "Lightning Ring 2", in list order, only where two entries in one list read the same (the plan's default; Diablo and Path of Exile show identical names and let the slot tell them apart, and a number is the shortest form of that on a strip with no slot labels).
- **Socketing in the add window:** a ring is socketed as it waits in the window, and the gem is spent then, as in the Edit window; backing out keeps the ring socketed in the spares (Diablo's and Path of Exile's sockets are filled on the item, not on the slot).
- **One spell on two rings:** the ring in use, so Switch Adapter picks which (B-310's rule for wear).

**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-514, D-222) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-222 sets the strip's keys, text and frame. What about its ends, the arrows, the mouse, its size and the lines for pieces with no held type? None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default (Breath of the Wild's quick-select, D-222's reference):
- **Ends:** left / right wraps from the last piece to the first (BotW's row wraps).
- **Arrows:** ▲ ▼ show on the highlighted tile only when it has more than one source.
- **Mouse:** a resting cursor never moves the highlight; only a moving one does, so A / D and the mouse wheel are not undone.
- **Size:** up to 9 pieces (two weapons, five adapters, the innate, the evolution); more are logged and not shown.
- **Type line:** an adapter's form ("Ring", "Pistol"); an evolution "Evolution"; the innate has no type, so its line is its source alone ("Fire").
- **IN HAND:** marks the entry in use (with a ring picked, the ring's tile).
- **Tiles with no icon:** the piece's name as text (the icon table has no ring, adapter or shield icons yet).

### Q-513 — The names for a weapon held with a shield or as a pair
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-513, Crown's PIE of the wheel) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the wheel, the strip and the Equipment screen named a sword and shield "Sword". What does the type line say for a weapon held with a shield, or as a pair? None of this is lore.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default:
- **With a shield:** "{type} and Shield": "Sword and Shield", "Axe and Shield".
- **A pair** (a Dual weapon, or a main hand with a same-type off hand, D-209): "Dual Swords", "Dual Daggers", "Dual Axes", "Dual Hammers", "Dual Spears", "Dual Greatswords", "Dual Staves", "Dual Scythes", "Dual Guns". Gauntlets stay "Gauntlets" (a pair already); a bow is never dual.
- An infused one puts the element first: "Fire Sword and Shield", "Fire Dual Axes".
- References: Monster Hunter names its classes "Sword and Shield" and "Dual Blades", and players read the class at a glance; Dark Souls and Elden Ring call two-handing a pair "dual wielding".
- The wheel's slices name the weapon itself ("Arming Sword"), as the weapon wheels of Doom Eternal and GTA V name each weapon; the centre and the strip keep the type.
- **B-513b (Crown):** a slice with a separate off-hand piece joins the two names: "Flame Sword and Void Shield", "Flame Sword and Excalibur"; the Equipment screen's entry row says the same (it read "A + B"). A single piece, a one-asset sword and shield included, shows its own name.
- **One form for pairs: the plural, "Dual Swords"** (Crown said "dual sword"): "dual" names two things, and the plural reads naturally for every type ("Dual Axes", "Dual Daggers", "Dual Staves"), as Monster Hunter's "Dual Blades" and Diablo's "dual wield" lines do. "Dual Sword" is the singular Crown may prefer: it is one line to change in `WoRGameNames.cpp`.
**Notes for Claude:** one place, `WoRGameNames::GetHeldWeaponTypeName`; the legacy loadout screen keeps its own "with shield" line (`GetWieldModeName`).

### Q-511 — The class default kit: its sockets, whose it is, the asset's name
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-511, D-221) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-221 gives every trial start a class default kit, minted once. Three things it leaves open, none of them lore: (1) Does a kit piece keep a crystal baked on its asset? (2) Is the kit kept per character or per class? (3) The plan names the asset `DA_ClassDefaultLoadouts`; the naming guide wants `DA_<Kind>_`.
**Answer:** by the reference-backed default:
- (1) **Sockets start empty.** The trial start resets them each run (B-512), and a crystal put back each run would make crystals from nothing. Hades and Dead Cells start every run from a bare base kit; upgrades come from the run.
- (2) **Per character, minted from its class's kit**, so each character owns its own pieces (D-117: each piece belongs to one character). They can't be sold, dismantled, given or put in the chest, so nothing piles up or moves between characters.
- (3) **`DA_Loadout_ClassDefaults`**, in `/Game/Data/Loadout/` (`Conventions/ContentNaming.md`, B-123).
- Saved in `FSavedPool::ClassDefaultKits` with no version bump: a save without it mints the kits again at the next trial start.

### Q-503 — The field attack's cycle: which entries, what is kept, what a pulse carries
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503, D-215) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-215 says LB cycles the field attack through the whole loadout. Five things it leaves open, none of them lore: (1) Is an evolution socketed in a weapon or ring its own stop? (2) Is "Innate" a stop for a character with no innate element? (3) What is saved: the entry or its place in the list? (4) Does a pulse carry the weapon's physical type (Slash / Pierce / Impact) into the fight? (5) Does cycling to a weapon put it in hand for the fight?
**Options:** (1) **A** yes, a pulse in its element besides the weapon's swing; **B** no, only the character's own evolution. (2) **A** no; **B** yes, a pulse with no element. (3) **A** its place in the list, falling back to the first entry when the list gets shorter; **B** the item itself. (4) **A** no, only a swing does; **B** yes, the weapon in hand's. (5) **A** no; **B** yes.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default.
- (1) A: D-215's own words are "each evolution". Expedition 33 gives the field both a melee hit and a ranged shot, and players use the shot to start a fight from a distance, so a pulse stop beside the weapon's swing is useful.
- (2) A: a stop that does nothing is a wasted press. Elden Ring and Dark Souls skip empty slots when cycling weapons and quick items.
- (3) A: Elden Ring and Dark Souls keep the slot you were on, not the item. Simple, and the list is in loadout order, so the place means the same thing until the loadout changes.
- (4) A: D-215 models the pulse on Expedition 33's ranged field burst, which is energy, not a blade; the physical type belongs to the weapon's swing (`ElementsOnTheWorld.md`: "weapons apply their damage type on the map too").
- (5) A: no outside reference; D-215 only names the field attack, and the weapon in hand is the loadout's choice (D-110). Changing the fight's opening weapon from a field key would surprise.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-503. The order is the primary weapon or ring, the secondary weapon, the Resonator's rings, the innate element, then evolutions (the character's own slot, gear sockets, apparel). A broken evolution is not a stop; a weapon or ring with a broken crystal stays a stop with no element. Saved with no version bump (an older save loads the first entry). The 2 s popup is a placeholder on the hub HUD, bottom left.

### Q-503b — The field swing: when the hit is read, and moving during it
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503b, D-215) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-215 says the field attack is the battle swing without the dash. Three things it leaves open, none of them lore: (1) Does the hit land at the press or when the blade does? (2) If the player cycles to another entry during the swing, which entry's element does the hit carry? (3) Can the character walk during the swing?
**Options:** (1) **A** when the blade lands (the montage's Impact notify), read from where the character stands then; **B** at the press. (2) **A** the entry at the press; **B** the entry when the blade lands. (3) **A** yes; **B** no, held still for the swing.
**Answer:** **A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default.
- (1) A: Expedition 33's and Metaphor: ReFantazio's field strikes connect on the swing's contact frame, so a swing that visibly misses doesn't start the fight. It is also how the battle times the same swing.
- (2) A: the swing on screen is the one the player started; changing its element mid-air would not match what they see.
- (3) A: Metaphor: ReFantazio lets the character keep moving through the field attack, which keeps the map fluid. The feet may slide until the swing is blended on the upper body; Crown judges it in PIE.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-503b. A second attack is refused until the first hit has landed. A weapon whose attack has no field montage hits at once, as before.

### Q-503r — An evolution in a weapon's socket: which infusion mode, and for which options
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503r, Crown: "whatever is attached in the socket decides") · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the rule leaves two things open, none of them lore: (1) does it hold for an adapter's socket too, or only a weapon's; (2) does a Raw action (HP, no element) with an evolution in the weapon's socket take the evolution's mode or the weapon's.
**Answer:** (1) **both sockets**: the rule is about what sits in the socket, not what holds it. (2) **The weapon's**: Raw pushes no element and uses no socket, so the socket has nothing to decide.
