In games where a socketed gem changes a skill (Path of Exile's support gems, Diablo II's runewords) the gem changes only what it is linked to, which players find clear.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-503r (`ResolveInfusionSource`, the `ModeOf` lambda). A crystal carries no mode (`FCrystalAttachment` has none), so a crystal socket keeps the weapon's or adapter's mode as before.

### Q-503n — The test gear grant: what it gives, the fill, the class variants
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503n) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** B-503n's first brief (three weapons, a ring, an evolution, one character) breaks the class rules (`LoadoutRules.h`: a Generic holds at most 2 weapons + 1 adapter, a Refractor 1 item, a Resonator 1 weapon + up to 5 adapters). So: (1) what does the command give, and who builds the loadout; (2) what does the quick fill put where; (3) how does a variant change the class when class and innate live on the shared character asset?
**Answer:** (1) **Give, then build in the real screen**: a plain sword, a ring and one F-tier Fire, Water and Lightning crystal in the pouch, through the same calls a pickup uses; nothing owned is given twice. Crown builds the loadout in `wor.UI.LoadoutV2`, so the screen itself is tested. Game cheats work this way (Skyrim's `player.additem`, Elden Ring's debug item give): the item lands in the inventory and the player equips it. (2) `fill` is the shortcut: the sword and the ring go in as entries where the class has room (refused ones are logged), then each empty crystal socket takes a crystal: a weapon Fire, then Lightning; the adapter Water; never an element already on the loadout. (3) The variant sets class and innate **in memory**, put back when PIE ends (not on a map change); every entry but the first comes out first so the loadout is legal for the new class. Refractor keeps its innate (Fire if it has none), Reality takes Reality, Broken Darkness takes Darkness plus the live BD flag, Resonator keeps the innate.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-503n (`Debug/Inventory/TestGearDebug.*`, spec `WoR.Debug.TestGear`). The gear given and the loadout built stay in the save like any gear; the variant's class does not.

### Q-503s — The wheel's pick in hand and in the parry: the ACTIVE weapon, a Generic's ring, the parry settings
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15: A yes; B a Generic's ring keeps the weapon, infused, only an adapter pulses; C yes; see D-218) (asked of the Operator / Crown, 2026-11-15; no fight rule changed until answered) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503s, D-218) · **Blocks:** the rest of B-503s
**Question:** (A) Does picking a weapon slice also make that weapon the loadout's ACTIVE one, so the fight starts with it in hand, or is it a field-only look reset by the fight? (B) Q-503m4 (2) has a Generic's ring slice swing the weapon infused ("Lightning Sword"); D-218 says an adapter pick puts the weapon away. Which holds for a Generic's ring? (C) The parry today reads two per-member settings (guard style Weapon / Unarmed, parry priority Weapon / Magic; cosmetic only). Proposed: a weapon pick = weapon guard, colour of the picked option; a source pick = unarmed guard, colour of the picked option (Ice, (R) Fire, Dark Fire too); the two settings stop being player choices and stay as the fallback (AI seats, never-picked members) and the debug commands.
**Recommendation:** (A) yes, the ACTIVE weapon: one truth, and the mesh already follows the active-weapon event. (B) and (C) as proposed.
**Notes for Claude:** B-503s built the part that is the same under every answer: a pulse pick puts the weapon away (`UFieldMovementComponent::ShowWeaponForFieldAttack`, `UWeaponMeshComponent::SetWeaponHidden`). The battle level has its own controller and pawns, so the put-away never reaches a fight.
**Built (B-503s rest, 2026-11-15):** (A) a weapon slice's pick sets the loadout's ACTIVE weapon (`StoreFieldAttackIndex` → `TryChangeGear`, `FFieldAttackEntry::WeaponId`), saved; the mesh follows `OnActiveWeaponChanged`. (B) Read as: a Generic's adapter of **Ring** form infuses the weapon; a **Pistol** or **Trainer** adapter (D-107's other forms) and an evolution throw the pulse. This reading is the default; Crown may change it. (C) `FPartyMember::bFieldAttackPicked` (saved, no bump) marks a member that has picked; then `ULoadoutComponent::GetFieldAttackPick` drives `GetGuardStyle` and `ParryVisuals::ApplyFieldPick` (Ice tints Ice). Never picked, or the fight swapped away from the picked weapon: the old settings. No player screen set them (debug commands only), so nothing was hidden.

### Q-503m5 — The toggle wheel: a click off it, the middle, the menu key
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503m5, D-218 "The wheel is a toggle") · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-218 makes the wheel a toggle with the mouse on it. Three small things are left: (1) what a click off the wheel does; (2) what the cursor in the middle circle does; (3) what the menu key does while the wheel is up.
**Answer:** (1) **Nothing**: the wheel stays up (a stray click doesn't throw the choice away or attack). (2) **Keeps the pick**; a click there confirms it. (3) **Closes the wheel with no change**, then opens the menu. Radial menus that stay open (Mass Effect's power wheel, The Witcher 3's sign wheel on PC) pick only from the slices, keep the last pick in the middle, and give way to the pause menu; widely used and not complained about.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-503m5. Q-503m4 (1) is superseded: the cursor picks the slice under it (`FieldSkillRules::GetFieldWheelSliceAt`); the pointer from mouse travel (`MoveFieldWheelPointer`) is flagged OBSOLETE. While the wheel is up the controller ignores move and look input, so the character stands and the camera holds whatever the pawn's own keys are.

### Q-503m4 — The wheel on RB: what the mouse does, what a ring infuses, what a tap of R does
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown has seen these defaults; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503m4, D-217 "The wheel on RB") · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-217's wheel on RB leaves five things open, none of them lore: (1) how the mouse picks a slice; (2) what a weapon user's ring, innate or evolution slice swings; (3) what a tap of R (keyboard) does, now RB's tap is the attack; (4) two entries of one element: one slice or two (Q-503k (2) said one stop per element); (5) which option a slice opens on when the stick moves to it.
**Answer:** (1) **the mouse moves a pointer on the wheel** from its travel (clamped to the rim), read like the stick; the camera holds still. GTA V's PC weapon wheel picks by mouse movement this way, widely used and liked. (2) **The weapon in hand, infused** ("Lightning Sword"), as D-217's strip names it; a weapon's own slice swings that weapon. (3) **Nothing**: the wheel is a hold, and Doom Eternal's and GTA V's wheel keys do nothing on a tap unless they double as another action (RB's tap is the attack). (4) **Two slices**: the slices are the loadout's entries (D-217), so each entry is its own slice; this supersedes Q-503k (2). (5) **Its first option**, or the kept one on the kept entry's slice (GTA V keeps each weapon's attachment choice).
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-503m4. The old held attack (`FIELD_ATTACK_HOLD_SECONDS`) is flagged OBSOLETE; Ice is the Water entry's option (`FFieldAttackEntry::bIce`). LB is off the field keys; RT stays free on the map.

### Q-503m3 — The LB wheel: which stick, which way the mouse wheel turns, what a tap does
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503m3, D-217) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-217's wheel leaves four things open, none of them lore: (1) which stick picks; (2) which way the mouse wheel steps; (3) what a tap steps to, now the hold steps sit on the wheel; (4) what an unarmed plain swing (no name, Q-503k (4)) shows on the wheel.
**Answer:** (1) **the right stick**, and the camera holds still while the wheel is up, so the left stick keeps moving the character (the radial wheels D-217 names, Doom Eternal's and GTA V's, take the pick from a stick while the view stays put). (2) **Down = the next entry**, as in any scrolled list. (3) **The next source**, skipping the hold steps, as before (a tap "still steps"); the hold steps are reached on the wheel. (4) **"Plain"**, on the wheel only; the strip still shows nothing.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-503m3; the wheel opens at `FIELD_SOURCE_HOLD_SECONDS` while the key is down; trial maps only, as the strip (D-217); in the hub a hold reads as a tap.

### Q-503m — The card fixes: where the level comes from, the bars' look
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503m, Crown's PIE of B-503k) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** the details Crown's notes left open: what decides hub vs trial cards; how the numbers sit on the bars; what marks the build-up bar; the R / LB hold time.
**Answer (default):** (1) the **map** decides: the hub map (`HubLevel`) shows names only, every other field map the trial card, run or not, so a map played from the editor shows its own card. (2) The number sits centred on its bar, white with a dark shadow, as E33's and most JRPG party frames draw HP / MP on the bar; EP's bar is blue, the common MP / energy colour (FF, Persona). An enemy's HP bar keeps no number (Q-391). (3) A dim "Status" label before the build-up bar and a faint empty-bar tint, so it reads as a meter that is empty, not a missing bar. (4) The hold is **0.5 s** (B-503m2; `docs/research/hold-duration.md`: the iOS / Android long-press and Unreal Hold-and-Release defaults, good range 0.4-0.6 s), a named value a setting can change later; a fill on the strip while held is its own job.

### Q-503l — Broken Darkness and Reality: what holding LB cycles on the map
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-15) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503k, D-217) · **Blocks:** B-503l
**Question (for Crown):** you said a Broken Darkness character on Darkness, or a Reality one, holds LB to "cycle their dark sources, based on whatever other gear they have". Today on the map: a press already steps each of their sources (innate, the weapon's crystal, the adapter, the evolution), and each has its own element: a BD character's innate is its active pool (Darkness, or an absorbed element), a Reality character's is Reality (players see it as Unknown, ED-42). So what should a hold change?
**Options:** **A** the strike stays Darkness / Reality and only the gear it goes through changes (the strip reads e.g. Darkness Sword then Darkness through the ring; nothing else on the map changes, since the field strike carries only an element, the weapon's physical type and build-up); **B** the hold steps the element through the elements of their other gear (Darkness, then the crystal's Fire, then the ring's Water), which is what a press does today; **C** BD: the hold steps its pools (Darkness and each absorbed element, CL-08's single active pool); Reality: every element (CL-08: a Reality Refractor may cast any element).
**Notes for Claude:** no outside reference fits (this is our own BD / Reality rule), so no default; B-503k ships press = source and hold = nothing for every other source.
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-15): B, with these names.** A hold steps the element through the elements of their other gear. **With a weapon** the strip reads the element and the type: "Darkness Sword". **Reality** on an innate, evolution or adapter source reads **"Unknown"** (matching the hidden element, ED-42: Reality is to be a premium class, like Warframe's Excalibur Prime, so players who don't have it see Unknown); while cycling on a hold it reads **"(R) Fire", "(R) Lightning"** and so on. Only innate and evolution sources cycle this way; **adapters cannot**. **Broken Darkness** scrolls **"Dark Lightning", "Dark Fire"** and so on.
**Also (Crown, 2026-11-15):** the UI side comes first; the pulse's per-element VFX from the magic pack waits until the UI is done.

### Q-503k — The field attack strip and the infusion cycle: whose card, which stops
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503k, D-217) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-217 puts the strip above the card and has LB step the infusion. Four things it leaves open, none of them lore: (1) Whose card gets the strip? (2) Two sources of one element (a Fire crystal and a Fire ring): one stop or two? (3) A Refractor's weapon crystal: a stop? (4) A weapon user with no weapon: what does the strip say?
**Options:** (1) **A** only the card of the member the player plays (the one who makes the field attack); **B** every card. (2) **A** one stop per element; **B** one per source. (3) **A** yes, a pulse in that element; **B** no. (4) **A** no strip until an infusion is picked (then just the element); **B** a word like Unarmed.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default.
- (1) A: only the played character attacks on the map (D-215); Expedition 33 and Tales of Arise show the field action for the leader alone. In co-op each player sees their own.
- (2) A: the strip would read the same twice: a wasted press (Q-503 (2), Elden Ring skipping empty slots).
- (3) A: D-217 names the character's available sources, and the combat menu offers the weapon crystal to every class.
- (4) A: the plain swing with no weapon has nothing to name; D-217's strip names a weapon type or an element.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-503k. The sources are the combat menu's (`UActionExecutor::GetAvailableInfusionSources`), Raw left out (no element). A Resonator's ring stop is its active ring only (as in combat), beside its weapon crystal and evolution; with two sources of one element the first in the menu's order names the stop. The kept index is the old `FieldAttackIndex` (an older save's index is clamped). The hold list is B-503l.

### Q-503c — The field pulse: where it goes, when it hits, what it costs
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-15; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-15 (B-503c, D-215) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-215 says a non-weapon entry throws an energy pulse about 7 m that engages. Four things it leaves open, none of them lore: (1) Where does it go? (2) Does it hit when it leaves or when the look arrives? (3) Do walls stop it? (4) Does it cost EP or have its own cooldown?
**Options:** (1) **A** where the character faces, level with the ground (as the swing); **B** where the camera looks, with an aim mode and a reticle; **C** it locks onto the nearest enemy ahead. (2) **A** when it leaves; **B** after a flight time. (3) **A** yes; **B** no. (4) **A** free, on the swing's 0.6 s cooldown; **B** an EP cost.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default.
- (1) A: Metaphor: ReFantazio's field attack goes where the character faces, and the swing here already does. Expedition 33's shot, which D-215 names, uses an aim mode with a reticle (B): that is a job of its own (a camera mode, a reticle, an input), so it is flagged here rather than built unasked.
- (2) A: Expedition 33's field shot hits the instant it is fired. The fight's own opening (the join window and the shatter) covers the look's flight.
- (3) A: every reference stops a shot at a wall; a pulse through walls would start fights the player can't see.
- (4) A: Expedition 33's field shot is free. It only starts the fight; the element's buildup is the same as the swing's.
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-503c. The sweep is 7 m long and 50 cm in radius from the body's centre, so an enemy a little above or below on a slope is still hit; one on a ledge is not. The look starts at the right hand. Held Water shows in Ice's colour.

### D-214 — Rest points are the services: each one is a themed object you rest at and use
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14; changes Q-447 and Q-449's NPC stand-ins) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** the trial's non-boss rest points and its NPCs merge. Each rest point is a unique object that is also a service: you rest there and use it as that service, e.g. an **anvil** is the Blacksmith a **ritual tree** is the Spiritualist (Crown, 2026-11-14), and a **crystal pedestal** is the Jeweler (for now, Crown). (1) The **pillar-point seller** becomes a rest point type (e.g. a shrine) that rotates: a different rest point offers it each run, the same one for the whole run. (2) **V's extract** is a rest point too: V himself or a mirror with V's picture (look to be decided with the art). **Boss rest spots are a campfire** (Crown, 2026-11-14): their rules are unchanged, only the look.
**Reference:** Dark Souls' bonfires and Elden Ring's Sites of Grace (rest and level up at one spot), Hollow Knight's benches (rest and change charms): one stop does everything and the map reads at a glance; all three are widely praised for it.
**Notes for Claude:** B-447 and B-449 built the seller and V as NPC actors; B-502 turns them into rest point types. Until the art, test boxes stand in (purple rest points, shop colours for the service).

### Q-502 — Rest point services: which rest points can be the shrine, where the point is bought, what the anvil opens
**Status:** ANSWERED (approved by the reference-backed default, 2026-11-14; Crown may change) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 (B-502, D-214) · **Blocks:** —
**Question:** D-214 makes rest points the services. Four things it leaves open, none of them lore: (1) Which rest points can be the pillar-point shrine in a run? (2) What does a shrine do in a run where it is not the one? (3) Where is the point bought? (4) What do the vendor rest points (the anvil, the ritual tree, the crystal pedestal) open?
**Options:** (1) **A** only the rest points placed as shrines: one of them sells each run; **B** any non-boss rest point takes the shrine on top of its own service. (2) **A** it still rests the party and says it is quiet this run; **B** it does not appear. (3) **A** on the rest point's Level up screen, where points are placed; **B** a separate buy panel. (4) **A** that vendor's own screen (the Blacksmith's, the Spiritualist's, the Jeweler's), with the run's rolled part of its stock (ER-18); **B** a new smaller menu.
**Answer:** **A, A, A, A**, by the reference-backed default.
- (1) A: each rest point is one recognisable object (D-214: "a unique object that is also a service"), so an anvil stays an anvil. Hades moves Charon's shop and the Well between chambers each run while the object stays the same; players like knowing what a thing is on sight and not knowing where it will be.
- (2) A: a rest point always rests, as every bonfire in Dark Souls and every bench in Hollow Knight does; the quiet line tells the player to look for the other shrines.
- (3) A: Elden Ring's Sites of Grace and Dark Souls 3's bonfire put levelling in the rest menu; buying and placing a point in one screen saves a trip.
- (4) A: one vendor screen for every vendor is already the rule (D-193), and a vendor met in a trial already shows its rolled stock (ER-18).
**Notes for Claude:** built in B-502. With one shrine placed, that shrine sells every run (no rotation until a second is placed). The names "Anvil", "Ritual tree", "Crystal pedestal", "Shrine" and "V" are placeholders until the art (`WoRGameNames::GetRestServiceName`). The old `ATrialNPC` and its panel stay until the maps are re-placed and Crown's PIE pass, then go (Sweep.md).

### D-212 — No loading screen into a battle: a designed transition instead
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):** touching an enemy does not open a loading screen. A short designed transition (the clash, then the fight) plays instead, and any loading hides behind it. The current transition is not nice to look at, so it gets a design job first (B-490), then the build. Loading screens stay for hub and trial travel only (D-203).
**Reference-backed default (Crown may change):** about one second, an effect in the game's own style (a refraction shatter or prism flash), like Expedition 33's swirl, Persona 5's shatter and the Final Fantasy battle swirl, which players like because the pace never stops. If a fight ever needs more than about 2 seconds to load, a small spinner shows over the effect, never a full screen.
**Design picked (Crown, 2026-11-14, after B-490 and Expedition 33's encounter clip):** option A's refraction shatter, done in the world like Expedition 33 (the world stays on screen, never a black card), with B4's prism inside it: the hit flashes white, the light runs through the cracks and splits into the spectrum across the shards (each shard catches a colour), the shards blow away onto the battle; the load holds on drifting coloured shards. Built as real 3D shards (thick, lit, bevelled) with bloom, motion blur, depth of field and a small camera push, so it is not flat; if the rough version still looks flat, a Fab glass/crystal shatter pack is bought as the base. Built one piece at a time with Crown's PIE check each (B-500 to B-500d).
**Answer (Crown, 2026-11-14):** the effect starts the moment a fight begins either way: the player hits an enemy, or an enemy hits or engages the player. The 2-second join window runs under it (as Expedition 33's starts on the hit); the window itself is unchanged.

### D-211 — A pair's slots: the average of the two tiers
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14, with the reference-backed default for rounding; changes D-210's "slots follow the main hand's tier") · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 · **Blocks:** B-477
**Answer (Crown):** an entry with two pieces (two weapons, later a weapon and a shield) gets ability slots from the **average of the two tiers** (F 1 … B 5, A 6, S 6, CL-20), plus the entry's crystal's extra slots (CL-21), at most 12 (CL-31). A one-weapon entry is unchanged: its tier plus its crystal's.
**Default (reference-backed, Crown may change):** a half is **rounded down** (a B with a C gives 4). Destiny's power level is the average of the gear worn, rounded down, and players read it at a glance; rounding down also stops a low piece riding a high one.


### D-210 — Loadout entries, the two augment sockets, and where evolutions may go
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14; changes D-209's rows and "both crystals' stats", D-141's one crystal socket + one stone socket) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 · **Blocks:** B-476, B-477, B-479, B-480
**Answer (Crown):**
- **No Primary / Secondary rows.** The Weapons tab starts with one empty frame, **"Add gear"**; the Add gear window has **Main hand, Off hand, Augment socket 1, Augment socket 2**. Confirming makes one **entry**, and a new empty frame shows the cells left ("3/5 left"). Repeat until the class limit or the cells run out.
- **The sockets belong to the entry**, not to each weapon: a dual pair shares them. A dual entry gives **both weapons' stats plus its sockets**, and **one merged skill list, like a single weapon** (Crown): its slot count follows the **main hand's** tier (CL-20, plus the socket crystal's extra slots; reference-backed default, matching the main hand setting the stance), it can hold **single-blade and dual-blade abilities** of that weapon type, and both weapons' built-in abilities pre-fill it (replaces D-209's "both crystals' stats" and the per-weapon lists).
- **The two augment sockets take a crystal (or an evolution) and a stone, or two stones** (two of the same stat stack; the player chooses, Crown). Costs as D-206: crystal 1, stone 1, evolution 2.
- **Entries per class:** **Generic: at most 2 weapon entries and 1 adapter entry** (evolved weapons allowed). **Resonator:** 1 weapon entry and adapter entries (at most 5, D-206). **Refractor:** 1 entry.
- **Where evolutions may go:** a **Resonator can't put an evolution on a weapon** (only on adapters); a **Generic can't put an evolution on an adapter** (only on weapons); a **Refractor may do either** (its one entry). **The character's own evolution (Crown):** picked in the Add gear window like gear, one per character, and it **costs cells (2, an evolution alone)**, so it asks before attaching; on the Weapons tab it is one line, "Evolution: <name>". **Screen (Crown):** no character frame; the "Add weapon" frame sits at the bottom of the list with no description (help in a tooltip); sockets show icons only (stones and crystals have their own icon shapes); X opens an entry's details. A **Cosmetics** tab after Skills is planned (later).
**Defaults (reference-backed, Crown may change):** the window asks **weapon or adapter** first; Switch Weapon cycles a Generic's two weapon entries (as Switch Adapter cycles).

### D-209 — Generic weapon sets: dual wield, shields, and the Add gear window
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14; changes D-206's Generic "3 items at most" and CL-23) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 · **Blocks:** B-476, B-477, B-478
**Answer (Crown):**
- **Each Generic row is a weapon set.** The primary row has 3 cells, the secondary 2 (D-206 costs). A set holds one weapon, or **two one-handed weapons (dual wield)**, or later a one-handed weapon and a **shield** (shields become their own gear piece; until then a shield stays part of its weapon). **At most one adapter per loadout**, in either row. The cells are the only limit: e.g. a primary of an evolved sword and a sword with a crystal (2 + 1 = 3) and a secondary of a weapon and an adapter (1 + 1), or two dual-wield sets.
- **A dual set gives both weapons' abilities and both crystals' stats.** Switch Weapon (and D-207's follow-the-skill setting) swaps the whole set.
- **How a weapon can be held is an enum on the weapon (Crown):** `EWeaponHandling` = **SingleOnly** (held alone, never paired), **DualOnly** (only as a same-type pair; can't be equipped alone), **SingleOrDual** (either). Picking the same two weapons in the other order gives the other weapon's dual stance, so the order matters (Crown). Replaces the toggle below.
- **Dual wield, same type only** (Crown, simplest for the animations): a weapon has a **"can dual wield"** toggle and **two stance entries**, one for holding it alone and one for dual wielding. Only two weapons of the same type with the toggle on pair (two swords, two daggers), and the pair uses the **first-chosen weapon's** dual-wield stance (Crown). Mixed pairs (sword and dagger) can come later with their own animations.
- **Loadout screen (changes the v2 mockups):** a confirmed set shows as its own column. Instead of empty cells, the row shows its **free cells as a number**, and an **"Add gear"** button opens a window to build a valid set: pick the pieces (sword, shield, crystal; or sword, sword, evolution). For an adapter: pick a **ready-made one**, or an **empty adapter plus the crystal** to put in it. Pieces that don't fit stay listed, greyed with the reason.
**Reference:** Elden Ring's powerstancing (two weapons, both counting) and Dark Souls' separate hands; Guild Wars 2's weapon-set swap.
**Notes for Claude:** B-477 replaces B-473 (retired). The AI loadout and the save (v39) take the set shape.

### D-208 — Apparel's rolled bonus line
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14, with the reference-backed default for the size) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 · **Blocks:** B-475
**Answer (Crown):** besides HP, Defence and Amp (CL-50, CL-50c), each apparel piece rolls **one bonus line** when it drops (the per-instance roll system): either a **sub-stat** (never Turn Speed, which is retired) or a **pillar's value** (+X% to one pillar's composed value, like an over-cap point, ER-24c). It adds no pillar points, so it never counts toward skill requirements. Apparel stays at **2 slots**.
**Default (reference-backed):** a pillar line starts at **+5%** (the over-cap point's value); sub-stat lines are small, `// TODO: tune`. Genshin's artifact sub-stats and Diablo's affixes (one rolled line that makes two copies of a piece differ) are well liked; Honkai: Star Rail's rolled Speed became the one stat everyone chased, so speed stays off gear.

### D-207 — Changing the active weapon: follow the skill, or L3
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 · **Blocks:** B-474
**Answer (Crown):** with the new battle menu a skill can be picked from either weapon, so: (1) a setting **"Switch to the weapon of the skill you use"**, **on by default** (reference-backed default): using a skill makes its weapon (or adapter) active; switching stays free (ET-19). Octopath Traveler uses whichever carried weapon the chosen attack needs, with no swap step, and is well liked for it. (2) With the setting off, **L3** switches the active weapon in a fight (keyboard stays **6**); D-pad Left's Switch Weapon moves to L3. `InputMap.md` gets both rows in the same change. **On the loadout screen** (reference-backed default, Crown 2026-11-14 asked for a way): **L3 on a focused weapon entry sets it active** (the entry a fight starts with), also listed as "Set active" in Y Options.
**Rules kept:** the weapon's own passives work while it is active (CL-19); an evolution on gear gives its passives only to an action that gear powers at infusion level 2 (CL-28c). The weapon swap swaps passives and stance as today.

### D-206 — Loadout costs: the gear is free, only what is in it costs
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-14; replaces Q-357's "frame + attachment" and changes D-110, CL-03, CL-04, CL-10, CL-11, CL-12) · **Raised:** 2026-11-14 · **Blocks:** B-472, B-473, B-476
**Answer (Crown):**
- **A weapon or adapter itself costs 0.** A crystal costs **1**, an augment stone **1**, an evolution **2** (it sits in the crystal socket). An evolution alone (a primary or the seat) costs **2**. So a weapon with a crystal costs 1, the same as an adapter with a crystal.
- **Budgets:** Generic **5**, Resonator **5**, Refractor **2** (was 3; still 1 item: an evolved weapon, an evolved adapter, an evolution alone or a weapon with a crystal and a stone all fit; evolved gear plus a stone does not).
- **Generic:** a primary row and a secondary row (shown as 3 and 2 budget cells, `menus/loadout/v2`); each holds a weapon or an adapter, **3 items at most: two weapons and one adapter**. The "one evolved weapon" limit goes: two evolved weapons and an adapter with a crystal (2 + 2 + 1) fit. Only the active weapon gives its abilities (CL-17).
- **Resonator:** one weapon and adapters, **at most 5 adapters** (reference-backed default: with bare adapters free, the budget alone no longer limits them, CL-11).
**Notes for Claude (from the 2026-11-14 check):** keep the **pouch capacity** costs (`InventoryConstants::WEAPON_BASE_SLOT_COST` / `RING_BASE_SLOT_COST`, used by `InventoryComponent`, `EconomyService`) separate from the loadout costs: new loadout constants feed only `FLoadoutRules::SlotCost`. `SlotCost` today charges only the crystal socket (`HasAttachment`), so the stone is new. The Refractor over-budget finding becomes clearable (drop the stone). No save goes over budget (every cost falls); B-473's third slot is save v39. Expedition 33's Luminas (only what you equip costs, a fixed point cap) is the reference.

### D-189 — What each ending of a run pays
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-13, via the desktop operator session; the last of three notes that day, which replaced the two before it) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 · **Blocks:** B-451
**Answer (Crown):** (changes RunStructure §6 and Q-187's "falling pays in full")
- **Beating the boss:** the full payout, and the run's upgrades.
- **Extracting:** half (`RunRewards::LEAVE_EARLY_SHARE`), and the run's upgrades.
- **Running out of wipes:** **25% of the run's currencies and no upgrades** ("their story ended").
- **Quitting:** everything is lost (D-176).
**Notes for Claude:** today running out of wipes pays the tally in full and keeps everything (`UTrialRunSubsystem::ExitTrial`); **B-451** changes it. What "upgrades" covers is **Q-451**.

### D-187 — No enemy rank is ever shown to the player
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-13, via the desktop operator session) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 · **Blocks:** B-450
**Answer (Crown):** never label an enemy **"mini-boss"** (or any rank: Normal, Elite, Mini-boss, Boss) to the player: it sets expectations and spoils the encounter. The **wipe card names the opponent that dealt the last blow** instead. The battle's **enemy target card drops "Rank"** and shows the tier only.
**Notes for Claude:** **B-450** sweeps the player-facing strings. The battle HUD mockups (`BattleHUDRefinePlan.md`, the UI Designer's) still draw Rank on the target card: tell their owner. The rest menu's "Revive boss" (D-182) names a rank in Crown's own words: **Q-450**.

### D-186 — Extracting: "Extract", only at a boss's rest spot (and a trial NPC)
**Status:** ANSWERED (Crown, 2026-11-13, via the desktop operator session) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 · **Blocks:** B-448, B-449
**Answer (Crown):** (adds to D-172, D-176; renames D-182's "Leave Trial")
- The rest menu's "Leave Trial" is named **"Extract"**.
- **Only rest spots at bosses are extract points.** A rest spot out in the wild has no Extract option.
- There is **also an NPC in the trial who can extract the party** (design note; its job comes later).
- **Payout:** the current half share (`RunRewards::LEAVE_EARLY_SHARE`) stays the default unless Crown changes it.

**Notes for Claude:** "a rest spot at a boss" is read as the rest spot a beaten mini-boss leaves (ER-09c); beating the main boss ends the run by itself. Today every rest spot's menu offers "Leave the trial" (`WoRRestSpot::LeaveRun`): **B-448** renames it and limits it; **B-449** is the NPC. Rules ER-65c.

### D-175 — The Ability Stone is retired
**Status:** DECIDED (Crown, 2026-11-13, via the desktop operator session) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 · **Blocks:** — (B-437)
**Answer (Crown):** the **Ability Stone** is removed from the game; every other augment stone stays. A weapon's ability slots come only from **the weapon's tier** (F 1 … S 6) **plus the attached crystal's extra slots** (B-098 / B-410). An augment stone no longer adds slots, so a weapon holds **at most 12 abilities** (S tier 6 + an S gem's 6).
**Notes for Claude:** removed in code by **B-437**; old saves per **Q-437**. Supersedes the Ability Stone part of D-145 and Q-351b (its slots by tier) and its essence line in ER-05 / ER-31. Rules CL-20, CL-31.

### D-174 — Same-element infusion stacking
**Status:** DECIDED (Crown, 2026-11-13, via the desktop operator session) · **Raised:** 2026-11-13 · **Blocks:** — (B-435)
**Answer (Crown):** when **two or more** of a character's infusion sources share an element (innate, weapon crystal, adapter, evolution), an infused action uses **all of them**. **Each source pays its own cost** (crystal wear for a crystal source; EP for innate / evolution, plus the HP backlash at level II, ED-30 / ED-32). The hit gets **+25% status buildup per extra source**, capped at **+50%** (`// TODO: tune`). The battle UI shows the element **once, marked ×N**.
**Notes for Claude:** built in **B-435** (rules ED-51). Today an action takes one source (`UActionExecutor::ResolveInfusionSource`); the damage band and size stay the picked level's (only buildup grows). Two named constants: `SAME_ELEMENT_BUILDUP_PER_EXTRA_SOURCE` (0.25) and `SAME_ELEMENT_BUILDUP_BONUS_CAP` (0.50). A Generic (inherit) source counts as the character's element; a broken source gives no element (CL-24), so it doesn't stack.
**Spells (Crown, 2026-11-14, Q-435 B):** spells stack too: a spell stays locked to its origin source's element (adapter, weapon crystal, innate or evolution); every other source the character has with that element joins the cast, each paying its own cost (so a free adapter spell starts costing innate EP when innate shares the element), and each extra source adds +25% buildup, same cap. **Exception:** a Reality or Broken Darkness character may infuse a spell with their own element **or** its source element; whichever they pick, the same stacking applies. Built in B-435b.

### D-172 — No saved runs: extract to leave a run
**Status:** DECIDED (Crown, 2026-11-12, via the desktop operator session) · **Raised:** 2026-11-12 · **Blocks:** — (B-408)
**Answer (Crown):** there are **no saved runs**. To leave a run the party must **extract**; a run can't be quit and resumed later.
**Notes for Claude:** this makes Q-096's "a resumed run starts at x1.0" gap moot. B-208 (save the trial run, Q-090) and the run pieces that followed (`USaveSubsystem::RestoreRun`, B-187c's saved marks and revivals, the run tally) were built for quit-and-resume; **B-408** surveys them against this ruling (what to remove, what to keep, e.g. crash recovery or co-op host hand-over). Survey only: nothing is deleted until Crown agrees.

### D-171 — Hidden quests: found in secret, then tracked in plain words
**Status:** DECIDED (Crown, 2026-11-09, desktop operator session) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 · **Blocks:** — (B-397)
**Answer (Crown)** (model: Shangri-La Frontier's Unique Scenarios; changes D-100's and HiddenQuestSystem's "no text ever says what the next step is"):
- **Starting a hidden quest stays fully hidden:** no hints, no listing, no text about how to trigger it (D-100's "no hints" stands for the start).
- **Once a player triggers it,** that player alone gets a private notice, **"Hidden quest discovered: <title>"** (D-100's private unlocks), and the quest appears in their journal's **Quests** page.
- **From then on, the current step's conditions are shown in plain words** (the step's `Goal` text, the journal list `GetVisibleQuests` from B-388). **Only the current step** is shown; later steps stay hidden until reached. **The end memory and its rewards stay hidden until earned.**
- **Co-op:** each player sees only their own discovered hidden quests.
**Notes for Claude:** hidden quest assets need a `Title` and a `Goal` per step (designer-written `FText`; both fields exist since B-388; placeholder text is fine until Crown writes them). Job: B-397.

---

### D-170 — Visual effects and parry rules: weapon VFX fixed, two guards (weapon / unarmed), crystal parry effect
**Status:** DECIDED (Crown, 2026-11-09, desktop operator session) · **Raised:** 2026-11-09 · **Blocks:** —
**Answer (Crown):**
- **Weapon attack VFX are fixed** per weapon.
- **Adapter and spell-cast VFX:** each adapter type has a default (rings are the default). There is **no per-adapter override**; adapters share the character's own setting with Refractors and Generic.
- **Generic with both a weapon and an adapter:** the class preference decides which VFX shows.
- **Two guards:**
  - The **unarmed guard** (the body) is the default for adapter users, Refractors and Generic, and can be customised per character.
  - The **weapon guard** is that weapon type's own and can't be customised.
  - A per-character setting picks which is used: with a weapon equipped, either parry with it or put it away and parry unarmed. Equipping a weapon makes the weapon guard the default.
- **Landing a parry** (corrected by Crown, 2026-11-09): there is a **parry-effect cosmetic** (e.g. a magic circle), one for the weapon guard and one for the unarmed guard. Weapons always have the default sparks; unarmed has nothing by default. The equipped cosmetic plays on a landed parry, coloured by the **element behind it**: a Refractor's source element, a Resonator's active adapter, or a weapon's own element or its crystal. With no element behind it, unarmed shows nothing. When more than one source applies, a **loadout setting** says which takes priority.
- **Parry timing** is identical in both guards: looks only (reference-backed default: Expedition 33's parry plays the same for every character).
- **There is no separate parry-style cosmetic** beyond the unarmed guard's customisation (Crown changed this the same day).
**Notes for Claude:** plan in `docs/Design/VisualGameplayPlan.md`; jobs B-391 to B-395.
